山水情怀
中文

2. 山水情怀

楠溪江风光秀丽。楠溪江又是小邹鲁。秀丽的风光，与文化中热爱自然之美的传统一起，哺育了楠溪江人的山水情怀。每当丝雨迷濛、烟云舒卷的春季，遍青山开满了杜鹃。山红初谢，又是洁白的桐花烂漫。到了秋深，乌桕树浓艳如血。澄澈的江水，随着时序，映照各种鲜明的颜色。深深受到传统文化熏染的楠溪江文士们，对自然之美很敏感，一种清醒的环境审美意识，在楠溪江人的心中氤氲，并且转化为村落的魅力。它们与美的自然融为一体，还引用自然来装饰点缀，房舍的风格又最朴实。自然是楠溪江乡土建筑的魅力所在。

（1）江山有幸

江山多娇，江山也有幸。公元422年，刘宋永初三年，谢灵运来到永嘉郡任太守。他陶醉于永嘉的山水，"肆意游遨"，写出了中国第一批山水诗。谢灵运守永嘉不到一年，却在楠溪江留下了不少足迹。斤竹涧、白岸、石室涧、绿嶂山，他都题过诗。

乾隆《永嘉县志·舆地》说："往者谢康乐为郡，好游名山，由是此郡山水闻于天下。天下之士行过是邦者亦莫不俯仰留连，吟咏不辍，以诧其胜。"在这些俯仰流连的人里，据乾隆《永嘉县志·名胜志》载，有萧梁的陶弘景。他的名诗《答齐高帝诏》就写于绿嶂山。诗道：

山中何所有？岭上多白云；

只可自怡悦，不堪持寄君。

所以后来就有地名白云岭。乾隆《大若岩记》又说他在小楠溪的大若岩修炼，写了重要的道教典籍《真诰》和《本草集注》、《补阙肘后百一方》两部医书。他修炼的地方叫陶公洞，附近也有一座白云岭。不远的水云村里还造了一座白云亭。传说千古名篇《答谢中书书》描写的就是楠溪江风光。

唐代诗人孟浩然、罗隐和崔道融到过永嘉。白居易到过楠溪江，在大若岩捐钱开水道，筑了两处堤防（见《大若岩记》）。宋代到过永嘉的有陆游，明代则有李东阳。清初学者朱彝尊抗清失败后来到楠溪江，曾住在廊下村。《曝书亭集》里的《永嘉杂咏》，有几首是在廊下村写的。如《华坛望雁荡山歌赠方十三朱生、朱十八振嘉》：

登华坛之绝顶，眺雁岩之回峦。云容容兮欲雨，水嘈嘈兮下山。遥岭出没不可胜数，但见哀禽离兽日暝而俱还。

自康乐公启端，历代诗人们对楠溪江山水"吟咏不辍"。苏轼又钦佩又自信地写诗道："自言长官如灵运，能使江山似永嘉。"永嘉的江山真是幸运。

生活在锦绣河山之中，心田里受着千余年文化的滋润，楠溪江的乡村文人们，对山川草木的美非常敏感也非常热爱。以耕读自娱的读书人，"寄志林泉"，"或临流而歌啸，或倚石而垂钓"，以他们的整个生活和诗文表现对大自然的感情。花坛村的朱伯清，明代人，"丰神秀逸，嗜学有文，不乐仕进，志存林壑。……家事付之诸子，惟以文墨自娱。凡石、树、虫、鱼、水泉、花药之会心寓目者，咸属吟咏其间。遇风和日暖，角巾鹿裘，从以弟子，徘徊乎水光山色。拂云坐石，手挥丝桐，目送飞鸿，逍遥自乐"（《珍川朱氏宗谱·伯清公珍川十咏序》）。所以乾隆《永嘉县志》说，楠溪江"山峰挺秀，涧水呈奇，人生其地者，皆惠中而秀外，温文而尔雅"。

奇秀的山水涵养出楠溪江乡民独有的气质和人文的特色。

这种气质，化而为楠溪江建筑的风格。他们的村落、房舍和园林都朴实无华，然而都舒展开朗。他们善于利用天然材料的本形、本性、本色，使建筑与天地和谐。家家使用不加錾凿的蛮石墙，弧形放足，粗犷有力，矗立在大块卵石铺就的地面上，整个村落仿佛从荒古时代与山岩同时生成。他们也爱用天然原木，不加斤斧，随弯就曲，巧妙地把它们安装在恰当的位置上，它们就好像应该有那样的弯曲和粗糙。带着生命原有的形状，它们与庭前的树木和一切有生命的相呼应。

从上到下，建筑绝少雕琢，木石最大限度地保存着天然本色；少量的加工，出自人类双手天然的能力。这就孕成了楠溪江建筑给人的亲切感。

建筑物的形式也是很自然的。宜廊则廊，宜堂则堂，轻巧的披檐，自在地遮挡着一切应该遮挡的地方。翘曲飘洒的屋顶，像搏击长空的鹰翼那样灵动。

楠溪江人的环境审美意识，也表现在村落的选址、规划、绿化、园林、构图等等上面，使人为居住环境与美丽的山川构成统一的景观。

（2）意在山水之间

开发楠溪江的先人们，没有例外，都把村落建在风景优美的地方。谢灵运的后裔本来定居在郡城，有一次："冼五五公游楠溪，见鹤垟之胜，又自郡城迁居鹤垟。"（《重修鹤垟谢氏宗谱序》）其他各村的宗谱里，也都记载着类似的选址故事。如塘湾村，始迁祖"爱楠溪山水之胜"，而来落户；渠口村始迁祖也是"爱其山水之胜，遂家焉"，等等。

豫章村在小楠溪南岸，进村之前，先要穿密林，过荒江野渡，再穿密林。村后如刻如削的峰峦，层层叠叠，向两侧涌伸伸展。山脚下平畴如带，竹树掩映中，参差百十户人家。江水在村左村右萦回成宽阔的碧潭，隔江正对一座青翠的狮子山。《豫章胡氏宗谱·旧序》（康熙三十四年，1695年）说：

永嘉山水，秀丽无如楠源大小二岩，巉岩耸挺，空兀崆峒，历有仙灵凭居托迹。下此而称名胜，莫如豫章。文峰现沼锺其奇，玉笏横头著其异。其间降岳发祥，代多伟烈，居其下者，则有胡氏。……宗族殷蕃，子孙秀蔚……宗支派衍，霞蒸林郁。

珍溪上游的廊下村，在一条山沟里，四周山形奇突。溪水从东北来，掠过村北、村西，由西南流向花坛村。《珍川朱氏合族副谱·廊下即景诗序》里说：

山连雁荡，入径已觉清幽；地肖龙头，过岭方知奥旷。水环如带，可数游鳞；峰列为屏，时度飞鸟。桑麻蔽其野阡，枫授藏乎盈山。仿佛乎桃源之幽隐，盘谷之窈深焉。

楠溪江两岸古村落的风光大多如此，傍山就水，充分展现环境的美。

（3）保护、点缀、增益

楠溪江人，以他们的文化素养感受环境的美，细腻鲜明。《坦下陈氏宗谱》载康熙时人玠侯先生五言律，描绘坦下村的风景：

团团一派石，绿竹间青松。沙外溶溶水，门前叠叠峰。

山花开屿岸，野鸟唤春风。锄犁能读史，□□振飞鹏。

经过文人们揣摩品评，楠溪江许多村落都有"十景"、"八景"之类，宗谱里也多有《名胜》专篇。谢道宁《鹤垟八景诗》之一《锦嶂春晖》：

万仞屏环仰照临，阳和随见破群阴。岩花呈秀分高下，野树浮光间浅深。

适兴岂无人蹑屐，寻幽还有客抚琴。西郊只隔疏林外，多少红尘乱扑襟。

诗把风光的美和一种自然的生活态度融合在一起。通过追慕先祖康乐公，又把这种生活态度和千年的文化传统联系在一起。

经这些在乡文士倡导，楠溪江人很重视保护自然环境，包括树木。村村有风水林，不许砍伐。村头村尾，古树浓荫蔽天。《棠川郑氏宗谱·新宫坳樟树记》载，新宫坳里有太阴宫，"宫右侧有樟木一株，其大可丈围，其高难尺计。……葱茏在望，经雨露而弥新；新秀可餐，阅风霜而不改。斯固刍荛之所栖息，抑亦竖牧之婆娑也。然蔽烈日，御罡风，位置得宜，其又有关于风水乎？"但是，竟有一些见利忘义之徒，企图砍伐这棵树。"于是村中知事者不敢袖手以旁观，斟酌再三，集款买归宗祠之业，立有字据，永后并不许砍断。"

流连，吟咏，保护，都不足以充分表达楠溪江人的山水情怀，他们进而要为山川生色。大小村落，往往都有台榭亭阁之类的风景小品建筑物。记载最早的是北宋进士、宝章阁侍制陈除师在蓬下村，"筑一笑、拨云二亭于富岩以观瀑"（见《两源陈氏大宗祠谱》）。比较著名的则有谢公后裔的鹤垟村里几座小品建筑。宗谱载，元代至正年间，谢毅孙"襟怀潇洒，雅爱宾客。……善音乐，喜与人吟咏，……与西席陈宪宾主义洽，相与筑台于东山之上，植兰种竹，取康乐公遗言镌其岩曰兰玉台。师生经暇，憩息其间，以消长夜。朋友倡和，成《兰玉台集》"。

到了明代中叶，任职锦衣卫的谢廷循"创楼于鹤溪之西，因楼傍清流，故名曰'临清'，以为宴宾吟咏之所"，同时在"村北水溪"造了一座"临流亭"。廷循绘临流亭图呈明宣宗，宣宗竟题了一首诗：

临流亭馆净无尘，落涧泉声处处闻。半湿半干花上露，飞来飞去岭头云。

翠迷洞口竹千个，白占林梢鹤一群。此地清幽人不到，惟留风月与平分。

这座亭子因此也叫"宝翰亭"。

对山水点缀增益，仍然不能满足文士们的一往情深，他们动手创建。楠溪江的"隐士"大多喜欢"锄园种花，凿池开圃"，布置私家小园。《乐安珍川朱氏宗谱》载廊下村朱映峰作《隐居歌》道：

非士亦非农，半耕还半读。傍山数顷田，临水几间屋。筑园又凿池，栽花复种竹。……花自吐清香，竹亦言芳郁，池水漾芰荷，园蔬借蓓苜。

明代中叶，花坛村的朱谧，"家颇饶，经营堂构，馀址凿池开圃，植花养鱼，以为宗戚朋旧壶觞吟咏笑傲处"。约略同时，同村朱闿轩有《石假山》诗一首，首联是"谁欤挺秀若天然，叠翠层峦景万千"（均见《珍川朱氏宗谱》），可见当时山村造园已经有叠假山技艺。岩头村的上花园，苍坡村的水月堂，芙蓉村芙蓉书院山长住宅和"大宅"都有小园，至今古木参天，花墙和假山残迹依稀可见。

山水情怀，造就了楠溪江村落最大的特色之一，大型的公共园林。岩头、苍坡、溪口、渠口、埭头、珠岸、西岸、鹤盛等村的公共园林大体还保存着。埭头村、鹤盛村和鹤垟村的公园在山坡上，西岸村的在江边，古木森森，远远就能望见。岩头村的公园，纯藉人力，内容最丰富、布局最多变化，是明代嘉靖年间兴造的。它包含一座小小的汤山，一片镇南湖、一片进宦湖，和它们间的琴屿半岛，还有一条300米长的丽水湖。两个湖都由人工堤坝拦蓄溪水形成，堤上古木数株，绿荫下一座精巧玲珑的小亭，汤山顶上有塔，山麓有楼阁和庙。湖中荷花盛开，岛上芙蓉灿烂如霞。

公园是村民们公共活动场所，尤其是乡村文人们的。岩头村的《十景诗》描绘了他们在公园里的活动。《长堤春晓》诗句有："结伴连朝频载酒，行吟不惜绕长堤"；《丽桥观荷》诗有句："坐对嫣然如解语，乘风散步纳凉时"；《清沼观鱼》诗有句："绍堤花柳可行歌，笑看游鳞跳锦波"；《曲流环碧》诗有句："不知把钓垂竿子，坐对渔矶乐若何？"这些诗里，乡村文士们的生活和意趣宛然可见，也可见他们如痴如醉的山水情怀。

这些乡村文士在公园里过着高品位的文化生活，必然会把他们深湛的文化修养带到公园的一草一木中去。所以这些园林和它们的建筑的风格都很轻逸淡雅。诗情画意，不脱乡土本色。

贯穿在楠溪江中游村落建设中的山水情怀和生活文化，是楠溪江中游乡土建筑不朽的魅力所在。

English

2. landscape sentiment

Nanxi River scenery is beautiful. Nanxi River is also "Little Zou-Lu" (Confucian cultural heartland). Beautiful scenery, together with the cultural tradition of loving natural beauty, nurtured Nanxi River people's landscape sentiment. Every spring, when misty rain and rolling clouds fill the air, azaleas bloom across green mountains.

As mountain red fades, white paulownia flowers burst forth. In deep autumn, tallow trees turn vivid as blood. Clear river water, following seasons, reflects various bright colors.

Nanxi River literati, deeply influenced by traditional culture, are very sensitive to natural beauty; a sober environmental aesthetic consciousness permeates Nanxi River people's hearts, and transforms into village charm. They integrate with beautiful nature, also引用 nature for decoration, while house style is simplest.

Nature is the charm of Nanxi River vernacular architecture.

(1) Blessed Mountains and Rivers

Mountains and rivers are beautiful, and also blessed. In 422 CE, Liu-Song Yongchu third year, Xie Lingyun came to Yongjia Commandery as governor. He was intoxicated by Yongjia's mountains and waters, "recklessly roaming," writing China's first batch of landscape poetry.

Xie Lingyun governed Yongjia less than a year, yet left many traces in Nanxi River. Jinzhu Stream, Bai'an, Shishi Stream, Lüzhang Mountain — he all wrote poems about.

Qianlong "Yongjia County Annals · Geography" says: "formerly Duke Kangle (Xie Lingyun) as governor, loved touring famous mountains, hence this commandery's mountains and waters became known worldwide.

Scholars passing through this region all linger, chanting endlessly, praising its beauty." Among those who lingered, according to Qianlong "Yongjia County Annals · Scenic Spots," was Xiao-Liang's Tao Hongjing. His famous poem "Reply to Emperor Gao of Qi's Edict" was written at Lüzhang Mountain:

What is in the mountains? Many white clouds on the ridges;

Only for self-enjoyment, cannot be sent to you, my lord.

Hence later place name Baiyun (White Cloud) Ridge.

Qianlong "Daluo Rock Record" also says he practiced Daoist cultivation at Daluo Rock on Minor Nanxi River, writing important Daoist text "Zhengao" and two medical books "Collected Commentaries on Materia Medica" and "Supplementary Elbow-side Remedies." His cultivation place is called Tao Gong Cave; nearby is also a Baiyun Ridge.

Not far away in Shuiyun village, a Baiyun Pavilion was built. Legend says the eternal masterpiece "Letter in Reply to Secretary Xie" describes Nanxi River scenery.

Tang poets Meng Haoran, Luo Yin and Cui Daorong visited Yongjia. Bai Juyi visited Nanxi River, donating money to open water channels and build two dikes at Daluo Rock (see "Daluo Rock Record"). Song dynasty Lu You visited Yongjia; Ming dynasty Li Dongyang.

Early Qing scholar Zhu Yizun, after failing in anti-Qing resistance, came to Nanxi River and lived in Langxia village. In "Baishuting Collection," "Yongjia Miscellaneous Poems" has several pieces written in Langxia village. Such as "Huatan Viewing Yandang Mountain Song, Presented to Fang Shisan, Zhu Sheng, Zhu Shibazhenjia":

Climb Huatan's highest peak, gaze at Yandang's winding ridges. Clouds swelling, about to rain; water rushing, descending mountains. Distant ridges appear and disappear beyond count, only mournful birds and separated beasts return at dusk.

Since Duke Kangle began it, generations of poets "chanted endlessly" about Nanxi River mountains and waters. Su Shi, both admiring and confident, wrote: "Saying the governor is like Lingyun, can make mountains and rivers like Yongjia." Yongjia's mountains and rivers are truly blessed.

Living amid splendid mountains and rivers, hearts nourished by over a thousand years of culture, Nanxi River rural literati are very sensitive to and deeply love the beauty of mountains, rivers, plants and trees.

Scholars who amused themselves with farming and reading, "lodged aspirations in forests and springs," "sometimes sang by flowing water, sometimes angled leaning on rocks," expressing their feelings for nature through their entire life and poetry.

Huatan village's Zhu Boqing, Ming dynasty person, "elegant bearing, fond of learning and literary, disliking official service, aspirations in forests and valleys. ... Family affairs entrusted to sons, only amused himself with writing and ink.

Whatever rocks, trees, insects, fish, springs, flowers and herbs caught his heart and eye, all became subjects of chanting. When weather warm and sunny, wearing scholar's cap and deerskin robe, followed by disciples, wandering among water and mountain light.

Brushing clouds, sitting on rocks, playing qin, watching flying geese, carefree and joyful" ("Zhenchuan Zhu clan Genealogy · Preface to Boqing Gong's Zhenchuan Ten Poems"). Therefore Qianlong "Yongjia County Annals" says Nanxi River "peaks graceful, streams wondrous; people born there are wise within and elegant without, gentle and refined."

Wondrous mountains and waters nurtured Nanxi River villagers' unique temperament and humanistic character.

This temperament transformed into Nanxi River architectural style. Their villages, houses and gardens are all plain and unadorned, yet open and bright. They are skilled at using natural materials' original form, nature and color, making architecture harmonious with heaven and earth.

Every household uses uncarved rough stone walls, curved and broad, rugged and powerful, standing on ground paved with large cobblestones — the whole village seems to have been born with the rocks from primeval times.

They also love using natural raw timber, without axe or chisel, following curves, cleverly installing them in appropriate positions — they seem meant to have that curvature and roughness. Bearing life's original shape, they echo with courtyard trees and all living things.

From top to bottom, architecture rarely carved; timber and stone maximally preserve natural color; minimal processing, from human hands' natural ability. This nurtures the亲切感 of Nanxi River architecture.

Building forms are also very natural. Where corridor suits, corridor; where hall suits, hall. Light lean-to eaves freely shelter whatever should be sheltered. Curved and flowing roofs, as nimble as eagle wings striking the sky.

Nanxi River people's environmental aesthetic consciousness also manifests in village site selection, planning, greening, gardens, composition, etc., making man-made living environment and beautiful mountains and rivers form a unified landscape.

(2) Intent Amid Mountains and Waters

Pioneers who developed Nanxi River, without exception, built villages in scenic places. Xie Lingyun's descendants originally settled in commandery city; once: "Xian Wuwu Gong toured Nanxi, saw Heyang's beauty, then moved from commandery city to Heyang" ("Preface to Rebuilding Heyang Xie clan Genealogy").

Other villages' genealogies also record similar site-selection stories. Tangwan village, founding ancestor "loved Nanxi mountains and waters' beauty," came to settle; Qukou village founding ancestor also "loved its mountains and waters' beauty, then made home there," etc.

Yuzhang village is on south bank of Minor Nanxi River; before entering village, must first pass dense forest, cross desolate river crossing, then pass dense forest again. Behind village, carved and chiseled peaks, layer upon layer, stretch outward on both sides.

At mountain foot, flat fields like ribbons; amid bamboo and trees, uneven hundred-some households. River winds around village left and right into broad green pools; across river faces a verdant Lion Mountain. "Yuzhang Hu clan Genealogy · Old Preface" (Kangxi 34th year, 1695) says:

Yongjia mountains and waters, none as beautiful as Nan Source's two large and small rocks; craggy and towering, hollow and deep, through ages immortals dwelt and left traces. Below these as scenic spots, none like Yuzhang. Literary Peak reflected in pool captures its wonder; Jade Tablet across head marks its uniqueness.

Here auspiciousness descended, generations of great achievements; those dwelling below are the Hu clan. ... clan prosperous, descendants elegant... clan branches spread, like rosy clouds and lush forests.

Langxia village, upper reaches of Zhenxi Stream, lies in a mountain valley with strangely shaped surrounding mountains. Stream comes from northeast, passes village north and west, flows southwest toward Huatan village. "Zhenchuan Zhu clan Combined Genealogy · Preface to Langxia Scenic Poems" says:

Mountains connect to Yandang; entering path already feels secluded; land resembles dragon head, crossing ridge only then knows depth and openness. Water rings like a belt, one can count swimming fish; peaks array as screen, birds pass from time to time. Mulberry and hemp cover field paths, maples hide full mountains. Like the seclusion of Peach Blossom Spring, the depth of Pan Valley.

Nanxi River两岸 ancient villages' scenery is mostly like this — leaning on mountains and following waters, fully displaying environmental beauty.

(3) protection, Embellishment, Enhancement

Nanxi River people, with their cultural cultivation, feel environmental beauty delicately and vividly. "Tanxia Chen clan Genealogy" records Kangxi-era Mr. Jiehou's five-character regulated verse describing tanxia village scenery:

Round stretch of rocks, green bamboo interspersed with pine. Beyond sand, flowing water; before door, layered peaks.

Mountain flowers bloom on isle shores, wild birds call in spring breeze. Plow and hoe can read history, □□ soar like rocs.

After literati's appreciation and evaluation, many Nanxi River villages have "Ten Scenes," "Eight Scenes" and the like; genealogies also often have "Scenic Spots" special chapters. Xie Daoning's "Heyang Eight Scenes Poems" one "Brocade Cliff Spring Glory":

Ten-thousand-ren screen surrounds, welcoming light; warmth follows, breaking group shadows. Cliff flowers show beauty high and low; wild trees float light between shallow and deep.

For enjoyment, aren't there people in clogs? Seeking seclusion, still guests play qin. West suburb only separated by sparse forest, how much red dust rushes to collars.

Poetry integrates scenic beauty with a natural life attitude. Through admiring ancestor Duke Kangle, this life attitude connects with thousand-year cultural tradition.

Advocated by these local literati, Nanxi River people value protecting natural environment, including trees. Every village has fengshui forest, forbidden to cut. village heads and tails, ancient trees cast dense shade.

"Tangchuan Zheng clan Genealogy · Record of Camphor Tree at Xingong'ao" records: at Xingong'ao there is Taiyin Palace, "right of palace stands a camphor tree, circumference over a zhang, height beyond measure. ... Lush in view, fresher after rain and dew; new beauty to savor, unchanged through wind and frost.

It is where woodcutters rest, where shepherds linger. Yet it shields fierce sun, withstands strong winds, well-positioned, and also relates to fengshui?" However, some profit-forgetting people attempted to cut this tree.

"Thus village leaders dared not stand by; after careful consideration, collected funds to buy it as ancestral hall property, established documents, forever forbidding cutting."

Wandering, chanting, protecting — none sufficient to fully express Nanxi River people's landscape sentiment; they further sought to enhance mountains and rivers. Large and small villages often have scenic accessory buildings like terraces, pavilions and towers.

Earliest recorded: Northern Song jinshi and Baozhang Pavilion Attendant Chen Chushi at Pengxia village, "built Yixiao and Boyun two pavilions at Fuyan to view waterfalls" (see "Liangyuan Chen clan Main ancestral hall Genealogy"). More famous are several accessory buildings in Heyang village of Xie Gong descendants.

Genealogy records: Yuan Zhizheng era, Xie Yisun "broad-minded, loved guests. ... skilled in music, fond of chanting with others, ... with tutor Chen Xianbin in harmony, together built terrace on East Mountain, planted orchids and bamboo, taking Duke Kangle's last words to carve on rock: Lanyu Terrace.

Teacher and student, in leisure, rested there, to pass long nights.

Friends exchanged poems, forming 'Lanyu Terrace Collection'." By mid-Ming, Xie Tingxun of Embroidered-Uniform Guard "built tower west of Hexi Stream, because tower beside clear stream, named 'Linqing' (Facing Clarity), as place for banqueting guests and chanting"; simultaneously built "Linliu Pavilion" (Facing Stream Pavilion) at "village north stream." Tingxun painted Linliu Pavilion and presented to Ming Xuanzong; Xuanzong actually inscribed a poem:

Facing-stream pavilion pure without dust, falling stream spring sounds heard everywhere. Half-wet half-dry dew on flowers, flying back and forth clouds on ridge heads.

Green迷 cave mouth thousand bamboos, white占 forest tips a flock of cranes. This place secluded, people don't come, only wind and moon share equally.

This pavilion hence also called "Baohan Pavilion" (Precious Calligraphy Pavilion).

Embellishing and enhancing mountains and waters still couldn't satisfy literati's deep affection; they took to creating. Nanxi River "hermits" mostly liked "hoeing gardens and planting flowers, digging pools and opening plots," arranging private small gardens. "Le'an Zhenchuan Zhu clan Genealogy" records Langxia village Zhu Yingfeng's "Hermit Song":

Not scholar nor farmer, half farming half reading. By mountains several qing of fields, by water several rooms. Build garden and dig pool, plant flowers and bamboo. ... Flowers emit fragrance, bamboo also speaks of fragrance; pool water ripples with lotus, garden vegetables借 tender shoots.

Mid-Ming, Huatan village's Zhu Mi, "family fairly wealthy, built halls, on remaining site dug pool and opened garden, planted flowers and raised fish, as place for relatives and friends to drink, chant and laugh." Around same time, fellow villager Zhu Kaixuan had "Rock Artificial Mountain" poem, first couplet: "Who stands graceful as if natural, layered green peaks scenery myriad" (both in "Zhenchuan Zhu clan Genealogy") — showing mountain village gardening already had artificial mountain stacking technique by then.

Yantou village's Upper Garden, Cangpo village's shuiyue hall, Furong village's Furong academy mountain-chief residence and "Great Mansion" all had small gardens; to this day ancient trees tower, flower walls and artificial mountain remnants faintly visible.

landscape sentiment created one of Nanxi River villages' greatest features: large public gardens. Public gardens of Yantou, Cangpo, Xikou, Qukou, Daitou, Zhu'an, Xi'an, Hesheng villages etc. are largely preserved. Daitou, Hesheng and Heyang villages' parks are on hillsides; Xi'an village's by the river, ancient trees dense, visible from afar.

Yantou village's park, purely by human effort, richest in content and most varied in layout, was built in Ming Jiajing era. It includes a small Tang Mountain, a Zhennan Lake, a Jinhuan Lake, the Qinyu Peninsula between them, and a 300-meter Lishui Lake.

Both lakes formed by人工 dikes blocking stream water; on dikes several ancient trees, under green shade an exquisite small pavilion; Tang Mountain top has pagoda, mountain foot has pavilion and temple. In lake, lotus blooms abundantly; on peninsula, hibiscus brilliant as rosy clouds.

Parks are villagers' public activity places, especially for rural literati. Yantou village's "Ten Scenes Poems" depict their activities in the park.

"Long Dike Spring Dawn" has lines: "Companions together repeatedly carry wine, walking and chanting not begrudging circling long dike"; "Li bridge Viewing Lotus" has: "Sitting facing her as if understanding speech, riding wind walking to enjoy cool"; "Clear Pool Viewing Fish" has: "Shao dike flowers and willows can sing along, laughing at swimming scales leaping in brocade waves"; "Winding Stream Encircling Green" has: "Don't know the angler with rod, sitting by fishing rock how joyful?" In these poems, rural literati's life and taste are vividly visible, as is their intoxicated landscape sentiment.

These rural literati living high-grade cultural life in parks inevitably bring their profound cultural cultivation to every plant and tree of the park. Therefore these gardens and their buildings' style are all light and elegant. Poetic and picturesque, yet not departing from vernacular character.

The landscape sentiment and life culture running through Middle Nanxi River village construction is the immortal charm of Middle Nanxi River vernacular architecture.

本篇术语 Key Terms

山水情怀 / Landscape sentiment 小邹鲁 / Little Zou-Lu 谢灵运 / Xie Lingyun 陶弘景 / Tao Hongjing 山水诗 / Landscape poetry 白居易 / Bai Juyi 孟浩然 / Meng Haoran 陆游 / Lu You 李东阳 / Li Dongyang 朱彝尊 / Zhu Yizun 苏轼 / Su Shi 大若岩 / Daluo Rock 陶公洞 / Tao Gong Cave 真诰 / Zhengao (Daoist text) 绿嶂山 / Lüzhang Mountain ...等30个
8
宗族组织的作用
中文

1. 宗族组织的作用

楠溪江的村落几乎都是血缘村落，一村一姓，一个宗族。虽然有政府的行政系统，但是在小农经济的农业社会，宗族组织实际上是血缘村落的政权机构。所以，尽管在村子里有大大小小的宗祠，有些村子甚至有宗祠十几座，却没有一个村子有地方行政机构的公廨。

两汉、南北朝和隋唐沿袭了将近一千年的门阀制度，到了宋代彻底消失。代之而起的社会组织力量就是宗族。范仲淹、欧阳询和苏轼都曾经顺应历史潮流，为加强宗族地位和作用有所创建。欧阳询和苏轼倡导宗谱学，各自设计了谱式，沿用了一千来年。范仲淹则提倡宗族设义田，抚老恤贫，保证族人"日有食、岁有衣、嫁娶婚葬皆有赡"，从而加强了宗族的社会功能，加强了它的凝聚力。宋代的理学家重修身，吕大钧首先提倡《乡约》，在家乡陕西蓝田推行之后，"关中风俗为之一变"。乡约是理学家用他们所阐释的儒家礼乐来教化乡民的生活公约，它规范乡民们的思维方式、行为方式和价值观念。宗族是推行乡约的组织力量。

宋室南渡之后，朱熹在南方推行吕氏乡约。楠溪江的主要宗族大都在这时期形成，产生了自己的理学家，并与朱熹有来往。

到了明代，先任永嘉县令，后来又任温州知府的文林，在知府任上于弘治十一年（1498年）制定了一套《族范》，基本内容与吕氏《乡约》一脉相承，在温属五邑推行，楠溪江"各族应之"，纷纷写入宗谱，成为"家训"、"族规"或者"宗范"。

《族范》是宗族的"基本法"，它的《掌教名数》则是宗族的组织法，主要内容是：

一、族宾（按：无说明）。

二、族献（按：无说明）。

三、族长：族长乃一族之统领，必先正己而后正人，务秉至公以御群子弟。……必使上和下睦，同敦雍厚之风。

四、族正：设学行兼优四人为族正，以匡族长之不逮也。凡族内有事，先当咨于族正，然后白于族长之前，为之分辨曲直，析正是非，庶有统属而无紊乱，抑亦正风俗而免事端矣！

五、主籍：择公直子弟二人为主籍，掌管善恶文簿……如此则劝惩之法立。

六、司学：择取性行端庄、学识明达者一人主之，专一训诲弟子，讲习诗书礼义等事。……

七、司讼：推族内洞明事理二人为司讼。凡族之有讼者凭其禀白族长、族正，劝谕不止乃率于官治之，毋得轻造公庭。

八、司恤：司恤二人。凡族人及乡里亲戚贫穷患难，丧葬嫁娶力不赡者，司恤为之白于族长前，劝谕族中之有力者周恤之。……

九、司直：推性刚直，遇事敢言者二人为司直，凡族长有过，从容指陈于族正前，以凭劝勉。族正有过，陈于族长前，以凭规戒。族众有过，轻则直接以屈之，重则白于族长斥之，使之改过自新而后已。

十、司纠：推举铁面无私、不避嫌怨者七人司之。凡族人为恶不听劝谕者，司纠率至家庙，由族长重责。再不听，从官惩之。

十一、值月：举老幼各一人为值月，凡一月之内，一应大小族人所为善恶及词讼等事不报于主籍者，值月必询访而报于各执事，各执事书于簿籍，以白于族长。

这个《掌教名数》把宗族组织规定成很完备的政权机构。《枫林徐氏宗谱·族范八条》里，甚至规定可以动用"祖宗家法"，把"孽深害大，素性又终不肯改移"的盗窃犯处死，"令其全身自毙"。宗族也可以在必要时组织"乡勇"、"义兵"这样的武装力量，进行战争。

宗族组织要负责非一家一户所能办的农田水利的建设和管理。《珍川朱氏宗谱·宗法》里规定，由族中派人"包灌稻田水，节省人工、水料，避免争端，此乃联宗睦族之道"，并且详细写明了各种气候情况下各类地块的灌溉顺序和方法。

宗族有公有经济，包括公田、祭田、学田、义田之类的收入。宗谱里严格规定，公有田产不得变卖。

宗族又是培植和维护社会意识的强大力量。文林的《族范》规定：

凡遇春秋祭祀之时，朔望参谒之日，族长、族正以下，依次而坐，令弟子三人北面而立，读太祖高皇帝《旧制》。其词曰："孝顺父母，尊敬长上，和睦乡里，教训子孙，各安生理，毋作非为。"族属皆跪听。又读古灵陈先生《劝谕文》，曰："为吾民者，父义、母慈、兄友、弟恭、子孝；夫妇有恩、男女有别、子弟有学、乡闾有礼；贫穷患难、亲戚相救，婚姻死丧、邻保相助；毋惰农业，毋作盗贼，毋学赌博，毋好争讼；毋以恶凌善，毋以富骄贫；行者逊路，耕者让畔，斑白者不负戴于道路，则为礼义之俗矣。"族属立听。

有些宗谱里规定得更隆重，朗诵每篇之前要击鼓三声。朗诵时"各要悚然而听，如有在班诒笑闲谈者，族正举于族前，量行责罚，以警将来"。

物质力量加上精神力量，使得宗族组织的力量非常强大，吞没了政府机构。这样强大的宗族力量，足可以管理关系到整个宗族利益的村落规划和建设了。这是楠溪江村落大多有完整的规划，有管理得很好的公共生活中心的主要原因。但是，除了族长的任命按惯例"以齿不以爵，以齿不以尊"之外，其他从族正以下各种职司其实都只能由读书有文化的人担当。其结果就是，农村的各项权力基本都掌握在士绅乡贤手里。可以说，他们的素质决定村落的命运，决定它的面貌。

从各村的宗谱里可以见到，村落里比较重要的建设成就，都是由几位热心公益的乡绅文人的创议和主持，并且得到宗族的组织保证。《棠川郑氏宗谱》里有三篇重要的文章：《长堤记》、《池塘记》和《新城记》，它们很详明地记述了事关全村安危的重要工程的建设和管理规矩。

English

1. Role of clan Organization

Nanxi River villages are almost all kinship villages — one village, one surname, one clan. Although there is government administrative system, in an agricultural society of small-scale peasant economy, clan organization was actually the political institution of kinship villages.

Therefore, although villages have ancestral halls large and small, some even over a dozen, no village had a local administrative office.

The clan system (menfa) that persisted for nearly a thousand years through Han, Northern and Southern Dynasties, Sui and Tang completely disappeared by Song dynasty. The social organizational force that replaced it was the clan.

Fan Zhongyan, Ouyang Xun and Su Shi all followed historical trends, creating institutions to strengthen clan status and role. Ouyang Xun and Su Shi advocated genealogy studies, each designing genealogical formats used for over a thousand years.

Fan Zhongyan advocated clans establishing charity fields (yitian), caring for the elderly and poor, ensuring clan members "have food daily, clothing yearly, weddings and funerals all supported" — thereby strengthening clan's social function and cohesion.

Song Neo-Confucians valued self-cultivation; Lü Dajun first advocated "village Covenant" (Xiangyue), which after implementation in his hometown Lantian, Shaanxi, "changed Guanzhong customs." village Covenant was a life convention by which Neo-Confucians used their interpreted Confucian rites and music to educate villagers; it regulated villagers' thinking, behavior and values.

The clan was the organizational force for implementing village covenants.

After Song court moved south, Zhu Xi promoted Lü's village Covenant in the south. Nanxi River's main clans mostly formed in this period, producing their own Neo-Confucian philosophers who associated with Zhu Xi.

In Ming dynasty, Wen Lin — first Yongjia County magistrate, later Wenzhou Prefect — in his prefect tenure in Hongzhi eleventh year (1498) formulated a set of "clan Norms" (Zufan), basic content in same lineage as Lü's "village Covenant," implemented in five Wenzhou counties; Nanxi River "all clans responded," writing them into genealogies as "family instructions," "clan rules" or "clan norms."

"clan Norms" was the clan's "basic law"; its "Names and Numbers of Teaching Officers" was the clan's organizational law, main content:

1. clan Guest (note: no explanation).

2. clan Worthy (note: no explanation).

3. clan Head: clan head is leader of the clan, must first rectify self then rectify others, must uphold perfect fairness to govern all juniors. ... Must make upper harmonious and lower friendly, together cultivating generous ethos.

4. clan Rectifiers: Appoint four of both learning and conduct as clan rectifiers, to assist clan head's shortcomings. Whenever clan matters arise, first consult clan rectifiers, then report to clan head, to distinguish right and wrong, analyze truth — thus there is order without chaos, also rectifying customs and avoiding disputes!

5. Chief Registrar: Select two fair and upright juniors as chief registrars, in charge of good and evil record books... thus reward and punishment methods established.

6. Education Officer: Select one of upright character and clear learning to lead, solely instructing juniors, studying poetry, books, rites and righteousness. ...

7. Litigation Officer: Select two of clear reasoning in clan as litigation officers. Whenever clan members have disputes, they report to clan head and rectifiers; if persuasion fails, then lead to official treatment, not lightly bringing to court.

8. Relief Officer: Two relief officers. Whenever clan members and relatives suffer poverty and hardship, unable to afford funerals, weddings and marriages, relief officers report to clan head, persuading capable clan members to assist. ...

9. Candid Officer: Select two of upright character and bold speech as candid officers; whenever clan head has fault, calmly point out to clan rectifiers for persuasion. Whenever clan rectifier has fault, report to clan head for admonition. Whenever clan members have fault, minor directly corrected, major reported to clan head for reprimand, until they reform.

10. Discipline Officer: Select seven of iron-faced impartiality, avoiding neither suspicion nor resentment, as discipline officers. Whenever clan members do evil and ignore persuasion, discipline officers lead them to ancestral hall, where clan head severely punishes. If still不听, then official punishment.

11. Monthly Rotation: Appoint one elder and one junior as monthly rotation; within one month, all good and evil deeds and litigation matters not reported to chief registrar, monthly rotation must inquire and report to each officer, each officer records in books, to report to clan head.

This "Names and Numbers of Teaching Officers" defined clan organization as a quite complete political institution.

"Fenglin Xu clan Genealogy · Eight clan Norms" even stipulated that "ancestral family law" could be used to execute thieves "whose harm is deep and great, whose nature ultimately refuses reform," "letting them die naturally." clans could also when necessary organize armed forces like "local braves" (xiangyong) and "righteous soldiers" (yibing) for warfare.

clan organization was responsible for farmland water conservancy construction and management beyond individual households.

"Zhenchuan Zhu clan Genealogy · clan Law" stipulated that clan-appointed persons "manage paddy field irrigation, saving labor and water, avoiding disputes — this is the way to unite clans and harmonize kinship," and detailed irrigation order and methods for various plots under various weather conditions.

clans had public economy, including income from public fields, sacrificial fields, study fields, charity fields. Genealogies strictly stipulated public fields could not be sold.

clans were also powerful forces cultivating and maintaining social consciousness. Wen Lin's "clan Norms" stipulated:

Whenever spring and autumn sacrifices, or first and fifteenth day visits, clan head, rectifiers and below sit in order; let three juniors face north and read Emperor Taizu Gao's "Old Regulations." Text: "Be filial to parents, respect elders, harmonize village, teach juniors, each settle in livelihood, do no wrong." All clan members kneel and listen.

Then read Mr.

Guling Chen's "Admonition": "As my people: father righteous, mother kind, brother friendly, younger brother respectful, son filial; husband and wife have affection, men and women have distinction, juniors have learning, village has rites; poverty and hardship — relatives rescue; marriage and death — neighbors assist; do not be lazy in farming, do not be thieves, do not gamble, do not litigate; do not bully good with evil, do not arrogant to poor with wealth; walkers yield road, farmers yield field borders, gray-haired not burdened on roads — then it is a rite-and-righteousness custom." All clan members stand and listen.

Some genealogies stipulated more ceremoniously: before reciting each text, beat drum three times. During recitation "all must listen with awe; if anyone jokes or chats in ranks, clan rectifier raises before clan, punishes proportionally, to warn future."

Material force plus spiritual force made clan organization very powerful, swallowing government institutions. Such powerful clan force was sufficient to manage village planning and construction concerning entire clan interests. This is the main reason most Nanxi River villages had complete planning and well-managed public life centers.

However, except clan head appointed by convention "by age not rank, by age not status," all other positions from clan rectifier down actually could only be held by educated literate people. Result: rural power basically held in hands of gentry and local worthies. One can say their quality determines village's fate and appearance.

From villages' genealogies, one sees village's more important construction achievements were all proposed and led by several public-spirited local gentry literati, with clan organizational guarantee.

"Tangchuan Zheng clan Genealogy" contains three important articles: "Long Dike Record," "pond Record" and "New City Record" — they detail construction and management rules of important projects concerning whole village safety.

本篇术语 Key Terms

宗族组织 / Clan organization 血缘村落 / Kinship village 宗祠 / Ancestral hall 门阀制度 / Aristocratic clan system (Menfa) 范仲淹 / Fan Zhongyan 苏轼 / Su Shi 义田 / Charity fields (Yitian) 吕大钧 / Lü Dajun 乡约 / Village covenant (Xiangyue) 朱熹 / Zhu Xi 文林 / Wen Lin 族范 / Clan norms (Zufan) 族长 / Clan head 族正 / Clan rectifier 司学 / Education officer ...等30个
9
士绅的作用
中文

2. 士绅的作用

楠溪江村落的士绅乡贤，主要是辞官还乡的、绝意仕进的和举业不成的读书人，他们组成了乡里社会的领导阶层。他们受过良好的文化教育，在论学吟诗之余，大多关心乡里，乐于"善举"，从而把他们的文化修养带进了村落的建设。

村落的规划一般都在建村之初进行，大多失于记述，早已没有可靠的资料。但各族宗谱里，都记载许多有功于乡里建设的人，也记载了一些有长远影响的建设工程。

例如岩头村，《金氏宗谱》记载了第二世祖日新公（生元中统丙子，卒元至正戊子，即1348年）建设水利的事："时厥土苦于旱潦，频岁不登，府君相地宜，顺水性，浚两渠于渎头之南，达泉下灌，常获丰稔，两都农业，迄今赖之。"其后又有桂林公（生明弘治甲寅，卒隆庆已已，1494-1569年），宗谱说他："由始迁岩头以来，列祖非无建造，而兴利之多，功德之盛，应推府君为第一。"他的作为是"培风水、兴地利、置祭田、建公业。

""本族地址颇高，田苦旱涸，升四公(按即桂林公)捐旧废资，开凿长河一带，以备蓄泄，并筑高埠，培闸风水。建亭造塔于其上，垂成，归之大宗祠，为通族公业。"约略与桂林公同时，嘉靖进士金昭（霞峰公）建设了上花园、下花园、大宗祠，还为自己建了个牌楼。先后有几个人从事乡里建设，所以岩头村的水系、街道、公共建筑、园林等等，在楠溪江都是最出色的。

苍坡村和芙蓉村，规划严谨、水系完备、街道整齐，苍坡村还有大片的园林，这些也都靠两村历来有热心建设的人士。筑长堤蓄水成湖，在苍坡大约是南宋时代（1178年）的事，早于岩头几近四百年。芙蓉村陈氏的有绪公（生康熙壬申，卒乾隆癸酉，1692-1753年），"有经济济才，读书知大义。……乐于济施。创神庙，建祖祠，增置祀田，造舟以济往来，筑防以利灌溉。公平生所为，有功于族党者未易枚举"（《两源陈氏宗谱》）。

《棠川郑氏宗谱》里有一篇《长堤记》，记的是清代初年，乾隆丁卯（1747年）春，宗人郑西献捐资并组织人力建造堤堰的事。记中写道：

棠川胜区在双溪会所，衔远山、吞长流，前有雷峰九嶂之异，后有马石天岩之险，至若屏风纳日，和合留云，在在称奇。惟路尚缺一堤，虽乔木荫翳，水声潺湲，而往来行人，往往苦此。今则累石为堤，不惜工程，自东岭至西岭，约数十馀丈，状如江塘，平坦可以车行。

除了水利、宗祠、庙宇、道路之外，书院历来是乡里建设的重点，吸引了士绅们最多的注意。《珍川朱氏合族副谱·慎轩公传》说，公"生平乐善好施，而于读书一事尤其所笃，尝虑族子弟辈无肄业所，不克振长人材，作兴后学，爱捐重赀，襄诸乡之知名者创建文昌阁两庑，以为讲学之所"。也有一些对环境绿化有特殊兴趣的，如花坛村的朱复翁，"好读书，朴而不滞，惟亲贤取友以自励。……洪武初，诏山林隐逸，郡县强荐于京，授朝列大夫，乞归，许之，声名益重。建宗祠，置祭田，以奉祀先人。筑室芳岙，植松树数万株以自蔽，因以云松自号。……啸傲云松间，商榷古今，抚琴作诗"（《明征授朝列大夫云松公墓志铭》）。

这些乡贤士绅，在乡土建设中主要起的作用当然是倡议、擘划、捐资，至于工程的主持，就要依靠宗族的力量。例如，康熙二十一年（1682年）六月，为重建渠口叶氏大宗祠，由宗人叶健等发起，"会同两房商议，仰体先人创建之心，各出己资，重建宗祠。择本年八月初六起工切木，来春二月初一日竖造。当日估算，材木几何，椽木几何，考工几何，约用银陆百两"。两房依人口比例分担，长房出肆百两，二房出二百两。粗工由长房负责。这件事显然是由宗族主持的。（见《渠川叶氏宗谱·叶氏合同议据》）

有些宗族，把修桥补路当作一种义务写进家规，如《岩头金氏宗谱·家规》里写道："桥、路、渡舟倾坏，子孙倘有馀资，当助修治。"在《渠口叶氏宗谱》里有《石马岩石栏杆志》和《前山楼梯岩志》两篇文字。前者记一条"上峻壁、下深潭"的宽仅四尺许的山路，"时有夜行客人至此倾跌而下，其危险莫可言状"。于是，一批村人"醵集钜资，建造石栏数十丈。自斯以往，该处变险为夷"。后者记渠口村前山"有名楼梯岩者，高六丈许，陟降必经之处，峻无阶级。樵夫至此，莫不栗栗危惧"，也有一批善人，集资雇石匠，"将高峻处开凿阶级如楼梯焉"。这个宗谱里还有关于修路的记载二十几则。

把这些善举记录在宗谱里，是对当事人的榆扬，"传之永久"，以为子孙后代表率。宗族用这个崇高的荣誉来激励有馀财的人从事乡里建设。如《岩头金氏宗谱·家规》载，"祠堂、坟屋稍遇倾圮，亟当议修，量以坟租、祠租内暂行抽贮，以给支。如有贤孝子孙捐资乐助者，当登记于谱"。更有在记入宗谱之外再加碑刻。例如《鹤垟谢氏宗谱》记第五世端先公"敦尚德义，轻财好施，凿溪港以疏壅滞，乡里赖之。因勒石于鹤岭溪滨，内镌：'宋绍熙五年（1194年）谢十八居士开港'十二字，以志其绩"。

一般修造庙宇和石桥也往往把捐资人的姓名刻在碑上或桥上。最突出的是岩头村的桂林公，在他去世之后，村人为纪念他对村子建设的重大贡献，把他创建的书院改成了奉把他的专祠，称为水亭祠。

在乡文士，或是直接、或是通过宗族组织，从事乡里建设，他们的文化素养和山水情怀就转化在这些建设之中了。楠溪江村落里文化建筑的类型和数量很多，建筑的风格大方素雅，不事奢华的雕琢，而且追求建筑与自然山水的融洽。例如乾隆年间造的花坛村文昌阁，"门临曲水，地耸云端，拱群峰之突屹，面万木之郁葱，珍川之胜，于此称第一焉。登斯阁者，晨夕之赏心各异，四时之兴趣不穷，文思秀发，颖悟宏开"（《珍川朱氏合谱·慎轩公传》），要的是在山川胜景中秀发文思，当是清新俊逸。

English

2. Role of Gentry

Nanxi River village gentry and local worthies were mainly scholars who had resigned and returned home, those who had abandoned official aspirations, and those who had failed examinations — they formed the leadership stratum of village society.

They received good cultural education; between discussing learning and composing poetry, most cared about their hometowns, delighted in "good deeds," thereby bringing their cultural cultivation into village construction.

village planning generally occurred at village founding, mostly unrecorded, long without reliable资料. But each clan's genealogy records many people who contributed to hometown construction, and some construction projects with long-term impact.

For example, Yantou village: "Jin clan Genealogy" records second-generation ancestor Rixin Gong (born Yuan Zhongtong Bingzi, died Yuan Zhizheng Wuzi, i.e., 1348) building water conservancy: "At that time the land suffered drought and flood, years of poor harvest; the lord assessed terrain, followed water nature, dredged two canals south of Dutou, reaching springs for irrigation, consistently obtaining abundant harvests; agriculture of both districts relies on it to this day." Later there was Guilin Gong (born Ming Hongzhi Jiayin, died Longqing Jisi, 1494-1569); genealogy says: "Since first moving to Yantou, ancestors did have constructions, but in number of benefits and abundance of merit, this lord ranks first." His achievements: "cultivate fengshui, promote land productivity, establish sacrificial fields, build public property." "Our clan's site is rather high, fields suffer drought; Shengsi Gong (i.e., Guilin Gong) donated old unused funds, dug a long river for storage and drainage, built high embankment, cultivated fengshui gate.

Built pavilion and pagoda on it, upon completion donated to main ancestral hall as clan-wide public property." Around same time as Guilin Gong, Jiajing jinshi Jin Zhao (Xiafeng Gong) built Upper Garden, Lower Garden, main ancestral hall, and also built a memorial archway for himself.

Several people successively engaged in hometown construction, so Yantou village's water system, streets, public buildings, gardens etc. are the most outstanding in Nanxi River.

Cangpo and Furong villages have rigorous planning, complete water systems, orderly streets; Cangpo also has large gardens — these also relied on generations of construction-minded people in both villages. Building long dike to store water into lake, in Cangpo roughly Southern Song era (1178), nearly four hundred years earlier than Yantou.

Furong village Chen clan's Youxu Gong (born Kangxi Renshen, died Qianlong Guiyou, 1692-1753), "had economic talent, studied and understood great principles. ... Delighted in charity. Built temples, built ancestral hall, added sacrificial fields, made boats for crossing, built dikes for irrigation.

What he did in life, merits to clan and kin cannot be enumerated" ("Liangyuan Chen clan Genealogy").

"Tangchuan Zheng clan Genealogy" contains "Long Dike Record," recording early Qing, Qianlong Dingmao (1747) spring, clansman Zheng Xixian donating funds and organizing labor to build dike. Record writes:

Tangchuan scenic district at confluence of two streams, embracing distant mountains, swallowing long flow; before has Leifeng Nine Peaks' wonder, behind has Mashi Tianyan's peril; as for Screen Mountain receiving sun, Hehe retaining clouds — everywhere marvelous.

Only road lacked a dike; though tall trees shade, water murmurs, travelers often suffered this. Now stones piled as dike, sparing no effort, from East Ridge to West Ridge, about several tens of zhang, shaped like river embankment, flat enough for carts.

Besides water conservancy, ancestral halls, temples, roads — academies were traditionally the focus of hometown construction, attracting gentry's most attention.

"Zhenchuan Zhu clan Combined Genealogy · Biography of Shenxuan Gong" says the lord "lifelong delighted in good deeds, especially devoted to study; once worried clan juniors lacked place to study, unable to cultivate talent and promote later learning, thus donated heavy funds, with local notable persons built wenchang pavilion two wings, as place for lectures." Some had special interest in environmental greening, such as Huatan village's Zhu Fuweng, "loved reading, simple but not stagnant, only sought worthy friends for self-improvement.

... Early Hongwu, imperial edict sought mountain recluses; county and prefecture forcibly recommended to capital, appointed Court Gentleman; requested return, granted, fame grew. Built ancestral hall, established sacrificial fields to worship ancestors.

Built house at Fang'ao, planted tens of thousands of pine trees for shelter, hence called himself Yunsong (Cloud Pine). ... Roamed freely among clouds and pines, discussed past and present, played qin and composed poetry" ("Epitaph of Ming Appointed Court Gentleman Yunsong Gong").

These local worthies and gentry, in vernacular construction, of course mainly played roles of proposing, planning, donating; as for project direction, that relied on clan power.

For example, Kangxi twenty-first year (1682) June, to rebuild Qukou Ye clan main ancestral hall, initiated by clansman Ye Jian etc.: "consulted both branches, respecting ancestors' founding intention, each contributed own funds to rebuild ancestral hall. Selected August sixth of this year to start cutting timber, next spring February first to erect.

That day estimated: how much timber, how much rafter wood, how much labor, approximately six hundred taels of silver." Both branches shared by population ratio: main branch four hundred taels, second branch two hundred taels. Rough labor by main branch. This matter obviously directed by clan.

(See "Quchuan Ye clan Genealogy · Ye clan Contract Agreement")

Some clans wrote bridge and road repair as obligation into family rules, e.g., "Yantou Jin clan Genealogy · Family Rules": "bridges, roads, ferry boats damaged — if descendants have surplus funds, should assist repair." In "Qukou Ye clan Genealogy" there are two texts: "Shima Rock Stone railing Record" and "Qianshan Stair Rock Record." The former records a mountain road "above steep cliff, below deep pool" only four chi wide, "sometimes night travelers fell here, danger beyond description." So a group of villagers "collected large funds, built stone railing several tens of zhang.

From then on, this place turned danger to safety." The latter records Qukou village's Qianshan "has place called Stair Rock, about six zhang high,必经 route for ascent and descent, steep without steps.

Woodcutters arriving here all tremble with fear"; also a group of good people raised funds to hire stone masons, "carved steps into steep place like stairs." This genealogy also has over twenty records of road repair.

Recording these good deeds in genealogy was to praise the parties, "transmit forever," as example for descendants. clan used this lofty honor to激励 those with surplus wealth to engage in hometown construction.

E.g., "Yantou Jin clan Genealogy · Family Rules": "ancestral halls and grave houses slightly damaged — should promptly discuss repair, allocate from grave rents and hall rents temporarily for expenses. If filial descendants donate and gladly assist, should register in genealogy." Some added stone inscriptions beyond genealogy.

E.g., "Heyang Xie clan Genealogy" records fifth-generation Duanxian Gong "valued morality and righteousness, generous with wealth, dug stream channels to clear blockages, village relied on him.

Hence carved stone at Heling stream bank, inscribed: 'Song Shaoxi fifth year (1194) Xie Eighteenth Layman opened channel' twelve characters, to record his merit." Generally building temples and stone bridges also carved donors' names on steles or bridges.

Most outstanding: Yantou village's Guilin Gong — after his death, villagers, to commemorate his major contribution to village construction, converted the academy he founded into a shrine dedicated to him, called Shuiting shrine (Water Pavilion shrine).

Local literati, either directly or through clan organization, engaged in hometown construction; their cultural cultivation and landscape sentiment transformed into these constructions.

Nanxi River villages have many types and quantities of cultural buildings; architectural style is generous and elegant, without luxurious carving, and pursues harmony between architecture and natural mountains and waters.

For example, Huatan village's wenchang pavilion built in Qianlong era: "Gate faces winding water, ground rises to clouds, embraced by sudden peaks, facing lush ten thousand trees; Zhenchuan's beauty ranks first here.

Those ascending this pavilion, morning and evening delights differ, four seasons' interests endless; literary thought flourishes, understanding broadens" ("Zhenchuan Zhu clan Combined Genealogy · Biography of Shenxuan Gong") — what was sought was flourishing literary thought amid mountain-water scenery, fresh and elegant.
