堪舆师的作用
中文

3. 堪舆师的作用

堪舆风水之说对楠溪江村落的规划和建设有很大的影响，而阴阳师或者地理师，大都出自乡土文人这个阶层。所以，风水堪舆之说是乡贤士绅影响农村规划和建设的又一个途径，阴阳师或者地理师，实际在一定程度上起着类似规划师的作用，但他们所依据的是迷信。

明代嘉靖年间对岩头村的建设作了重大贡献的桂林公，其实就是一位地理师。《岩头金氏宗谱·桂林公行状》说他"屡试不中，转而习青囊，相宅卜地"。传说他还得到宁王幕下谋士"国师"李自实的帮助。他所兴建的，可能都经过堪舆风水的考究。与桂林公大致同时，还有些士绅好星象之学，如嘉靖年间岩头村还有一位九峰先生，他"凡经史以及诸子百家无不娴，且究心星学，陶情音律，至于制艺歌词，皆其余事耳"（《岩头金氏宗谱·九峰先生五旬寿序》）。

清代中叶咸丰年间，蓬溪村有一位谢文波，《宗谱》有一篇《谢公文波六旬初度寿序》说："近百年来，临尚武力，独文波笃内行，嗜书史，学涉赅博，家多藏书，插架林立，于壬奇、星数、音韵反切之学，旁及琴棋八法，无不精通，风雅过人远甚。"这位先生的著作有《东瓯杂俎》、《因音求字》、《四声正误》、《反切法》和《草药谱》等等。

这些记载说明，当时习风水和壬奇、星数的人，都是些饱读经典诗书的儒者。儒学是上层的雅言文化，术数是下层的民俗文化，本来属于不同的两类。不过，早在汉代独尊儒术的时候，就已经把它与阴阳五行之类结合起来了。从那时以后，儒者把堪舆风水和壬奇、星数当做一般文化知识来学，并不以为与儒学的正统精神有根本的矛盾。只在迷信得太似乎"怪、力、乱、神"，有悖于情理的时候，才加以驳斥，例如长期停厝不葬，"以死者为生者祈福"，就受到严厉批评。

堪舆风水是迷信邪说，但阴阳师和主持村落建设的士绅们都是当时当地有知识的人。他们是双重人格的，他们会在一些问题上愚妄可笑，但由于他们富有阅历、经验，也会对一些问题作出合乎实际的判断，如对村落选址、水系规划、村落结构布局、防御工程、街道网、重要建筑物的位置和朝向等等，可能有一些比较好的建议。为了保护他们的职业，或者仅仅为了增强他们对自己的实际判断的自信心和他们的建议对公众的说服力，他们便使他们的经验知识附会风水堪舆的"理论"。塘湾村的筑城和凿池，显然都是为了迫切的实际需要，但都附会了一套风水说法。看来这说法虽然是些无根游谈，但有助于号召村民们同心同德，出钱出力，来办成一些公益事业。

楠溪江中游的村子，例如苍坡、岩头和芙蓉，都流传着"国师"李自实规划村落的故事。例如说，南宋淳熙五年（1178年），苍坡村九世祖李嵩请李自实规划了寨墙、街道和池塘、水渠。他使主街直指西方远处的笔架山，命名主街为笔街，以利于发荣科甲。但笔架山又像火焰，为防笔街引火烧村，就在它东端造了两口池塘，以水克火。池塘之一称砚池，再以村子附会纸张，两块石条附会墨锭，与笔街一起组成"文房四宝"，这就更加有利于科甲了。宗族便以这种无根游谈激励子弟刻苦读书。

又例如说，明代嘉靖年间，桂林公请李自实规划岩头村。他问桂林公，希望"紧发"还是"慢发"。紧发即将寨墙包住全姓聚居范围，数代之内就可以发达，但前途有限；慢发即把杂姓聚居地也围在寨墙里，发达虽比较慢，但前途更加繁荣。桂林公选择了"紧发"，寨墙就是现存的状况。李自实还故弄玄虚说，村东的屿山是一条大蟒幻化的，会对村人不利，所以，在村里规划了四条东西向的窄巷，象征四支"箭"，两条在东端分叉的小巷，和一条东端曲折的小巷象征两把半"镗"，这七件"武器"就把屿山大蟒镇住了，可以保护村人平安。

据传说，元至正元年（1341年）重建芙蓉村的时候，也是李自实看风水，做规划的。

这些故事荒诞无稽，而且一个"国师"李自实，从南宋历元代而直到嘉靖年间还在，显然也是虚构。但这些故事却说明，当时确实有这类人对村子的规划性问题提出建议，不论是合理的，还是虚妄的。

虽然有些士绅从事堪舆风水之术，但是对它的宿命论的消极作用也已经有人注意到了。不语"怪力乱神"的儒家文化，对属于俗文化的风水术是很有批判能力的。例如，伪托朱熹写的《雪心赋》大肆鼓吹风水，说"将相公侯，胥此焉出；荣华富贵，何莫不由"。但是，这种宿命论不但没有丝毫的伦理教化意义，甚至有碍于儒家的伦理教化，破坏了儒家教化的价值观。因此，朱熹在《大学衍义补·家乡之礼》里写道："世有选择之法存焉，不能不用之以代卜筮。但其所谓福禄不可信，其趋吉避凶之说亦不可。"堪舆家也有"灵山灵穴，有德者居之"，"山地好，不如心地好"这

English

3. Role of fengshui Masters

fengshui (geomancy) theories had great influence on Nanxi River village planning and construction, while yin-yang masters or geomancers mostly came from the local literati stratum.

Therefore, fengshui geomancy was another途径 by which local gentry influenced rural planning and construction; yin-yang masters or geomancers actually played a role similar to planners to some extent, but what they relied on was superstition.

Guilin Gong, who made major contributions to Yantou village construction in Ming Jiajing era, was actually a geomancer.

"Yantou Jin clan Genealogy · Biography of Guilin Gong" says he "repeatedly failed examinations, then turned to studying Qingnang (geomancy), assessing houses and selecting sites." Legend says he also received help from "National Teacher" Li Zishi, a strategist under Prince of Ning. What he built may all have undergone fengshui scrutiny.

Around same time as Guilin Gong, some gentry were fond of astrology; e.g., in Jiajing era Yantou village there was Mr. Jiufeng, who "was adept in all classics and hundred schools, furthermore delved into astrology, found joy in music, as for examination essays and lyrics, all were secondary matters" ("Yantou Jin clan Genealogy · Preface to Mr.

Jiufeng's Fiftieth Birthday"). Mid-Qing Xianfeng era, Pengxi village had Xie Wenbo; genealogy has "Preface to Mr.

Xie Wenbo's Sixtieth Birthday": "In recent hundred years, Lin favored martial force; only Wenbo was devoted to inner conduct, loved books and history, learning broad and deep, family had many books, shelves lined up; in Renqi (divination), astrology, phonology and fanqie, extending to qin, chess and calligraphy, all mastered — elegance far surpassed others." This gentleman's works include "Dongou Miscellany," "Finding Characters by Sound," "Correction of Four Tones," "Fanqie Method" and "Herbal Manual" etc.

These records show that those who studied fengshui, Renqi and astrology were all Confucian scholars well-read in classics. Confucianism was upper-class literate culture; numerology was lower-class folk culture, originally belonging to different categories.

However, as early as Han dynasty when Confucianism was独尊, it had already been combined with yin-yang and five elements. From then on, Confucian scholars studied fengshui, Renqi and astrology as general cultural knowledge, not seeing fundamental contradiction with Confucian orthodoxy.

Only when superstition became too much like "strange forces, chaos and spirits," contrary to reason, did they refute it — e.g., long-delayed burial, "using the dead to pray for blessings for the living," was severely criticized.

fengshui is superstitious heresy, but yin-yang masters and gentry who directed village construction were knowledgeable people of their time and place.

They had dual personalities: on some issues they were foolish and ridiculous, but due to rich experience, they also made practical judgments on some issues — such as village site selection, water system planning, village structural layout, defense works, street networks, location and orientation of important buildings etc.

— possibly giving some good suggestions.

To protect their profession, or merely to增强 their confidence in their practical judgments and the persuasiveness of their suggestions to the public, they attached their experiential knowledge to fengshui "theory." Tangwan village's wall-building and pool-digging were obviously for urgent practical needs, but both were attached with a set of fengshui explanations.

It seems these explanations, though groundless talk, helped mobilize villagers to unite, contribute money and labor, to accomplish some public welfare projects.

Middle Nanxi River villages, such as Cangpo, Yantou and Furong, all circulate stories of "National Teacher" Li Zishi planning villages. For example: in Southern Song Chunxi fifth year (1178), Cangpo village ninth-generation ancestor Li Song invited Li Zishi to plan village walls, streets, ponds and canals.

He made the main street point directly at distant Bijia (Brush-holder) Mountain to the west, named main street Brush Street, to promote examination success. But Bijia Mountain also looked like flame; to prevent Brush Street from drawing fire to village, he built two ponds at its east end, using water to overcome fire.

One pond called Inkstone Pool; then village associated with paper, two stone slabs associated with ink sticks, together with Brush Street forming "Four Treasures of the Study" — even more favorable for examination success. The clan used this groundless talk to激励 juniors to study hard.

Another example: in Ming Jiajing era, Guilin Gong invited Li Zishi to plan Yantou village.

He asked Guilin Gong whether he desired "rapid prosperity" or "slow prosperity." Rapid prosperity meant village wall enclosing only same-surname settlement area — within several generations could prosper, but future limited; slow prosperity meant enclosing mixed-surname settlement too — prosperity slower but future more prosperous.

Guilin Gong chose "rapid prosperity"; the village wall is as existing.

Li Zishi also mystified: Yushan east of village was transformed from a giant python, harmful to villagers; therefore he planned four east-west narrow lanes in village, symbolizing four "arrows"; two lanes branching at east end and one winding lane at east end symbolizing two-and-a-half "tang" (halberds); these seven "weapons" subdued the Yushan python, protecting villagers' peace.

According to legend, when Furong village was rebuilt in Yuan Zhizheng first year (1341), Li Zishi also assessed fengshui and made the plan.

These stories are absurd and baseless; and one "National Teacher" Li Zishi, from Southern Song through Yuan until Jiajing era still alive — obviously fictional. But these stories说明 that at that time there确实 were such people giving suggestions on village planning issues, whether reasonable or delusional.

Although some gentry practiced fengshui, some had already noticed its fatalistic negative effect. Confucian culture, which does not speak of "strange forces, violence, chaos and spirits," had strong critical ability toward fengshui as folk culture.

For example, "Xuexin Fu" falsely attributed to Zhu Xi vigorously advocated fengshui, saying "ministers and generals all emerge from this; glory and wealth all come from it." But this fatalism not only had no ethical-moral significance, even hindered Confucian ethical education, destroying Confucian educational values.

Therefore, Zhu Xi wrote in "Daxue Yanyi Bu · Hometown Rituals": "The world has site-selection methods; cannot but use them to replace divination.

But its so-called fortune and blessing cannot be trusted; its sayings of seeking good and avoiding evil also cannot be [trusted]." fengshui practitioners also have sayings: "Spirit mountains and spirit caves, inhabited by the virtuous," "Good mountain land is not as good as good heart" —

本篇术语 Key Terms

堪舆师 / Fengshui master (geomancer) 风水 / Fengshui (geomancy) 阴阳师 / Yin-yang master 地理师 / Geomancer 青囊 / Qingnang (geomancy) 国师 / National Teacher 李自实 / Li Zishi 桂林公 / Guilin Gong 九峰先生 / Mr. Jiufeng 谢文波 / Xie Wenbo 壬奇 / Renqi (divination) 星数 / Astrology 术数 / Numerology 阴阳五行 / Yin-yang and five elements 文房四宝 / Four Treasures of the Study ...等30个
11
工匠的作用
中文

4. 工匠的作用

楠溪江的建筑工匠体制和他们传统的设计、计算、施工等等方法以及有关的民俗行为，已经无从查考。不过，推断在长期小农自然经济社会中，工匠的专业化程度不高，没有组成经济实体、以父子师徒方式传承、有一些行业习俗，大致合乎实际。

工匠大多不是士人、富人，也无从襄赞善行义举，所以不载于宗谱。偶然有几位因为有功于建设，品行超卓，才得以占几行楮墨。例如《两源陈氏宗谱》载明代的工匠："有福公，字佑如，号兰轩，智巧绝伦，有造风阁龙楼之技。家贫不能读书，然颇知大义。岁戊子建文广公祖祠，公匠心独运，不惮艰辛。"另一位也是明代的，见于《坦下陈氏宗谱》："讳士商，字兆霖。气象温和，与人无忤。少习公输之业，精专其事，相材度木，适中其度。所以遐迩创厦新宇，皆任于君。至今妖蝤有继，家道渐宁，然能颇知尊宗敬祖，凡有义举，靡不竭力。"从"匠心独运"、"相材度木"来看，这两位匠人是兼任了设计者的大匠。

由于父子师徒传承，建筑行业的各作都有了专业村。例如"大木老司出罗坑，石头老司出中堡，烧瓦老司出敬仁，泥水老司出绿嶂"。

虽然有专业村，但工匠的专业化程度还不高。大木老司也做床柜、书桌、琴凳，甚至织布机和风车；泥水老司也垒锅灶；打石老司也做捣臼、磨盘、石柱、牌坊、各种石器，甚至刻碑文。砌墙老司的行当尤其驳杂，要造石拱桥、铺路、筑矶步、垒城墙、挖地道、砌田坎等等。最使这些老司的手艺发挥得淋漓尽致的，是做各种装饰。泥水老司会塑飞禽走兽、人物山水、狮子麒麟。"堆龙就像龙一色，塑凤就像凤飞腾。"打石老司会打这些题材，还要把"狮子捧球打镂空"。砌墙老司则会用卵石在地面镶出各种图案，叫做"插石花"。

关于这些匠作，楠溪江都有歌谣吟咏。雅言文化的宗谱所不取的，在民俗文化中却代代流传。民俗文化，基本上是劳动者的文化。一首《大木老司》的歌谣是：

大木老司手艺精，手控丈杆量得清。

曲尺木斗线弹准，墨划梁柱分寸明。

双退大屋两边轩，能造高楼大厦厅。

梁上叠梁斗叠斗，红油栏杆雕花名。

宫庙寺宇造古董，亭台楼阁八角井。

丈杆或称制尺，在上面划定整幢房子的全部大小尺寸。它相当建筑设计图，工匠依靠它能造出双退住宅、宫庙寺宇和亭台楼阁各种类型和形制的房屋，包括相当复杂的斗拱。不过以造"古董"为能，则是农业社会中的保守心理，反映出生活的停滞和小农眼光的狭隘。

有一首长长的歌谣详细描述了房屋的兴建过程和工匠的劳作：

深山采来沉香木，鲁班祖师造新厅。

择定黄道吉庆日，起木发兴做不停。

大木老司来做工，画好图样定屋形。

石匠老司定磉石，阴阳定向遇吉星。

清吉良辰来拼木，多少功夫利排成。

黄道吉日开柱眼，竹匠柱头箍得紧。

竖柱喜遇黄道日，上梁巧逢紫微星。

梁上重梁斗叠斗，四面花窗映花明。

屋顶盖落滚椽瓦，地砌玉砖斗七星。

四面搭起禽兽头，墙头嵌镜发光明。

九曲游廊团圆走，平池着板大和珍。

玲珑花窗腾落阔，窗下书桌摆现成。

前后镇落对门，开门关门凤凰声。

前有亭栽栖凤竹，后有池养化龙鱼。

兴造房屋关风水，财聚小康振家声。

这首歌谣值得注意的两点是：一、明说木匠老司"画好图样定屋形"；二、明说石匠老司"阴阳定向遇吉星"，这工作是在"定磉石"的时候做的。看来，石匠老师在单幢房屋范围里掌管着风水，可能是遵照《鲁班经》里的一些规矩，因为前面说："鲁班祖师造新厅。"《三十六行歌》开篇唱："鲁班祖师是亲师父，传授老司手艺精。"

这首歌谣也表达了乡人们对建筑的审美理想和对匠人技艺的称赞。

English

4. Role of Craftsmen

Nanxi River's architectural craftsman system and their traditional design, calculation, construction methods and related folk customs can no longer be verified.

However, inferring from long-term small-scale peasant natural economy society, craftsmen's specialization degree was not high; they did not form economic entities, transmitted through father-son and master-apprentice, with some trade customs — roughly conforming to reality.

Craftsmen were mostly not scholars or wealthy, nor could they assist in good deeds and charity, so they were not recorded in genealogies. Occasionally a few, due to contributions to construction and outstanding character, earned a few lines of ink.

For example, "Liangyuan Chen clan Genealogy" records a Ming craftsman: "Youfu Gong, courtesy name Youru, hao Lanxuan, ingenious beyond compare, skilled in building pavilions and towers. Poor, unable to study, yet understood great principles.

In Wuzi year, when building Wenguang Gong ancestral hall, he used original craftsmanship, not sparing hardship." Another, also Ming, in "Tanxia Chen clan Genealogy": "Named Shishang, courtesy name Zhaolin. Gentle demeanor, harmonious with all.

From youth learned Lu Ban (master carpenter) trade, specialized in it, assessing materials and measuring timber, appropriately proportioned. Therefore near and far building new mansions all entrusted to him.

To this day successors continue, family gradually stable, yet he knew to respect clan and ancestors, whenever righteous undertakings, spared no effort." From "original craftsmanship" and "assessing materials and measuring timber," these two craftsmen were master builders who also served as designers.

Due to father-son and master-apprentice transmission, various construction trades had specialized villages. For example: "Great carpenters come from Luokeng, stone masons from Zhongbao, tile kilns from Jingren, plasterers from Lüzhang."

Although there were specialized villages, craftsmen's specialization degree was still not high. Great carpenters also made beds, cabinets, desks, qin stools, even looms and windmills; plasterers also built cooking stoves; stone masons also made mortars, millstones, stone pillars, memorial archways, various stone implements, even carved inscriptions.

Wall masons' trade was especially diverse: building stone arch bridges, paving roads, building stepping-stones, piling city walls, digging tunnels, laying field banks etc. What most brought these craftsmen's skills to full play was making various decorations. Plasterers modeled birds and beasts, figures and landscapes, lions and qilin.

"Piling dragon looks exactly like dragon, modeling phoenix looks like phoenix soaring." Stone masons carved these subjects, and also "hollowed out lion holding ball." Wall masons used cobblestones to inlay various patterns on ground, called "inserted stone flowers."

About these crafts, Nanxi River has ballads chanting them. What literate genealogies did not record, folk culture passed down generation after generation. Folk culture is basically the culture of laborers. A ballad "Great carpenter" goes:

Great carpenter's skill exquisite, hand holding measuring rod clear.

Square and ink line accurate, ink marking beams and pillars precise.

Double-retreat great house with side wings, can build tall buildings and halls.

Beams stacked on beams, brackets on brackets, red oil railings carved with flower names.

Palaces, temples, monasteries built antique, pavilions, towers, octagonal wells.

The measuring rod, or ruler, marked all dimensions of the entire house. It相当于 architectural design drawing; craftsmen relied on it to build double-retreat residences, palaces, temples, monasteries, pavilions and towers of various types and forms, including fairly complex bracket sets.

However, taking ability to build "antiques" as excellence reflects conservative psychology in agricultural society, showing stagnation of life and narrowness of small-farmer outlook.

A long ballad details house construction process and craftsmen's labor:

From deep mountains gathered aloeswood, Lu Ban ancestor builds new hall.

Selected auspicious day, timber raised and work never stops.

Great carpenter comes to work, draws good design, sets house form.

Stone mason sets foundation stones, yin-yang orientation meets lucky star.

On auspicious morning assemble timber, how much effort arranged.

Auspicious day open mortises, bamboo craftsman tightens column hoops.

Erecting columns meets auspicious day, raising beam encounters Purple Star.

Beams stacked on beams, brackets on brackets, four-sided lattice windows reflect flowers.

roof covered with rolling rafters and tiles, floor paved jade bricks in Seven Stars pattern.

Four sides set beast and bird heads, wall embedded mirrors shine bright.

Nine-bend winding corridors circular walk, flat pool with boards precious.

Exquisite lattice windows spacious, desk under window ready.

Front and back settle facing doors, opening closing doors sounds phoenix.

Front has pavilion planting phoenix-perching bamboo, back has pool raising dragon-transforming fish.

Building house concerns fengshui, wealth gathers, moderate prosperity elevates family name.

Two points worth noting in this ballad: first, explicitly says carpenter "draws good design, sets house form"; second, explicitly says stone mason "yin-yang orientation meets lucky star," this work done when "setting foundation stones." It seems stone mason, within single house scope, managed fengshui, possibly following rules in "Lu Ban Jing" (Lu Ban Classic), because earlier it says: "Lu Ban ancestor builds new hall." "Thirty-six Trades Song" opens: "Lu Ban ancestor is true master, transmitting craftsmen's exquisite skill."

This ballad also expresses villagers' aesthetic ideals for architecture and praise for craftsmen's skills.

本篇术语 Key Terms

工匠 / Craftsman 大木老司 / Great carpenter (Damu Laosi) 泥水老司 / Plasterer (Nishui Laosi) 打石老司 / Stone mason (Dashi Laosi) 砌墙老司 / Wall mason (Qiqiang Laosi) 烧瓦老司 / Tile kiln worker (Shaowa Laosi) 专业村 / Specialized village 丈杆 / Measuring rod (Zhanggan) 曲尺 / Carpenter's square 墨斗 / Ink line marker (Modou) 斗拱 / Dougong (bracket set) 鲁班 / Lu Ban (master carpenter deity) 鲁班经 / Lu Ban Jing (Lu Ban Classic) 定磉石 / Setting foundation stones 插石花 / Inserted stone flower patterns ...等30个

第二篇：规划篇

Part II: Planning
1
选址
中文

1. 选址

楠溪江中游古村落的建设大都经过完整的规划。建设得比较早，并且把早期的规划基本保存到现在的，有苍坡村、塘湾村、芙蓉村和岩头村等。苍坡于955年建村，从北宋至和二年（1055年）起，重要的建设《苍坡李氏宗谱》都有记载，包括南宋淳熙五年（1178年）的一次全面的规划。这些记载的情况与苍坡村的现状大体符合，水系、主街和公共建筑一一都在。例如淳熙五年九世祖李嵩在村东南筑坝蓄水形成东池、西池。李嵩死后，夫人楼溪刘氏继续完成了寨墙、笔街和寨门，并挖了环泮水渠。

塘湾村始建于宋代，《棠川（按即塘湾）郑氏宗谱》里有一则资料，提到绍兴年间的前街、中街，它们至今还是塘湾村的主要街道。芙蓉村也初建于宋代，南宋末因抗元而被完全焚毁，元至正元年（1341年）重建，大体保持了原来的格局。岩头村的规划在明代中叶，清代中叶被大规模破坏，但原状还可以追迹，它的水系和园林绿地在楠溪江村落里是成就最高的。

虽然没有文献资料，从现状看，规划的主要内容包括选址、水系(沟洫渠道、池塘、井)、布局（街巷网、功能分区、公共中心、园林绿地）、防御系统（寨墙、溪门、谯亭）等。这些规划内容的一部分项目，在其他地区的村落里也可能见到，但全面综合地实施它们，而且如此整齐，在其他地区就很少见，因而成为当今楠溪江村落的重要特色。

至于局部地保存下来的规划建设就几乎村村都有了。

楠溪江的村落，在建村之初，都很重视选址。选址的主要考虑，是自给自足的自然经济之下的农业生产和生活。影响生产和生活的基本元素是水、地、山。附会水、地、山对居民凶吉祸福关系的堪舆风水之说相当活跃。

楠溪江人对生存环境的认识是很全面而有综合性的。《渠川叶氏宗谱·重合族谱牒告成附书于后》写道：

渠口，吾祖光宗公发祥之所也。阅世三十有三，历年千百有馀。围绕者数百家，沿缘者七八里。凤山翥其西，雷峰峙其东，南有屿山，而其外有大溪环之。中穿一渠，可以灌田。而其北则层峦叠翠，不一其状。有径可通四处，田高下横逵，布列如画挂然。泉流涓涓，声与耳谋。地僻非僻，山贫不贫。有檀可采，有秫可种，有美可茹，有鲜可食。桑麻蔽野，禾稼连畦。迕笋地而挑衣，趋茶天而焙牛，民赖其利益已久矣！（道光二十年三十一世裔孙举东志）

这一段描写，写到山川形势、交通、水利、农业、林业和渔业。《荀子·强国》里说："其固塞险，形势便，山林川谷美，天材之利多，是形胜也。"渠口正是这样一个形胜之地，它现在是楠溪江中游的大村之一。

（1）村落选址的第一个考虑是要有足够的可耕地，使农产可以自给。正如《管子·度地》所说："圣人之处国者，必于不倾之地而择其形之肥饶者，……乃以其天材，地之所生，利养其人，以育六畜。"

堪舆名著《雪心赋》说到"发福之大居"应是"明堂平旷，万象森罗，众水归朝，诸山聚会，草盛木繁，水深土厚"，也是这个意思。

楠溪江中游的村落，大多分布在河谷冲积平地上。这里土层肥沃，水源丰沛，交通便利，农业生产的条件最好。两侧的山坡，又可以育薪炭林和果木林。条件尤其好的是地形比较开阔的盆地，其中最大的是以岩头村为中心的盆地，这个盆地里村落最多、最密、规模也比较大。除了岩头村之外，重要的有苍坡村、霞美村、周宅村、港头村、渡头村、芙蓉村、溪南村和枫林村等等，都是楠溪江人文最发达的村落。它们的主姓从福建迁来，建村比较早，而且经济、文化一直处于领先地位。其他几个人文发达的大村落，也大多位于小盆地中心，如花坛村、溪口村、桐州村、渠口村、豫章村等等。《珍川朱氏合族副谱》里有一篇《伯清公珍川十咏序》，描写花坛村的环境道：

陵阜夹川，陂陀下弛，衍为原隰。林麓蔽荫，水田环绕，居民耕植其中，熙熙如也。……是盖乾坤清澈之气所锺聚融结，必有玮瑰俊秀杰出乎其间。

由于水土好，有利于农业经济的发展，就能培育出人才来。花坛村确实是个人才辈出的地方，尤其在明代。

宽敞的盆地里，村落大多占用平地，形态集中而且方正。狭窄的河谷平川里，村落大多傍山坡建造，让出宝贵的耕地。而且村落规模不大，顺等高线呈带形。例如蓬溪村、埭头村和水云村。水云村的谷地稍稍宽一点，以致《大岩岩志》说它："村民多集于溪西大岩若北马鞍山麓，土地平旷，山泉下注，涧别渠分，阡陌交通。屋舍比连，安居乐业者二百馀户，大小宗祠檐垣相望也。"

有少数村落，例如周宅村，它北侧的渡头村和南侧的港头村，为了让出耕地，造在河滩上。它们相接呈带形，甚至与南侧的方巷村连接了起来，长度在两公里以上。

有一些沿江村落很少农田，就靠发展手工业，如西岸村，位于一座小山和江流之间，村民以制作粉干，运销温州，辅助收入。还有一些则做木工、烧窑、使船等。

（2）村落选址的第二个考虑是避水害，抗洪排涝。楠溪江流域年年夏季都有台风暴雨。每逢暴雨，江水骤涨，奔腾咆哮，摧毁力极强。康熙年间的一次大水，就把豫章村临江的"外宅"部分一冲而光，至今还是一个积沙的高地。

为了防洪，除了要有足够的高程之外，弯弯曲曲的江流两岸，村落大都造在沉积岸一侧而避开冲刷岸。按照堪舆风水的说法，就是村落前面要有"腰带水"，不可以有"反弓水"。堪舆书《山龙语类》说："反背水，形如反弓，一名反跳水。此水漏泄堂气，无情之水也。"相传为刘基著的《堪舆漫兴》里写道："金城弯曲抱身，如如月弓产凤麟；若是反弓不揖冢，石崇富豪亦须贫。"金城水也叫玉带水或腰带水。《阳宅十书》说："门前若有玉带水，高官必定容易起；出入代代读书声，荣显富贵耀门闾。

"鹤湾、鹤垟、鹤盛、东皋、枫林、西岸、桐州、碧莲、豫章、花坛、廊下等等许多大一点的村子都处于"腰带水"的位置。蓬溪村虽然北面对鹤盛溪的反弓，但迎着反弓的是霞港头坚硬高耸的石壁，而一条不大的小溪却在蓬溪村的东面形成腰带，并且潴而成大约2000平方米的一个湖，在风水上正好是"水聚天心"，大吉。芙蓉村在南宋时候曾经出过十八位高级京官，村人至今津津乐道那"十八金带"，归因于芙蓉村的"前横腰带水，后枕纱帽岩，三龙捧珠，四水归心。"芙蓉村陈氏大宗祠里有一副楹联，写的是：

地枕三崖崖吐名花明昭万古；

门临象水水生秀气荣荫千秋。

说的就是这个风水。

关于沉积岸和冲刷岸影响村落安危的认识，就这样被风水迷信吞没了。风水师说"腰带水"有情，仅仅从它的环抱姿态引申而来，幸好没有违背常识的判断。

因为楠溪江流域多火山流纹岩，质地坚硬，有的地段，"反弓水"对江岸的冲刷危险不大，所以仍有一些村落造在"反弓水"的位置。至于风水上的凶煞，可以用一些办法来"破除"，例如，迎着"反弓水"造一座关帝庙就足以"逢凶化吉"了。蓬溪村面对鹤盛溪的"反弓水"，就是这样处理的。关帝是"伏魔大帝"，是"恩主公"，能够镇辟一切灾祸。

村落造在沉积岸，除了可以减弱洪水对地基的冲刷之外，"腰带水"还能够形成一个村落的自然边界，从而造成领域感。领域感会加强村落居民的内聚倾向，这对于血缘村落来说是很重要的。自然边界也有利于减少邻村之间的纠纷。

（3）选址的第三个重要考虑是安全。这是因为，许多村落的始迁祖都是为了避乱才来到楠溪江的，而楠溪江又并非世外桃源。例如，下园村的始祖为避晚唐黄巢之乱而来；苍坡、枫林、溪口、芙蓉等不少村落的始祖为避五代末年闽国王延钧之乱而来；渠口村始祖在北宋末为避方腊之乱而来；豫章村始祖是随宋室南渡辗转经江西而来的。他们饱经离乱，千里颠簸，来到楠溪江，为的是找一块平静的土地，休养生息。所以，他们时常钟情于闭塞的环境，或者易守难攻的环境。

一个例子是蓬溪村。它四面被高山峻岭包围，只在北面有个小小口子却又被鹤盛溪阻隔。要进入这个袋形谷地，唯一的路是架设在溪边悬崖峭壁上的栈道。

坦下村和塘湾村是另一类例子。它们三面环山，山势陡峭，不能通行。另一面不宽，筑起高峻的寨墙，很利于防守。为了选择这个地形，竟不顾村子的朝向，以致坦下村的住宅都朝西南而塘湾村的住宅绝大多数朝东北，虽然两村隔岸相望。

还有一些村子，背山面水，只有踏过长长的石矶步，才能到达寨门。例如廊下村、东皋村、白岩村、鹤垟村等等。西岸村造在山之东、水之西，山水合围，加上一段寨墙，唯一的寨门开在山的南麓，形势十分险固。

选择这种闭塞的，或易守难攻的环境建村，固然是为了安全，但有时也是为了遁世深隐。花坛村的始祖祖操隐公，南宋时在永嘉任县尉，《珍川朱氏宗谱·始祖操隐翁朱公墓志》说他：

见世荒乱，民多聚盗，弃官不仕，家于温。初居城东花柳塘。初欲隐，但目击理乱，关心竟不能释。再迁罗浮，而大乱扣城。对其子曰："此不足以隐吾迹矣！"东观西望，乃定居于清通乡之珍川。其地山明水秀，禽鸟和鸣，林深谷邃，景物幽清。乃置功名于度外，付理乱于不闻。

后来果然"陶然林下"，过着桃花源里的生活。

有些村落位于开阔地上，没有天险可守，就只好全仗筑墙了，例如芙蓉村、岩坦村。

正是由于村落整体的设防，才使村落内部房屋能够开敞外向，一片太平景象。这是血缘村落的特点之一。

（4）选址的第四个重要考虑是要有良好的小气候。楠溪江各地的小气候，因为地形多变，差别很大，因此，村落选址的时候，就要选择比较有利的小气候。相传为金丞相兀钦仄注的《青鸟先生葬经》说，理想的居住环境应该是"草木郁茂，吉气相随。……或本来空缺通风，今有草木郁茂，遮其不足，不觉空缺。故生气自然，草木充塞，又自人为"。说的是好的小气候有利于草木生长，草木生长又能改善小气候，当然有利于农业生产和居民的日常生活。

鹤垟村的始祖谢氏洗五五公，从郡城迁来楠溪江塘下村，有一天，"雪后登山，望见兰台山前积雪先融，遂定居焉，后果蕃昌"（见《鹤垟谢氏宗谱》）。鹤垟村在一个被"腰带水"紧紧从三面抱住的向阳山坡上，前有"鹤溪漾碧"，后有"锦屏叠翠"，土地高燥，光照充足，所以"积雪先融"。

渠口村的发展也是适应了当地的小气候。村子的东、西、北三面有山环抱，挡住冬季的寒风。南面不远处有一座小山，比较低矮，在东南和西南形成两个豁口。一到夏季，季候风沿楠溪江干流北上，一部分到坦下村顺小楠溪河谷折向西北，正好吹进东南豁口。所以渠口村向西发展，迎着季候风，而在东部植林，因为这位置吹不到季候风，却正迎着西晒太阳的炙烤。

隔小楠溪相对的坦下村和塘湾村相距不远，一朝西南而一朝东北，朝西南的坦下村固然有"三阳"高照，朝东北的塘湾村，则因为在漫长而燠热的夏季可以有季候风而补偿了阳光不足的缺憾。

（5）村落选址的第五个重要考虑是风景优美。溪口戴氏大宗祠的门联，有一副：

水秀山明常出仁智；地灵人杰永传声名。

好山水就是好风水，好风水就出好子弟。

楠溪江本来就以风景之美著称，几乎处处都有好景致。从谢灵运、陶弘景以来，楠溪江人的环境意识很强，山水情怀成为地方的文化传统。宋、明两朝，楠溪江中游许多村落文风极盛，受过较高文化教养的乡村士绅们，对自然之美有很敏锐的感受力，科名之余，他们流连于秀水明山之间，吟咏不辍。这种文化氛围，当然影响到他们对村落的选址。

例如一座芙蓉峰，有三块高耸峭拔的悬崖，就有下园村、芙蓉村、岩头村、溪南村都拿它做建村的地标，拿它做乡里风光的借景。《岩头金氏宗谱》记载，岩头村始迁祖安福公（生宋淳祐庚戌，卒元延祐戊午，1250-1318年）"始居西巷，……延祐间来相芙蓉三岩之胜，遂居焉。"又例如塘湾，也是因为风景优美而被郑氏始祖迁祖选中的。《棠川郑氏宗谱·重修棠川郑氏宗谱序》记载，始迁祖"至其地，见夫奇峰突兀，怪石峥嵘，面临雷璧，背枕天岩。九峰围屏，共巽山而拱秀；双溪环带，合曲涧而流芬。福地奚多让乎？"于是在这里建村定居。

各个村落的宗谱里，都有非常夸饰的文字盛赞本村风景的奇特和秀美，字里行间，洋溢着对乡里的浓烈感情。《蓬溪谢氏宗谱·同治甲子（1864年）重修族谱序》描绘山川形胜道：

楠溪形胜，惟蓬川最奇。迎逆流四十馀里，过棠洄荡漾，潴而后泄。守水口者，则有若狮、象、龟、鱼，突怒峭竖，险恶畏人。又有文笔峰撑寿星岩，镇屏山对列赢屿。横墙诸峰如观音坐莲、美女梳妆、鹰捕蛇、狮捉象。仰天湖、瀑布泉、将军、仙人、牛鼻、虎头、燕巢、鸡冠等胜，亦皆秀异可观。余足迹所及，历数之，未有过于此者也。

English

1. Site Selection

Most ancient villages in Middle Nanxi River were built with complete planning. Those built relatively early and whose early planning is basically preserved to this day include Cangpo, Tangwan, Furong and Yantou villages.

Cangpo was founded in 955; from Northern Song Zhihe second year (1055), important constructions are all recorded in "Cangpo Li clan Genealogy," including a comprehensive planning in Southern Song Chunxi fifth year (1178). These records大体 match Cangpo village's current state — water system, main street and public buildings all present.

For example, in Chunxi fifth year, ninth-generation ancestor Li Song built a dam southeast of village to store water forming East Pool and West Pool. After Li Song's death, his wife Lady Liu of Louxi continued completing village wall, Brush Street and village gate, and dug the surrounding moat canal.

Tangwan village was founded in Song; "Tangchuan (i.e., Tangwan) Zheng clan Genealogy" has a record mentioning Front Street and Middle Street from Shaoxing era, which remain Tangwan's main streets to this day.

Furong village was also first built in Song, completely burned in late Southern Song due to anti-Yuan resistance, rebuilt in Yuan Zhizheng first year (1341),大体 maintaining original layout.

Yantou village's planning was mid-Ming, massively damaged in mid-Qing, but original state can be traced; its water system and garden green space are the highest achievement among Nanxi River villages.

Although no documentary资料, from current state, main planning contents include site selection, water system (canals, channels, ponds, wells), layout (street network, functional zoning, public center, garden green space), defense system (village wall, stream gate, watchtower pavilion) etc.

Some of these planning items may be seen in villages elsewhere, but comprehensively implementing them all, and so orderly, is rare elsewhere — thus becoming an important feature of today's Nanxi River villages.

As for partially preserved planning and construction, almost every village has it.

Nanxi River villages, at founding, all valued site selection highly. Main consideration for site selection was agricultural production and life under self-sufficient natural economy. Basic elements affecting production and life were water, land, mountains. fengshui theories attaching auspicious/inauspicious relations to water, land and mountains were quite active.

Nanxi River people's understanding of living environment was comprehensive and integrated. "Quchuan Ye clan Genealogy · Postscript to Recompiled Genealogy" writes:

Qukou is where our ancestor Guangzong Gong flourished. Thirty-three generations, over a thousand years. Surrounded by several hundred households, stretching seven-eight li. Feng Mountain soars west, Lei Peak stands east, south has Yushan, beyond which great stream encircles. A canal runs through, can irrigate fields.

North has layered green peaks, varied forms. paths connect all directions, fields high and low with horizontal roads, arranged like a painting. Springs flow gently, sound meets ear. Place remote yet not remote, mountains poor yet not poor. Has sandalwood to gather, glutinous millet to plant, delicacies to eat, fresh fish to consume.

Mulberry and hemp cover fields, grain stretches continuous. Bamboo shoots in ground touch clothes, tea days roast cattle — people have long benefited from its advantages! (Daoguang twentieth year, thirty-first-generation descendant Judong zhi)

This passage describes mountain-river form, transportation, water conservancy, agriculture, forestry and fishery. "Xunzi · Strong State" says: "Its fortresses strategic, terrain favorable, mountains forests streams valleys beautiful, natural resources abundant — this is strategic terrain." Qukou is exactly such a strategic place; it is now one of the large villages in Middle Nanxi River.

(1) First consideration for village site selection: sufficient arable land for self-sufficient agriculture. As "Guanzi · Land Measurement" says: "The sage establishing a state must choose level land and select fertile terrain... thus using natural resources, what the land produces, to benefit and nourish its people, to raise livestock."

fengshui classic "Xuexin Fu" says "great fortune residence" should be "bright hall flat and open, all phenomena arrayed, all waters converge, all mountains gather, grass lush and trees dense, water deep and soil thick" — same meaning.

Middle Nanxi River villages are mostly distributed on river valley alluvial plains. Here soil fertile, water abundant, transportation convenient, agricultural conditions best. Hillsides on both sides can grow firewood forests and fruit forests.

Especially favorable are relatively open basins; the largest is centered on Yantou village — this basin has most, densest, and relatively large villages. Besides Yantou, important ones include Cangpo, Xiamei, Zhouzhai, Gangtou, Dutou, Furong, Xinan and Fenglin villages etc. — all are Nanxi River's most culturally developed villages.

Their main surnames migrated from Fujian, founded relatively early, and economy and culture consistently led. Other culturally developed large villages also mostly located at small basin centers, such as Huatan, Xikou, Tongzhou, Qukou, Yuzhang villages etc.

"Zhenchuan Zhu clan Combined Genealogy" has "Preface to Boqing Gong's Zhenchuan Ten Poems" describing Huatan village environment:

Hills flank the stream, slopes descend, spreading into marshy plains. Forests shade, paddy fields encircle, residents farm and plant within, prosperous and harmonious. ... This is where heaven and earth's clear qi gathers and congeals, surely there must be outstanding talents emerging among them.

Good water and soil, beneficial to agricultural economy development, can nurture talent. Huatan village indeed was a place of talent, especially in Ming.

In spacious basins, villages mostly occupy flat land, form concentrated and square. In narrow river valleys, villages mostly built against hillsides, yielding precious farmland. And village scale not large, following contour lines in band form. Examples: Pengxi, Daitou and Shuiyun villages.

Shuiyun village's valley slightly wider, so "Daluo Rock Record" says: "villagers mostly gather west of stream at north foot of Ma'an Mountain near Daluo Rock; land flat and open, mountain springs flow down, streams divide into canals, fields connected.

Houses adjacent, over two hundred households living in peace and contentment, large and small ancestral halls visible to each other."

A few villages, e.g. Zhouzhai, with Dutou north and Gangtou south, to yield farmland, built on river beaches. They connect in band form, even linking with Fangxiang village south, length over two kilometers.

Some riverside villages have little farmland, rely on developing handicrafts — e.g. Xi'an village, between a small hill and river, villagers make rice noodles, sell to Wenzhou, supplementing income. Others do carpentry, fire kilns, operate boats etc.

(2) Second consideration for village site selection: avoiding water damage, flood control and drainage. Nanxi River basin has typhoon rains every summer. Whenever heavy rain, river surges, roaring and奔腾, extremely destructive. A great flood in Kangxi era completely washed away Yuzhang village's riverside "outer estate" — to this day it is a sandbar highland.

For flood control, besides sufficient elevation, along winding river banks, villages mostly built on depositional banks while avoiding erosional banks. In fengshui terms: village front should have "belt water" (yaodai shui), not "reverse-bow water" (fangong shui).

fengshui book "Shanlong Yulei" says: "Reverse-back water, shaped like reverse bow, also called reverse-jump water.

This water leaks hall qi, heartless water." Legendary Liu Ji's "Kanyu Manxing" writes: "Gold city curved embracing body, like moon bow produces phoenix and qilin; if reverse bow not bowing to tomb, even Shi Chong's wealth must become poor." Gold city water also called jade belt water or belt water.

"Yangzhai Ten Books" says: "If before door there is jade belt water, high office surely easy to attain; generations going out and in hear reading sounds, glory and wealth shine on gate." Hewan, Heyang, Hesheng, Donggao, Fenglin, Xi'an, Tongzhou, Bilian, Yuzhang, Huatan, Langxia etc. — many larger villages are at "belt water" positions.

Pengxi village, though north faces Hesheng Stream's reverse bow, but facing reverse bow is Xiagangtou's hard towering cliff; while a small stream forms belt east of Pengxi village, pooling into about 2,000 sqm lake — in fengshui exactly "water gathering at heart center," great fortune.

Furong village in Southern Song produced eighteen high-ranking capital officials; villagers to this day relish that "Eighteen Gold Belts," attributing to Furong's "front crossed by belt water, back resting on official-hat rock, three dragons embracing pearl, four waters converging at heart." Furong Chen clan main ancestral hall has a couplet:

Land rests on three cliffs, cliffs emit famous flowers shining through ages;

Gate faces elephant water, water gives birth to elegant qi nourishing generations.

This is exactly that fengshui.

Understanding of depositional and erosional banks affecting village safety was thus swallowed by fengshui superstition. fengshui masters say "belt water" is affectionate, merely derived from its embracing posture — fortunately not contradicting common-sense judgment.

Because Nanxi River basin has much volcanic rhyolite, hard texture, in some sections "reverse-bow water" poses little bank erosion danger, so some villages still built at "reverse-bow water" positions.

As for fengshui inauspiciousness, can use methods to "break" — e.g., building a Guandi temple facing "reverse-bow water" suffices to "turn misfortune into fortune." Pengxi village facing Hesheng Stream's "reverse-bow water" was handled this way. Guandi is "Demon-Subduing Great Emperor," "Grace Lord," able to suppress all disasters.

villages built on depositional banks, besides reducing flood erosion of foundations, "belt water" also forms village's natural boundary, creating sense of territory. Territorial sense strengthens villagers' inward cohesion — very important for kinship villages. Natural boundaries also help reduce disputes between neighboring villages.

(3) Third important consideration for site selection: safety. This is because many villages' founding ancestors came to Nanxi River to escape chaos, and Nanxi River was not a Peach Blossom Spring.

For example, Xiayuan village's founding ancestor came escaping late Tang Huang Chao rebellion; Cangpo, Fenglin, Xikou, Furong and many other villages' founding ancestors came escaping late Five Dynasties Min Kingdom Wang Yanjun's chaos; Qukou village founding ancestor came escaping Fang La rebellion in late Northern Song; Yuzhang village founding ancestor came with Song court's southern migration,辗转 through Jiangxi.

They experienced turmoil, traveled a thousand li颠簸, came to Nanxi River seeking peaceful land to recuperate. Therefore they often favored enclosed environments, or easily defendable environments.

One example: Pengxi village. Surrounded on four sides by high mountains, only a small opening north blocked by Hesheng Stream. To enter this pouch-shaped valley, the only road was a plank road built on cliff by the stream.

Tanxia and Tangwan villages are another type. Surrounded by mountains on three sides, steep and impassable. The other side not wide, with high village wall built, very defensible. To choose this terrain, they even disregarded village orientation — so Tanxia residences all face southwest while Tangwan residences mostly face northeast, though the two villages face each other across the stream.

Some villages, back to mountain and facing water, only reachable by crossing long stone stepping-stones to village gate. E.g. Langxia, Donggao, Baiyan, Heyang villages etc. Xi'an village built east of mountain, west of water, mountains and water encircling, plus a section of village wall, only village gate at south foot of mountain — position very strategic.

Choosing such enclosed or easily defendable environments for village building, of course for safety, but sometimes also for deep reclusion. Huatan village's founding ancestor Cao Yin Weng, Southern Song county magistrate in Yongjia, "Zhenchuan Zhu clan Genealogy · Epitaph of Founding Ancestor Cao Yin Weng Zhu Gong" says he:

Seeing world in chaos, people gathering as bandits, resigned office, settled in Wenzhou. Initially lived east of city at Hualiu Tang. Initially wanted reclusion, but witnessing order and chaos, heart could not let go. Moved again to Luofu, then great chaos struck city.

Said to his son: "This cannot hide my traces!" Looking east and west, settled at Zhenchuan in Qingtong Township. Its land mountains bright and waters clear, birds sing in harmony, forests deep and valleys remote, scenery secluded and clear. Put fame and rank aside, ignored order and chaos.

Later indeed "contented under trees," living Peach Blossom Spring life.

Some villages on open land, no natural defense, had to rely entirely on walls — e.g. Furong, Yantan villages.

Precisely because villages were overall fortified, houses inside could be open and outward-facing, an atmosphere of peace. This is one feature of kinship villages.

(4) Fourth important consideration for site selection: good microclimate. Nanxi River's various microclimates, due to varied terrain, differ greatly; therefore when selecting village site, choose more favorable microclimate.

Legendary "Qingniao Xiansheng Burial Classic" annotated by Jin Chancellor Wu Qinze says ideal living environment should be "plants lush, auspicious qi follows. ... Or originally open and ventilated, now plants lush, covering its deficiency, not feeling open.

Thus life qi natural, plants filling, also by human effort." Good microclimate benefits plant growth, plant growth improves microclimate, of course beneficial to agricultural production and residents' daily life.

Heyang village's founding ancestor Xie Xian Wuwu Gong moved from commandery city to Tangxia village in Nanxi River; one day, "after snow, climbed mountain, saw snow at front of Lantai Mountain melt first, then settled there, later indeed prospered" (see "Heyang Xie clan Genealogy").

Heyang village is on a sunny slope tightly embraced on three sides by "belt water," front has "crane stream rippling green," back has "brocade screen layered green," land high and dry, ample sunlight — hence "snow melts first."

Qukou village's development also adapted to local microclimate. East, west, north surrounded by mountains, blocking winter cold winds. South not far has a relatively low small hill, forming two gaps southeast and southwest.

In summer, monsoon blows north along Nanxi River main stream; part reaches tanxia village, turns northwest along Minor Nanxi River valley, exactly blowing into southeast gap. Therefore Qukou village developed westward, facing monsoon, while planted forests east — because that position gets no monsoon, but faces scorching western sun.

Tanxia and Tangwan villages facing each other across Minor Nanxi River, not far apart — one faces southwest, one northeast. Southwest-facing Tanxia of course has "three suns" shining; northeast-facing Tangwan, in long hot summer, gets monsoon compensating for insufficient sunlight.

(5) Fifth important consideration for village site selection: beautiful scenery. Xikou Dai clan main ancestral hall gate has a couplet:

Waters beautiful and mountains bright, always produce the benevolent and wise;

Land auspicious and people outstanding, forever transmit fame.

Good mountains and waters are good fengshui; good fengshui produces good descendants.

Nanxi River is already renowned for scenic beauty, almost everywhere has fine views. Since Xie Lingyun and Tao Hongjing, Nanxi River people have strong environmental awareness; landscape sentiment became local cultural tradition.

In Song and Ming dynasties, many Middle Nanxi River villages had flourishing literary culture; rural gentry with higher cultural education had keen sensitivity to natural beauty; between examination successes, they wandered among beautiful waters and mountains, chanting endlessly.

This cultural atmosphere certainly influenced their village site selection.

For example, Furong Peak, with three towering sheer cliffs, had Xiayuan, Furong, Yantou, Xinan villages all using it as village landmark, as borrowed scenery for local scenery. "Yantou Jin clan Genealogy" records Yantou founding ancestor Anfu Gong (born Song Chunyou Gengxu, died Yuan Yanyou Wuwu, 1250-1318) "initially lived West Lane...

in Yanyou era came to assess Furong Three Rocks' beauty, then settled there." Another example: Tangwan, also selected by Zheng clan founding ancestor due to beautiful scenery.

"Tangchuan Zheng clan Genealogy · Preface to Rebuilding Tangchuan Zheng clan Genealogy" records founding ancestor "arrived at that place, saw strange peaks towering, weird rocks jagged, front facing Thunder Cliff, back resting on Heaven Rock.

Nine peaks enclose as screen, together with Xun Mountain presenting beauty; twin streams encircle as belt, joining winding ravine flowing fragrance. How could blessed land be inferior?" Thus built village and settled there.

Each village's genealogy has highly exaggerated text praising local scenery's strangeness and beauty, between lines brimming with strong affection for hometown. "Pengxi Xie clan Genealogy · Tongzhi Jiazi (1864) Preface to Rebuilt Genealogy" describes mountain-river scenery:

Nanxi's scenic beauty, only Pengchuan most wondrous. Going upstream over forty li, passing Tanghui swirling, pooling then releasing. Guarding water mouth are lion, elephant, turtle, fish — suddenly rising sheer, sinister and daunting. Also Writing Brush Peak props Longevity Star Rock, Town Screen Mountain faces Ying Isle.

Hengqiang peaks like Guanyin seated on lotus, beauty dressing, eagle catching snake, lion catching elephant. Yangtian Lake, Waterfall Spring, General, Immortal, Ox Nose, Tiger Head, Swallow Nest, Rooster Comb and other sights — all wonderfully beautiful. Wherever my feet have gone, counting them all, none surpass this.

2
水系
中文

2. 水系

村落的选址决定之后，规划与建设的起点就是兴修水系。

水是农业生产的命脉，在农业社会里，兴水利，除水害，是第一等的大事。所以治水的禹被尊为掌水的"大帝"，与掌天的尧，掌地的舜，合称为"三官大帝"。在楠溪江，田间村头，最多的就是三官庙，几乎到处都有。自古以来，治水也是地方官的首要任务之一，北宋王安石倡导的新政，就有一条"起堤堰，决陂塘，为水陆之利"。水也是村庄赖以生存的命脉。居民饮食要水，洗涤要水，农产品加工和家庭手工业要水，救援火灾也要水。水又是改善小气候、美化环境的必要因素。因此，和其他各地一样，楠溪江各村落的宗族组织和乡贤士绅，都把主持和资助水利建设当作一件经常性的善事来做。

农田水系和村落水系通常是统一规划的。对村落来说，水系的功能，一是供水，把水引进村子里，尽可能方便家家户户日常使用；二是排水，把生活污水、雨水和山洪导出村外。供水和排水都靠自流，所以，沟渠系统就成了村落规划的先决因素，它决定了村落的竖向设计，也就是决定了每幢房屋的相对标高，它也迫使村落必须有一定程度的规划。楠溪江流域夏季多暴雨，雨过之后，村子里没有积水，就是因为建设了合理的沟渠系统的缘故。芙蓉村的北部，内外两道寨墙之间，过去虽然有过几幢大宅，被毁于对岩头村的械斗，但大部分并没有造过房屋，然而却已有整齐的石子路和沟渠。它们证明，村子的建设，是先修筑道路和沟渠，然后才在它们界划出来的地块里造房子的。

楠溪江中游村落的水系，大致有两大类，一类是平川地的，一类是山坡地的。此外还有两类混合的和少数很特殊的。

（1）苍坡、芙蓉、岩头这些平地上的村落，街道和水系与农田相似。方整的街道网把村子大体均匀地界划成若干地块，沟渠沿主要街道流经全村。在农田里为种庄稼的地块，在村子里则建造房屋。《大岩岩志》说水云村的农田"道路修整，沟塍画然"。道路和沟塍的规划建设都依仗农民在农地里引水灌溉的经验。北宋时任永嘉太守的杨蟠则在一首咏永嘉的诗里说：

水如棋局分街陌，山似屏帏绕画楼。

村民们熟悉农田的自流灌溉，把建设农田沟塍系统的方法移植到村落规划中来，是很自然的事。二者在原则上是一样的。

风水师则在水渠的流向上有讲究，如苍坡、岩头和芙蓉三村，水渠都从西北进村，除芙蓉村的从正东出村外，另两村的都从东南出村。堪舆家认为，"凡地西北高东南下水流出辰巳问吉"（见宋·王洙：《地理新书》）。这三村的选址和相应的沟渠规划基本上是合乎这个吉祥的地形的。芙蓉村主渠出东门前，沿"演乐台"南侧曲折，这是避免"去水无情"，而要它多情地滞留一下。村民们把这曲折叫做"锁"，锁住"风"和"气"。

沟渠傍主要街道的一侧，宽度大小不等。最宽的是岩头村浚水街西侧的水渠，有2.7米左右，最窄的是芙蓉村如意街南侧的，只略宽于0.5米。苍坡村的笔街南侧，水渠宽约1米。因为村子里绝大多数的房屋朝同一个方向，所以，水渠通常流经房屋的后门或侧门，门内便是厨房，在有门口的地方，架上石板通行。

水渠边上隔不远便有一两步石级，也有横跨水渠的石板，用来供妇女们洗涤时当搓板用，或者放置桶、盆和衣物。沿着长街短巷，年轻的妇女们蹲踞在清清的沟渠边浣洗。孩子们绕着鹅兜嬉戏，乖巧的小姑娘也会拿一块帕子，学妈妈的样子在石板上揉搓。潺潺水声应和着笑语声，在街上回荡。柔和温暖的生活气息，漫溢在整个村落。也只有在这样的村落里，才会有"秋风催木叶，万户捣衣声"的诗境。

由于水渠同时也流泄各家的生活污水和雨水，所以以前村子都有村规，保护渠水的一定的清洁度。例如岩头村，每天日出之前不许洗衣洗菜，让各家把澄清了一夜的水打回家去作饮食用水；渠内不许放鹅鸭，不许洗沾有粪便的衣物，每年清理一次渠道；等等。虽然有这些规定，有些村子的村民们的饮食用水，仍多汲自水井。苍坡村水井比较多，至少有十三口。岩头村因为渠水量大流急，所以村民挑清晨的渠水饮食，村里没有水井。

许多村子里都有池塘，由水渠的水汇潴而成。除了供洗涤等日常用途之外，还更有防火的作用。由于水池比较开阔，在村子里造成空间的变化，又由于水池特有的美学品质，所以，它们常常成为村子里重要的环境因素。

芙蓉村中央，主要街道如意街的南侧，有芙蓉池，池中设一座芙蓉亭。枫林镇的中心，圣旨门对面，有圣旨门池，也是长方形的，池中造了一座亭子。

渠口村的池塘比较大，在村子前沿，北岸边也有一座长方形的凉亭造在水中。这些水池和亭子，都是村子最重要的休闲场所，尤其是老年人交流几十年友情的场所。有些水池成为公共园林的主要因素，例如溪口村、苍坡村和岩头村的水池，它们的面积都比较大。

以水池为主体的村子里的休闲中心和广阔的公共园林，是楠溪江村落最有特色的景观之一。

（2）塘湾、坦下、埭头、水云这些山坡上的村落，水系的规划与平地村子大不一样，它们的水系最重要的作用是泄山洪。主要的泄洪道大多是天然的冲沟，两岸人工衬砌加固。村子的规划，既要利用泄洪沟排水，又要防山洪的冲击。

山洪来也猛，去也疾，而且洪沟很深，所以不便日用，村子里一般不修沿街引水的水渠，街道上只有二三十厘米宽的排水沟，把雨水和污水送进洪沟。村民的用水，一靠筑池塘汇蓄，如塘湾和埭头；二靠凿井或挖泉源池，如坦下和塘湾；三靠用毛竹当输水管从山上引泉水到家，如水云、埭头、塘湾等村子。

蓬溪村所靠的山比较高、比较深，所以，除了山洪之外，还有常年不断的溪水。蓬溪村的水系，因而也是复合式的，既有山洪冲沟，也有沿主街道的水渠，而且水量丰沛，水流湍急，比较清洁。

除了上面两种水系之外，还有少量特殊的水系。

例如溪南村，它只有一条三五米宽的水渠，弯弯曲曲，反复来回盘绕，村里的房屋就顺势造在这九曲十八弯的水渠两侧。村民日常用水靠这条水渠，街巷里只有大约30厘米宽的排水沟，把雨水和下水排入这条大渠。沿渠有石级、石板、石桥，供妇女洗涤或取水时使用。岸边多种树木，因而这道水渠在村子里形成一条宽阔的绿化带，大大美化了村子的生活环境和改善了小气候。另外在它的卢氏大宗祠前面还有一口池塘，恰好倒映着芙蓉三峰。由于房屋造在弯曲的渠道两岸，所以朝向偏侧，各各不同。楠溪江中游盆地里的村子，房屋大多共朝一个方向，溪南村是个例外。不过它东部建房比较晚近，街巷修直，房舍整齐，与其他村落相似，居民用水靠南边的水渠。

另一个例子是西岸村。它造在一座小山的东坡，面临大楠溪而隔着一片乱石滩。山小没有水源，江滩太阔不便于日常取水，所以村民只好开凿几处大口井。大口井很特别，先挖一个面积很大的深坑，坑壁和坑底都用大块卵石衬砌，坑的平面如同一只瓠瓜，在"瓜"的尽头上宽阔处是储水的井，"瓜"的长柄渐窄处是下去的长台阶。这种水井因此就叫做瓠瓜井。掘井而饮，对整个村子的规划布局就没有多大的影响。

English

2. Water System

After village site selection is decided, the starting point of planning and construction is building the water system.

Water is the lifeline of agricultural production. In agricultural society, developing water conservancy and eliminating water hazards were the first priority.

Therefore Yu the Great, who controlled floods, was honored as the "Great Emperor" governing water, together with Yao governing heaven and Shun governing earth — collectively called the "Three Officials Great Emperor." In Nanxi River, the most common temples in fields and villages are Three Officials temples, almost everywhere.

Since ancient times, flood control was also one of local officials' primary tasks; Northern Song Wang Anshi's reform included "building dikes and weirs, opening ponds, for benefit of land and water." Water is also the lifeline villages depend on.

Residents need water for drinking, washing, agricultural product processing and household handicrafts, and fire rescue. Water is also a necessary factor for improving microclimate and beautifying environment.

Therefore, like elsewhere, Nanxi River villages' clan organizations and local gentry all regarded sponsoring and funding water conservancy construction as a regular charitable act.

Farmland water systems and village water systems are usually planned together. For villages, water system functions: first, water supply — bringing water into village, as convenient as possible for every household's daily use; second, drainage — channeling domestic sewage, rainwater and mountain floods out of village.

Both supply and drainage rely on gravity flow, so canal system becomes the prerequisite factor of village planning; it determines the village's vertical design, i.e., the relative elevation of each house, and forces villages to have a certain degree of planning.

Nanxi River basin has heavy summer rains; after rain, villages have no standing water precisely because of reasonable canal systems.

In northern Furong village, between inner and outer village walls, although there were once several large mansions destroyed in clan fighting with Yantou, most had no houses built, yet already had orderly stone paths and canals. They prove village construction first built roads and canals, then built houses in the plots they demarcated.

Middle Nanxi River village water systems roughly fall into two categories: flatland and hillside. Additionally there are mixed types and a few very special ones.

(1) Flatland villages like Cangpo, Furong and Yantou: streets and water systems resemble farmland. Square street network divides village into roughly uniform plots; canals run along main streets through whole village. In farmland these plots grow crops; in village they hold houses.

"Daluo Rock Record" says Shuiyun village's farmland has "roads neat, field boundaries clearly drawn." Road and field boundary planning relied on farmers' irrigation experience. Northern Song Yongjia prefect Yang Pan wrote in a poem about Yongjia:

Water like chessboard divides streets and paths;

Mountains like screens surround painted towers.

villagers familiar with farmland gravity irrigation naturally transplanted farmland canal system methods into village planning. The two are identical in principle.

fengshui masters had讲究 on canal flow direction. For Cangpo, Yantou and Furong villages, canals all enter from northwest; except Furong's exits due east, the other two exit southeast. fengshui experts believe "where land northwest high southeast low, water flowing out between Chen and Si is auspicious" (see Song Wang Zhu: "Geography New Book").

These three villages' site selection and corresponding canal planning basically conform to this auspicious terrain. Furong village's main canal, before exiting east gate, curves south of "Performance platform" — to avoid "water leaving heartlessly," wanting it to linger affectionately. villagers call this curve "lock," locking "wind" and "qi."

Canals run alongside main streets, widths varying. Widest is Yantou village's Junshui Street west canal, about 2.7 meters; narrowest is Furong village's ruyi street south canal, just over 0.5 meters. Cangpo village's Brush Street south canal is about 1 meter wide.

Because most houses in village face same direction, canals usually flow past house back or side doors; inside door is kitchen; at doorways, stone slabs bridge for passage.

Along canals, every short distance there are one or two stone steps, and stone slabs spanning canals, used by women as washboards or for placing buckets, basins and clothes. Along long streets and short lanes, young women squat by clear canals washing.

Children play around goose-shaped basins; clever little girls take a handkerchief, imitating mothers rubbing on stone slabs. Gurgling water echoes with laughter, reverberating in streets. Soft warm life atmosphere permeates whole village.

Only in such villages can there be the poetic scene of "autumn wind urges leaves, ten thousand households hear washing sounds."

Because canals also drain household sewage and rainwater, villages previously had village rules protecting canal water cleanliness.

For example Yantou village: before sunrise each day no washing clothes or vegetables, allowing households to draw water clarified overnight for drinking; no geese or ducks in canal; no washing clothes soiled with feces; canals cleaned once yearly; etc. Despite these rules, some villages' drinking water still came mostly from wells.

Cangpo village has relatively many wells, at least thirteen. Yantou village, because canal water volume large and flow rapid, villagers draw early morning canal water for drinking; village has no wells.

Many villages have ponds, formed by canal water pooling. Besides daily uses like washing, they also serve fire prevention. Because water pools are relatively open, creating spatial variation in village, and because of water's unique aesthetic quality, they often become important environmental factors in village.

In center of Furong village, south of main street ruyi street, is Furong Pool, with a Furong Pavilion in middle. At center of Fenglin Town, opposite Imperial Decree Gate, is Imperial Decree Gate Pool, also rectangular, with a pavilion built in it.

Qukou village's pond is relatively large, at village front; north bank also has a rectangular pavilion built in water. These pools and pavilions are village's most important leisure places, especially for elderly exchanging decades of friendship. Some pools become main elements of public gardens — e.g. Xikou, Cangpo and Yantou villages' pools, all relatively large in area.

Leisure centers and expansive public gardens centered on pools are one of Nanxi River villages' most distinctive landscapes.

(2) Hillside villages like Tangwan, Tanxia, Daitou and Shuiyun: water system planning differs greatly from flatland villages; their water system's most important function is discharging mountain floods. Main flood channels are mostly natural gullies, with both banks artificially lined and reinforced.

village planning must both use flood channels for drainage and protect against mountain flood impact.

Mountain floods come fiercely and leave quickly, and flood channels are deep, so inconvenient for daily use; villages generally don't build street-side water canals, only 20-30 cm wide drainage ditches on streets sending rainwater and sewage into flood channels. villagers' water supply: first, building ponds to collect and store — e.g.

Tangwan and Daitou; second, digging wells or spring pools — e.g. Tanxia and Tangwan; third, using bamboo as pipes to channel mountain spring water to homes — e.g. Shuiyun, Daitou, Tangwan villages.

Pengxi village's backing mountains are relatively high and deep, so besides mountain floods, there is also year-round stream water. Pengxi village's water system is therefore composite: both mountain flood gullies and main-street canals, with abundant water, rapid flow, relatively clean.

Besides the above two water system types, there are a few special ones.

For example Xinan village: it has only one 3-5 meter wide canal, winding and repeatedly looping; village houses are built along both sides of this nine-bend eighteen-turn canal. villagers' daily water relies on this canal; streets only have about 30 cm wide drainage ditches sending rainwater and wastewater into this big canal.

Along canal are stone steps, slabs and bridges for women washing or drawing water. Banks planted with many trees, so this canal forms a wide green belt in village, greatly beautifying living environment and improving microclimate. Additionally in front of its Lu clan main ancestral hall is a pond, exactly reflecting Furong Three Peaks.

Because houses built on both sides of winding canal, orientations are oblique and varied. In Middle Nanxi River basin villages, houses mostly face one direction; Xinan village is an exception. However its eastern area built relatively late, streets straight, houses orderly, similar to other villages; residents use south canal for water.

Another example: Xi'an village. Built on east slope of a small hill, facing Great Nanxi River across a boulder beach. Hill small with no water source; river beach too wide for convenient daily water drawing, so villagers had to dig several large-mouth wells.

Large-mouth wells are very special: first dig a very large deep pit; pit walls and bottom lined with large cobblestones; pit plan resembles a gourd melon — at the "melon's" wide end is the water-storing well, at the "melon's" long handle narrowing is the long descending steps. This type of well is therefore called gourd-melon well.

Digging wells for drinking had little impact on whole village's planning layout.

3
边界
中文

1. 边界

楠溪江中游村落大多有明确的边界。除了自然物如山、水等等可以界定范围之外，更重要的是寨墙、防洪堤、拦水坝等等。这道边界是防御外敌用的，因而也是团结本村人用的。所以，寨墙之外没有零散的房屋，寨墙之内却有供发展之用的空地，如芙蓉村、花坛村和廊下村，这空地很宽阔。

（1）规划建设这样防御性很强的村落，需要村民之间有很强的内聚力。因此，楠溪江的村落都只能是排他的、单姓的血缘村落。有些村子，如蓬溪、水云、渠口、廊下等，早年原来是杂姓的，后来弱姓渐渐被强姓逼走，变成了单姓村。

由于边界确定，寨墙外不再建造房屋，所以，一旦人口增殖到一定限度，就得有一些人另寻地方建立新村。旧姓大宗祠如芙蓉陈氏、花坛朱氏、渠口叶氏、鹤垟谢氏、西岸金氏等，都有房派分出去另建村落。这样分了又分，有些姓氏，如谢氏、陈氏，在楠溪江就分别有二十几个和十几个村子。

经济最发达的岩头村，情况很特殊。金姓族人都住在寨墙之内，还有些杂姓聚集在寨墙外的东北部。后来在这里发展起了楠溪江中游最繁荣的商业。而包围在寨墙之内的一个个村子里，商业受到宗法制度的轻视和限制，发展得很缓慢。

（2）村落的范围大小相差很远。岩头村大约有18.5公顷，芙蓉村大约14.3公顷；苍坡村只有9.4公顷。决定村落大小的因素很复杂，但其中有一个是耕地的多少。最大的村子在中游大盆地中央，小盆地里的村子大于河谷里的，平地的村子大于山坡上的，等等。一般说来，年代比较早的村子，小于年代比较晚的。以中游盆地为例，晚唐的下园村、五代的周宅村和苍坡村都不大，而最大的岩头村和枫林村的寨墙都建于明代。这种情况说明，作为边界的寨墙一旦建成，村子的规模就不再扩大了。

在平地的村落，边界轮廓大多比较规整，一般近于方形或略呈长方形，方位也比较正。贴近水边或靠山坡的村落，边界轮廓或顺江岸或依等高线而有成带形的趋势，例如水云村。在特殊自然环境中的，则由环境的条件决定它们的形状，如坐落在圈椅形的山坳里的坦下村和塘湾村。

（3）作为村落边界的寨墙，用蛮石和大块卵石砌成，高约两三米左右，上沿厚度大约40厘米，下部撇脚很大，达1.5米左右，很厚实封闭。每村只有一座大门，其余都是小门，村里村外的区别是很强烈的。

在自然经济条件下，开辟寨门主要为了方便农业生产。例如芙蓉村，朝大路的东面最长（约400米），只有一个门，朝主要农田的西面最短（约300米），却有三个门。苍坡村的情况也是这样，南面朝向大路，只开一个门，其他朝向农田各面有两三个门。不过，朝向大路的门是正门，乡人称为"溪门"，很重视造型。芙蓉村的是一座三开间的两层楼阁式建筑物，苍坡村的是牌楼式的，斗拱壮丽而华丽，艺术水平很高。在多数村落，这座称为溪门的正门，是村里主要街道的起点，而且和大宗祠等组成村子的礼制中心，如芙蓉村。

岩坦村竟有两层围墙，是个例外。东皋村的西门和北门紧靠溪流，堡垒式的，很壮观。

English

1. Boundary

Most Middle Nanxi River villages have clear boundaries. Besides natural features like mountains and water that can define scope, more important are village walls, flood dikes, water-retaining dams etc. This boundary served to defend against external enemies, and thus also to unite villagers.

Therefore, outside village walls there are no scattered houses; inside walls there is vacant land for development — e.g. Furong, Huatan and Langxia villages, this vacant land quite spacious.

(1) planning and building such highly defensive villages required strong cohesion among villagers. Therefore Nanxi River villages could only be exclusive, single-surname kinship villages. Some villages, e.g. Pengxi, Shuiyun, Qukou, Langxia, were originally multi-surname in early years; later weaker surnames were gradually forced out by stronger ones, becoming single-surname villages.

Because boundary fixed and no houses built outside walls, once population grew to a certain limit, some people had to find other places to establish new villages. Old-surname main ancestral halls — e.g. Furong Chen, Huatan Zhu, Qukou Ye, Heyang Xie, Xi'an Jin — all had branch lineages splitting off to build new villages.

Splitting again and again, some surnames, e.g. Xie and Chen, respectively have over twenty and over ten villages in Nanxi River.

Economically most developed Yantou village is special. Jin clan members all live inside walls; some miscellaneous surnames gathered northeast outside walls. Later here developed the most prosperous commerce in Middle Nanxi River. While within walled villages, commerce was despised and restricted by patriarchal clan system, developing very slowly.

(2) village scope varies greatly in size. Yantou village about 18.5 hectares, Furong village about 14.3 hectares; Cangpo village only 9.4 hectares. Factors determining village size are complex, but one is amount of arable land.

Largest villages are in central middle basin; villages in small basins larger than those in river valleys; flatland villages larger than hillside ones, etc. Generally, earlier-founded villages are smaller than later ones.

Taking middle basin as example: late Tang Xiayuan village, Five Dynasties Zhouzhai and Cangpo villages are all not large; while largest Yantou and Fenglin villages' walls were both built in Ming. This shows once village wall as boundary is built, village scale no longer expands.

In flatland villages, boundary outlines mostly regular, generally near square or slightly rectangular, orientation also fairly straight. villages close to water or against hillsides have boundary outlines trending band-shaped along river banks or contour lines — e.g. Shuiyun village.

In special natural environments, shape determined by environmental conditions — e.g. Tanxia and Tangwan villages nestled in armchair-shaped mountain hollows.

(3) village walls as boundaries are built of rough stones and large cobblestones, about 2-3 meters high, top thickness about 40 cm, lower footing very wide, up to about 1.5 meters, very thick and enclosed. Each village has only one main gate, rest are small gates; distinction between inside and outside village is very strong.

Under natural economy, opening village gates mainly for convenient agricultural production. For example Furong village: east side facing main road longest (about 400m), only one gate; west side facing main farmland shortest (about 300m), yet three gates.

Cangpo village similar: south facing main road, only one gate; other sides facing farmland have two-three gates each. However, gate facing main road is main gate, villagers call it "stream gate" (ximen),造型 highly valued.

Furong's is a three-bay two-story pavilion-style building; Cangpo's is paifang-style, with magnificent and ornate bracket sets, high artistic level. In most villages, this main gate called stream gate is starting point of village's main street, and together with main ancestral hall etc. forms village's ritual center — e.g. Furong village.

Yantan village actually has two layers of walls, an exception. Donggao village's west and north gates close to stream, fortress-style, very imposing.

街巷网
中文

2. 街巷网

楠溪江中游的村落，凡地势平坦的，如苍坡、芙蓉、岩头等，一般街巷网方正整齐，成直角相交。但大多数是丁字路口，十字路口极少。这种界划方式，与农田十分相似。甚至地形并不十分平坦的村子，如蓬溪、西岸等，街巷网也力求方正。

街巷大致分为三级，主次分明。

（1）每村都有一条主要大街，笔直贯穿全村，如苍坡的笔街、芙蓉的如意街、岩头的进士街（今名金苍路）。溪口村、花坛村、蓬溪村、西岸村、枫林镇等等，主街也都长而且直，贯穿全村。与主街垂直的几条街是次街，次街之间由更小一级的巷子连接。苍坡村中心有横街。岩头村，次街如浚水街、中央街、菊花街和花前街与主街平行，而由贯穿村中央的横街连系它们。主街、次街和巷子的宽度不同，地面的铺砌不同，功能也有所不同。

大多数村落，主街的走向与村子的总朝向相垂直，如岩头，房子都朝东，进士街南北走向；苍坡，房子都朝南，笔街东西走向，等等。只有芙蓉村，如意街与房子都朝东。这大约与风水有关，例如苍坡的笔街，对着西面的笔架山，岩头的祖山朝东。

主街的一端，村落的边缘，往往是村子最重要的礼制中心，包括溪门和大宗祠，如岩头村进士街的北端为仁道门和金氏大宗祠，芙蓉村如意街的东端是溪门和陈氏大宗祠，花坛村主街的西端是"溪山第一"门和朱氏大宗祠，渠口村主街的东端是叶氏大宗祠，等等。苍坡村笔街的东端是李氏大宗祠，而溪门却在大宗祠的南侧，是个例外，不过溪门仍然和与大宗祠一起组成礼制中心。这些大宗祠的主轴大多与主街平行，而不一定与全村的住宅同朝向，大约是为了一起顺从风水。少数村子，大宗祠与主街相垂直，如溪口的戴氏大宗祠和西岸的金氏大宗祠，但二者都不贴临主街。

村子里的重要建筑物和休闲中心，一般也在主街上。如芙蓉村如意街中段有芙蓉池和芙蓉亭组成的休闲中心，池旁有芙蓉书院，等等；枫林镇的主街中段有圣旨门、圣旨门湖和湖中凉亭形成的建筑群，主街因而被称为圣旨门街。花坛村的主街很特殊，它在寨墙所包围的范围的正中，自西而东，把这范围划分为大致相等的两半。北半是建成区，南半是水田，是预留的发展区。这主街成了建成区的南立面。在这条街上，有大宗祠、宪台祠和敦睦祠，有溪山第一门，至少有五座以上的牌楼，还有三座三官庙，一在西端。一在东端，还有一座在中段。它的景观很丰富，有层次，有变化，作为建成区的立面，是很壮观的。

（2）次要街道都为居住区服务。据岩头和芙蓉两村情况，它们的间距大致在50-60米左右。它们走向南北，而住宅一律朝东，50米的间距，正是三进两院的住宅的总进深。60米的，可以有一所后花园。次街的西侧是住宅的前门，东侧是后墙，贴后墙流着水渠。苍坡的次街也走向南北，但住宅一律朝南，次街40米的间隔还大于一般七开间住宅的总面阔，因而有余地造厕所和猪圈之类，或者种些树木。次街两侧除了少数住宅的正门外，大多是不高的院墙，墙头树木成荫，景观很富画意。

苍坡村住宅的正门少数开在主街的北侧，还开在次街之间的横街上，多数开在次街上，以厢房的南端尽间为大门门屋，从侧面进住宅。各村的三进两院的住宅，往往并不由一家独用，后院因此侧向开一个门。

（3）楠溪江中游村落的街道，比起其他地区的村落来，宽度要大得多。苍坡村的主街笔街，连水渠的宽度达4.85米，次街宽度也在2米以上，有达到3米的。芙蓉村的主街如意街，连水渠宽3.50米，次街宽度将近3米。岩头村的街道最宽，而且次街与主街宽度基本一样。如浚水街，连水渠竟宽7.2米，比较窄的花前街也有5.5米宽，与主街进士街相近。

街巷宽阔，加以建筑密度低（芙蓉村23.1%，苍坡村34%），绿化用地多（芙蓉村17.0%，苍坡村13.8%），街道两侧围墙高度在2米左右，屋檐高度在3米左右，住宅山墙是悬山式的，木构架裸露，又往往退后于围墙，掩映于竹树之中，街上又不时有小小的空场，安置着石板凳，所以，街景很开敞，明朗，光亮，笼罩着一层祥和的气息，教人觉得乡里的亲切和安宁。夕阳下或晚风里，街边水渠旁石上坐着劳累了一天的人们，款款谈着天南地北的古事旧闻和眼前的水旱丰歉，交流着心底的喜悦或者忧虑。

街巷成了公共活动空间，培养着乡邻乡亲的情谊。

这种街巷景象，与阴暗、狭窄、曲折，被挤压在高高的、封闭的马头墙之间的街巷景象完全异趣，也就使楠溪江的村落景象与南方许多地区的完全异趣。楠溪江村落的这种景象，反映着这地区独特的文化历史背景，表现着它的人文精神。它像老农那样坦诚朴实，又像文士那样儒雅灵秀。

（4）山坡地的村落，受到地形的限制，不能像平地上的那样方整，街

English

2. Street Network

In Middle Nanxi River villages, where terrain flat — e.g. Cangpo, Furong, Yantou — street networks are generally square and orderly, intersecting at right angles. But most are T-junctions, crossroads extremely rare. This demarcation method closely resembles farmland. Even villages with not very flat terrain, e.g. Pengxi, Xi'an, street networks strive for squareness.

Streets and lanes roughly divided into three levels, primary and secondary clear.

(1) Each village has one main street, straight through whole village — e.g. Cangpo's Brush Street, Furong's ruyi street, Yantou's jinshi Street (now named Jincang Road). Xikou, Huatan, Pengxi, Xi'an villages, Fenglin town etc. — main streets also long and straight, through whole village.

Several streets perpendicular to main street are secondary streets; secondary streets connected by even smaller lanes. Cangpo village center has cross street. In Yantou village, secondary streets like Junshui, Zhongyang, Juhua and Huaqian streets parallel main street, connected by cross street through village center.

Main street, secondary streets and lanes differ in width, pavement and function.

In most villages, main street orientation is perpendicular to village's overall orientation — e.g. Yantou houses all face east, jinshi Street runs north-south; Cangpo houses all face south, Brush Street runs east-west, etc. Only Furong village: ruyi street and houses both face east. This probably relates to fengshui — e.g.

Cangpo's Brush Street faces west's Brush-rack Mountain; Yantou's ancestral mountain faces east.

At one end of main street, at village edge, is often village's most important ritual center, including stream gate and main ancestral hall — e.g.

Yantou jinshi Street north end is Rende Gate and Jin clan main ancestral hall; Furong ruyi street east end is stream gate and Chen clan main ancestral hall; Huatan main street west end is "Xishan First" gate and Zhu clan main ancestral hall; Qukou main street east end is Ye clan main ancestral hall, etc.

Cangpo Brush Street east end is Li clan main ancestral hall, while stream gate is south of ancestral hall — an exception, but stream gate still together with ancestral hall forms ritual center.

These main ancestral halls' main axes mostly parallel main street, not necessarily same orientation as village houses — probably to together comply with fengshui. In a few villages, main ancestral hall perpendicular to main street — e.g. Xikou Dai clan main ancestral hall and Xi'an Jin clan main ancestral hall, but neither adjacent to main street.

village's important buildings and leisure centers are generally also on main street. E.g.

Furong ruyi street mid-section has leisure center of Furong Pool and Furong Pavilion, beside pool Furong academy, etc.; Fenglin town main street mid-section has building complex of Imperial Decree Gate, Imperial Decree Gate Lake and lake pavilion — main street thus called Imperial Decree Gate Street.

Huatan village's main street is special: it runs west to east through exact center of walled area, dividing it into roughly equal halves. North half is built-up area, south half paddy fields — reserved development area. This main street becomes built-up area's south facade.

On this street: main ancestral hall, Xiantai ancestral hall, Dunmu ancestral hall; Xishan First Gate; at least five or more paifangs; and three Three Officials temples — one at west end, one at east end, one in middle. Its landscape rich, layered, varied — as built-up area's facade, very imposing.

(2) Secondary streets all serve residential areas. According to Yantou and Furong villages, their spacing roughly 50-60 meters. They run north-south, while houses all face east; 50-meter spacing is exactly total depth of three-courtyard two-hall house. 60-meter spacing can have a back garden.

Secondary street west side is house front doors, east side is back walls, with canal flowing along back wall. Cangpo's secondary streets also run north-south, but houses all face south; 40-meter secondary street spacing greater than general seven-bay house's total width, thus room for toilets and pigsties etc., or planting trees.

Besides a few house main doors, secondary street两侧 mostly low courtyard walls, wall tops shaded by trees, landscape very picturesque. Cangpo village house main doors: a few on main street north side, also on cross streets between secondary streets, most on secondary streets — using wing room's south end bay as gatehouse, entering house from side.

Each village's three-courtyard two-hall houses often not used by one family alone; back courtyard therefore has a side door.

(3) Middle Nanxi River village streets, compared to villages in other regions, are much wider. Cangpo main street Brush Street, including canal, reaches 4.85 meters wide; secondary streets over 2 meters, some up to 3 meters. Furong main street ruyi street, including canal, 3.50 meters wide; secondary streets nearly 3 meters.

Yantou village streets widest, and secondary streets basically same width as main street. E.g. Junshui Street, including canal, actually 7.2 meters wide; narrower Huaqian Street also 5.5 meters wide, close to main jinshi Street.

Streets wide, plus low building density (Furong 23.1%, Cangpo 34%), much green land (Furong 17.0%, Cangpo 13.8%), street-side walls about 2 meters high, eaves about 3 meters high, house gable walls of overhanging-gable type with exposed wooden frames, often set back from walls, half-hidden among bamboo and trees; streets occasionally have small open spaces with stone benches.

Therefore streetscape very open, bright, luminous, shrouded in auspicious atmosphere, making one feel village warmth and peace. At sunset or in evening breeze, people tired from day sit on stones by street-side canals, chatting leisurely about old stories and current harvests, sharing inner joys or worries.

Streets become public activity space, cultivating neighborly affection.

This street scene, completely different from dark, narrow, winding streets squeezed between high enclosed horse-head walls, makes Nanxi River village scenery completely different from many southern regions. Nanxi River villages' this scene reflects region's unique cultural-historical background, expresses its humanistic spirit.

It is as frank and simple as an old farmer, yet as refined and elegant as a scholar.

(4) Hillside villages, limited by terrain, cannot be as square as flatland villages; streets
