功能分区
中文

3. 功能分区

楠溪江中游村落的布局虽然简单，功能分区还在萌芽状态，却也有大致的模式。例如，全村公有的庙宇一般都在"水门"或者"水口"。岩头的三圣庙、娘娘庙和太阴宫集中在村北一公里外的双浚头，那儿是水门，而塔湖庙又位于两个水口之一。塘湾的山隍庙和蓬溪的关帝庙都在水口。大宗祠和旌表性建筑一般都在溪门近旁。商店出现于过境交通所经之处，或者在村里主街的街口上。

在岩头村的浚水街、中央街、菊花街和花前街上，曾经各有过比肩七座很堂皇的三进两院式大宅。因此，可以说岩头村的西半部是富户区。同治元年（1862年），因枫林镇人告发岩头人勾通太平天国，清兵烧毁了岩头村许多房屋，这一区完全被焚。现在，浚水街上还可以清晰地辨识相邻的六座大宅的遗址，中轴间距为48.3米。其他三条街上还可以各见到一两座遗址。遗址上都已经建起了小型住宅。

楠溪江村落最重要的特点之一，是在村落里有几种不同功能的公共活动空间。比较重要的，有礼制中心、休闲中心和公园。有些供水比较集中的地方是妇女们聚会的中心。

（1）礼制中心的主体是大宗祠，加上近旁的溪门、旌表性纪念建筑和小广场。构图比较完整的是芙蓉村溪门里以陈氏大宗祠为主的那个建筑群，不过它们没有旌表性建筑。有些村的大宗祠不近溪门，不靠主街，但也与小广场和一些小品建筑形成礼制中心，如港头、周宅、坦下、塘湾等村子。礼制中心比较严肃，而且偏于一侧，所以平日不常有人。大宗祠往往又有主要戏台，演戏时附近村落的人也会赶来看戏，很杂乱，所以大宗祠以在村边甚至村外的为多。

礼制中心是血缘村落最重要的建筑群，它是宗族的象征。大宗祠告诉村民们，他们的脉管里流着同样的血，要彼此相敬相爱如父老兄弟。贞节牌坊和进士牌楼之类，则提醒村民们珍惜家族的荣誉和尊严。它们同时也显示着族权的强大。

（2）休闲中心有两类，一类是由街道交会、曲折或其他原因形成的小空场，贴墙根架上几条石板或者放上几枚石礅，冬午有暖阳，夏夜有凉风，四季都会聚集着一些男子汉，尤其是老年人。每天来坐坐的人大多是常客，家住得不远，卖鲜肉的也每天都要来歇脚，放下竹筐箩，吹起牛角号，呜呜的，招呼主妇们。属于这类偶然形成的休闲中心，有水云村的"石头蛋"和塘湾村的"上马石"。"石头蛋"在一个丁字路口，路口略呈喇叭形，把3米宽的街道空间扩大为6.5米。路南就是村边，一直望到溪流和远山，更使广场显得空阔。

沿边放了成排的天然圆形石凳，所以叫"石头蛋"。"上马石"在塘湾村中心，后街的中段，也是一个丁字路口，大约有60平方米。这里原来立着石牌坊，牌坊倒坍之后，留下残柱脚和牌坊前两块石头，日长天久，村人不知道牌坊，却因旧日牌坊所对的一段街被附会为"状元街"，旁边的一幢房子被附会为南宋进士郑伯熊的住宅，所以，这几块残柱脚就被附会为"上马石"。这种小中心没有经过规划设计，面貌全由机缘，但是它们比较多，从而丰富了街道和村落的空间变化。对村民来说，它们的好处是近便和随意。

更重要的是，它们吸引人们把他们生活的一部分放在公共空间里进行，突破了住宅里家庭生活的私密性和封闭性，促进了人们的感情交流，有利于加强社区的凝聚力。

楠溪江中游的村落里，大多又有一个经过规划和设计的休闲中心，它相当宽阔、整齐，建筑艺术质量比较高。最著名的是芙蓉村的芙蓉池和芙蓉亭，它们在主街如意街的中段南侧，西邻芙蓉书院。芙蓉池东西长43.0米，南北宽13.0米。芙蓉亭在池中央偏东，是个两层楼阁式歇山顶的方亭，四棵金柱、十二棵檐柱。从池子的南、北两岸都有石板桥通达亭子。池子四周有建筑物环列，边界十分明确肯定，因而空间完整、安定，气氛宁静而有向心力，容易使处身其中的人有一种亲和感。池南，南北向的中央街对着亭子而稍偏，正好看见翻飞的翼角。

这是一种最佳的对景设计。池北也有一条南北向的路，正对着亭子，所见景象就失于呆板。在三面粉墙的衬托下，从如意街上欣赏清亮的水池和玲珑的亭子，画面很生动活泼。水亭周边设美人靠，从早到晚都有老人闲坐聊天。池子的南北两岸设置石级和石板，给妇女们搓洗衣服。这里时时刻刻都呈现着温暖的生活场景。

枫林镇的休闲中心是圣旨门、圣旨门湖和湖中央的水亭所组成的建筑群，格局与芙蓉池很相似。圣旨门是明代成化年间宪宗皇帝为旌表徐尹沛三兄弟的友爱而赐建的，名叫"尚义之门"。门在主街圣旨门街之北，湖在街南，二者隔街相对，相距13米。湖东西长26.7米，面北宽7.0米，周边有树木，景观与芙蓉池不同。水亭偏在西侧，长4.0米，宽2.4米。两层、三开间、歇山顶的圣旨门和池边一小块照壁，使画面比芙蓉池复杂一些。门前地面上有一方卵石镶嵌的美丽图案。这个建筑群是枫林镇的"十景"之一，叫"瑶亭御风"。

溪口村的水池最大，叫莲池，长100米左右，池中也有一座亭子，是石质的。又有一座"明文里门"，是供奉南宋戴蒙办东山书院时所得宋光宗御

赐"明文"二字匾额的。门三开间，单层，悬山顶，跨丁字路口，也是村人们休闲的场所。

蓬溪村谢氏是谢灵运的后裔，村中有座"康乐亭"，亭前有个梯形小空场，是五条村路的交会点，这里也是一个休闲中心。

这类经过规划设计的休闲中心，地位很重要。它们一般设在村子的中央，有观赏性很高的建筑物，甚至有不小的水池，造成村子里空间和景观的强烈变化，成为村子的趣味焦点。它们同时也具有礼乐教化的意义。尚义门、明文门、康乐亭和芙蓉书院，都是旌表性的或者文教性的建筑物。休闲中心的建立，本意并不是为了给人一个闲聊家常的场所，而是为了更充分地发挥那些建筑物的教化作用。

（3）除了礼制中心和休闲中心，楠溪江村落往往还有一些其他性质的建筑中心或者综合性的中心。周宅村的中心是一个长方形的广场，东西长18.0米，南北宽12.5米，面积大约200平方米。它西端有一座土地庙，庙不大，形制却有些变化。广场前沿偏左立着一座小小的双柱门，宽度只有1.8米，但四层丁头棋层层挑出，形式不简单，风格很有力量。门前下台阶约1米便是沿村边的路。门柱上有楹联：

两柱门台垂古迹；六步阶级有遗风。

从双柱门到土地庙，穿广场，有一条2.2米宽，大约20米长的路，用细卵石砌了"十八对莲花"的图案。双柱门下小广场（5.20米×9.80米）地面，也有一方卵石砌成的图案，题材是白鹤、花篮和一对茶花。这几片卵石镶嵌，是周宅村的标志，村人见到眼生的人，上前打问，能说出这几片卵石镶嵌的，就是自己人，说不出的，就是可疑的闯入者。这个广场不是供休闲用的，这是一个村民聚会的场所和宗教活动的场所。

塘湾村中街西北端的"上桥头"是全村最重要的中心。这里有郑氏大宗祠、五桂祠和松房祠，是一个礼制中心。但它又是商业中心和休闲中心，功能是综合的。它的整个空间组织也很特殊，由大宗祠界划为两部分：一边硬，广场；一边软，水池。

（4）所有的休闲中心都被男子汉占用，妇女们忙于家务，没有时间休闲。她们的社交多在浣洗劳作中进行。池塘边和水渠边是她们活动的地方。但也有些村子有经过规划设计的集中的洗涤场所。比较有特色的是苍坡村、下园村和西岸村的"洗涤中心"。

苍坡村的西池，大而深，妇女洗涤时，尤其年老的或者带小孩子的，很不安全。因此，在西池的西岸，设了三个小小的浅水池，给人使用，渠水从村西北引来，沿笔街南侧流到这里，先后经过三个浅水池再注入西池。为了保持一定程度的卫生，村中规定，第一个池洗食物，第二个洗衣服，第三个洗农具。浅水池的西侧又有一口井。

西岸村东部边缘有一条水渠，沿渠造了两个洗涤场。它们是矩形的，下沉式，三面砌成层层宽阔的阶台，尽管水位高低变化，都可以有适当的阶台便于洗涤。由于这洗涤场是个围合的空间，能够有效地在浣洗妇女心里造成亲和感，促进友谊的交流。有一个这样的洗涤场就在村子东北角的瓠瓜井附近，长14.2米，宽6.2米。这一区有七棵直径在1米以上的苦槠树和樟树。树外是一层层的滩林，无边的浓绿。妇女们就在这样优美的环境里劳作。

风景同样优美而更有浑厚的历史感的，是下园村的"洗涤中心"。它在寨门之外，寨门是个石券门，两侧还有残石墙。门在坡上，一条石块路从门里伸出，渐渐下坡，一侧是石墙，另一侧下一道高高的石坎就是这洗涤中心，有两个椭圆形石池子，还有一口泉眼井。这一片形式多变、高低起伏的石头造景，以雄壮的大块毛石砌的寨门寨墙为衬托，非常苍劲而有古意，又整个笼罩在高大树木的浓荫之下。

这些经过规划设计的"洗涤中心"，与村子里的池塘和水渠边的各种各样的洗涤设施一起，表现出楠溪江村落的建设者们，细致地考虑到了日常生活的需要，在当时可能的条件下认真地满足了这些需要。正是这种对生活的关切和对人的体贴，使楠溪江村落洋溢着人情的美。

English

3. Functional Zoning

Although Middle Nanxi River village layouts are simple and functional zoning still in embryonic state, there are general patterns.

For example, village-owned temples are generally at "water gate" or "water mouth." Yantou's Three Sages temple, Goddess temple and Taiyin Palace are concentrated at Shuangjuntou one kilometer north of village — that is the water gate — while Tahu temple is at one of two water mouths.

Tangwan's Mountain Guardian temple and Pengxi's Guandi temple are both at water mouths. Main ancestral halls and commemorative buildings are generally near stream gates. shops appear at transit routes or at village main street entrances.

On Yantou village's Junshui, Zhongyang, Juhua and Huaqian streets, there once stood seven adjacent grand three-courtyard two-hall mansions on each. Therefore, Yantou's western half can be called a wealthy household district.

In Tongzhi first year (1862), because Fenglin townspeople reported Yantou people colluding with Taiping Heavenly Kingdom, Qing troops burned many Yantou houses; this district was completely burned. Now on Junshui Street one can clearly identify remains of six adjacent large mansions, with central axis spacing of 48.3 meters.

On other three streets one or two remains each can be seen. Small houses have been built on the remains.

One of Nanxi River villages' most important features is having several types of public activity spaces with different functions. Important ones include ritual center, leisure center and park. Some places with concentrated water supply are women's gathering centers.

(1) Ritual center's main body is main ancestral hall, plus nearby stream gate, commemorative buildings and small plaza. Most compositionally complete is Furong village's building complex inside stream gate centered on Chen clan main ancestral hall, though without commemorative buildings.

Some villages' main ancestral halls not near stream gate or main street, but still form ritual centers with small plazas and small buildings — e.g. Gangtou, Zhouzhai, Tanxia, Tangwan villages. Ritual centers are relatively solemn and off to one side, so not often visited on ordinary days.

Main ancestral halls often also have main stage; during performances people from nearby villages come to watch, very crowded, so main ancestral halls are mostly at village edge or even outside village.

Ritual center is kinship village's most important building complex; it is the clan's symbol. Main ancestral hall tells villagers they share the same blood, should respect and love each other like elders and brothers. Chastity paifangs and jinshi paifangs etc. remind villagers to cherish family honor and dignity. They also demonstrate the power of clan authority.

(2) Leisure centers are of two types. One type: small open spaces formed by street intersections, bends or other causes, with a few stone slabs or stone blocks along walls; winter noon has warm sun, summer evening has cool breeze; year-round gather men, especially elderly.

Daily visitors mostly regulars, living nearby; fresh meat sellers also come daily to rest, put down bamboo baskets, blow buffalo horn — woo woo — calling housewives.

Such casually formed leisure centers include Shuiyun village's "Stone Egg" and Tangwan village's "Mounting Stone." "Stone Egg" is at a T-junction, junction slightly trumpet-shaped, expanding 3-meter-wide street space to 6.5 meters. South of road is village edge, looking out to stream and distant mountains, making plaza feel spacious.

Rows of natural round stone benches along edge, hence "Stone Egg." "Mounting Stone" is in Tangwan village center, mid-section of back street, also a T-junction, about 60 sqm.

Originally a stone paifang stood here; after paifang collapsed, remaining pillar bases and two stones in front; over time villagers forgot paifang, but because street once facing paifang was fancifully called "Zhuangyuan Street," adjacent house fancifully called Southern Song jinshi Zheng Boxiong's residence, these remnant pillar bases were fancifully called "Mounting Stone." Such small centers were not planned; appearance entirely by chance, but they are relatively numerous, enriching street and village spatial variation.

For villagers, their advantage is convenience and casualness. More importantly, they attract people to conduct part of their lives in public space, breaking through privacy and enclosure of home family life, promoting emotional exchange, strengthening community cohesion.

Most Middle Nanxi River villages also have a planned and designed leisure center, quite spacious and orderly, with relatively high architectural art quality. Most famous are Furong village's Furong Pool and Furong Pavilion, on south side of main ruyi street mid-section, west adjacent to Furong academy. Furong Pool 43.0m east-west, 13.0m north-south.

Furong Pavilion slightly east of pool center, a two-story pavilion-style square pavilion with hip-gable roof, four gold columns, twelve eave columns. Stone slab bridges from both south and north banks reach pavilion.

Buildings surround pool, boundary very clear and definite, thus space complete and stable, atmosphere tranquil and centripetal, easily giving those within a sense of affinity. South of pool, north-south Central Street faces pavilion slightly off-center, exactly seeing fluttering wing corners — an optimal opposite-view design.

North of pool also has a north-south road directly facing pavilion, but view appears stiff. Against three sides of white plaster walls, from ruyi street appreciating clear pool and exquisite pavilion, picture very lively. Water pavilion surrounded by beauty-rails; from morning to night elderly sit and chat.

South and north banks have stone steps and slabs for women washing clothes. Here warm life scenes present at all times.

Fenglin Town's leisure center is building complex of Imperial Decree Gate, Imperial Decree Gate Lake and central water pavilion, layout similar to Furong Pool.

Imperial Decree Gate was bestowed by Ming Chenghua era Emperor Xianzong to commend Xu Yinpei three brothers' fraternity, named "Gate of Righteousness." Gate north of main Imperial Decree Gate Street, lake south of street, facing each other across street, 13 meters apart.

Lake 26.7m east-west, 7.0m north width, surrounded by trees, landscape different from Furong Pool. Water pavilion偏 west side, 4.0m long, 2.4m wide. Two-story three-bay hip-gable roof Imperial Decree Gate and a small screen wall by pool make picture more complex than Furong Pool. In front of gate ground has a beautiful cobblestone-inlaid pattern.

This building complex is one of Fenglin Town's "Ten Scenes," called "Jade Pavilion Riding Wind."

Xikou village's pool largest, called Lotus Pool, about 100m long; pool also has a pavilion, stone. Also a "Mingwen Li Gate" — enshrining Southern Song Dai Meng's reward from Emperor Guangzong when establishing Dongshan academy

with "Mingwen" (Clear Culture) two-character plaque. Gate three-bay, single-story, overhanging-gable roof, spanning T-junction, also villagers' leisure place.

Pengxi village Xie clan are descendants of Xie Lingyun; village has a "Kangle Pavilion," in front a trapezoidal small open space, junction of five village roads — also a leisure center.

Such planned leisure centers are very important. They are generally at village center, with highly ornamental buildings, even sizable pools, creating strong spatial and landscape variation, becoming village's focal point of interest. They also have ritual-moral education significance.

Shiyi Gate, Mingwen Gate, Kangle Pavilion and Furong academy are all commemorative or cultural-educational buildings. Establishment of leisure centers was not originally meant as places for casual chat, but to more fully发挥 those buildings' educational function.

(3) Besides ritual and leisure centers, Nanxi River villages often have other types of building centers or comprehensive centers. Zhouzhai village's center is a rectangular plaza, 18.0m east-west, 12.5m north-south, about 200 sqm. West end has a Tudi (Earth God) temple, small but with varied form.

Front-left of plaza stands a small double-column gate, only 1.8m wide, but four layers of dingtou brackets projecting in layers, form not simple, style powerful. Down about 1m steps from gate is road along village edge. Gate pillars have couplet:

two-column gate platform bears ancient traces;

Six-step stairs carry inherited customs.

From double-column gate to Tudi temple, across plaza, a 2.2m wide, about 20m long road paved with fine cobblestones in "eighteen pairs of lotus" pattern. Small plaza below double-column gate (5.20m × 9.80m) ground also has a cobblestone pattern, themes: white crane, flower basket and pair of camellias.

These cobblestone inlays are Zhouzhai village's mark; villagers seeing strangers ask them — those who can describe these inlays are their own, those who cannot are suspicious intruders. This plaza is not for leisure; it is a villagers' gathering place and religious activity place.

Tangwan village's "Upper bridgehead" at northwest end of Middle Street is village's most important center. Here are Zheng clan main ancestral hall, Wugui ancestral hall and Songfang ancestral hall — a ritual center. But it is also commercial center and leisure center, functions comprehensive.

Its whole spatial organization special: divided by main ancestral hall into two parts — one hard, plaza; one soft, pool.

(4) All leisure centers are occupied by men; women busy with housework, no time for leisure. Their socializing mostly happens during washing labor. Pool sides and canal sides are their activity places. But some villages have planned concentrated washing places. Distinctive ones are Cangpo, Xiayuan and Xi'an villages' "washing centers."

Cangpo village's West Pool, large and deep; women washing, especially elderly or with small children, unsafe. Therefore on west bank of West Pool, three small shallow pools built for use; canal water from village northwest, flowing along south side of Brush Street to here, passing through three shallow pools then into West Pool.

To maintain some cleanliness, village rules: first pool wash food, second wash clothes, third wash farm tools. West of shallow pools another well.

Xi'an village east edge has a canal; along canal two washing places built. They are rectangular, sunken, three sides built into层层 wide stepped platforms; despite water level changes, appropriate steps available for washing. Because washing place is enclosed space, effectively creates affinity among washing women, promoting friendship exchange.

One such washing place is near village northeast corner's gourd-melon well, 14.2m long, 6.2m wide. This area has seven trees over 1m diameter — sawtooth oak and camphor. Outside trees layers of riverside forest, boundless deep green. Women labor in such beautiful environment.

Equally beautiful but with richer historical sense is Xiayuan village's "washing center." It is outside village gate; gate is stone arch gate,两侧 remaining stone walls.

Gate on slope; a stone road extends from gate, gradually descending, one side stone wall, other side down high stone坎 is this washing center, with two oval stone pools and a spring well.

This area of varied forms, undulating stone landscaping, set against massive rough-stone village gate and walls, very vigorous and archaic, entirely shaded by tall trees.

These planned "washing centers," together with various washing facilities by village pools and canals, demonstrate Nanxi River village builders carefully considered daily life needs, earnestly satisfying them under possible conditions. Precisely this concern for life and consideration for people makes Nanxi River villages brim with human warmth.

6
公共园林
中文

4. 公共园林

楠溪江流域山奇水秀，处处风景如画。这样的天然环境培育了人们的山水情怀，激起了他们兴建更美好的环境的愿望。村落里往往都有的公共园林，凝聚着村民们对山川之美的一片痴情，它们是楠溪江中游村落最动人的特点之一。

目前保存得比较好的公共园林是岩头村的塔湖庙——丽水湖景区和埭头村的卧龙冈，俗称"大头"。水面还在而绿地已毁的有溪口村的荷塘和苍坡村的东、西池。依稀只存残迹的是岩头村的上花园、鹤垟村的兰台山和鹤盛村的风水山。蓬溪村的凤凰山与霞港头和西岸村的瓠瓜井区，也有很浓的园林趣味。

（1）公园在村子里的位置，或者决定于自然条件，或者也有风水堪舆的考虑。岩头村的塔湖庙——丽水湖景区和苍坡村的东、西湖，都是筑堤坝拦蓄由水渠引经村子的山水而形成的。它们都位于村子的东南部，堪舆家认为："山起西北，水归东南"是吉壤。如宋代王洙等撰的《地理新书》所说："西北高，东南下，水流出巽，为天地之势也。"塔湖庙——丽水湖景区有塔湖庙，东、西湖之间有仁济庙和太阴庙，这两处公园，虽然处在村子范围之内，但却在渠水出村的地方，大约堪舆家把它们都看作"水口"了。

不过，苍坡村的李氏大宗祠紧靠着太阴庙和仁济庙，并不符合堪舆家关于宗祠"切不可近神坛寺观"的告诫。（见《宅谱指要·宗祠》）溪口村的公园也以水池为主体，位于村子的北端，水渠进入村子的地方，堪舆家称为"天门"的。

这几块水面都很大。苍坡村的东、西湖是整齐的长方形，东湖南北长147米，东西宽19米，西湖东西长80米，南北宽35米，两者之间还有水面连接，它长28米，宽16米。就在这一片连接水面的北岸，造了李氏大宗祠（初建于北宋仁宗至和二年，即1055年，以后多次重建）和它东侧的仁济庙（十世祖李伯钧于南宋孝宗淳熙七年，即1180年，辞官回乡后造）、仁济庙北的太阴庙。仁济庙三面临水，都造了轻快的敞廊，廊子边缘是美人靠。连李氏大宗祠的南侧原来也是敞廊临水。

庙宇和宗祠的形制一般都是很保守的，呆板而且封闭，苍坡的李氏大宗祠和仁济庙却勇敢地突破了陈规旧习。楠溪江人是通脱的，并不拘泥于传统；楠溪江人是爱美的，为园林艺术的和谐统一，对大宗祠这种重要到几乎神圣的建筑，也敢于出格创新。

东池的北端是"水月堂"，造在水中，以一座两米多长的小石板桥与西岸相接。这是一座三开间的房屋，悬山顶。两侧有廊，上覆披檐，与中间的悬山顶相组合，近似歇山顶。前面有个小院子，有水池和石假山。院子三面有围墙，墙上作漏空的砖花。据光绪《永嘉县志·杂志·遗闻》，宋徽宗时，李氏八世祖李汴（霞溪）任迪功郎（一说忠翊郎），因兄长成忠郎李邦（锦溪）随童贯征辽阵亡（北宋徽宗宣和二年，1120年），痛而退隐故里，"卜筑林塘扁湖之西，曰肯堂，湖之东，曰水月堂，寄兴觞咏，以终老焉。

"（康熙五十一年，即1712年，《苍坡李氏族谱·序》）十二世李澹轩，曾从师名儒，但屡试不举，于宋宁宗嘉定癸未（1223年）重修水月堂，作为会友吟诗的地方。到清咸丰年间，三十三世李西坡，仿杭州湖心亭再次重建水月堂，成为现状。西坡是名医，著有《医林释义》。这座建筑物很优美地结束了东池的北端，使这部分的构图很完整。在东池的南端偏东，与水月堂遥遥相对，有一座歇山顶的方形亭子，叫做"望兄亭"，这是李氏七世祖李嘉木于宋建炎二年(1128年)造的，为怀念迁往1公里外的方巷村的兄长李秋山。

在这座亭子上，不但可以欣赏东池的水光和仁济庙的轻盈的侧影，而且可以眺望村外丰腴的农田以及远处方巷村口的"送弟阁"，那是李秋山在同时造的。相传李氏兄弟十分亲爱，每天傍晚，嘉木都在望兄亭遥望兄长，有时到方巷村去与兄长相聚，返苍坡村的时候，兄长秋山必送到村外，就在那里造了这座"送弟阁"。

从东湖之东转折而到西湖之南，是蓄水的堤坝，高2.5米左右，宽达14米。堤上曾经是松柏成荫，还有柳树和各种观花植物。李西坡曾经在池的两岸种了许多名贵花木。这个总长300米左右的绿化带与两个大水池一起，构成了公园多变而美丽的景色。康熙五十一年（1712年）《苍坡李氏族谱·序》说：

苍坡之地，始有夹岸花堤十六咏，亭台莲塘广数里，山明水秀。

可见风光佳丽。当年东、西池里是种植荷花的。

据宗谱记载，东、西池的拦水堤坝是李氏九世祖李嵩在宋孝宗淳熙戊戌年（1178年）建造的。但据记载望兄亭建于建炎二年（1128年），则堤上现存的不但不是原构，且已不在原址。堤上仁济庙前，现有古柏三株，高可十余米，相传也是李嵩手栽的，已有八百多年的历史，鳞干虬枝，证明着堤坝的古老。

（2）溪口村的水池也很大，形状不规则，东西长97.0米，西窄东宽，最宽处大约25.5米。在最宽的东部，池中央有座亭子，由石板桥连接北岸。由亭子向西又造了一个石矶。这个水池又叫莲池或水亭塘，溪口村的"十景"有两景在这里，它们是"月台观荷"和"长湖秋月"。宗谱里有《南山十景诗》描写水心亭：

亭榭俯横塘，盈盈一水长；

柳丝披拂处，惊起两鸳鸯。

可惜现在池周围已几乎满是房屋，柳树和荷花早已没有，鸳鸯也不再来了。只有东北角的山坡上，还有一小片树林。

《合溪（即溪口）十景诗》里的《长湖秋月》写的是：

晓镜初开彻九垠，长湖秋色净无尘；

水天浑合澄空碧，形影分明见本真。

玉宇三千光偏烛，琉璃数顷冷侵人；

江楼此夕愁偏剧，知己遥遥莫问津。

如今水池与水亭尚在，这样的景色还可以见到。

（3）埭头村的卧龙冈在村子中央的后部，略略向前凸出，背后便是大山。卧龙冈并不高，但形势极好，是村子的风水山。传金丞相兀钦仄注的《青鸟先生葬经》说"草木郁茂，吉气相随"，卧龙冈既是风水山，为培育吉气，竹林古木都保养得很好，生气勃勃。珍川朱谰轩写的《小源埭头十景诗》说道是"卧龙盘处倍葱笼"，这景观至今依然。

在接近冈顶的地方，人工修整起一个平台，大约有209平方米。台中央一棵六人合抱的大樟树，覆盖了整个平台还有余。平台右侧有一道山洪冲沟，在平台下涵洞经过；然后曲折而下，陡峭峻急。一条石径，傍着冲沟，登上平台。平台上方，还有一个神坛，有胸墙围绕。拾级经一个小小砖门楼进入神坛，见石香案后的神龛上有一副对联，写的是：

陈八大王赐福多；段五大人惠埭川。

这陈八大王和段五大人就是受香火供奉的"神"。这种神其实不过是世俗的强人，所以他们的神坛并没有改变卧龙冈的清雅气氛。倒是神坛里又一棵大樟树，与平台上的那棵连成一片，以致在全村都能看到它们团团的、浓浓的、四季碧绿的树冠，仿佛笼罩了整个村落。神坛的小砖门，挺立在高台阶上，披一身青荫，衬着树隙里明亮的天空，给竹树掩映的卧龙冈添了几分精神。

有一条沿等高线的路横穿过平台，从平台又有两条山上坡，两条山路下坡。它是一个道路枢纽，但近处只有竹林而没有房舍，很宁静，意境深沉，引来男女老幼们在这里休息、嬉戏。卖鲜肉的挑子也每每到这里来，牛角的呜呜声透过竹林传遍全村，仿佛也有雅趣。卧龙冈上的视野很开阔，正前方对着的是卓笔峰，左右环列着锦屏山和九嶂山，都在埭头村的"十景"之中。

English

4. Public Gardens

Nanxi River basin has wondrous mountains and beautiful waters, scenery like paintings everywhere. Such natural environment cultivated people's landscape sentiment, arousing their desire to build even more beautiful environments. Public gardens often found in villages embody villagers' deep passion for mountain-river beauty; they are one of Middle Nanxi River villages' most touching features.

Currently best-preserved public gardens are Yantou village's Tahu temple—Lishui Lake scenic area and Daitou village's Wolong Ridge, commonly called "Big Head." Water surfaces remain but green spaces destroyed: Xikou village's lotus pond and Cangpo village's East and West Pools.

Only faint traces remain: Yantou village's Upper Garden, Heyang village's Lantai Mountain and Hesheng village's fengshui mountain. Pengxi village's Fenghuang Mountain and Xiagangtou, and Xi'an village's gourd-melon well area, also have strong garden charm.

(1) Park location in village either determined by natural conditions or by fengshui considerations. Yantou's Tahu temple—Lishui Lake scenic area and Cangpo's East and West Lakes were both formed by building dams to impound mountain water channeled through village via canals.

They are both located southeast of village; fengshui experts consider "mountains rise northwest, waters converge southeast" as auspicious land.

As Song Wang Zhu et al.'s "Geography New Book" says: "Northwest high, southeast low, water flowing out in xun direction is the way of heaven and earth." Tahu temple—Lishui Lake scenic area has Tahu temple; between East and West Lakes are renji temple and Taiyin temple.

These two parks, though within village boundaries, are at where canal water exits village — fengshui experts probably regarded them as "water mouths." However, Cangpo's Li clan main ancestral hall紧靠 Taiyin temple and renji temple, not conforming to fengshui experts' warning about ancestral halls "must never be near shrines temples or monasteries" (see "Zhaipu Zhiyao · ancestral halls").

Xikou village's park also centered on pool, located at village north end where canal enters village — fengshui experts call "heaven gate."

These water surfaces are all large. Cangpo's East and West Lakes are neat rectangles: East Lake 147m north-south, 19m east-west; West Lake 80m east-west, 35m north-south; between them a water connection 28m long, 16m wide.

On north bank of this connecting water, built Li clan main ancestral hall (first built Northern Song Renzong Zhihe second year, 1055, later rebuilt multiple times) and east of it renji temple (tenth-generation ancestor Li Bojun built after resigning and returning home in Southern Song Xiaozong Chunxi seventh year, 1180), and north of renji temple Taiyin temple.

renji temple faces water on three sides, all built with light open galleries, gallery edges with beauty-rails. Even Li clan main ancestral hall's south side was originally open gallery facing water.

temple and ancestral hall forms are generally conservative, rigid and enclosed; Cangpo's Li clan main ancestral hall and renji temple bravely broke old conventions.

Nanxi River people are free-spirited, not bound by tradition; Nanxi River people love beauty — for garden art's harmony and unity, even for main ancestral hall such an important almost sacred building, they dared to innovate beyond conventions.

North end of East Pool is "shuiyue hall" (Water-Moon Hall), built in water, connected to west bank by a 2m+ small stone slab bridge. It is a three-bay building, overhanging-gable roof. Both sides have galleries with shed roofs, combined with central overhanging-gable roof, approximating hip-gable roof.

Front has small courtyard with pool and stone rockery. courtyard three sides have walls with openwork brick patterns.

According to Guangxu "Yongjia County Gazetteer · Miscellany · Anecdotes," during Song Huizong era, Li clan eighth-generation ancestor Li Bian (Xiaxi) served as Digonglang (or Zhongyilang); because older brother Chengzhonglang Li Bang (Jinxi) died in battle accompanying Tong Guan's campaign against Liao (Northern Song Huizong Xuanhe second year, 1120), he pained and retired to hometown, "choosing site by forest and pool, naming west of lake Kentang, east of lake shuiyue hall, expressing feelings through wine and poetry, to end his days there." (Kangxi fifty-first year, 1712, "Cangpo Li clan Genealogy · Preface") Twelfth-generation Li Danxuan studied under famous Confucian but repeatedly failed exams; in Song Ningzong Jiading Guiwei (1223) rebuilt shuiyue hall as place for gathering friends and composing poetry.

In Qing Xianfeng era, thirty-third-generation Li Xipo, imitating Hangzhou's Mid-Lake Pavilion, rebuilt shuiyue hall again, becoming current state. Xipo was famous physician, author of "Yilin Shiyi" (Medical Forest Explanations). This building beautifully terminates East Pool's north end, making this part's composition complete.

At southeast of East Pool, facing shuiyue hall from afar, is a hip-gable roof square pavilion called "Wangxiong Pavilion" (Gazing-at-Brother Pavilion), built by Li clan seventh-generation ancestor Li Jiamu in Song Jianyan second year (1128), to remember older brother Li Qiushan who moved to Fangxiang village 1km away.

From this pavilion, one can not only appreciate East Pool's water light and renji temple's graceful side silhouette, but also overlook fertile farmland outside village and distant Fangxiang village entrance's "Songdi Pavilion" (Seeing-off-Brother Pavilion), built by Li Qiushan at same time.

Legend says Li brothers were very affectionate; every evening Jiamu gazed at brother from Wangxiong Pavilion; sometimes visiting Fangxiang to gather with brother, when returning to Cangpo, brother Qiushan would see him off to village edge, where he built this "Songdi Pavilion."

From east of East Lake turning to south of West Lake is the water-retaining dam, about 2.5m high, up to 14m wide. Dam once had pine and cypress shade, plus willows and various flowering plants. Li Xipo once planted many rare flowers and trees on both pool banks.

This green belt totaling about 300m long, together with two large pools, constituted park's varied and beautiful scenery. Kangxi fifty-first year (1712) "Cangpo Li clan Genealogy · Preface" says:

Cangpo's land, began with sixteen poems on flower-lined banks, pavilions and lotus ponds extending several li, mountains bright and waters clear.

可见 scenery was beautiful. East and West Pools once grew lotus.

According to genealogy, East and West Pools' water-retaining dam was built by Li clan ninth-generation ancestor Li Song in Song Xiaozong Chunxi Wuxu year (1178). But records say Wangxiong Pavilion built in Jianyan second year (1128), so existing dam structures are not original, nor at original site.

In front of renji temple on dam, three ancient cypresses now stand, over 10m tall, legend also planted by Li Song — over 800 years old, scaly trunks and twisted branches testifying to dam's antiquity.

(2) Xikou village's pool also large, irregular shape, 97.0m east-west, narrow west wide east, widest about 25.5m. In widest eastern part, pool center has a pavilion connected to north bank by stone slab bridge. West of pavilion a stone rockery built.

This pool also called Lotus Pool or Water Pavilion pond; two of Xikou village's "Ten Scenes" here: "Moon Terrace Viewing Lotus" and "Long Lake Autumn Moon." Genealogy has "Nanshan Ten Scenes Poems" describing Water Heart Pavilion:

Pavilions overlook horizontal pond, clear water stretches long;

Where willow strands brush, two mandarin ducks startled rise.

Unfortunately now pool surrounded almost entirely by houses; willows and lotus long gone, mandarin ducks no longer come. Only on northeast hillside a small patch of forest remains.

"Hexi (i.e. Xikou) Ten Scenes Poems"' "Long Lake Autumn Moon" writes:

Morning mirror first opens through nine boundaries, long lake autumn colors clean of dust;

Water and sky merge into clear empty blue, form and shadow distinctly see true self.

Jade universe three thousand lights shine, glass-like several acres cold invade;

River tower tonight sorrow especially intense, knowing friends far away ask not ford.

Now pool and water pavilion still exist, such scenery still visible.

(3) Daitou village's Wolong Ridge (Crouching Dragon Ridge) is at rear of village center, slightly protruding forward, behind it is great mountain. Wolong Ridge not high, but形势 excellent, is village's fengshui mountain.

Legendary Jin Chancellor Wu Qinze's annotated "Qingniao Xiansheng Burial Classic" says "plants lush, auspicious qi follows"; Wolong Ridge being fengshui mountain, to cultivate auspicious qi, bamboo forests and ancient trees all well maintained, full of vitality.

Zhenchuan Zhu Lanxuan's "Xiaoyuan Daitou Ten Scenes Poems" says "where crouching dragon coils, especially lush" — this scenery still remains today.

Near ridge top, a platform artificially leveled, about 209 sqm. platform center a large camphor tree requiring six people to embrace, covering entire platform with surplus. platform right side a mountain flood gully, passing through culvert under platform; then winding down, steep and rapid. A stone path, alongside gully, climbs to platform.

Above platform, a shrine altar, surrounded by parapet wall. Ascending steps through a small brick gate tower into shrine altar, on stone altar behind shrine a couplet reads:

Chen Eight Great King bestows much fortune;

Duan Five Great Lord benefits Dachuan.

These Chen Eight Great King and Duan Five Great Lord are "gods" receiving incense offerings. Such gods were actually secular strongmen, so their shrine altar did not change Wolong Ridge's elegant atmosphere.

Rather, another large camphor tree in shrine altar, joining with platform's tree into one expanse, so that from whole village one can see their round, dense, evergreen crowns, as if shrouding entire village.

shrine altar's small brick gate, standing on high steps, draped in green shade, against bright sky through tree gaps, adds几分 spirit to bamboo-tree-shaded Wolong Ridge.

A contour-line road crosses platform; from platform two roads go uphill, two go downhill. It is a road hub, but nearby only bamboo forest no houses, very tranquil, profound atmosphere, attracting men women old and young to rest and play here.

Fresh meat sellers' carrying poles also often come here; buffalo horn's woo-woo sound through bamboo forest传遍 whole village, as if also having elegant charm. Wolong Ridge's view very open; directly ahead Zhuobi Peak (Writing Brush Peak), left and right flanked by Jinping Mountain and Jiuzhang Mountain — all among Daitou village's "Ten Scenes."

本篇术语 Key Terms

7
村子立面
中文

5. 村子立面

楠溪江中游有一些村子的规划建设，还精心推敲了村子的主要立面，这在各地的乡村中是少见的，显示出楠溪江人很强的环境审美意识。

经过推敲的村子立面，至今还保存得很完好的，有苍坡村的东南立面，岩头村的东南立面和坦下村的朝向西南的整个正立面。与坦下村隔溪相望的塘湾村的正立面已因修公路而拆毁，花坛村的南立面也被新住宅它及商店遮住。东皋村和芙蓉村的溪门一带，廊下村的东北门左右，还有些局部残留。

苍坡村的主要立面，从寨门往西到望兄亭前寨墙东南角。这一段立面，包括寨门正面、李氏大宗祠侧面、仁济庙正面和望兄亭，大约90米长。房屋高低进退，错落有致，加上古柏参天，造成形体和色泽等的大变化、大对比。大约2.5米高的石筑寨墙、粗犷质重，一方面进一步增加了整个立面的变化和对比，一方面把上面轻盈的房屋和树木整个托住，连系成一体，使构图统一完整。寨门前的荷塘，更使景色妩媚活泼。西池的南堤，本来也长满了参天的松柏，郁郁葱葱，田园风光更显得生气勃勃。可惜现在松柏树已只剩几棵了。

岩头村的南立面，由南门向西南一百多米，构图意匠与苍坡村的近似。塔湖庙、森秀轩、接官亭、戏台等建筑物轮廓参差多变，再夹杂几棵浓荫蔽天的大樟树、樟树和柏树，景色也是非常丰富多变，而在下面，又有高度平均约3.5米的一带寨墙托住。

坦下村的正立面，就是它的全部寨墙，因为其余三面都由陡峭的山崖围绕。这道寨墙全长约100米，有寨门、谯亭、小影壁与几幢住宅的正面、院门和山墙组成节奏跳动的轮廓，而且形体的变化幅度很大。这个立面，背衬着圈椅式的一座岩石山，构图十分完整、匀称，富有画意。

东皋村的寨门面对鹤盛溪中的一条石矴。这石矴全长121米，有211步，是楠溪江最长的石矴。从石矴渡过溪流，迎面是一个大块蛮石铺砌的高坡，两侧有青青的树林。坡长约50米，坡顶就是一座木构的寨门。门很简单，异常轻巧，但两旁寨墙的石块，长度竟有一米多的。门和墙的对比很有奇趣。门侧一棵姿态夭矫古拙的老松树，俯身抚着寨门，又增加了许多趣味。松针落满了寨门的瓦顶，使它仿佛是茸茸的草顶，发着暗红的颜色。门外右侧有一座路亭，方的，歇山顶，上有飞檐翼角，下有美人靠栏杆，玲珑剔透，衬着沉雄的石寨墙，又给景色添加了一个层次。它上下与寨门、矴步呼应，使这一段主要立面不松散零落，而且很有纵深。

花坛村的南立面，原来是一条主要街道，它北面是建成区，南面是农田。这条街上，集中了全村最重要的建筑物，有大宗祠、敦睦祠、宪台祠、"溪山第一"门，三座三官庙，几座旌表性和纪念性的牌楼，等等。

这些立面证明，楠溪江的古村落，不但经过完整的规划，而且规划到了建筑环境的艺术质量。

English

5. village Facade

Some Middle Nanxi River villages' planning and construction also carefully refined the village's main facade — rare in rural areas elsewhere, demonstrating Nanxi River people's strong environmental aesthetic awareness.

well-preserved refined village facades include Cangpo village's southeast facade, Yantou village's southeast facade, and tanxia village's entire southwest-facing main facade. Tangwan village's main facade, facing Tanxia across stream, has been demolished due to highway construction; Huatan village's south facade is obscured by new houses and shops.

Donggao and Furong villages' stream gate area, and Langxia village's northeast gate area, have some partial remnants.

Cangpo village's main facade extends from village gate west to southeast corner of wall before Wangxiong Pavilion. This facade section, including village gate front, Li clan main ancestral hall side, renji temple front and Wangxiong Pavilion, about 90m long.

Buildings vary in height and setback, well-arranged; plus ancient cypresses reaching sky, creating great variation and contrast in form and color.

About 2.5m high stone village wall, rough and massive, on one hand further increases whole facade's variation and contrast, on other hand supports above light houses and trees, unifying them into one body, making composition complete and integrated. Lotus pond before village gate makes scenery even more charming and lively.

West Pool's south dam originally also full of towering pines and cypresses, lush and green, pastoral scenery even more vibrant. Unfortunately now only a few pine and cypress trees remain.

Yantou village's south facade, from south gate extending southwest over 100m, compositional intent similar to Cangpo.

Tahu temple, Senxiu Studio, Jieguan Pavilion, stage and other buildings' outlines varied and irregular, interspersed with several large camphor, camphor and cypress trees with dense shade; scenery also very rich and varied, while below, a band of village wall averaging about 3.5m high supports it all.

tanxia village's main facade is its entire village wall, because other three sides surrounded by steep cliffs. This village wall total length about 100m, with village gate, watchtower pavilion, small screen wall and several houses' fronts, courtyard gates and gable walls forming rhythmically跳动 outline, with great variation in form.

This facade, backed by an armchair-shaped rocky mountain, composition very complete, balanced, picturesque.

Donggao village's village gate faces a stone stepping-stone (dingbu) in Hesheng Stream. This stone stepping-stone total length 121m, 211 steps, is Nanxi River's longest stone stepping-stone. Crossing stream via stone stepping-stone,迎面 is a high slope paved with large rough stones, both sides green forests.

Slope about 50m long, slope top is a wooden village gate. Gate simple, unusually light, but stones of side village walls up to over 1m long. Contrast between gate and wall has quaint charm. Beside gate an old pine tree with graceful and ancient form, leaning over village gate, adds much interest.

Pine needles cover village gate's tile roof, making it resemble a soft grass roof, dark red color. Outside gate right side a road pavilion, square, hip-gable roof, with flying eaves and wing corners above, beauty-rails below, exquisite and transparent, against massive stone village wall, adding another layer to scenery.

It echoes with village gate and stepping-stone above and below, making this main facade section not loose or scattered, and with great depth.

Huatan village's south facade was originally a main street; north side built-up area, south side farmland. On this street concentrated village's most important buildings: main ancestral hall, Dunmu ancestral hall, Xiantai ancestral hall, "Xishan First" gate, three Three Officials temples, several commemorative and memorial paifangs, etc.

These facades prove Nanxi River ancient villages not only underwent complete planning, but planning reached the artistic quality of built environment.

8
风水堪舆
中文

6. 风水堪舆

在村落规划的各个方面，都常常附会有风水堪舆之说。堪舆风水无非是说自然的地形地物能决定人的命运，所谓"地吉人福、地凶人祸"，这是一种原始泛灵论的拜物教。宋代王洙等撰的《地理新书》说：

地之有丘陵川泽，犹天之有日月星辰。地则有夷险，天则有变动，皆有自然吉凶之应乎人者也。其吉凶安所生哉？在其象而已矣！

（1）堪舆风水是一种迷信。不过，从它的趋吉避凶里，倒也可以看出人们当时的理想、愿望和追求。楠溪江人，有王、谢以来多少博学太守们倡导的文风作为历史传统，又受宋代科举制度发展的影响，所以总是把读书进仕当作最高的目标，形成所谓"耕读"风气。他们在堪舆风水中表现出来的理想、愿望和追求，总是把科甲成就放在第一位。例如，《珍川朱氏合族宗谱序传》里说到自然环境的效应：

兹珍川之朱氏，凤林楠水之乌衣。山自东来，雁岩锺其灵秀；水由西去，蜃江接其澄泓。飞凤天马呈其奇；金钟玉屏挺其异。皇矣十景，弘彼千英；五代肇基，万年卜宅。巍科显仕，珠贯蝉联；隐德鸿儒，星罗棋布。

对人工的村落规划，就要更加着力造成有利于大发科甲的环境了。

最常见的是，在选址的时候，最好东南方，也就是巽位，有圆锥形的山峰，作为"文笔峰"，如塘湾、豫章、蓬溪、埭头等村子。巽位有山而不高，就人工培土，如岩头的汤山。还不够高，则再在上面造文峰塔，如塘湾的巽吉山，岩头的汤山后来也再造了一座塔。有了文笔峰，就要有墨沼或者砚池，除了蓬溪的以外，大多是人工开挖的。必须使文笔峰能够倒映在池里，如豫章村口的墨沼。埭头村在山坡上，很局促，也挖了一个墨沼。在岩头村，以水亭祠里的水池为墨沼。

还有一种风水规划，就是规划象征性的"文房四宝"，例如苍坡村和岩头村。苍坡村西有笔架山，村子的主街名为笔街，长约330米，由东向西，直对笔架山。西池又名为砚池。池北侧有两块长约4.5米的大石条，截面为0.5米×0.3米，这就是"墨"。"纸"就是方方正正的村子。

岩头以文峰塔为笔，以琴屿为砚。塔湖庙里天井的水池是砚头的水槽。也以石条为墨，以村子为纸。另一种说法是戏台后间为墨，塔湖庙前空地为纸。

对科举功名的热烈向往，过去在楠溪江深入人心。这种向往，推动了义塾、书院、文昌阁等文教建筑的建设，加上其他方面的条件和努力，才使楠溪江成了"小邹鲁"。这些文教建筑的位置，也都有风水的考虑。

（2）风水规划中，除了考虑山、水和村落本身之外，还要考虑到树木之类。阴阳师说它们也会影响到村民们的命运。例如塘湾村新宫坳的一棵大樟树，曾被人滥樵乱伐，由宗祠出钱买为公产加以保护：

自兹以往，行见绿阴匝地，翠盖遮天，凤凰巢其上，翡翠宿其间。心贞节劲，偕松竹以亭亭；色秀姿清，共柳梅而郁郁。有木如斯，蔚为盛瑞。异日精华流布，科甲蝉联；旺气发扬，财丁蜂聚。地之灵者人自杰，即卉木亦相与有荣矣！（见光绪四年，即1878年，《棠川郑氏宗谱·新宫坳樟树记》）

风水的效应，首要的还是科甲。自然经济条件下靠天吃饭的农民，对自己的命运毫无把握的能力，因此不得不向一切想象中有可能关系到自己吉凶祸福的东西祈求。对宗族整体来说，子弟的科甲成就是最高的理想。农事艰辛，皮焦肉枯，而摆脱这种困境的最佳办法，就是通过科举之路，走向社会的上层。所以，风水堪舆的兴趣中心也就在为科名的旺发创造机会。总之，规划村落，实际上就是规划村落的命运，规划它的发展道路。

English

6. fengshui and geomancy

In all aspects of village planning, fengshui geomancy theories are often attached. fengshui geomancy无非 says natural terrain and features can determine people's fate — so-called "auspicious land brings people fortune, inauspicious land brings disaster" — a primitive animism fetishism. Song Wang Zhu et al.'s "Geography New Book" says:

Land has hills streams and marshes, as heaven has sun moon and stars. Land has level and dangerous, heaven has changes, all have natural auspicious-inauspicious responses to people. Where do auspicious-inauspicious arise? In their images alone!

(1) fengshui geomancy is a superstition. However, from its seeking auspicious and avoiding inauspicious, one can see people's ideals, desires and pursuits at that time.

Nanxi River people, with literary tradition advocated by many learned prefects since Wang and Xie eras as historical heritage, and influenced by Song imperial examination system development, always regarded studying for officialdom as highest goal, forming so-called "farming-reading" ethos.

Their ideals, desires and pursuits expressed in fengshui geomancy always put examination success first. For example, "Zhenchuan Zhu clan Combined Genealogy Preface and Biographies" speaks of natural environment's effects:

This Zhenchuan Zhu clan, black-robed of Fenglin and Nan waters. Mountains come from east, Yan Rocks gather their spiritual beauty; waters go west, Shen River接 its clear depth. Flying phoenix and heavenly horse present their wonders; Gold bell and jade screen stand out their uniqueness.

Grand ten scenes, expand thousand heroes; five generations founded base, ten thousand years choose home. Distinguished examination success and prominent officials, pearls strung continuous; hidden virtue and great Confucians, stars scattered in array.

For artificial village planning, even more effort to create environment favorable for great examination success.

Most common: when selecting site, best if southeast, i.e. xun position, has conical mountain peak as "Writing Brush Peak" — e.g. Tangwan, Yuzhang, Pengxi, Daitou villages. If xun position has mountain but not high, artificially mound earth — e.g. Yantou's Tang Mountain. If still not high enough, then build wenfeng pagoda on top — e.g.

Tangwan's Xunji Mountain, Yantou's Tang Mountain later also had a tower built. With Writing Brush Peak, need Ink Pool or Inkstone Pool; besides Pengxi's, mostly artificially dug. Must make Writing Brush Peak reflect in pool — e.g. Yuzhang village entrance's Ink Pool. Daitou village on hillside, very cramped, also dug an Ink Pool.

In Yantou village, pool in Water Pavilion temple's courtyard serves as Ink Pool.

Another type of fengshui planning: planning symbolic "Four Treasures of the Study" — e.g. Cangpo and Yantou villages. Cangpo village west has Brush-rack Mountain; village's main street named Brush Street, about 330m long, east to west, directly facing Brush-rack Mountain. West Pool also named Inkstone Pool.

North of pool two long stone slabs about 4.5m long, cross-section 0.5m×0.3m — these are "ink." "Paper" is the square village.

Yantou uses wenfeng pagoda as brush, Qin Islet as inkstone. Pool in Tahu temple's courtyard is inkstone's water trough. Also uses stone slabs as ink, village as paper. Another说法: stage back room as ink, Tahu temple front open space as paper.

Warm yearning for examination success and fame, in the past deeply rooted in Nanxi River. This yearning promoted construction of cultural-educational buildings like charity schools, academies, wenchang pavilions; plus other conditions and efforts, made Nanxi River "Little Zou-Lu." These cultural-educational buildings' positions also had fengshui considerations.

(2) In fengshui planning, besides considering mountains, water and village itself, also consider trees etc. Yin-yang masters say they also affect villagers' fate. For example, Tangwan village's Xingong'ao large camphor tree, once recklessly cut by people, was bought by ancestral hall as public property for protection:

From now on, see green shade covering ground, emerald canopy blocking sky, phoenix nests above, kingfisher roosts within. Heart faithful and integrity strong, together with pine and bamboo standing tall; color elegant and form clear, together with willow and plum flourishing. Tree like this, becomes great auspicious sign.

In future essence spreads, examination success continuous; prosperous qi expands, wealth and people swarm. Land's spirit makes people outstanding, even plants share in glory! (See Guangxu fourth year, 1878, "Tangchuan Zheng clan Genealogy · Record of Xingong'ao Camphor Tree")

fengshui's primary effect is still examination success. Farmers living off nature under natural economy, with no control over their fate, therefore had to pray to everything imaginable that might relate to their fortune or misfortune. For clan as whole, younger generation's examination success is highest ideal.

farming hardships, skin burnt and flesh withered; best way to escape this困境 is through imperial examination path, moving to upper society. Therefore fengshui geomancy's center of interest is creating opportunities for flourishing examination fame. In short, planning village is actually planning village's fate, planning its development path.

本篇术语 Key Terms

风水堪舆 / Fengshui geomancy 地吉人福 / Auspicious land brings people fortune 地凶人祸 / Inauspicious land brings disaster 原始泛灵论 / Primitive animism 拜物教 / Fetishism 趋吉避凶 / Seeking auspicious avoiding inauspicious 耕读风气 / Farming-reading ethos 科甲成就 / Examination success 文笔峰 / Writing Brush Peak (Wenbi Feng) 巽位 / Xun position (southeast) 文峰塔 / Wenfeng Pagoda 墨沼 / Ink Pool (Mozhao) 砚池 / Inkstone Pool (Yanchi) 文房四宝 / Four Treasures of the Study 笔架山 / Brush-rack Mountain ...等30个
9
塘湾村
中文

1. 塘湾村

楠溪江中游村落的规划，村村不同，但大致可分为平地型和山坡型两类。平地型的，可以岩头村为代表；山坡型的，以塘湾村为代表。

塘湾村的规划虽然可以作为山坡村落的代表，但它却有一个很大的特点，就是附近几乎没有耕地。它位于大、小楠溪的汇合处，却并不发展手工业和水运业。《棠川郑氏宗谱·览前舆图有感》说：

棠川之地，后有崇山峻岭，维石岩岩，前有两源九嶂，山溪环绕，生其地者莫不以山水所阻，憾其田少而人多，欲谋衣食且不给，奚暇治礼义哉！

塘湾村的自然条件是很严酷的。

它三面环山，一面临江。光绪二十一年(1895年)，临江造了一道寨墙，两端接山，称作新城，塘湾村更加封闭。

（1）村子初建于南宋。《郑氏宗谱》里有南宋绍兴年间宁祖公为中街路和前街路铺砌石路面的记载。这两条路至今还在，对照记载和现状，宗谱所说的中街路为今后街，前街为今中街。现在还有一条前街。它们都是沿等高线的，但却都是笔直的，互相平行，由西北走向东南。但后街有三分之一的长度折而向南。这三条路是全村最重要的街道，从前街向后街，地势渐高，所以北端两条贯穿它们的横路有台阶。南端也有一条路贯穿它们，就在与这条路相交的路口，前街与中街相距43.1米，中街与后街又相距23.6米。

铺砌的街面并不很宽，下街2.6米，中街2.2米，后街2.7米。但中街一侧有2.7米宽的泄洪沟，沟对岸为4米宽的绿化带。后街串连几个小广场，最宽的"上马石"小广场有8米多宽。而且，街两侧并不是临街房屋鳞次栉比，而是多聚于街的一侧，且以院墙居多，高不过1.7-2.0米，墙头翠竹潇潇，橘柚累累，所以，三条街的街景都很开敞。

根据山形地势，塘湾村面向东北。后街与中街的房屋大都向东北。但中街与前街之间的房屋造得比较晚，大都面向东南。向阳还是好朝向。前街之外的房屋，造得更晚，位置和朝向都零乱，但因为挨近寨墙，所以还是朝东北的居多。这部分显然没有规划。

（2）位于山凹陡坡，所以没有流量稳定的水源来供水，只有山洪骤发骤去。村里有冲沟两条，一条发源于西南的天岩，经岩峰屏，沿村子的南缘向东。另一条发源于西北的和合峰，经碧泉涧进村，折向东南，傍中街路走向东南端与西南来的汇合，再一起向东，在村子的东南角出寨墙下名为外风桥的水关注入楠溪江。在傍中街路走的时候，冲沟的宽度大约是2.7米，深2.2米。与西南来的冲沟汇合后，迅速成为喇叭口形状，宽度到10米左右，深有5米。

前街、中街和后街在南端都有桥跨过冲沟。中街的叫下栏桥，是石板桥，前街的叫外关桥，是一座跨度比较大的石拱桥。中街上还有一座上方桥和一座中道桥。这四道桥被称为三关四锁。在风水上说，冲沟直出村中央，是"陡泻明堂"，不吉。转过两次弯，破了凶煞，加上四把锁，就更能避免"气"的一泄无余了。把冲沟从西北引向东南，横穿全村，有利于村子的排水，同时也符合风水的要求；"水流出巽，如天地之势也"。东南角是水口，造了一座山隍庙，一座路亭，与寨墙下的外风桥一起，来"掩映水口"、"关锁内气"。

山洪来得急，去得快，去后冲沟很深，不便于日常用水，更不利于救灭火灾和防旱灾。因此，有必要挖池蓄水。据《棠川郑氏宗谱·池塘记》叙述：

(光绪)戊申（1908年）之春，花朝三日，郑氏族众诸董事等议于村之西、南、北三隅各凿一池以庇风水。闻者踊跃，即为修畚揭、备锨锄，并力偕作，不数月而池成。但见滔滔文浪，青贮半湖；浩浩晴汶，光涵两岸。接林淑之鲜鳞，披云烟之绰态。加之美号，锡之芳名：一曰澄观池，以为空水澄鲜，湛然如涌，尽足以供观眺之娱；一曰明鉴塘，夫鉴之为言镜也，水天一色，明彻如镜，故以为名。于此左旋，循北则又有洗砚沼焉。是沼也，清波泛泛，碧浪悠悠，有时砚影鲜明。临流洗濯，而天香之薰染，墨麝之涵濡，果何如奇赏乎？

……引流灌水，池开三面，鼎峙一村。以之拯燎原，如毁之灾可淡；以之救旱暵，昭回之叹不惊。有备无患，孰谓此池为不亟之需哉？况乎源泉既往，灵秀自锺。绵绵生之，瓜瓞滋荫；籝籝有方，谷足阜财源。且自此墨海澜翻，藜光烛照，将见文人学士辈塞旧窗亿，未忘磨铁之心；宝匣新开，顿豁生花之目。庶几乎传家诗书，礼承先泽；华国文章，学显后昆。信然，信然。

这三口池塘，大约西边的是澄观池，南边的是明鉴塘，北边的是洗砚沼，本来不过是为了日用和防灾，却被堪舆家夸张得关系到塘湾郑氏的子孙繁衍，财谷丰阜，科名风发，诗礼雍睦。与这工程大致同时的修筑堤坝、寨墙，也附会了许多风水阐释。这大约是为了发动村人出工挖塘、筑墙，必须把效益说得多多的，大大的。

现在这三口塘中的两口还在，一在后街路南口桥边，一中街路北端郑氏大宗祠的西北边。

除了池塘之外，另有三口水井，供饮食用水，其中一口已填死。

塘湾村也有"十景"，穿村冲沟的上游，村西北角的碧泉涧，就是其中之一。有诗描写它：

泠泠碧润郑公乡，屈曲回旋泽孔长；

岸草漫夸书带秀，须繁时荐水泉香。

现在碧泉涧左右满是树木竹丛，郁郁葱葱，非常幽深。

碧泉涧到了松房祠，就被石拱覆盖，成了涵洞，高约2.3米，宽约1.7米。这涵洞过了松房祠，折向东南在中街路地下走，到上方桥南侧又出而成为明沟，总长将近200米。把这段冲沟做成涵洞，是为了在它的内侧形成塘湾村最大的公共中心。涵洞扩大了公共中心的面积，又使它与中街路连接，避免村子被冲沟切成两部分，从而保持塘湾村的统一感、整体感。

（3）这个公共中心是综合性的，以礼制意义为主。位于中央的是郑氏大宗祠，它的轴线朝向东北。在大门前原来有三对功名桅杆。大宗祠西侧是口大池塘，长25.4米，宽11.2米，可能是洗砚沼。以前在大宗祠角上有亭榭临水。沼的西岸，上坡，是松房祠，面朝东南，即朝向大宗祠。大宗祠的东侧是个小广场，长25.0米，宽12.0米。广场南北各有小商店数间，西南角上是五桂祠。在南侧小商店的背后，五桂祠与大宗祠之间，又有一个小广场。

前面小广场的东南端有石栏、石凳，凭栏可以俯瞰中街路和它旁边2米多深，将近3米宽的冲沟和冲沟另一侧青葱的绿化带。这个小广场又起商业中心和休闲中心的作用。在未造涵洞之前，广场东南端原来是上方桥，所以这个广场得名为上方桥头。这里整天都有人闲坐聊天，除了小商店之外，卖鲜肉的小贩和卖季节性商品如西瓜、冰糕等等的，也都来歇脚。郑氏大宗祠演戏的时候，这广场就起聚集人群的作用。

另一侧的池塘和松房祠，景观很好，西北角是竹树掩映的碧泉涧，背后和合峰高耸入云，有雾必雨；东北角是卧龙冈，有《十景诗》之一描写它：

江湖藏迹敛神奇，似此山阿意有之。

万里风云归撒土，有时变化跃天池。

虽然松房祠前有一块不小的台地，左右景色宜人，但比较偏僻，平日没有什么人。在20世纪30年代扩建大宗祠之前，它左右的两个性格完全不同的开放空间之间是通畅的，从上方桥头可以看到水池边的亭榭。自从大宗祠向前扩建了大约5米之后，两个空间之间的联系被阻隔了。

后街中段有一个丁字路口，原来有一座单间的石牌坊，叫太平坊，居全村的中心。现在太平坊已毁，残存石柱底脚一小段。它们以及它们前面的两块石头，乡民称为上马石。这个丁字路口就叫做上马石，是仅次于上方桥头的一个休闲中心，这四块石头和边缘的一些石块，都成了坐凳。每年正月初三，都要把新宫坳太阴宫前的陈十四娘娘神像迎请到上马石来祭拜。所以平常日子，老妇人们爱聚在这里，这是楠溪江中游村落里少有的以妇女为主的休闲中心。

塘湾村的东南角，山隍庙背后，是巽吉山，山上曾有过文峰塔，是楠溪江中游的两座文峰塔之一，可惜已经倒坍。东南为巽方，主文运，巽方有文笔峰或文峰塔都有利于村人的科甲功名。

从宣统年间绘制的塘湾村图上看，它和隔江相望的坦下村一样，寨墙的正面经过构图设计，亭阁参差，又有几座庙宇，从南到北山隍庙、静生祠、三官庙和松房祠，其中山隍庙为水口庙。寨门两个，都是拱券的，南侧的叫东惠门，北侧的叫通德门。

English

1. Tangwan village

Middle Nanxi River village planning varies village by village, but roughly divided into flatland type and hillside type. Flatland type represented by Yantou village; hillside type represented by Tangwan village.

Although Tangwan village's planning can represent hillside villages, it has a major feature: almost no arable land nearby. It is located at confluence of Great and Minor Nanxi Rivers, yet does not develop handicrafts or water transport. "Tangchuan Zheng clan Genealogy · Reflections on former Map" says:

Tangchuan's land, behind has high mountains and steep ridges, rocks towering; front has two sources and nine peaks, mountain streams encircle. Those living there all blocked by mountains and waters, regret few fields and many people, even seeking food and clothing insufficient, how could have leisure for rites and righteousness!

Tangwan village's natural conditions are very harsh.

It is surrounded by mountains on three sides, facing river on one. In Guangxu twenty-first year (1895), a village wall built along river, both ends connecting mountains, called New City, making Tangwan village even more enclosed.

(1) village first built in Southern Song. "Zheng clan Genealogy" records Southern Song Shaoxing era Ningzu Gong paving stone surfaces for Middle Street and Front Street roads. These two roads still exist; comparing records with current state, genealogy's Middle Street Road is today's Back Street, Front Street Road is today's Middle Street.

Now there is also a Front Street. They all follow contour lines, yet are all straight, parallel to each other, running northwest to southeast. But Back Street has one-third of its length turning south.

These three roads are village's most important streets; from Front Street to Back Street, terrain gradually rises, so two cross streets at north end connecting them have steps. South end also has a road connecting them; at intersection with this road, Front Street and Middle Street 43.1m apart, Middle Street and Back Street another 23.6m apart.

Paved street surfaces not very wide: Front Street 2.6m, Middle Street 2.2m, Back Street 2.7m. But Middle Street one side has 2.7m wide flood discharge gully, opposite bank 4m wide green belt. Back Street connects several small plazas; widest "Mounting Stone" small plaza over 8m wide.

Moreover, street两侧 not houses row upon row, but mostly gathered on one side, and mostly courtyard walls, no more than 1.7-2.0m high, wall tops with rustling bamboo, laden tangerines and pomelos — so all three streets' streetscapes very open.

According to mountain terrain, Tangwan village faces northeast. Back Street and Middle Street houses mostly face northeast. But houses between Middle Street and Front Street built relatively late, mostly face southeast. Facing sun is still good orientation.

Houses outside Front Street built even later, positions and orientations scattered, but because close to village wall, mostly still face northeast. This part obviously unplanned.

(2) Located in mountain hollow steep slope, no stable-flow water source for supply, only mountain floods sudden come and go. village has two gullies: one originates from southwest Tianyan, via Yanfengping, along village south edge eastward.

Another originates from northwest Hehe Peak, via Biquan Ravine entering village, turning southeast, alongside Middle Street to southeast end meeting southwest gully, then together eastward, exiting village wall at southeast corner under named Waifeng bridge water gate into Nanxi River.

When alongside Middle Street, gully width about 2.7m, depth 2.2m. After meeting southwest gully, rapidly becomes trumpet shape, width to about 10m, depth 5m.

Front Street, Middle Street and Back Street all have bridges crossing gully at south end. Middle Street's called Xialan bridge, stone slab bridge; Front Street's called Waiguan bridge, a relatively large-span stone arch bridge. Middle Street also has Shangfang bridge and Zhongdao bridge.

These four bridges called "Three Passes Four Locks." In fengshui terms, gully straight out village center is "steep discharge of bright hall," inauspicious. Turning twice breaks inauspiciousness, plus four locks, even more prevents "qi" from draining completely.

Guiding gully from northwest to southeast, crossing whole village, beneficial for village drainage,同时符合 fengshui requirement: "water flowing out in xun, as heaven and earth's tendency." Southeast corner is water mouth; built a Mountain Guardian temple, a road pavilion, together with Waifeng bridge under village wall, to "set off water mouth" and "lock internal qi."

Mountain floods come fast, go fast; after gone gully very deep, inconvenient for daily water use, even worse for fire fighting and drought prevention. Therefore necessary to dig pools for storage. According to "Tangchuan Zheng clan Genealogy · pond Record":

Guangxu Wushen (1908) spring, third day of Flower festival, Zheng clan elders and directors discussed digging one pool each at village west, south, north corners for fengshui protection. Hearers enthusiastic, immediately prepared baskets and hoes, working together, pools completed within months.

See surging literary waves, green storage half lake; vast clear reflections, light containing both banks. Fresh fish among trees, graceful mist and clouds.

Given beautiful names: one called Chengguan Pool (Clear-View Pool), as empty water clear fresh, deep as surging, enough for viewing pleasure; one called Mingjian pond (Bright-Mirror pond), mirror means reflecting glass, water and sky one color, clear as mirror, hence name. Turning left from here, north then has Xiyan Marsh (Wash-Inkstone Marsh).

This marsh, clear waves rippling, green waves leisurely, sometimes inkstone shadow distinct. Washing by stream, heavenly fragrance permeating, ink musk soaking — what wonderful appreciation! ... Diverting water to fill pools, pools open on three sides, standing like tripod in village.

For extinguishing raging fires, disasters can be mitigated; for relieving droughts, sighs of alarm need not arise. Preparedness prevents trouble — who says these pools are not urgent need? Moreover, once springs flow, spiritual beauty naturally gathers. Continuous generation, descendants flourish; baskets with methods, grain sufficient for wealth.

And from now ink sea waves surge, lamplight illuminates, will see scholars and literati filling old windows, not forgetting iron-grinding determination; treasure box newly opened, suddenly brightening flower-like eyes. May family pass down poetry and books, rites inherit former grace; glorious state writings, learning manifest later generations.

Truly so, truly so.

These three pools — west probably Chengguan Pool, south Mingjian pond, north Xiyan Marsh — originally merely for daily use and disaster prevention, yet exaggerated by fengshui experts as related to Tangwan Zheng clan's descendants prosperity, wealth and grain abundance, examination success flourishing, poetry and rites harmony.

Dams and village wall built roughly same time also attached many fengshui interpretations. This probably to mobilize villagers to dig pools and build walls, must describe benefits as many and great.

Now two of three pools still exist: one by Back Street south exit bridge, one northwest of Zheng clan main ancestral hall at Middle Street north end.

Besides pools, another three water wells for drinking water, one already filled in.

Tangwan village also has "Ten Scenes"; upstream of village-crossing gully, village northwest corner's Biquan Ravine, is one of them. Poem describes it:

Clear green Biquan in Zheng's hometown, winding and circling its bounty long;

Bank grass boasts book-ribbon beauty, dense times recommend water spring fragrance.

Now Biquan Ravine both sides full of trees and bamboo groves, lush and green, very deep and secluded.

Biquan Ravine at Songfang ancestral hall is covered by stone arch, becoming culvert, about 2.3m high, 1.7m wide. This culvert past Songfang ancestral hall, turns southeast under Middle Street, emerges as open ditch south of Shangfang bridge, total length nearly 200m.

Making this gully section into culvert was to form Tangwan village's largest public center on its inner side. Culvert expanded public center area, and connected it with Middle Street, avoiding village being cut into two parts by gully, thereby maintaining Tangwan village's sense of unity and wholeness.

(3) This public center is comprehensive, primarily ritual significance. At center is Zheng clan main ancestral hall, its axis facing northeast. Before gate originally three pairs of examination-success masts. West of main ancestral hall a large pool, 25.4m long, 11.2m wide, possibly Xiyan Marsh.

formerly at ancestral hall corner pavilions overlooking water. West bank of marsh, uphill, is Songfang ancestral hall, facing southeast, i.e. toward main ancestral hall. East of main ancestral hall a small plaza, 25.0m long, 12.0m wide. Plaza north and south each several small shops, southwest corner is Wugui ancestral hall.

Behind south small shops, between Wugui ancestral hall and main ancestral hall, another small plaza. Front small plaza southeast end has stone rails and stone benches; leaning on rail can overlook Middle Street and beside it 2m+ deep, nearly 3m wide gully and gully's other side lush green belt.

This small plaza also serves as commercial center and leisure center. Before culvert built, plaza southeast end originally Shangfang bridge, so this plaza named Shangfang bridgehead. Here all day people sit and chat; besides small shops, fresh meat vendors and seasonal goods sellers like watermelon, ice cream etc. also come to rest.

When Zheng clan main ancestral hall holds opera performances, this plaza serves as crowd-gathering place.

Other side's pool and Songfang ancestral hall, landscape very good; northwest corner bamboo-tree-shaded Biquan Ravine, behind Hehe Peak towering into clouds, fog means rain; northeast corner is Wolong Ridge, one of "Ten Scenes" poem describes it:

Rivers and lakes hide traces收敛神奇, like this mountain hollow meaning has it.

Ten thousand miles wind and clouds return to scattered earth, sometimes changes leaping into heaven pool.

Although Songfang ancestral hall front has a sizable terrace, left and right scenery pleasant, but relatively remote, usually few people. Before 1930s main ancestral hall expansion, between its left and right two completely different open spaces was通畅; from Shangfang bridgehead could see pavilions by pool. Since main ancestral hall expanded forward about 5m, connection between two spaces blocked.

Back Street mid-section has a T-junction, originally a single-bay stone paifang called Taiping Fang (Peace paifang), at village center. Now Taiping Fang destroyed, remaining stone pillar bases a short segment. These and two stones before them, villagers call Mounting Stones.

This T-junction called Mounting Stone, a leisure center second only to Shangfang bridgehead; these four stones and edge stones all become seats. Every year third day of first lunar month, Chen Shisi Niangniang goddess statue from Xingong'ao Taiyin Palace is welcomed to Mounting Stone for worship.

So on ordinary days, elderly women love gathering here — this is one of few women-dominated leisure centers in Middle Nanxi River villages.

Tangwan village's southeast corner, behind Mountain Guardian temple, is Xunji Mountain; on mountain once stood wenfeng pagoda, one of two wenfeng pagodas in Middle Nanxi River, unfortunately collapsed. Southeast is xun position, governing literary fortune; xun position having Writing Brush Peak or wenfeng pagoda both beneficial to villagers' examination success.

From Tangwan village map drawn in Xuantong era, like tanxia village across river, village wall front经过 compositional design, pavilions varied, plus several temples — from south to north: Mountain Guardian temple, Jingsheng temple, Three Officials temple and Songfang ancestral hall, among which Mountain Guardian temple is water-mouth temple.

Two village gates, both arch-type: south called Donghui Gate (East-Benefit Gate), north called Tongde Gate (Through-Virtue Gate).
