10
岩头村
中文

2. 岩头村

岩头村是楠溪江中游盆地最大的村子，位于盆地中央，东有楠溪江，北有五鹈溪，西离丘陵不远。东面直到江边都是广阔的农田，南面与芙蓉村为邻，相距不过二里。沃野千顷，沟渠纵横，农产十分丰饶。从乐清去缙云和仙居的道路从岩头村通过，带来了商业利益。

（1）岩头村的建村，大约从南宋初年开始。建村之后，第一件重大的建设就是兴修水利。《岩头金氏宗谱》记载二世祖日新公(?~1348年，按：宗谱载生年为元中统丙子，但中统无丙子)筑水渠的事："时厥土苦于旱潦，频岁不登，府君相地宜，顺水性，浚两渠于濮头之南，达泉下灌，常获丰稔，两都农业，迄今赖之。"这两条渠，是岩头村的命脉，不但满足了生产需要，也满足了生活需要，现在还是岩头村存在的依靠。

以后的重大建设在明代嘉靖年间。这时候有两位重要人物，一位是嘉靖乙丑（1565年）科进士金昭霞峰公，一位是桂林公(1494-1569年)。霞峰公曾任大理寺左寺右寺副，迁瑞州知府，在岩头建造过大宗祠、进士牌楼和上花园、下花园。现在进士牌楼完好，大宗祠还存二进大厅和钟、鼓楼，上花园部分遗址依稀可辨，下花园则已完全无迹可寻。桂林公对岩头村建设的贡献最大。《宗谱·桂林公行状》说："由始迁岩头以来，列祖非无建造，而兴利之多，功德之盛，应推府君为第一。"又说他："屡试不中，转而习青囊，相宅卜地"，所以，他的兴建都有多少的风水考虑。

桂林公的建设也是先从完善水利开始的。宗谱记载："本族地址颇高，田苦旱涸，升四公捐田废资，开凿长河一带，以备蓄泄，并筑高埠，培闸风水，建亭造塔于其上，垂成，归之大宗祠，为通族公业。"这是明世宗嘉靖三十五年（1556年）的事。长河一带，就是丽水湖，高埠就是村东、南的拦水堤坝，它拦住了穿村而来的渠水，形成了丽水湖。它们是抗旱的水库，同时也成了岩头村的公园，是整个楠溪江中游最美的公园。在这公园里，桂林公造了坝上的接官亭和汤山上的文峰塔，山前的塔湖庙(即孝祜庙)和庙右的书斋森秀轩。

在汤山北侧他又造了一座规模很大的书院。书院以北，沿三条街，桂林公主持建造了一批三进二院大宅。现在公园和它里面的建筑物都还在，大宅却都已被焚毁，但有不少遗迹仍然清晰可辨。

（2）岩头村的正门是北门，叫仁道门，为纪念始迁祖刘进之乐善好施而造。门里大街西侧是金氏大宗祠，朝南。它前面是金霞峰的进士牌楼，朝东，贴着大街。与大宗祠隔街相对的，是嘉庆戊辰年（1808年）造的石质谢氏贞节坊。仁道门、大宗祠、贞节坊和进士牌楼形成了岩头村最重要的礼制中心，它集中体现了宗族的骄傲与荣誉。

从这个礼制中心，有一条大街笔直往南，贯穿全村，原本叫门前街，后来金昭中了进士，就改叫进士街。岩头村的东门叫献义门，有一条街就叫横街，从它向西横过村子。它的西段还有两条直街，靠西边的一条叫浚水街，另一条叫中央街。这几条街是岩头村的大街，在它们之间有小巷，窄而且直，被称为"箭"。

在进士街、中央街和浚水街，从南到北，各有三进两院的大宅七座。各座中轴的间隔为48.3米左右，"箭"就是它们之间的夹道。各街之间相距55米左右，正好是大宅的总进深，大宅的前门在一条街，后门在相邻的另一条街。全村的住宅都朝东，所以，这几条街，西侧都是大门，东侧都是后门。水渠靠东侧。

桂林公主持建造的这一片大宅第，规划严整，气魄宏大，在楠溪江村落的规划建设中几乎是绝无仅有的。它们占了全村一半以上的面积，所以，它们每一幢显然都是数家合住的。这批大宅集中建造于村子西部，说明岩头村在明代已经有了初步的分区思想。清道光元年（1821年），枫林村人因争柴山与岩头村人结仇，密告岩头村人勾结"长毛"，清兵进剿，烧毁了岩头村大部分房屋，这批大宅无一幸免。现在，浚水街还可以看到六座大宅的遗迹，中央街和菊花街(原进士街南段)可以看到一两座。遗址上已经造满了中小型的住宅，很零乱。

因为这些住宅远没有大宅那样的进深，所以它们的出入布局都很随意。原来大宅中轴线现在成了巷子，阶条石、台阶和石板甬道，还历历可见。

这几条大街的宽度都在3米以上。浚水街宽4.5米，东侧另有2.7米宽的水渠。渠水流量很大。

（3）岩头村的水渠系统十分出色。它的起点在村北1公里五鹈溪畔的双浚头。在五鹈溪里筑坝，稍稍提高水位。在溪底铺有孔洞的石板，使水漏进"地下水库"。从这地下水库引水过堤坝后经明渠流到村西北角的上花园。在上花园分前浚、后浚。前浚顺北寨墙东流，进仁道门侧，然后傍进士街南流，陆续分到一些比较宽的小街供居民使用。后浚南流不远再度分叉，一支贴横街北侧向东流，一支沿浚水街东侧继续南流。向东的水渠，分合合，到东门，就是献义门，大部分水经地下涵道出南门，灌溉农田，到东面形成石亭湖、状元湖，再东流入楠溪江。

小部分水经另一涵洞汇到丽水湖，也就是由东面的堤坝拦蓄起来，备旱灾时灌田用。顺浚水街南下的水渠，到汤山脚下注入一股流急而丰沛的大溪，折而向东，在汤山东北角又分为两支。一支进入进宦湖，向东绕过琴屿，一部分水入丽水湖，一部分转而向西注入镇南湖。另一支先形成一个小小的智水湖，再向南，在塔湖庙后进入底下的涵洞流进庙南的右军池，然后，一部分出村浇灌农田，一部分经森秀轩下的涵洞向东北流入镇南湖。镇南湖和丽水湖都有暗渠穿出堤坝，可泄水浇田。从双浚头到村东注入楠溪江，水渠的长度约十余公里。

（按：不计分支等等）沿途有八道涵洞、两个节制闸、三间水碓，形成进宦湖、镇南湖、丽水湖、石亭湖、状元湖等五个人工水库。这一套水系设计很巧妙，如右军池内，用卵石堆了个分流坝，水量少的时候，全部水都出村浇田，水流大一点，就溢过分流坝流向镇南湖。智水湖和进宦湖之间的分流也是这样的。

岩头村的渠水水量充沛，流速比较大。浚水街侧的水渠，宽有2.7米宽，形成很有特色的街景。水光闪闪，水声潺潺，浣衣妇女，笑语盈盈。一到夏季，儿童们光着身子下到渠里游泳，更加使街道充满了欢乐。

（4）智水湖、进宦湖和镇南湖在岩头村的南端，丽水湖在东侧，与镇南湖隔一道石板的丽水桥。它们都是嘉靖三十五年（1556年）桂林公筑堤后形成的，是东南部的公园区的主体。岩头村的东南门正对着丽水桥，桥北侧镌："明嘉靖戊午年(1558年)仲秋吉旦金氏建"几个字。

丽水湖北起献义门，南止于丽水桥，长达300米，最狭处十余米，最宽处二十余米而西接苇塘，浩渺一片。东边是长堤，因为湖中植荷，所以长堤又名荷埼。堤宽13米。这丽水湖是岩头村"十八胜景"之一，长堤上有观景廊，廊上的楹联是：

萍风碧漾观鱼栏；柳浪翠泛闻莺廊。

丽水湖与长堤南端呈弧形，微微弯向西南。顺湖看去，远处正对芙蓉岩。近处则是轻盈地横过丽水湖高高架起的丽水桥和桥东头遮天蔽日的大樟树。树下，台阶上，东南门的北侧，是一座凉亭，叫乘风亭。亭上楹联之一是："五月秋先到；一年春不归"，上联写的是风凉，下联写的是美景。所以整天休闲的人不断，又有茶卖供应，人情与景色同美。

丽水桥的西侧是镇南湖，矩形的，长约67.0米，宽约9.0米。从丽水桥上望去，翠盖亭亭，隙中芙蓉三岩倒映如镜。王鹤龄《金山十景诗》里有《丽桥观荷》：

红蕖灼灼照清池，君子心情遗世姿；

坐对嫣然如解语，乘风散步纳凉时。

镇南湖的另一端，是素净的三间森秀轩。

过丽水桥西，左手隔渠就是琴屿。琴屿是个半岛，宽16.2米，在镇南湖和进宦湖之间。进宦湖也是矩形的，与镇南湖平行，大小也相仿，长80.0米，宽15.5米。这湖里不种菱荷，白羽悠悠，岸边浣衣女漾出一圈圈的涟漪，又是另一种景象。

琴屿上长满了花木，郁郁勃勃。夏季紫薇盛开，一团团云蒸霞蔚。到了秋天，又是美艳的芙蓉花如楠溪江少女的笑靥，娇嫩的粉红色，渐渐晕染玉一样的洁白。明人劳宜斋著的《瓯江逸志》里写"木芙蓉"：

温州芙蓉高与梧桐等，八月杪即放，九月特盛。遍地有之。登楼一望，但见红霞灿烂，亦奇观也。最妙者名醉芙蓉，晨起白色，午后淡红，晚则变为深红，殊堪赏玩。瓯江又名芙蓉江，盖谓此也。

芙蓉岩前芙蓉花，情景更加不同。

琴屿西南端是塔湖庙和它门前面向着它的戏台。庙朝向东北，三进两院，面阔三间。门屋的门板可以拆卸，便于看戏。每年正月十五日起，塔湖庙有七天庙会，天天演戏。平日里，这庙在很大程度上是个园林建筑，它后进楼上的大厅，向东南一面完全敞开，设美人靠，接纳田野四季秀色和芙蓉村的烟霭人家。

塔湖庙的后进，左是智水湖，右是右军池。池的面积约60平方米。智水湖西南侧汤山山坡有个文昌阁，是乾隆庚申年（1740年）造的。右军池东北有森秀轩，也是嘉靖年间桂林公造的，是他的书斋。桂林公在《森秀轩记》里说："森秀轩者，孝祜庙之香积厨也，地居形胜，厄于筑室之陋，不得大观者已然一日（按：疑有讹字，"然"或为"非"）。己未夏，稍为更张，不独轩之面目顿开，觉山水之灵秀焕然毕露矣！"他整修这三间香积厨，不仅为读书，更是为了完美这一片风景区。在这简朴的书斋里，他过着高品位的文化生活，寄托着文人的闲情逸致。

森秀轩的东南侧就是拦水坝。坝的西南端抵汤山山麓，东北端在丽水桥接续长堤。在距丽水桥还有18米处，坝上造了一座"接官亭"，又叫"花亭"。亭是正方形的，重檐攒尖顶，两层檐密接，宝顶硕大，以致亭子风格很庄重。它对丽水桥的一面是三开间，其余三面是单开间，形制很特别，所以有楹联："名师留奇迹；怪匠逗行人。"这一段堤坝上没有其他建筑物，接官亭的庄重和高大，与环境很配称。亭侧有数人合抱的苦槠树、樟树和柏树，浓荫蔽日，与琴屿上阳光下的紫薇、芙蓉形成生动的对照。

花亭又是村里调解民间纠纷的场所，有一副楹联写的是："情理三巡酒；理情酒三巡。"息事宁人，劝人和睦相处。

塔湖庙背靠汤山。山不高，大约浚凿几个湖的时候，还曾经培过土，以利植树。山上有文峰塔，塔也不高，六角形，楼阁式，用灰白色大理石造的。1985年倒坍。汤山在岩头村东南，即巽位，山上建文峰塔；堪舆巽风水迷信，它大有利于科甲。

桂林公又在汤山之西造了一座书院，主要用于族中子弟课读。院里有水池，池中央有亭。这水池正好倒映文峰塔，在风水上叫做"文笔蘸墨"，最好不过。《金山十景·水亭秋月》诗写道：

绕栏银浪涌层霄，倒影苍茫映碧寥；

烂醉不妨亭上卧，清光相伴到来朝。

这书院在很大程度上是公园的一部分。

岩头村的这一座公园，包括湖、岛、山、堤、桥、庙、阁、轩、塔、树木花草，内容丰富，景观多变化，岩头村的"金山十景"中有八景在这里，计有：长堤春晓、丽桥观荷、清沼观鱼、琴屿流莺、笔峰耸翠、水亭秋月、曲流环碧和塔湖印月（另两处：苍山积雪、南麓锦鹃，未能确知在何地）。几百年来，乡村文士们吟咏不辍。由吟咏中可见他们在这公园里散步、钓鱼、纳凉、赏花、听鸟，还要载酒行歌、流觞赋诗。正是这种散淡而潇洒的生活，被标榜为高洁文人最完美的理想、追求。桂林公在《森秀轩记》里说：

今日之森秀，美矣丽矣，设不幸而不能发其秀、显其奇，则虽曲水也而乏流觞之咏，长堤也而无走马之欢。紫薇夹岸，自开自落；绿柳垂门，谁歌谁舞？盖未忽黑云之沉，莲房悲粉粉之坠。求其如轩之少长咸集，论列品评，或高卧羲皇之枕，或沉醉阮籍之杯。献诗煮茗，消溽暑于青荫；酌酒谈棋，披熏风于曲槛。明于斯，晦于斯。风雨流连而不息者，安可得乎！

小小山村里科场失意的士绅，说着与两千年来知识分子一模一样的话。岩头村的这座园林，凝聚着中国文化的价值观，寄托着乡村文人们的山水情怀和耕读理想。乡村文士们的高品位文化生活，他们的理想爱好和追求，给了这个园林以深刻的、丰富的文化内涵。

English

2. Yantou village

Yantou village is the largest village in Middle Nanxi River basin, located at basin center, east has Nanxi River, north has Wuti Stream, west not far from hills. East to river all vast farmland, south adjacent to Furong village, no more than two li apart. Fertile fields thousands of qing, canals crisscrossing, agricultural produce very abundant.

Road from Yueqing to Jinyun and Xianju passes through Yantou village, bringing commercial benefits.

(1) Yantou village's founding, roughly began in early Southern Song. After founding, first major construction was water conservancy.

"Yantou Jin clan Genealogy" records second-generation ancestor Rixin Gong (?~1348, note: genealogy says born Yuan Zhongtong Bingzi, but Zhongtong has no Bingzi year) building canals: "At that time land suffered from drought and waterlogging, frequent years of poor harvest; Sir assessed land suitability, followed water nature, dredged two canals south of Putou, reaching springs for irrigation, often obtaining abundant harvests; both districts' agriculture, to this day rely on them." These two canals are Yantou village's lifeline, not only meeting production needs but also living needs; now still Yantou village's reliance for existence.

Later major construction in Ming Jiajing era. At this time two important figures: one is Jin Zhao Xiafeng Gong, jinshi in Jiajing Yichou (1565); one is Guilin Gong (1494-1569).

Xiafeng Gong served as Dali temple Left temple Right Assistant, promoted to Ruizhou prefect; in Yantou built main ancestral hall, jinshi paifang, and Upper Garden and Lower Garden.

Now jinshi paifang intact, main ancestral hall still has two main halls and bell/drum towers, Upper Garden partial ruins faintly discernible, Lower Garden completely gone. Guilin Gong's contribution to Yantou village construction greatest.

"Genealogy · Guilin Gong Biography" says: "Since first moving to Yantou, ancestors not without construction, but in number of benefits and abundance of merit, should rank Sir first." Also says he: "repeatedly failed exams, turned to studying fengshui, choosing sites and divining land" — so his constructions all had some fengshui consideration.

Guilin Gong's construction also began with improving water conservancy.

Genealogy records: "Our clan's site rather high, fields suffered drought; Sisigong donated fields and spent funds, excavated long river stretch, for storage and discharge, and built high embankment, cultivated fengshui, built pavilions and pagodas on it, upon completion, gave to main ancestral hall as clan public property." This was Ming Shizong Jiajing thirty-fifth year (1556).

Long river stretch is Lishui Lake; high embankment is village east and south water-retaining dam, which blocked canal water through village, forming Lishui Lake. They are drought-resistant reservoirs, and simultaneously became Yantou village's park — the most beautiful park in entire Middle Nanxi River.

In this park, Guilin Gong built Jieguan Pavilion on dam and wenfeng pagoda on Tang Mountain, Tahu temple (i.e. Xiaohu temple) in front of mountain and study Senxuan Studio right of temple. North of Tang Mountain he also built a large-scale academy.

North of academy, along three streets, Guilin Gong supervised construction of一批 three-courtyard two-hall mansions. Now park and buildings inside still exist; mansions all burned, but many relics still clearly discernible.

(2) Yantou village's main gate is north gate, called Rende Gate, built to commemorate founding ancestor Liu Jinzhi's generosity and charity. Inside gate, west side of main street is Jin clan main ancestral hall, facing south. In front of it is Jin Xiafeng's jinshi paifang, facing east, against main street.

Opposite main ancestral hall across street is stone Xie clan chastity paifang built in Jiaqing Wuchen year (1808). Rende Gate, main ancestral hall, chastity paifang and jinshi paifang form Yantou village's most important ritual center,集中 embodying clan's pride and honor.

From this ritual center, a main street runs straight south, through whole village, originally called Menqian Street; later after Jin Zhao passed jinshi, renamed jinshi Street. Yantou village's east gate called Xianyi Gate; a street called Heng Street runs west from it across village.

Its west section has two straight streets: western one called Junshui Street, other called Zhongyang Street. These streets are Yantou's main streets; between them small lanes, narrow and straight, called "arrows."

On jinshi, Zhongyang and Junshui streets, from south to north, each has seven three-courtyard two-hall mansions. Each mansion's central axis spacing about 48.3m; "arrows" are the alleys between them. Streets about 55m apart, exactly mansion's total depth; mansion's front door on one street, back door on adjacent street.

Whole village houses face east, so on these streets west side all front doors, east side all back doors. Canals on east side.

This batch of mansions supervised by Guilin Gong, planning rigorous, grandeur imposing, almost unique in Nanxi River village planning and construction. They occupied over half village area, so each mansion obviously shared by several families.

This batch of mansions集中 built in village west,说明 Yantou village in Ming already had preliminary zoning idea.

In Qing Daoguang first year (1821), Fenglin villagers due to firewood mountain dispute with Yantou villagers formed feud, secretly reported Yantou villagers colluding with "Long Hair" (Taiping); Qing troops suppressed, burning most Yantou houses, this batch of mansions none spared.

Now on Junshui Street can see remains of six mansions; Zhongyang Street and Juhua Street (former jinshi Street south section) can see one or two. Ruins now filled with small-medium houses, very disorderly. Because these houses far less depth than mansions, their entry layouts随意.

Original mansion central axes now become lanes; step stones, stairs and stone slab paths still clearly visible.

These main streets all over 3m wide. Junshui Street 4.5m wide, east side another 2.7m wide canal. Canal water flow large.

(3) Yantou village's canal system is outstanding. Its starting point at Shuangjuntou by Wuti Stream 1km north of village. In Wuti Stream build dam, slightly raising water level.

At stream bottom lay perforated stone slabs, letting water leak into "underground reservoir." From underground reservoir divert water over dam then via open channel to village northwest corner's Upper Garden. At Upper Garden divide into Front Dredge and Rear Dredge.

Front Dredge flows east along north village wall, enters Rende Gate side, then south along jinshi Street, gradually distributing to wider small streets for residents. Rear Dredge flows south not far then forks again: one branch east along north side of Heng Street, one continues south along east side of Junshui Street.

Eastward canal, dividing and merging, to east gate (Xianyi Gate), most water via underground culvert out south gate, irrigating farmland, to east forming Shiting Lake and Zhuangyuan Lake, then east into Nanxi River. Small part via another culvert into Lishui Lake — i.e. impounded by east dam, reserved for drought irrigation.

Canal south along Junshui Street, at Tang Mountain foot injects into a fast-flowing abundant large stream, turns east, at Tang Mountain northeast corner divides into two. One enters Jinhuan Lake, east around Qin Islet, part into Lishui Lake, part turns west into Zhennan Lake.

Other first forms small Zhishui Lake, then south, behind Tahu temple enters underground culvert into temple-south Youjun Pool, then part out village irrigating farmland, part via culvert under Senxuan Studio northeast into Zhennan Lake. Zhennan Lake and Lishui Lake both have hidden culverts through dam, can discharge water for fields.

From Shuangjuntou to village east into Nanxi River, canal length about over ten km. (not counting branches etc.) Along route eight culverts, two control gates, three water-powered trip hammers, forming Jinhuan Lake, Zhennan Lake, Lishui Lake, Shiting Lake, Zhuangyuan Lake — five artificial reservoirs. This water system design ingenious: e.g.

in Youjun Pool, cobblestone diversion dam piled; when water low all water out village irrigating fields; when water higher, overflows diversion dam to Zhennan Lake. Diversion between Zhishui Lake and Jinhuan Lake same way.

Yantou village canal water abundant, flow relatively large. Junshui Street side canal 2.7m wide, forming distinctive streetscape. Water light sparkling, water sound gurgling, washing women, laughter abundant. In summer, children naked swim in canal, making street full of joy.

(4) Zhishui Lake, Jinhuan Lake and Zhennan Lake at Yantou village south end; Lishui Lake east side, separated from Zhennan Lake by stone slab Lishui bridge. They all formed after Guilin Gong built dam in Jiajing thirty-fifth year (1556), main body of southeast park area.

Yantou village's southeast gate directly faces Lishui bridge; bridge north side engraved: "Ming Jiajing Wuwu year (1558) mid-autumn auspicious day Jin clan built."

Lishui Lake north from Xianyi Gate, south to Lishui bridge, 300m long, narrowest over 10m, widest over 20m connecting west to reed pond, vast expanse. East side long embankment; because lake planted lotus, long embankment also called Heqi (Lotus Bank). Embankment 13m wide. This Lishui Lake is one of Yantou village's "Eighteen Scenic Spots"; long embankment has viewing gallery, gallery couplet:

Duckweed wind green ripples fish-watching rail;

Willow waves green overflow oriole-hearing gallery.

Lishui Lake and long embankment south end arc-shaped, slightly curving southwest. Looking along lake, distant directly faces Furong Rock. Nearby is light Lishui bridge high over Lishui Lake and east bridgehead's sky-shading large camphor tree.

Under tree, on steps, north of southeast gate, is a pavilion called Chengfeng Pavilion (Riding-Wind Pavilion). One of pavilion couplets: "Fifth month autumn first arrives; one year spring never returns" — first line writes cool wind, second writes beautiful scenery.

So all day leisure people constant, also tea sold, human warmth and scenery equally beautiful.

West of Lishui bridge is Zhennan Lake, rectangular, about 67.0m long, 9.0m wide. From Lishui bridge looking, green canopies亭亭, gaps reflect Furong Three Rocks like mirror. Wang Heling's "Jinshan Ten Scenes Poems" has "Lishui bridge Viewing Lotus":

Red lotus brightly shining clear pool, gentleman's mood transcending world;

Sitting facing charming as if understanding speech, riding wind walking enjoying cool.

Zhennan Lake's other end is plain three-bay Senxuan Studio.

Crossing Lishui bridge west, left across canal is Qin Islet (Lute Islet). Qin Islet is a peninsula, 16.2m wide, between Zhennan Lake and Jinhuan Lake. Jinhuan Lake also rectangular, parallel to Zhennan Lake, similar size, 80.0m long, 15.5m wide. This lake no water chestnut or lotus, white feathers leisurely,岸边 washing women creating circles of ripples — another kind of scene.

Qin Islet covered with flowers and trees, lush and vigorous. Summer crape myrtle in full bloom, clusters like clouds and mist. In autumn, beautiful hibiscus flowers like Nanxi River girl's smile, delicate pink gradually晕染 jade-like white. Ming author Lao Yizhai's "Oujiang Yizhi" writes "wood hibiscus":

Wenzhou hibiscus as tall as paulownia, end of eighth month already blooms, ninth month especially abundant. Everywhere. Climbing tower looking, only see red clouds brilliant, also wondrous sight. Most wonderful called drunk hibiscus: morning white, afternoon light red, evening becomes deep red, very worthy of appreciation. Oujiang also called Hibiscus River, referring to this.

Before Furong Rock, hibiscus flowers, scene even more different.

Qin Islet southwest end is Tahu temple and its stage facing it. temple faces northeast, three-courtyard two-hall, three bays wide. Gate house door panels removable, convenient for watching opera. Every year from fifteenth day of first lunar month, Tahu temple has seven-day temple fair, daily opera.

On ordinary days, this temple largely a garden building; its rear building upper hall, southeast side completely open, with beauty-rails, receiving fields' four-season beauty and Furong village's mist and households.

Tahu temple's rear, left is Zhishui Lake, right is Youjun Pool. Pool area about 60 sqm. Zhishui Lake southwest side Tang Mountain slope has wenchang pavilion, built in Qianlong Gengshen year (1740). Youjun Pool northeast has Senxuan Studio, also Jiajing era built by Guilin Gong, his study.

Guilin Gong in "Senxuan Studio Record" says: "Senxuan Studio is Xiaohu temple's incense kitchen; location scenic, constrained by humble building, not able to get grand view for already one day (note: suspected typo, 'ran' maybe 'fei').

Jiwei summer, slightly renovated, not only studio's face suddenly opened, feel mountains and waters' spirit and beauty brilliantly revealed!" He renovated these three incense kitchens not only for reading, but more for perfecting this scenic area. In this simple study, he lived high-grade cultural life, entrusting literati's leisure sentiments.

Senxuan Studio's southeast side is water-retaining dam. Dam's southwest end reaches Tang Mountain foot, northeast end at Lishui bridge connects long embankment.

18m before Lishui bridge, on dam built "Jieguan Pavilion" (Official-Receiving Pavilion), also called "Flower Pavilion." Pavilion square, double-eave pyramidal roof, two eaves close together, treasure top large, giving pavilion solemn style.

Its side facing Lishui bridge three bays, other three sides single bays, form very special, hence couplet: "Famous master leaves miracle; strange craftsman teases passersby." This dam section no other buildings; Jieguan Pavilion's solemnity and height well matches environment.

Pavilion side several trees requiring several people to embrace — sawtooth oak, camphor and cypress, dense shade, forming vivid contrast with Qin Islet's sunlit crape myrtle and hibiscus.

Flower Pavilion also village's place for mediating folk disputes; a couplet writes: "Reason and emotion three rounds of wine; emotion and reason wine three rounds." Pacifying matters, persuading harmony.

Tahu temple backs Tang Mountain. Mountain not high; about when excavating several lakes, also piled soil to facilitate tree planting. Mountain has wenfeng pagoda, pagoda also not high, hexagonal, pavilion-style, built of gray-white marble. Collapsed in 1985. Tang Mountain at Yantou village southeast, i.e.

xun position; mountain built wenfeng pagoda — fengshui xun superstition, greatly beneficial for examination success.

Guilin Gong also built an academy west of Tang Mountain, mainly for clan children's study. courtyard has pool, pool center has pavilion. This pool exactly reflects wenfeng pagoda; in fengshui called "writing brush dipping ink," best possible. "Jinshan Ten Scenes · Water Pavilion Autumn Moon" poem writes:

Around rail silver waves surging layer sky, reflection vast reflecting blue emptiness;

Drunk不妨 lying on pavilion, clear light accompanies to dawn.

This academy largely part of park.

Yantou village's this park, including lakes, island, mountain, embankment, bridge, temple, pavilion, studio, pagoda, trees and flowers — content rich, landscape varied; Yantou village's "Jinshan Ten Scenes" has eight here: Long Embankment Spring Dawn, Lishui bridge Viewing Lotus, Clear Pool Viewing Fish, Qin Islet Orioles, Brush Peak Towering Green, Water Pavilion Autumn Moon, Winding Stream Encircling Green, and Tahu Lake Reflecting Moon (other two: Cang Mountain Snow, South Foot Brocade Cuckoo, location uncertain).

For hundreds of years, rural literati endlessly composed. From poems可见 they in this park walked, fished, enjoyed cool, admired flowers, listened to birds, and also carried wine singing, floating cups composing poetry. Precisely this casual and elegant life, held up as pure literati's most perfect ideal and pursuit.

Guilin Gong in "Senxuan Studio Record" says:

Today's Senxuan, beautiful and gorgeous; if unfortunately cannot express its beauty, show its wonder, then even winding stream lacks floating-cup chanting, long embankment lacks horse-riding joy. Crape myrtle both banks, blooming and falling alone; green willows hanging gate, who sings who dances?

Not yet noticing black clouds sinking, lotus seed pods grieving pink falling. Seeking like studio's young and old gathered, discussing and evaluating, or high sleeping on Fuxi's pillow, or drunk on Ruan Ji's cup. Offering poems brewing tea, dispelling summer heat in green shade; pouring wine discussing chess, embracing warm wind in curved rail.

Bright here, dark here. Wind and rain lingering without rest — how could obtain!

Small mountain village's examination-failed gentry, speaking exactly same words as intellectuals over two thousand years. Yantou village's this garden embodies Chinese culture's values, entrusts rural literati's landscape sentiments and farming-reading ideals. rural literati's high-grade cultural life, their ideals hobbies and pursuits, gave this garden profound, rich cultural connotation.

本篇术语 Key Terms

岩头村 / Yantou Village 仁道门 / Rende Gate (Benevolence-Virtue Gate) 进士牌楼 / Jinshi paifang (Presented-Scholar archway) 贞节坊 / Chastity paifang 礼制中心 / Ritual center 进士街 / Jinshi Street 献义门 / Xianyi Gate (Offering-Righteousness Gate) 横街 / Heng Street (Cross Street) 浚水街 / Junshui Street (Water-dredging Street) 中央街 / Zhongyang Street (Central Street) 箭 / Arrow (narrow lane) 三进两院大宅 / Three-courtyard two-hall mansion 分区思想 / Zoning idea 双浚头 / Shuangjuntou (Double-dredge Head) 五鹈溪 / Wuti Stream ...等40个

第三篇：建筑篇

Part III: Architecture
1
历史的痕迹
中文

七、居住建筑

兴造住宅，是最基本的建筑活动。住宅的数量，也远非任何一种其他建筑可比。住宅是决定楠溪江村落面貌的最重要因素之一。《黄帝宅经》说："宅者，人之本。人以宅为家，居若安，即家代昌吉，若不安，即门族衰微。"所以家家户户都重视住宅的营造。

虽然有祠堂、牌楼的巍峨，有戏台、庙宇的华丽，最有创造性的建筑，却是住宅。它的形制比较多，形式也多变化。住宅的风格也跟祠堂庙宇不同，它开敞而不封闭、亲切而不庄肃，它用蛮石素木顺其自然而不事雕琢。祠堂庙宇的模式化程度比较高，跟其他各地的差不多，真正最有本乡本土特色而与他处不同的，是住宅。住宅是最基本的乡土建筑。

1. 历史的痕迹

按照惯例，宗谱不记载私家住宅情况。经过近几十年来几度剧烈的社会变动，有关住宅的私人文件已片纸不留。现在各住宅的住户，尤其大型住宅的，往往不是故家旧主，所以对住宅的过去一无所知。要勾画楠溪江村落里住宅的演进历史是很困难的。

但不少村落里，都有一些古老的住宅，如花坛村马湾的几幢老宅，后院有一口井，井圈上刻着的"大宋宝庆二年丙戌"（1226年）几个字，还隐约可辨。乡人都叫这几幢住宅为"宋宅"。被乡人认为是宋代遗构的，还有蓬溪村"状元衙"东口南侧的"李时靖宅"和塘湾村"上马石"东北侧的"郑伯熊宅"，等等。虽然现在并没有能确证它们是宋代建筑，但它们确实很古老，这是可以从梁架尺度和门限的磨损程度上判断的。明代的住宅更多，一些古村里都有。

一般说来，它们的规模比较大，三进两院的不少，而且工程质量也比较考究，阶条石有用4~5米长的大石条的。院子中央的甬路也铺整齐的大石条。一直到清代初年，不少村里还在造些规模大、质量考究的大型住宅，如芙蓉村西北角的"司马第"，造于康熙年间。再往下，住宅的规模小了，比较自由了，用料也随便多了，质量显然下降，反映出嘉靖年后永嘉县经济、文化的衰落。

（1）花坛村的"宋宅"在马湾。一条小巷走向南北，在它的北端，有三幢古老的住宅。一幢在巷子西侧，长条形，单层，七开间，明间开间达8.3米。屋架大致是抬梁式与穿斗式的混合形式，很高大，前檐柱高6.4米，明柱高9.0米，脊柱高11.5米，进深11.2米。构件也很粗大，相似于常见的宗祠的梁架。全部露明。明间减去了两根明柱和两根檐柱，分别由两根粗大的枋子来支承本来应该由它们支承的两榀屋架，所以开间竟达到一般开间的两倍，内部空间很高旷。这幢房子四面都是板壁和木板装修。柱子之间有地袱，有上、中、下槛，它们之间钉木板。窗子多是直棂窗，两侧有立颊。柱础是木质的。

巷子东侧有两幢古宅，一幢是长条形的，五开间，另一幢是一座四合院，从它的形制和前面的遗迹看，它本来是一座三进两院的大宅子。现在的四合院，是它的后半部，前院的厢房还有几间残存，全毁的是它的门屋部分。它是两层的，正屋七开间，两厢三开间。梁架比巷西那一幢的小，而与晚近的相仿，进深倒也是11.2米。虽然是四合院，它的一圈外墙还是板壁和木装修，也是在柱子之间设地袱和上、中、下槛，开直棂窗，窗两侧有立颊。用木柱础。从梁架看，东侧的两幢大概比西侧的晚。

这三座房子都已经非常老旧，大约35厘米高的门槛，已经快被多少年来人们的双脚磨断。虽然目前还没有确定它们的年代，但可以大致判定，它们是楠溪江中游最古老的住宅之一。如果从它们后院的那口井看，则这几幢古宅，至少初建于南宋，即使后来经过修缮或者改造。

蓬溪村的"李时靖宅"，也是一座古老的住宅。它正屋七间，总阔27.0米，两厢三间，宽9.0米。正屋进深9.5米，有前檐廊，深1.5米；两厢没有檐廊，进深8米。它前临蓬溪村的主街，现状没有院墙和门楼，院子直接向街敞开，二者之间隔一条引水渠。它四面全部是板壁和木装修，正屋明间前檐有三个双扇门，次间是直棂窗。两厢的前檐安拼板活络扇，上面开窗洞，用直棂。柱子不高，下用木础，也有地袱。

这座房子的左侧是一条石板铺的极整齐的"状元街"。传说李时靖中了"状元"（按实为咸淳乙丑，即1265年，进士）之后，回乡造了这条街，还没有改建故居，就携李姓族人全都迁走了。谢灵运的后裔从东皋来到蓬溪，买下了宗祠和这座住宅。

和花坛村的"宋宅"一样，它确实十分古老，风格很朴实，厚重得有点拙。

塘湾的"郑伯熊宅"在村中心的"上马石"东北，也就是原来的太平坊的东北。现在是一座四合院，但照遗迹推断，原来也是一座三进两院的大宅子。同样是正屋七间，厢房三间。正屋进深10.5米，两厢进深7.0米。正屋明间阔4.7米，次间阔3.3米。不过两厢的开间很窄，明间3.0米，次间2.70米。它也是四圈板壁，木装修，用直棂窗。

这几幢相传为宋代的住宅，都没有用砖。砖是明代以后才普遍使用的建筑材料。

（2）楠溪江中游村落里，明代的住宅遗存还比较多，溪口、蓬溪、廊下、花坛、苍坡、周宅、珠岸等村子里都有。岩头村和芙蓉村有一些明代住宅的遗迹。

这些明代的住宅大多是三进两院的大型住宅。根据它们判断，明代大约是楠溪江中游村落建设的高峰时期。住宅不但规模比较大，形制整齐，而且材料和施工质量都比较高。溪口村的一幢明代大宅，大门竟有五开间，明间有雕花的石鼓凳，阶条石长达4~5米，台阶的垂带也有精致的浮雕。院里铺块石，中央用条石铺甬路。据《明会典》，公侯以下至一二品官，第宅门屋都不过三开间。溪口村的这座大宅，大大逾越了规制。"天高皇帝远"，在这种远离京师、地方官力不能及的山区，朝廷的规制的约束力是很弱的。

不过，这些明代住宅大多已经不能保持完整的原状，多多少少经过拆改或者扩充。

蓬溪村中心有一座三进两院的明代住宅，总体还保存得比较好。它占地总宽45.0~50.0米，总进深39.0米。面阔十三开间，左右还有小天井，形成"日月井"的格局，都有附加房间。全屋总间数不下五十。大门前，是一个深7.5米，宽28.0米的广场，地面铺块石，中央部分用卵石镶成图案。第一进门屋进深4.1米，后面的小院面积为5.2米×10.2米，整个是个水池，中央有一条甬路通过。正屋进深10.6米，有前后廊，后进进深8.3米，有前廊。它们之间又是一个水池，面积8.0米×12.0米，周围有回廊。这是楠溪江中游少有的大宅之一，它的两个水池院，也是少见的。

这幢大宅子北侧现在是砖墙，其余的墙壁全是木板的，用直棂窗。

岩头村在嘉靖年间由金氏桂林公主持，进行了大规模的规划和建设。在浚水街、中央街和进士街，各造了七幢三进两院的大宅，比肩而立，占了岩头村的一半。道光元年（1821年），因为被告发与太平军有联系，清军烧毁了岩头村大量房屋，包括这21幢大宅。现在在浚水街可以看到6幢的遗迹，在中央街和菊花街(为原进士街南段)可以见到两幢的遗迹，在花前街还可以见到一幢的遗迹。浚水街所见的6幢，它们的中轴线现在成了小巷，院落中的甬路还很完整。

每幢之间，包括被称为"箭"的小巷在内，轴线距是48.3米左右，进深60多米，从前到后，轴线上的尺寸大致是：第一进台明深4.7米，第一个院子深8.25米，第二进台明深13.10米，第二个院子深8.7米，第三进台明深14.90米。前门在一条街，后门在西侧相邻的另一条街。有一个后花园。中央街上的，进深62.5米。花前街和菊花街上的，进深是50.0米，没有后花园。这些大宅的形制和大小尺寸很统一，排列很整齐。

从大宅的间距看，参考蓬溪村的明代住宅，它们很可能也曾经有左右侧的小天井和侧屋，形成"日月井"的格局。

现在在浚水街上马巷还有一块当初的柱顶石，鼓镜的直径竟有76厘米，可以推知这些大宅的梁架的尺度是很大的。遥想四百五十年前，这一大片住宅区初建成的时候，岩头村的面貌很壮观，并不像后来在它们的废墟上重建的住宅那样自由活泼，那样明朗亲切。

在芙蓉村，像这样规模的遗址，清晰可辨的，至少有五座，它们的建造年代已经无从考证了。

（3）明代前期，楠溪江中游村落营建之盛，得力于经济、文化的繁荣。《鹤垟谢氏宗谱》记载，第十一世祖裕孙公（生元至大辛亥，卒明洪武乙卯，即1311-1375年），"性爱淡素，不尚浮靡，且殖业繁蓄，创第宏敞"，殖业繁蓄，恐怕是商业资本或高利贷资本的滋利。有了钱，便造宏敞的第宅。至于性爱淡素，不尚浮靡，则是楠溪江乡土文化的特色，士绅们在儒家传统和"老带庄襟"影响之下，以淡泊相标榜，虽然实际上未必如此。但以淡泊、耕读相标榜，这种文化心理和价值取向，必定会在建筑上有所表现，尤其是与生活密切相关的居住建筑。

同是鹤垟村，宣德年间供职锦衣卫的谢廷循，在家里造了一所静乐轩，宗谱说："士大夫与之游者皆为赋静乐之诗。"连明宣宗皇帝也写了一首《静乐诗》："暮色动前轩，重城欲闭门。残霞收赤气，新月破黄昏。已觉乾坤静，都无市井喧。阴阳有恒理，斯与达人论。"

也是这个鹤垟村，洪武、永乐年间有一位谢德玹，家里有一间书斋，濒临澄江。他自制《临水书斋》诗：

碧流湛湛涵长天，小斋横枕清堪怜。牙签插架三万轴，灯火照窗二十年。长日尘埃飞不到，常时风月阅无边。已知圣道犹如此，乐处寻来即自然。

谢廷循的朋友，豫章村的胡宗韫，宣德年间任中书舍人，归田的时候，同僚赠诗送行，有句："诛茆今日野，把剑旧时心。畬药晴檐短，安书夜榻低。"（陈斌）"烟霞三亩宅，霜露百年心。黄菊陶潜兴，清风梁父吟。"（陈中）这至少是他们标榜的生活的文化意蕴。胡宗韫在故宅"中翰第"造了一座紫微楼，以为养闲之所。《宗谱》说他"植竹种花，终日坐卧其间，临帖墨迹，随兴吟诗，优游自乐，或与密友笑谈、围棋、饮酒，如是二十馀年"。

《茗川胡氏大宗祠谱》里有一篇《碧云楼记》，详尽描绘了乡村士绅营建宅第的兴致和在其中的生活：

彦通（按：永乐间人）纯实谨愿，不为薄习。遇高人硕士，辄倾怀于觞酒间。乃度其所居堂之后，夷地幽闲，宜楼居，乃构楼若干楹。楼之左右宜竹，而又植以竹也。重檐峻出，四窗虚敞，而朝云暮雨，散旭敷晴，则前连溪碧，翠接山寒。夫楼中之佳致也，多在于竹。彦通每于风朝月夜，携朋挈侣，施施然游息于斯楼之上以极其潇洒者，盖其襟度宏深，神情超畅，能不以天地间事物为心虑也。

从这些诗文里，可以看到，当年一些乡贤士绅们，在家里过着品位很高的精神文化生活，他们对住宅的要求，不仅仅是物质性的和仪礼性的，他们要有雅洁的书斋，爽垲的书楼。可惜，现今在楠溪江的村落里，这些书斋和书楼已经完全消失了。

当然，在这些高质量的、规模比较大的宅第兴起的同时，村落里也一定会有另批住宅，简陋而湫隘。《两源陈氏宗谱》里有一首资叟公的诗，名叫《田家》，写的是：

颓屋矮檐四五家，腰镰荷笠事桑麻。

耳边不涉风波事，欸乃声中日未斜。

田家的典型生活环境是"颓屋矮檐"，可见是普遍情况。不过，这些居住建筑，当然更加禁不住时光的摧残，现在完全找不到了。

English

VII. Residential Architecture

Building residences is the most basic architectural activity. The number of residences far exceeds any other building type. residences are one of the most important factors determining the appearance of Nanxi River villages. "Yellow Emperor's residence Classic" says: "residence is the foundation of people.

People take residence as home; if living is secure, family prospers generation after generation; if not, clan declines." Therefore every household values residence construction.

Although ancestral halls and paifangs are majestic, stages and temples gorgeous, the most creative architecture is the residence. Its forms are more varied, shapes more changeable.

Residential style also differs from ancestral halls and temples: it is open rather than enclosed, intimate rather than solemn; it uses rough stone and plain timber following nature without elaborate carving.

ancestral halls and temples are more standardized, similar to elsewhere; what truly has local character and differs from other places is the residence. residence is the most basic vernacular architecture.

1. Traces of History

By convention, clan genealogies do not record private residences. After several drastic social changes in recent decades, private documents about residences have completely disappeared. Current residents, especially of large residences, are often not original owners, so know nothing of residence's past. Sketching the evolution history of residences in Nanxi River villages is very difficult.

But many villages have some ancient residences, such as several old houses at Mawan in Huatan village; backyard has a well, well ring engraved "Great Song Baoqing second year Bingxu" (1226), still faintly discernible.

villagers call these houses "Song residences." Also considered Song-era remains by villagers: "Li Shijing residence" south of "Zhuangyuan Ya" east entrance in Pengxi village, and "Zheng Boxiong residence" northeast of "Mounting Stone" in Tangwan village, etc.

Although not definitively proven as Song architecture, they are indeed very old — can be judged from beam-frame scale and threshold wear. Ming residences more numerous, found in some old villages.

Generally, their scale relatively large, many three-courtyard two-hall type, and construction quality refined; step stones sometimes 4-5m long large stone slabs. courtyard central path also paved with neat large stone slabs.

Until early Qing, many villages still built large-scale high-quality residences, such as "Sima Di" at northwest corner of Furong village, built in Kangxi era. Later, residence scale smaller, more free, materials more casual, quality clearly declined — reflecting Yongjia County's economic and cultural decline after Jiajing era.

(1) Huatan village's "Song residence" at Mawan. A small lane runs north-south; at its north end, three ancient residences. One on lane west side, long strip shape, single-story, seven bays, central bay opening reaches 8.3m.

roof frame roughly mixed form of tailiang (beam-lifting) and chuandou (column-and-tie), very tall: front eave column 6.4m high, central column 9.0m, ridge column 11.5m, depth 11.2m. Components also very thick, similar to common ancestral hall beam frames. All exposed.

Central bay removed two central columns and two eave columns, respectively supported by two thick lintels bearing the two roof frames they should support — so bay opening reaches twice normal, interior space very lofty. This house all four sides are plank walls and wood finishes.

Between columns have ground sills, upper/middle/lower thresholds, between them nailed planks. Windows mostly straight-lattice windows, both sides have side jambs. Column bases are wooden.

Lane east side has two old houses: one long strip five bays, other a courtyard house; from its form and front remains, originally a three-courtyard two-hall mansion. Current courtyard house is its rear half; front courtyard wing rooms several remain, completely destroyed is its gate house part.

It is two-story, main house seven bays, two wings three bays. beam frame smaller than lane west one, similar to later ones, depth also 11.2m.

Although courtyard house, its一圈 exterior wall still plank walls and wood finishes, also between columns set ground sills and upper/middle/lower thresholds, straight-lattice windows, window sides have side jambs. Wooden column bases. From beam frame, east two probably later than west one.

These three houses all very old; threshold about 35cm high, almost worn through by feet over countless years. Although age not yet determined, can roughly judge they are among oldest residences in Middle Nanxi River. Judging from backyard well, these old houses at least initially built in Southern Song, even if later repaired or altered.

Pengxi village's "Li Shijing residence" is also an ancient residence. Main house seven bays, total width 27.0m; two wings three bays, width 9.0m. Main house depth 9.5m, has front eave porch 1.5m deep; two wings no eave porch, depth 8m.

It faces Pengxi village main street; current state no courtyard wall or gate tower, courtyard directly open to street, separated by a water channel. All four sides plank walls and wood finishes; main house central bay front eave has three double doors, secondary bays straight-lattice windows.

Two wings front eave installed assembled-plank movable panels, above open window holes with straight lattice. Columns not tall, below wooden bases, also ground sills.

Left of this house is a stone-slab paved extremely neat "Zhuangyuan Street" (Top-Scholar Street). Legend says after Li Shijing passed "zhuangyuan" (actually jinshi in Xianchun Yichou, 1265), returned home built this street; before remodeling old residence, took entire Li clan and moved away. Xie Lingyun's descendants came from Donggao to Pengxi, bought ancestral hall and this residence.

Like Huatan village's "Song residence," it is indeed very ancient, style very plain, heavy and somewhat clumsy.

Tangwan's "Zheng Boxiong residence" northeast of village center "Mounting Stone," i.e. northeast of original Taiping Fang. Now a courtyard house, but from remains推断 originally also a three-courtyard two-hall mansion. Same main house seven bays, wing rooms three bays. Main house depth 10.5m, two wings depth 7.0m.

Main house central bay 4.7m wide, secondary bay 3.3m. But two wings' bays very narrow: central 3.0m, secondary 2.70m. Also four-sided plank walls, wood finishes, straight-lattice windows.

These several residences traditionally considered Song-era, all did not use brick. Brick became commonly used building material only after Ming dynasty.

(2) In Middle Nanxi River villages, Ming residence remains relatively numerous — found in Xikou, Pengxi, Langxia, Huatan, Cangpo, Zhouzhai, Zhuan and other villages. Yantou and Furong have some Ming residence remains.

These Ming residences mostly three-courtyard two-hall large residences. Judging from them, Ming was approximately peak period of Middle Nanxi River village construction. residences not only relatively large scale, neat form, but also materials and construction quality relatively high.

A Ming mansion in Xikou village: gate actually five bays, central bay has carved stone drum stools, step stones 4-5m long, step side panels also have exquisite relief. courtyard paved with block stones, central path with stone slabs.

According to "Ming Huidian" (Ming Institutions), from dukes/marquises down to first-second rank officials, residence gate houses no more than three bays. Xikou village's this mansion greatly exceeded regulations.

"Heaven high, emperor far" — in such mountain areas far from capital, beyond local officials' reach, court regulations' binding force very weak.

However, these Ming residences mostly cannot maintain complete original state, more or less altered or expanded.

Pengxi village center has a three-courtyard two-hall Ming residence, overall relatively well preserved. It occupies total width 45.0-50.0m, total depth 39.0m. Facade thirteen bays, left and right also small courtyards, forming "sun-moon well" pattern, all with附加 rooms. Total rooms no less than fifty.

Before gate, a plaza 7.5m deep, 28.0m wide, ground paved block stones, central part inlaid with cobblestone patterns. First gate house depth 4.1m, behind small courtyard area 5.2m×10.2m, entirely a pool, central has a path through. Main house depth 10.6m, has front and rear porches; rear building depth 8.3m, has front porch.

Between them another pool, area 8.0m×12.0m, surrounded by covered corridor. This is one of rare large mansions in Middle Nanxi River; its two pool courtyards also uncommon.

This mansion's north side now brick wall,其余 walls all wooden planks, straight-lattice windows.

Yantou village in Jiajing era, under Jin clan Guilin Gong's supervision, carried out large-scale planning and construction. On Junshui, Zhongyang and jinshi streets, each built seven three-courtyard two-hall mansions, standing shoulder to shoulder, occupying half of Yantou village.

Daoguang first year (1821), due to being reported connected with Taiping rebels, Qing troops burned大量 Yantou houses, including these 21 mansions. Now on Junshui Street can see remains of 6 mansions; on Zhongyang and Juhua streets (former jinshi Street south section) can see remains of 2; on Huaqian Street can see remains of 1.

The 6 on Junshui Street — their central axes now become lanes, courtyard paths still complete.

Between each mansion, including lanes called "arrows," axis spacing about 48.3m, depth over 60m; from front to back, axis dimensions roughly: first building platform depth 4.7m, first courtyard depth 8.25m, second building platform depth 13.10m, second courtyard depth 8.7m, third building platform depth 14.90m.

Front door on one street, back door on adjacent west street. Has a back garden. On Zhongyang Street, depth 62.5m. On Huaqian and Juhua streets, depth 50.0m, no back garden. These mansions' form and size dimensions very uniform,排列 very neat.

From mansion spacing, referencing Pengxi village Ming residence, they likely also once had left/right small courtyards and side rooms, forming "sun-moon well" pattern.

Now on Junshui Street Shangma Lane still has one original column base stone, drum mirror diameter actually 76cm — can infer these mansions' beam frame scale very large. Imagining four hundred fifty years ago, when this large residential area first completed, Yantou village's appearance very grand, not like later residences rebuilt on their ruins — free and lively, bright and intimate.

In Furong village, ruins of such scale, clearly discernible, at least five; their construction ages no longer verifiable.

(3) Early Ming, Middle Nanxi River village construction prosperity, due to economic and cultural flourishing. "Heyang Xie clan Genealogy" records 11th-generation ancestor Yusun Gong (born Yuan Zhida Xinhai, died Ming Hongwu Yimao, i.e.

1311-1375): "by nature loved plainness, did not value extravagance, and accumulated wealth abundantly, built residences spacious." Accumulating wealth abundantly — probably commercial capital or usury capital profit. With money, built spacious residences.

As for loving plainness, not valuing extravagance — this is Nanxi River vernacular culture's characteristic; gentry under Confucian tradition and "old-belt village-lapel" influence, boasted of simplicity, although in reality not necessarily so.

But boasting simplicity and farming-reading, this cultural psychology and value orientation必定 manifests in architecture, especially residential architecture closely related to life.

Same Heyang village, Xuande era Xie Tingxun served in Embroidered Uniform Guard, at home built a Jingle Studio (Quiet-Joy Studio); genealogy says: "Scholar-officials who associated with him all composed poems on quiet joy." Even Ming Xuanzong Emperor wrote a "Quiet Joy Poem": "Twilight moves front pavilion, heavy city about to close gate.

Remaining sunset gathers red air, new moon breaks dusk. Already feel heaven-earth quiet, no market noise. Yin-yang has constant principle, this discusses with wise man."

Also this Heyang village, Hongwu-Yongle era had Xie Dexuan, home had a study overlooking Chengjiang. He composed "Waterside Study" poem:

Green flow clear contains long sky, small studio横枕 clear and pitiable.

Toothpick bookends on shelves thirty thousand scrolls, lamp light through windows twenty years.

Long days dust cannot fly, constant wind and moon view boundless.

Already know sage way still like this, joy found comes naturally.

Xie Tingxun's friend, Hu Zongyun from Yuzhang village, Xuande era served as Central Book Secretary; when returning to fields, colleagues presented poems seeing him off, with lines: "Thatching today wilderness, sword old-time heart.

Herbal medicine sunny eave short, arranging books night bed low." (Chen Bin) "Mist and haze three-mu residence, frost and dew hundred-year heart. Yellow chrysanthemum Tao Qian interest, clear wind Liangfu chant." (Chen Zhong) This at least is the cultural meaning of life they boasted.

Hu Zongyun at old residence "Zhonghan Di" built a Ziwei Tower (Crape Myrtle Tower) as leisure place. Genealogy says he "planted bamboo and flowers, all day sitting/lying among them, practicing calligraphy, composing poems at will, leisurely self-enjoying, or with close friends laughing, playing go, drinking, thus over twenty years."

"Mingchuan Hu clan Main ancestral hall Genealogy" has a "Biyun Tower Record," detailed depicting rural gentry's enthusiasm for building residences and life therein:

Yantong (note: Yongle era person) pure and honest, not superficial. Meeting superior scholars, always pours heart over wine. Then measured behind his residence hall, leveled land secluded and quiet, suitable for tower dwelling, built tower several bays. Tower left/right suitable for bamboo, and planted bamboo.

Double eaves protruding, four windows open, and morning clouds evening rain, scattered sunlight clear sky — then front connects stream green, green meets mountain cold. The tower's fine scenery, mostly in bamboo.

Yantong every windy morning moonlit night, takes friends, leisurely rests on this tower to utmost elegance — because his mind broad, spirit free, can not be troubled by worldly things.

From these poems and texts, can see that year some rural gentry lived high-grade spiritual cultural life at home; their requirements for residence not merely material and ritual, they wanted elegant studies, bright towers. Unfortunately, now in Nanxi River villages, these studies and towers have completely disappeared.

Of course, while these high-quality relatively large-scale residences rose, villages certainly also had another batch of residences, crude and cramped. "Liangyuan Chen clan Genealogy" has a poem by Zisou Gong, called "Farm Family," writes:

Dilapidated houses low eaves four-five families, waist sickle shoulder hat doing mulberry and hemp.

Ears not involved in storm affairs, oar sound sun not yet slanted.

Farm family's typical living environment is "dilapidated houses low eaves" —可见 common situation. However, these residential buildings, of course even less able to withstand time's destruction, now completely cannot be found.

居住建筑现状
中文

2. 居住建筑现状

楠溪江各村落里现有的居住建筑，是从南宋以来，几百年历史的积累。可惜资料阙如，现在不可能一一判明它们建造的年代，加以历史的考察。甚至，由于近年来为造新房子而拆除大量传统住宅，就现状对它们作统计性的分析也毫无学术价值了。

（1）虽然住宅屡经翻改，但村子的规划结构基本没有变动，所以住宅与村子的整体之间，大体还保持着原始的关系。

中游盆地的村子，以岩头、苍坡和芙蓉三村为代表，规划格局都很整齐；一条主街，几条与主街相垂直的次街，次街之间的小巷。大多数的住宅，正门开在次街。这三村的次街都是南北走向。岩头和芙蓉的次街，间距50米左右，本来正相当于一幢三进两院大宅的进深。但几经兵火，现有的住宅大多是进深不到50米的中小型住宅，只能从小巷里侧面进门，门内是前院，正屋仍然向东。还有一些，是从小巷开一段专用的岔巷，正门开在岔巷里，例如芙蓉村的"将军屋"，岩头村的"枕琴庐"。

更有一些简单的长条形小住宅，不围院落，全面敞开，则从小巷由一些人行步道出入。岩头和芙蓉两村都有不少在大宅废墟上建造的住宅，非常零乱，有造在原来两厢台基上的，就朝北、朝南，有造在正屋台基上的，就朝东。这些住宅，出入的路径很随便，往往很曲折，甚至穿过别家堂屋。岩头村浚水街以西，中央街与菊花街之间，都有这样的情况。

苍坡村除了中央部分有东西向的横街外，多数的次街也是走向南北的，但它的住宅也都向南，因此，多数住宅就从侧面入门。为适应住宅的总宽度，两条次街之间的距离只有40米。但住宅的两侧仍有小小的余地。侧面入门之后，一般是自由空间，绕过厢房，才是住宅的前院。也有个别住宅比较宽，厢房贴邻次街，于是侧门就开在厢房前端的一间，以这一间为门厅。苍坡村有一条九间巷，一条三退巷，过去比较大的住宅大多集中在这两条巷子里。这两条巷子的名字就夸耀着它们的宅子的规模不同平常。但现在已经没有这样的大宅子了。

山坡上的村子，如水云村、埭头村、西岸村等，住宅基地狭窄，成院落的就少了，大多顺等高线延伸。为了满足宗法制大家庭的需要，有长达15开间的。例如水云村中心最高点的一座，咸丰年间造的，有"钦命兵部侍郎右都御史浙江巡抚部院罗□□"题的匾："登崇俊良"。房子主人是贡生陈福。道路也是顺等高线走的，这些住宅平行于道路，因此就把整个村子拉长了。它们前后的间距不得不压缩得很小，建筑密度因而很高。但是，只要稍有可能，这些村子里的住宅也还是争取成为院落式的。

在各种不同地形的村子里，住宅有朝东、朝南和朝北的，但没有朝西的。楠溪江流域夏季炎热，朝西难耐酷晒。而且，风水术也以朝西为不吉。例如《阳宅会要·论福元》里说："宜住坐北向南宅，上上吉。坐南向北宅，上吉。坐西向东宅，亦吉。惟坐东向西宅不宜居。"

楠溪江的建筑虽然大多保留自己形象的独立完整，表现自己的个性特色，但都在统一的村落规划里，所以不大以个体来考虑风水。不过也有例外。廊下村前街的两幢"进士第"，一幢背对天马山的马头，一幢面对天马山的马鞍。据宋朝王洙等编的《地理新书》，"山如马鞍，主公卿宰辅"，所以有一幢朝向马鞍。而马的首、尾及足都主凶，所以另一幢就背向马头。

（2）楠溪江的住宅，以长条形的和三合院式的居多。三合院式的有一种变体：加一个不大的后院，平面呈"H"形。四合院和三进两院的大宅，各村几乎都有，但不多。各村落住宅的规模渐小，质量渐差，是因为明末清初以后，楠溪江经济文化的大衰退。也因为大家族制度逐渐式微。到了民国年间，才有一些在温州经商的人，回乡造了些砖门楼的质量比较好的住宅。

长条形的房子，除了在山坡地不得已而为之的以外，一般质量都比较低，五间或七间的小型住宅，显然是经济条件比较差的人家的。它们大多是四面板壁，用直棂窗，堂屋前檐完全敞开，没有装修。有楼层，但常常不设楼梯，而用竹爬梯上下。结构用料很粗糙，弯弯曲曲的原木，也比较细。它们不筑院墙，房前屋后种树栽竹。四个立面都不封闭，两面山墙有窗，偶然有门。虽是长条形的，但形体并不单调。这种小型住宅的构图最善于变化。只要平面上有一点变化，立面上就能引发出很大的变化来。

而且年代久了，总会有些增扩，多有厕、储物间和猪栏等小建筑物附加在左右，造成体形的层次和穿插。一般说来，它们比院落式的更活泼多变，更富有创造性。因为它们数量比较多，造成了整个村子开敞、明朗，体形活泼、绿化丰富的景观。

三合院的住宅是楠溪江中游最基本的形制，比较模式化。一正两厢，有楼层。正屋7~9开间，除去两厢所占，前院宽度大于三间、五间，而两厢不过三开间，所以前院很宽敞。院前不一定有墙，即使有的，院墙高度也低于厢房底层披檐檐口许多，总在墀头以下，大约略高于2米。院内的树木、竹子，甚至瓜、豆，都能越墙而出。更有特色的是，虽然有前院，正屋和两厢的山墙面和背面大都仍旧作木板壁，开门，开窗。所以，这种住宅的性格也还是开敞、明朗的。只有比较晚近一些的三合院，在温州市住宅建筑的影响下，后墙和山墙都用砖封护，显得封闭。这种房子各村都有一些。

三合院住宅的基本模式是在前院墙正中设院门，单开间，左右各有一楹小小的屋架，有前后檐柱和山柱。两棵山柱间立门扉一对。屋顶为悬山式，有阴阳坡。讲究一些有简单的斗拱，也有在山柱向前作四跳丁头拱的。还有常见的一种，没有前檐柱，由山柱向前作丁头拱两跳，承托檐檩和挑檐檩，而后坡作半楹小小的梁架。木门楼虽小，造形推敲却很精致，深入到细节。它们很优美典雅，舒展大方，不但使住宅生辉，也使街巷添色。晚近的一些三合院，使用砖砌封护墙的，院门也都改成砖的了。它们远远没有木门楼那样轻快、明朗，富有空间层次和结构美。

但它们有雕塑性，檐下装饰化了的仿木构和屋脊两端空灵飞扬的花饰，与下部质重的墙垛相对比，产生很强的上升运动感。木的和砖的门楼，都比院墙高，有一些还在两侧作八字墙，形象很完整、突出。

楠溪江住宅的一个重要特点是院落宽敞，充满了阳光。地面满铺块石，晴天可以曝晒庄稼粮食，雨天排水通畅，与浙中、浙西、皖南、赣北，甚至云南、四川的传统民居里那种狭小、潮湿、阴暗的天井大异其趣。正是这些宽敞的院落，不高的院墙，和四面都有的门窗一起，使楠溪江的居住建筑摆脱了封闭性而异常开畅。

从这个明亮开阔的前院到住宅室内，有一个过渡性的空间，就是从阶条石算起宽达2米多的檐廊。多数住宅，连两厢也有檐廊。当地气候温和，这个半露天的檐廊是日常生活主要的场所。家务、手工业、休息、读书、儿童嬉戏，都在廊下，有些人家在这里进餐。夏天纳凉，冬天负暄，廊下都是好地方。

住宅所有的房间里，正屋明间是第一重要的。这是堂屋，当地人叫"上间"。它的面阔一般在4.5米左右，有的达到6米以上。浙中、浙西的天井式四合院，堂屋前檐没有装修，空间与天井直接融合。而楠溪江住宅的堂屋，却绝大多数有前檐装修，用三对双扇门。

堂屋的主要功能是礼仪性的，在它的后部，就是后明柱的位置上，设一道太师壁，壁前置条几。条几上陈设着插屏、掸瓶之类的东西。有些人家，在太师壁上部，悬挂着一个精工细做的架子，供奉家庭近祖的神主。相应的，条几上就会有香炉，天天焚烧高香。正是为了突出堂屋的尊贵地位，各类住宅，包括简陋的长条形的，都对称布局，把堂屋放在中央。

据《鲁班经》说，住宅的大门，前后四棵檐柱，加上左右中柱间横一道门，形如"日"字。正房堂屋比较宽，加上太师壁，形如"曰"字。它们在一起就组成一个"昌"字。所以《鲁班经》力主堂屋要宽。

太师壁两侧有门，通向后面小半间，通常把主要的楼梯造在这里。凡比较老式的住宅，后檐墙都是板壁，直棂窗，少数有后檐廊，面对一个宽度为通面阔的空地，种些树木，搭一些厕之类的小屋。新式一点的，山墙和后檐墙都用砖墙封护的，则大多设后天井，两侧各有一间房间，平面就成了"H"形，也有三面设檐廊的。无论哪一种情况，堂屋太师壁后面的小半间，都是家庭日常起居的重要地方，常常用作餐厅。

前院的厢房，大多三开间，也以中央一间为堂屋，同样有太师壁，不过比较简单。

堂屋是宗法制家庭的象征。这里有作为家庭凝聚力中心的祖先神主。年时节下，生辰忌日，这里都要设祭行礼。婚丧大典，都在这里举行。平素有贵客临门，也在这里接待。这里也是对子弟进行庭训的场所。这是家庭的礼制中心、教化中心。当地习俗，凡兄弟析产分居，新屋必须有堂屋，才能算成立了新家。所以营新居的，都重视这间堂屋。

无论正屋还是两厢，房子的进深都很大，一般为8~10米，通常都用板壁分为前后间，这是后檐也有门窗的原因。次间的卧室是最重要的，住长辈，由堂屋侧面出入。卧室光线很暗，地面潮湿，白昼很少有人愿意枯坐在里面。

厨房往往设在正屋的尽间，由前檐廊的一端进去。在"H"形的住宅里，它更常常设在后天井的一侧。厨房是住宅最忙碌的角落，维持着整个住宅的生气。它占一间，面积比较宽松。因为用柴火灶，灶台很大，一般两个火口，烧火人坐的长木凳后面就是柴堆。厨房近旁必定有一间仓房堆放柴禾。水缸、粮柜、碗橱、餐桌、咸菜坛等等是必备的。秋收之后，年关之前，还在厨房里酿酒、春年糕。有些人家，厨房里有大酒缸，有年糕缸。为了防鼠，鱼、肉、菜看等等常用竹篮子挂在梁上。

柴灶的烟囱用砖砌，并不伸出屋顶，也不伸出墙外，而是拔起2米多高之后，转折抵住外墙，在墙上开一个洞口就成了。在巷子里看，砖墙上一个个洞口，袅袅地冒出青烟，在墙面上熏一片黑。不做高烟囱，是为了省柴。厨房有一个门通向小巷，另一个门开向后院。梢间的前后卧室往往在厨房开门而不在前后檐开门。灶上有灶神像的小龛，都在烟囱的里侧，像下设香炉和烛台。灶神是家庭的保护神，待人宽厚。所以虽然大小事情都要向他报告，神龛却很简陋。

三合院或它的变体"H"形住宅在建造的时候或许是为一个大家庭用的，但一两代之后，家庭人口多了，就要析产分炊，于是就会增添厨房。所以一所旧宅，往往是两厢各有厨房，正屋有一两个厨房。一个厨房代表一个家庭。厨房与堂屋一样，是家庭的象征，一个是经济性的，一个是礼制性的。

楼上的房间乱堆杂物，并不住人，也没有什么重要用处，楼板薄而简陋，上面露瓦。局部楼板加固，存放粮囤。运粮上楼的办法，有一种是在明间或一个次间里，楼板开个洞口，正对洞口的檩条上装一副滑轮。

四合院或者三进两院的住宅，基本原则与三合院一致。四合院增加了"倒轩"，就是第一进门屋。门屋的明间为门厅，一定比堂屋窄，二者仍然形成"昌"字。三进两院是把四合院在轴线上重复一次。

在芙蓉村，有两幢形制特殊的大型住宅。一幢是造在康熙年间的"大屋"，也有人叫它"司马第"，在村子的西北角。它有三条平行的轴线，三座四合院并肩组合成一所房屋。连正屋带厢房，一共46个整间。总面宽大约70米左右。三所四合院各有自己的门，但院子间又可经过厢房前后的夹道连通。大屋后面有自己的水井，前面左侧有自己的三家塾，右侧有花园。正门之前有磨砖照壁，照壁和大屋在路的同一侧，所以在照壁左右各有一个小前院，从前院折向正门。从正门到阶前，还有大约18米的距离，在这段空地里划分了几个大院落。大屋的左侧，也就是北侧，曾经有水渠和池塘，现在已经干涸。

芙蓉村的北部还有一幢复合的四合院。中央的院子横向很长，前面对称开两个门，每个门一进来，先有自己小小的天井，实际上是个小四合院。由天井再进大院子。正屋左右又各有小天井，天井外侧还有些房间，这是一对"日月井"。

（3）三合院住宅，四合院和三进两院住宅，质量都比较好。前檐装修全用槅扇，少数用槛窗。槅扇的格心花色很多变化。最上面有一块绦环板，都有很精美的浮雕。槅扇前面是檐廊，檐廊的梁架，构件都做装饰性处理，与柱子外侧承托挑檐檩的斜撑、牛腿或出翘一起，构成相当华丽的组合。有一些质量更好的，檐廊上做卷棚轩或井口轩。《正字通》释"轩"：

檐宇之末曰轩，取车象也。殿堂前檐特起，曲椽无中梁者，亦曰轩。

所说的正是檐廊上的轩。这种轩，尤其是"曲椽无中梁"的卷棚轩，装饰性很强。堂屋里，太师壁和它两侧的门额做重点装饰，是小木作的重要作品。供奉神主的架子，制作尤其精美。

楠溪江住宅的一个很特殊的做法，是明间檐柱之间没有枋子连系，在这里形成一个缺口。这个做法的理由，推测起来，大约是因为《鲁班经》里说，人站在堂屋的太师壁前向外望的时候，应该看到门口的上槛背后衬着天空，不应该看到柱子间的枋子、檐口或其他东西。否则就是"凶相"。这里根本不架设额枋，而檐口又高，就避免那样的"凶相"了。

结构上的另一个特点是，正屋檐柱连线与厢房檐柱连线相交的阴角，没有柱子，只把厢房的檐枋架在正屋的枋子上，而在相交的位置，倒吊下一个雕刻十分精致的圆柱形装饰构件。这个构件根据它的形状和雕饰而叫做"垂莲柱"、"花篮柱"、"冬瓜柱"等等。

结构上还有一个很别致的特点，就是正屋有挑檐檩，厢房没有，因此，为了在两端结束正屋的挑檐檩，从垂莲柱或花篮柱上按45°斜角出一段短短的挑梁，把挑檐檩端点架住。在这个位置上也有雕饰。

砖门楼的住宅有两处灰塑装饰，一前一后。一处就在砖门楼和它两侧的八字墙上。门楼的灰塑主要是檐下的隐出斗拱。有些砖门楼没有坡屋顶，中央做一片曲线花式的小山墙，两侧立灰塑的花盆万年青之类。八字墙的上部，有灰塑的方框，里面塑花卉或者人物故事。另一处在后天井正对堂屋的照壁上，这里往往是整幅的大型构图，主题性的，大多是散仙高士的故事，抒写乡村文人们的隐逸情愫和读书之乐。芙蓉村的"将军屋"，岩头村的"枕琴庐"，都还保存着这样的大幅灰塑画。

砖门楼的住宅还常用空砖花做装饰，大多用在前院墙上，尤其是门楼旁的八字墙上部。花坛村和埭头村的砖门楼和空砖花尤其变化繁多。因为花坛村是明代以后比较发达的村子，其他多数老村子在这时候已经渐趋没落。而埭头村则是建筑业的专业村，有大量木匠、泥水匠，多在温州谋生，他们为建设家乡大大施展才能，门楼、花墙，百花争艳，尽情抒发他们对生活的热爱。

同治年间，在蓬溪村造了一座住宅，叫"近云山舍"。主人谢文波是个邑庠生，在同治五年（1866年）到苏州遍访名第大宅，采绘建筑式样。回来后造了这所房子。它是座四合院，院落扁宽，被两道空花砖墙分隔为三部分。厢房前形成一个小院子，墙根设花坛。左边厢房名"听香斋"，由翁同龢书额。因为与鹤盛人为争柴山而成仇，山舍被烧毁，花墙只剩下左侧的一片。1939年，谢雪仙兄弟重建正屋的"倒轩"（按：即门屋倒座）。现在这爿花墙还在，它的精致，在楠溪江建筑里是少有的。

（4）住宅是楠溪江建筑风格的代表。楠溪江建筑的开朗亲切、活泼灵巧、朴素真实、纯净自然，都最鲜明地表现在住宅上。主要是住宅决定了楠溪江村落的面貌。

住宅的基本材料，蛮石和原木，直接从自然取来，保持自然的形态。它用轻灵的木构架，又不加掩饰地把构架展现出来。它以简便的方法造成屋面复杂的翘曲，柔和舒展。屋脊、檐口和山墙上屋面的侧缘，曲线流畅圆润。屋顶的前后出檐和左右出山，都很宽阔，以显露在白粉壁上的轻盈细巧的木构架承托，更显得飘然如鸟似翠。山墙是住宅最优美的部分。两层的房子都有腰檐，它们在山墙上挑出薄薄的一片斜面，或者转折过来，而在转角上断开，形成一个小小的山尖，穿插很富机智。

为支承很宽的披檐，山墙上偶或用一排细长的斜撑，它们增加了山墙前空间的层次和形式的变化，也把重力的负荷和传递表现得轻松自如。深暗色的木构件，带着天生的弯曲和裂纹，在粉墙上画出方格图案，几个恰到好处的窗子，使图案更加生动有神。山墙面上，建筑材料的种类也最多，因此色彩、质感和形体的变化也最多，提供了不少组合的可能性。而使山墙的构图最后达到完美境地的，是它上面两坡屋顶的精致的曲线和它轻逸飘洒的上升动势。楠溪江的建筑匠师们深深知道山墙的美，常常利用它们充当重要的角色。

三合院的住宅，不但以厢房的两个山墙朝前，而且后面的两个角，每个都向后和向外侧面做山墙，侧面的一个是正屋的，后面的一个是厢房的。山墙上各种因素参差错落，有出有进，有正有斜，有直有曲，有明有暗，有硬有软，有粗有细，有虚有实，有黑有白，巧妙地组合搭配，十分丰富，却又出奇地朴素。这些山墙，造成了村落的轮廓线，起伏跳动，十分活泼，既表现了每幢住宅的独立品格，又取得了整个环境的统一和谐。

砖砌的封护山墙，五花的和弓背的都有，数量不多，但艺术水平很高。

三合院住宅的院墙不高；正屋和两厢的后檐墙大多是板壁，开直棂窗，有门，山墙开朗活泼。因此"冂"形住宅的性格比较说来也是开敞的，外向的。加上村子里许多的长条形小住宅，没有院墙，或者只有很低矮的院墙，街道的空间一直扩散到它们面前。所以街景也是开敞的，外向的，有时候简直是穿通的。住宅前后的树木竹丛，也都进入了这些村子的街景。苍坡、芙蓉、鹤垟、港头、周宅、渡头、霞美、坦下、埭头、溪南、塘湾等村子，都是绿荫处处，禽鸟和鸣。

住宅与村落环境的交融，最出色的例子是埭头村的"松风水月"住宅。这住宅并不大，不过是七开间的一长条。它在村子的后部，山坡上，前面一个8.0米宽的院子，院子外，地形下降1.5米左右。但堡坎的下面，竟是一个4.1米宽、28.1米长的水池。池外才是道路。从水池的一端上几步台阶，进一个砖门楼，再向右转，上台阶，才到院门。院门有互成直角的两片。迎着台阶，让人出入的门洞，与住宅垂直，这片真正的门形式比较简单。

另一片与住宅平行，比较高大，有脊，看来是门的正面，但门洞外却下临水池，不能出入，而在门洞装设美人靠（已残，也可能是花盆架）。倚着栏杆，望水池边妇女们浣洗衣服，笑语盈盈，情趣盎然。在"松风水月"宅，利用地形高差，造成住宅内外的空间交融和生活场景交融，而且，为了这交融，大门的形制突破了常规，大胆出新。埭头村是建筑匠人的专业村，匠师们确是身手不凡，富有创造性。

"松风水月"宅右前方有一座小宗祠。内部木构件的艺术加工水平很高。侧面封护山墙的轮廓很有弹性，很有生气。

楠溪江村落建筑环境里，景观的丰富多彩，在很大程度上取决于居住建筑形式的多样化，以及它们相互之间、它们与街道之间的关系的多样化。村落景观的清新，又在很大程度上取决于居住建筑形式的富有创造性，时时可以见到独出心裁的造形，使人惊喜。

居住建筑是最基本的乡土建筑。它的风格与官式建筑的对照最强烈。乡村里的礼制建筑和崇祀建筑，则介乎二者之间，并且因此而有分化，有些更偏向居住建筑，有些更偏向官式，表现出中间状态的不稳定性。

English

2. Current State of Residential Architecture

Existing residential architecture in Nanxi River villages is accumulation of several hundred years of history since Southern Song. Unfortunately资料 lacking, now impossible to determine construction ages one by one for historical study. Even, due to recent demolition of大量 traditional residences for new houses, statistical analysis of current state has no academic value.

(1) Although residences repeatedly altered, village planning structure basically unchanged, so residences and village overall大体 still maintain original relationship.

Middle basin villages, represented by Yantou, Cangpo and Furong, planning layouts very neat: one main street, several secondary streets perpendicular to main street, alleys between secondary streets. Most residences' main doors open on secondary streets. These three villages' secondary streets all run north-south.

Yantou and Furong secondary streets spacing about 50m, originally equivalent to depth of a three-courtyard two-hall mansion. But after repeated wars, existing residences mostly small-medium under 50m depth, can only enter from side through alleys, inside is front courtyard, main house still faces east.

Some have dedicated branch alleys from小巷, main door opens in branch alley, e.g. Furong village's "General House," Yantou village's "Pillow-Lute Studio." More simple long-strip small residences, no enclosed courtyard, fully open, enter from alleys via pedestrian paths.

Yantou and Furong both have many residences built on mansion ruins, very disorderly: some built on original wing platforms face north/south, some built on main house platforms face east. These residences' entry paths casual, often winding, even through another's main hall.

Yantou village west of Junshui Street, between Zhongyang and Juhua streets, all have such situations.

Cangpo village besides central east-west cross street, most secondary streets also north-south, but its residences also face south, therefore most residences enter from side. To accommodate residence total width, distance between two secondary streets only 40m. But residence两侧 still small余地.

After side entry, generally free space, around wing room, then residence front courtyard. Some individual residences wider, wing adjacent to secondary street, so side door opens in front bay of wing, using this bay as entry hall.

Cangpo village has a Nine-Bay Alley and a Three-Retreat Alley; past larger residences mostly concentrated in these two alleys. Their names boast of unusual mansion scale. But now no such mansions remain.

Hillside villages, such as Shuiyun, Daitou, Xi'an villages etc., residential sites narrow, courtyard-type fewer, mostly extend along contour lines. To satisfy patriarchal clan large family needs, some up to 15 bays long. E.g.

one at Shuiyun village center highest point, built in Xianfeng era, has plaque inscribed by "Imperially appointed Minister of War Right Censor-in-Chief Zhejiang Governor Luo □□": "Promote Outstanding Talents." House owner was gongsheng Chen Fu. Roads also follow contour lines; these residences parallel to roads, thus stretching entire village.

Their front-back spacing不得不 compressed very small, building density therefore high. But whenever slightly possible, these villages' residences still strive to become courtyard-type.

In villages of various terrains, residences face east, south and north, but none face west. Nanxi River basin hot in summer, west-facing unbearable scorching. Moreover, fengshui also considers west-facing inauspicious. E.g. "Yangzhai Huiyao · On Fortune Origin" says: "Suitable to live north-facing-south residence, top-top auspicious.

South-facing-north residence, top auspicious. West-facing-east residence, also auspicious. Only east-facing-west residence not suitable to live."

Nanxi River architecture although mostly retains independent complete image, expressing individual character, but all within unified village planning, so rarely considers fengshui by individual. However exceptions exist.

Langxia village front street two "jinshi residences": one backs Tianma Mountain's horse head, one faces Tianma Mountain's horse saddle. According to Song dynasty Wang Zhu etc. compiled "Geography New Book," "mountain like horse saddle, lords ministers and officials," so one faces saddle.

Horse's head, tail and feet all inauspicious, so other backs horse head.

(2) Nanxi River residences, mostly long-strip and three-courtyard (sanheyuan) type. Three-courtyard type has a variant: add a small back courtyard, plane呈 "H" shape. Four-courtyard (siheyuan) and three-courtyard-two-hall mansions, almost every village has some, but not many.

village residences' scale gradually smaller, quality gradually worse, because after late Ming-early Qing Nanxi River economic culture great decline. Also because large family system gradually declined. By Republican era, some people doing business in Wenzhou returned home built some brick-gate relatively good quality residences.

Long-strip houses, besides on hillside不得已, generally quality lower; five-bay or seven-bay small residences, obviously families with poorer economic conditions. They mostly four-sided plank walls, straight-lattice windows, main hall front eave completely open, no finishes. Have upper floor, but often no staircase, use bamboo ladder.

Structural materials rough, crooked logs, also thinner. They don't build courtyard walls, plant trees and bamboo front/back. All four facades not enclosed, both gable walls have windows, occasionally doors. Although long-strip, form not monotonous. This small residence composition best at variation.

As long as plane has slight change, facade can trigger great change. And over years,总会 some expansions, mostly toilets, storage rooms and pigpens small buildings attached left/right, creating form layers and interpenetration. Generally, they more lively and varied than courtyard-type, more creative.

Because they relatively numerous, create entire village open, bright, lively form, rich greenery landscape.

Three-courtyard residence is Middle Nanxi River most basic form, relatively standardized. One main house two wings, with upper floor. Main house 7-9 bays; excluding two wings, front courtyard width greater than three/five bays, while wings no more than three bays, so front courtyard very spacious.

Front of courtyard not necessarily walled; even if walled, courtyard wall height much lower than wing ground-floor lean-to eave, always below gable head, about slightly over 2m. courtyard trees, bamboo, even melons/beans, can overtop wall.

More distinctive: although has front courtyard, main house and two wings' gable walls and backs mostly still plank walls, with doors and windows. Therefore this residence's character also open and bright.

Only relatively late three-courtyard residences, under Wenzhou city residential architecture influence, back and gable walls brick-sealed, appear enclosed. Such houses found in every village some.

Three-courtyard residence basic model: set gate in center of front courtyard wall, single bay, left/right each a tiny roof frame with front/rear eave columns and gable columns. Between two gable columns stand a pair of door leaves. roof is overhanging gable (xuanshan) type, with yin-yang slopes.

Refined ones have simple dougong, also some with four-tier dingtou-gong from gable column forward. Another common type: no front eave column, from gable column forward two-tier dingtou-gong supporting eave purlin and overhanging eave purlin, while rear slope makes a tiny half-bay beam frame.

Wooden gate tower although small, form refinement exquisite, down to details. They elegant and graceful, relaxed and generous, not only brighten residence but also add color to streets. Late three-courtyard residences using brick sealed walls, gates also changed to brick.

They far less light/bright than wooden gate towers, less spatial layering and structural beauty. But they have sculptural quality: decorated imitation timber under eaves and ethereal flying ornaments at ridge ends, contrasting with heavy lower wall piers, producing strong upward motion.

Wooden and brick gate towers both higher than courtyard wall; some also make splayed walls on both sides, image complete and prominent.

An important feature of Nanxi River residences: spacious courtyards full of sunlight. Ground fully paved with block stones; sunny days can dry crops/grain, rainy days drainage通畅 — very different from narrow, damp, dark patios in traditional residences of central Zhejiang, western Zhejiang, southern Anhui, northern Jiangxi, even Yunnan and Sichuan.

Precisely these spacious courtyards, low courtyard walls, and doors/windows on all four sides together make Nanxi River residential architecture摆脱 enclosure and exceptionally open.

From this bright open front courtyard to residence interior, there is transitional space: eave porch over 2m wide counting from step stones. Most residences, even two wings, have eave porches. Local climate mild; this semi-outdoor eave porch is main place for daily life.

Housework, handicrafts, rest, reading, children playing — all under porch; some families eat here. Summer enjoying cool, winter basking in sun — porch always good place.

Among all residence rooms, main house central bay is most important. This is main hall (tangwu), locals call "upper room." Its width generally about 4.5m, some over 6m. Central Zhejiang/western Zhejiang patio-type four-courtyard residences, main hall front eave no finishes, space directly merges with patio.

But Nanxi River residence main halls, vast majority have front eave finishes, using three pairs of double doors.

Main hall's primary function is ritual; at its rear, i.e. rear central column position, set a taishi wall (grand-master wall), in front of wall set a long altar table. On table display standing screens, dusting vases etc. Some families, above taishi wall, hang an exquisitely made shelf, enshrining family recent ancestors' spirit tablets.

Correspondingly, on altar table there is incense burner, daily burning high incense. Precisely to突出 main hall's noble status, all residence types, including crude long-strip ones, symmetrically layout, placing main hall at center.

According to "Lu Ban Jing" (carpenter Classic), residence main gate: front/rear four eave columns, plus horizontal door between left/right central columns, forms "日" (sun) character. Main house hall relatively wide, plus taishi wall, forms "曰" (say) character. Together they form "昌" (prosperous) character. Therefore "Lu Ban Jing" strongly advocates main hall should be wide.

taishi wall both sides have doors, leading to rear small half-room; usually main staircase built here. Older residences, rear eave walls all plank walls, straight-lattice windows,少数 have rear eave porch, facing a space of full facade width, planting trees, building small huts like toilets.

Newer ones, gable and rear eave walls brick-sealed, mostly set rear patio, both sides each a room, plane becomes "H" shape, also some with eave porches on three sides. In any case, small half-room behind main hall taishi wall is important place for family daily living, often used as dining room.

Front courtyard wings, mostly three bays, also central bay as main hall, similarly with taishi wall, but simpler.

Main hall is symbol of patriarchal clan family. Here has ancestor spirit tablets as family cohesion center. festivals, birthdays/death anniversaries, here all hold sacrifices and rituals. Weddings/funerals grand ceremonies, all held here. Normally distinguished guests arrive, also received here. Here also place for instructing children.

This is family's ritual center, moral education center. Local custom: when brothers divide property and live separately, new house must have main hall to count as established new family. Therefore those building new residences all value this main hall.

Whether main house or two wings, house depth very large, generally 8-10m, usually divided by plank walls into front/back rooms — this is why rear eave also has doors/windows. Secondary bay bedrooms most important, for elders, entered from main hall side. Bedrooms very dark, floors damp, daytime few people willing to sit idly inside.

Kitchen often set in main house end bay, entered from one end of front eave porch. In "H"-shape residences, more often set on one side of rear patio. Kitchen is residence's busiest corner, maintaining entire residence's vitality. It occupies one bay, area relatively spacious.

Because using firewood stove, stove very large, generally two fire mouths; behind long wooden bench where fire-tender sits is firewood pile. Near kitchen必定 a storage room for firewood. Water vat, grain cabinet, dish cupboard, dining table, pickle jars etc. essential.

After autumn harvest, before New Year, also brew wine, pound New Year cakes in kitchen. Some families have large wine jars, New Year cake jars in kitchen. To prevent rats, fish, meat, vegetables etc. often hung in bamboo baskets on beams.

Firewood stove chimney built of brick, does not extend above roof or outside wall, but rises over 2m then turns to abut exterior wall, opening a hole in wall. Looking in alley, brick wall one hole after another, curling blue smoke, blackening wall surface. Not making high chimney is to save firewood.

Kitchen has one door to alley, another door to rear courtyard. End bay front/back bedrooms often open door to kitchen rather than front/rear eaves. On stove small niche for Kitchen God image, all inside chimney side, below niche incense burner and candle holders. Kitchen God is family's protective deity, generous to people.

So although all matters must report to him, shrine very simple. Three-courtyard or its variant "H"-shape residences when built perhaps for one large family, but after one or two generations, family population grows, must divide property and cook separately, thus add kitchens.

So an old residence often has kitchens in both wings, one or two in main house. One kitchen represents one family. Kitchen like main hall is family symbol — one economic, one ritual.

Upper floor rooms clutter with杂物, not lived in, no important use; floorboards thin and crude, above exposed tiles. Local floorboards reinforced, storing grain bins. Method of lifting grain upstairs: one type in central bay or a secondary bay, floor opens a hole, on purlin directly above hole installs a pulley.

Four-courtyard or three-courtyard-two-hall residences, basic principles consistent with three-courtyard. Four-courtyard adds "daoxuan" (reverse porch), i.e. first bay gate house. Gate house central bay is entry hall, must be narrower than main hall; both still form "昌" character. Three-courtyard-two-hall repeats four-courtyard once on axis.

In Furong village, two large residences of special form. One built in Kangxi era called "Great House," also called "Sima Di," at village northwest corner. It has three parallel axes, three four-courtyard residences side by side combined into one house. Including main house and wings, total 46 full bays. Total facade width about 70m.

Three four-courtyard residences each own gate, but courtyards can connect via passages before/behind wings. Great House behind has own well; front left has own family school, right has garden.

Before main gate there is brick-screen wall; screen wall and Great House on same side of road, so left/right of screen wall each a small front courtyard, from front courtyard turn to main gate. From main gate to steps, still about 18m distance; in this space divided into several large courtyards. Great House left side, i.e.

north side, once had canal and pond, now dried up.

Furong village north also has a compound four-courtyard residence. Central courtyard横向 very long, front symmetrically opens two gates; each gate entering first has own small patio, actually a small four-courtyard. From patio then enter large courtyard. Main house left/right each small patio, outside patios some rooms — this is a pair of "sun-moon wells."

(3) Three-courtyard, four-courtyard and three-courtyard-two-hall residences, quality all relatively good. Front eave finishes all use lattice doors (geshan),少数 use sill windows. lattice door grid patterns many variations. Topmost has a taohuan board, all with exquisite relief.

In front of lattice doors is eave porch; eave porch beam frames, components all decoratively treated, together with diagonal braces, corbels or upturns outside columns supporting overhanging eave purlin, form quite ornate combination. Some better quality, eave porch makes rolling-roof pavilion (juanpeng xuan) or well-ceiling pavilion (jingkou xuan).

"Zhengzi Tong" (Correct Character Dictionary) explains "xuan":

End of eave called xuan, taking carriage image. Hall front eave specially raised, curved rafters no middle beam, also called xuan.

What described is precisely eave porch xuan. This xuan, especially "curved rafters no middle beam" rolling-roof xuan, highly decorative. In main hall, taishi wall and its两侧 door lintels重点 decorated, important works of small carpentry. Shelf enshrining spirit tablets,制作 especially exquisite.

A very special practice in Nanxi River residences: between central bay eave columns no lintel connecting, forming a gap here.

Reason for this practice,推测, approximately because "Lu Ban Jing" says: when person stands before main hall taishi wall looking outward, should see gate's upper threshold backed by sky, should not see lintels between columns, eave or other things.

Otherwise is "inauspicious appearance." Here simply no header beam erected, and eave high, thus avoiding such "inauspicious appearance."

Another structural feature: at inside corner where main house eave column line intersects wing eave column line, no column; only wing's eave lintel rests on main house's lintel, and at intersection position, hangs down an exquisitely carved cylindrical decorative component.

This component according to shape and carving called "drooping lotus column," "flower basket column," "winter melon column" etc.

Another very别致 structural feature: main house has overhanging eave purlin, wings do not; therefore, to terminate main house's overhanging eave purlin at both ends, from drooping lotus column or flower basket column at 45° angle extends a short bracket beam, supporting overhanging eave purlin endpoint. At this position also carvings.

Brick-gate residences have two places of gray plaster decoration, one front one back. One at brick gate tower and its两侧 splayed walls. gate tower gray plaster mainly hidden dougong under eaves. Some brick gate towers no pitched roof, center makes a curved decorative small gable, both sides stand gray plaster flowerpots with evergreen etc.

splayed wall upper part has gray plaster square frames, inside塑 flowers or human stories. Other at rear patio screen wall facing main hall; here often entire large composition, thematic, mostly stories of wandering immortals and lofty scholars, expressing rural literati's reclusive sentiments and joy of reading.

Furong village's "General House," Yantou village's "Pillow-Lute Studio," still preserve such large gray plaster paintings.

Brick-gate residences also commonly use open brick lattice decoration, mostly on front courtyard walls, especially upper part of splayed walls beside gate. Huatan and Daitou villages' brick gate towers and open brick lattice especially varied.

Because Huatan village was relatively developed village after Ming, most other old villages by then already declining.

Daitou village则 is professional village of construction industry, has大量 carpenters and masons, many making living in Wenzhou; they greatly施展 talents building hometown — gate towers, flower walls, hundred flowers blooming, fully expressing their love of life.

Tongzhi era, in Pengxi village built a residence called "Jinyun Mountain Cottage" (Near-Cloud Mountain Cottage). Owner Xie Wenbo was county student; in Tongzhi fifth year (1866) traveled to Suzhou visiting famous mansions, recording architectural patterns. Returned built this house.

It is four-courtyard, courtyard扁 wide, divided by two open-brick lattice walls into three parts. Before wings forms a small courtyard, wall base sets flower beds. Left wing named "Listening-to-Fragrance Studio," inscribed by Weng Tonghe. Because feud with Hesheng people over firewood mountain, cottage burned, flower wall only left side one片.

1939, Xie Xuexian brothers rebuilt main house's "daoxuan" (i.e. gate house reverse seat). Now this flower wall still exists; its refinement rare in Nanxi River architecture.

(4) residence is representative of Nanxi River architectural style. Nanxi River architecture's openness and intimacy, liveliness and dexterity, simplicity and authenticity, purity and naturalness — all most clearly expressed in residences. Mainly residences determine Nanxi River villages' appearance.

residence basic materials, rough stone and raw logs, directly taken from nature, maintaining natural forms. It uses light timber frame, and unreservedly reveals frame. It uses simple methods to create complex roof curvature, soft and舒展. Ridge, eave and gable roof side edges, curves smooth and rounded.

roof front/rear overhang and left/right gable projection, all very wide, supported by light delicate timber frame exposed on white plaster walls, appearing even more floating like birds. gable wall is residence's most beautiful part.

Two-story houses all have waist eaves; they project thin sloped surface on gable wall, or turn over, and break at corner, forming small gable peak, interpenetration very witty.

To support wide lean-to eaves, gable walls occasionally use a row of slender diagonal braces; they increase spatial layering and form variation before gable wall, and also express gravity load and transmission轻松自如.

Dark timber components, with natural curves and cracks, draw grid patterns on plaster walls; several well-placed windows make pattern more vivid. On gable wall surface, building material types most varied, therefore color, texture and form variation most, providing many combination possibilities.

And what makes gable wall composition finally reach perfection, is its two-slope roof's exquisite curve and its ethereal upward momentum. Nanxi River building craftsmen deeply know gable wall beauty, often use them as important roles.

Three-courtyard residences, not only use two wing gable walls facing front, but also rear two corners each make gable wall backward and outward-side — side one is main house's, rear one is wing's.

On gable walls various factors错落, protruding/receding, straight/curved, light/dark, hard/soft, coarse/fine, void/solid, black/white, cleverly combined, very rich yet surprisingly plain.

These gable walls create village silhouette, undulating and lively, both expressing each residence's independent character and achieving entire environment's unified harmony.

Brick sealed gable walls, five-petal and arch-back types both, not many, but artistic level high.

Three-courtyard residence courtyard walls not high; main house and two wings' rear eave walls mostly plank walls, straight-lattice windows, with doors, gable walls open and lively. Therefore "冂"-shape residence character relatively open and outward.

Plus village's many long-strip small residences, no courtyard wall, or only very low courtyard wall, street space spreads directly to their fronts. So streetscape also open, outward, sometimes简直 penetrative. Trees and bamboo before/behind residences also enter these villages' streetscapes.

Cangpo, Furong, Heyang, Gangtou, Zhouzhai, Dutou, Xiamei, Tanxia, Daitou, Xinan, Tangwan etc. villages — all green shade everywhere, birds singing.

residence and village environment blending, most outstanding example is Daitou village's "Pine Wind Water Moon" residence. This residence not large, just a seven-bay long strip. It at village rear, on hillside; front an 8.0m wide courtyard; outside courtyard, terrain drops about 1.5m. But below retaining wall, actually a 4.1m wide, 28.1m long pool.

Outside pool is road. From one end of pool up several steps, enter a brick gate tower, then turn right, up steps, then reach courtyard gate. courtyard gate has two panels at right angles. Facing steps, entry doorway for people, perpendicular to residence; this real door form relatively simple.

Other panel parallel to residence, taller, with ridge, looks like door front, but outside doorway descends to pool, cannot enter; instead in doorway installs beauty-rails (damaged, possibly flower pot stand). Leaning on rail, watching pool-side women washing clothes, laughter abundant,情趣盎然.

In "Pine Wind Water Moon" residence, using terrain height difference, creates residence interior-exterior spatial blending and life-scene blending; and for this blending, main gate form breaks convention, boldly innovative. Daitou village is professional village of building craftsmen; craftsmen indeed skillful and creative.

"Pine Wind Water Moon" residence right front has a small ancestral hall. Interior timber components artistic processing level very high. Side sealed gable wall outline very elastic, very lively.

In Nanxi River village architectural environment, landscape richness depends largely on residential architecture form diversification, and their mutual relationships and relationships with streets diversification. village landscape freshness depends largely on residential architecture form creativity; constantly can see ingenious forms, surprising.

Residential architecture is most basic vernacular architecture. Its style contrasts most strongly with official-style architecture. village ritual buildings and worship buildings stand between the two, and therefore differentiate: some lean more toward residential, some lean more toward official, expressing intermediate state instability.
