宗祠的作用和地位
中文

八、礼制建筑

礼制建筑，主要的是宗祠。宗祠是血缘村落里最高品位的公共建筑，它表征着宗族或者宗族之下房派的经济、社会和政治地位，炫耀它的过去，企求它的未来，所以它的建筑受到普遍的关注。由于重要，也由于年时节下祭祀祖先或在演出戏剧的时候，众人聚集，所以，宗祠在村落的布局中也起结构性作用。

1. 宗祠的作用和地位

在封建宗法制度下的村落里，宗祠是宗族的象征，团结整个宗族、维护人伦秩序。塘湾村郑氏大宗祠里有一副楹联，写的是：

萃子孙于一堂，序昭序穆；

享祖宗以万稷，报德报功。

这是对宗祠作用的简要概括。

宗祠的首要用途是供奉神主，"妥祖宗之先灵"，并且按时举行各种祭祀。供奉着祖先的神主，宗祠对宗族就有了神圣的意义，因此，常常被用来作为聚会厅、议事厅和法庭，讨论和处理有关全宗族的大事。某些意义重大的物品，如圣旨、祭器、祖先像、族谱等等，通常也保存在宗祠里。大宗祠和一部分小宗祠附设戏台，寓教化于娱乐，也有一种文化建筑的功能。宗祠也可以作为地方官吏的临时公馆，如《渠川叶氏宗谱·合同议据》记载：

宗祠未毁之时，从前各宪按临，或为国课，或为公务，凡驾至溪都（按：即为渠口村）必于此公署。……会倾圮已久，倘遇宪驾临溪，无从憩息，或淋雨而过他都，戴月而临别地，……鞅掌风尘，停骖无地，为民固有所不安，为子孙亦有所不肖。（康熙二十一年，1682年）

总之，宗祠是血缘村落中最重要的多用途公共建筑，其中大宗祠在村子里必是最宏大、最精致的建筑。《珍川朱氏合族副谱》里，有一篇《如在堂记》说："为子孙者，睹规制之伟宏、则思祖德之宽远；见栋宇之巍焕，则思祖业之崇深。岁时致享，敢不敬肃。"宗祠的建筑，就是一份宗族制度的教材。

宗祠的意义如此重大，连它们的命名都带有教化作用，如"孝思堂"、"敦睦堂"、"崇本堂"之类。明代，花坛村朱进轩主持建造一座宗祠，《珍川朱氏合族副谱·桂馥堂记》说到它的命名：

意者欲使后嗣子孙，顾名思义，上体父祖培植之勤，下笃子孙思绳之念，将书香振起。承俎豆者，玉树芳兰，翰墨生辉；答烝尝者，金蝉紫诰，科甲连登。家声不坠，簪缨继美，世泽常新矣！

因而名桂馥堂。一个堂名，寄托着对子孙的多少厚望！

English

VIII. Ritual Architecture

Ritual architecture, mainly ancestral halls. ancestral hall is highest-grade public building in kinship villages; it表征 clan or sub-branch's economic, social and political status, boasts of its past, seeks its future, so its architecture receives universal attention.

Because important, and because during festivals ancestral sacrifices or opera performances crowds gather, ancestral halls also play structural role in village layout.

1. Role and Status of ancestral halls

In villages under feudal patriarchal clan system, ancestral hall is symbol of clan, uniting entire clan, maintaining human ethical order. Tangwan village Zheng clan main ancestral hall has a couplet:

Gather descendants in one hall, ordering seniority and juniority;

Offer to ancestors for myriad years, repaying virtue and merit.

This is brief summary of ancestral hall's role.

ancestral hall's primary use is enshrining spirit tablets, "comforting ancestors' spirits," and holding various sacrifices on schedule. Enshrining ancestral spirit tablets, ancestral hall has sacred meaning to clan, therefore often used as assembly hall, council hall and courtroom, discussing and handling matters concerning entire clan.

Certain significant items, such as imperial edicts, sacrificial vessels, ancestor portraits, genealogy etc., usually also preserved in ancestral hall. Main ancestral halls and some smaller ancestral halls have attached stages, combining moral education with entertainment, also functioning as cultural buildings.

ancestral halls can also serve as temporary official residences for local officials, as "Quchuan Ye clan Genealogy · Contract Agreement" records:

Before ancestral hall destroyed, when various officials visited, either for national taxes or public affairs, whenever arriving at Xidu (i.e. Qukou village)必定 used this as official residence. ...

Since long collapsed, if official arrives at stream, no place to rest; perhaps passing through other district in rain, leaving for another place under moon ... busy with wind and dust, no place to stop horse — for people certainly uneasy, for descendants also unworthy. (Kangxi twenty-first year, 1682)

In short, ancestral hall is most important multi-purpose public building in kinship villages; among them main ancestral hall in village必定 is grandest and most refined building.

"Zhenchuan Zhu clan Combined Genealogy" has a "As-if-Present Hall Record" saying: "As descendants, seeing grand scale, then think of ancestors' broad virtue; seeing majestic buildings, then think of ancestors' profound enterprise. At seasonal offerings, dare not be reverent and solemn." ancestral hall architecture is a textbook of clan system.

ancestral hall's significance so great that even their naming has moral education function, such as "Filial-Piety Hall," "Harmony Hall," "Reverence-Root Hall" etc. Ming dynasty, Huatan village Zhu Jinxuan supervised construction of an ancestral hall; "Zhenchuan Zhu clan Combined Genealogy · Cassia-Fragrance Hall Record" says of its naming:

Intention is to make descendants, hearing name and thinking of meaning, above understand fathers/ancestors' diligent cultivation, below strengthen descendants' thought of continuing; will revive scholarly tradition.

Those inheriting sacrifices: jade trees and fragrant orchids, calligraphy shining; those answering offerings: gold cicadas and purple edicts, examination success consecutive. Family name not fallen, official succession beautiful, generation grace always new!

Thus named Cassia-Fragrance Hall. One hall name, entrusting how much hope for descendants!

本篇术语 Key Terms

礼制建筑 / Ritual architecture 宗祠 / Ancestral hall (Zongci) 大宗祠 / Main ancestral hall 小宗祠 / Branch ancestral hall 神主 / Spirit tablet (Shenzhu) 祭祀 / Sacrifice (Jisi) 昭穆 / Zhaomu (seniority order) 祭祖 / Ancestor worship 族谱 / Genealogy (Zupu) 祭器 / Sacrificial vessels 戏台 / Stage (Xitai) 孝思堂 / Filial-Piety Hall 敦睦堂 / Harmony Hall 崇本堂 / Reverence-Root Hall 桂馥堂 / Cassia-Fragrance Hall ...等23个

宗祠的建造
中文

2. 宗祠的建造

每个宗族都有一个大宗祠，奉祀本地本村的第一位"始迁祖"。随着子孙繁衍，一些有财力有地位的房派建造小宗祠，年长日久，宗祠越来越多。例如廊下村朱氏有宗祠18所，现存10所，芙蓉村陈氏有宗祠18所，现存12所，蓬溪村谢氏有12所宗祠。据《岩头金氏宗谱》所记，岩头村金氏的10所宗祠的建立缘起和经过是：

大宗祠为始祖伯二府君建也。府君肇基岩头，积功累仁，子孙昌炽，是为不祧鼻祖。暨二世、三世、四世、五世崇祀大宗祠。后惟游历仕宦及有大功于宗族者如端十九、端廿六、升四四、安廿四、安五十、谦三五诸府君配享其内，岁设春秋二祭，通族子孙皆与焉。东宗为英六府君建也。府君去古未远，规模草创，府君相地宜，谋乐利，开双圳以备旱潦，创族谱以明本支，特祠奉祀，不亦宜乎。五分宗祠为瑞廿六府君建也。府君为英六府君之孙，凡英六府君治谋之所未足者，府君能继志述事，以成全美，诚有功于祖宗者也，因特建祠以祀之。

老三房宗祠为升十五府君建也。府君怀仁抱义，济困扶危，有长发其祥之基，宜乎建祠以奉祀。二宅二房宗祠为升廿八府君建也。府君规模阔大，气量高深，有百年必世之思，宜乎建祠以奉祀。水亭祠升四四府君所手建，今即为府君之特祠也。府君怀远大之志，具亢宗之才，原创水亭为子孙课业计，自兹文学振兴，叨膺科第，至今胶序蝉联，绳绳不绝。尤能培风水，兴地利，置祭田，建公业。人之区画所不能至者，无钜无细，靡不一一经营，尽善尽美。由始迁岩头以来，列祖非无建造，而兴利之多，功德之盛，应推府君为第一。

府君殁，奉其主于水亭，因书塾而为堂，以伸其食报焉。六分祠为安廿四府君建也。府君孝行特著，遐迩慕之，且有盛德伟功，子孙咸蒙其福泽，于大宗祠右侧创祠奉祀，额之曰"顺应"，良不诬也。老二宅四分宗祠为恭十二府君建也。府君身承微弱，再传而椒口繁衍，瓜瓞绵延，非其积德累仁之功何以至于此，其食报也固宜。水亭五房三分宗祠为宽二百八二府君建也。府君襟怀旷达，磊落不凡，广施惠泽以济穷困，多置产业以遗子孙，迄今祀田盈野，祭仪丰隆，寝庙奕奕，子孙乐其乐而利其利焉。水亭四房宗祠为敏二百四二府君建也。

府君敦孝友，崇礼义，内外无间言，诗书继世，庠序蜚声，庙貌巍峨，亦崇德报功之意也。

大约子孙能扬名于世，有光奕叶，教子有成，获膺爵享，配享大宗祠。其馀克自振拔、不为凡庸与有功宗族者，俱得配享小宗，俾禋祀不朽。至于后嗣终斩，昭穆无绍，能捐产入祠助祭，亦得附跻本派之小宗。比矜怜孤独，曲全一本之义，皆不易之公论也。

由这篇记载可见，小宗的建立，多起于某房中有的杰出的人。又如蓬溪谢氏，祀大熙神主的二分祠，先于把他的父亲立勋太神主的三分祠而建，因为大熙太为本村争得屿山，立了功。既无子息，又无财产可捐的人，神主竟不得入宗祠。可见宗族内部，机会并不均等，而由财力和声名等决定。

宗祠的建造费用，或者来自祠下祭田的收入（按：祭田又称太公田），或者由士绅倡捐，也有按丁口摊派的，宗谱里大多有记载。因为建祠费用很大，所以宗祠往往由历代增扩而成最后的规模。如《渠川叶氏宗谱·重修叶氏大宗祠碑记》：

明弘治甲子（1504年），……肇建宗祠，敬宗收族。……祠仅一重，草创而已。本朝康熙癸亥（1683年）……重建，拓地二十馀弓，翼以两廊，奄有两重，规模略备。乾隆壬辰（1772年），……重建头门，前后历三重，宏敞高深，堂堂乎钜构矣！

由于屡屡改建、扩建，甚至迁址重建，记载又很简略，所以很难判定一所宗祠的建造年代。大致说来，楠溪江中游诸村现存的宗祠中，大宗祠多创建于明代。花坛村的朱氏大宗祠，据《珍川朱氏宗谱·桂芳堂记》说，初建于南宋淳祐己酉（1249年），"元末毁于兵火，所存仅前堂数楹，累世修葺之，未及前之雄壮。至我明景泰年间……因故址而重新之"。成化年间再扩大，清初毁于寇，康熙间重建。根据这样的记载，不能确定现有大宗祠是否还侥幸保存部分的宋构或明构。正殿里的柱子，至今还全存木础，但它们外观较新，而且直到20世纪初，楠溪江建筑仍有用木础的，所以不能依据它们来判断朱氏大宗祠正厅的建造年代。

English

2. construction of ancestral halls

Each clan has a main ancestral hall, enshrining the first "founding ancestor" of this locality/village. As descendants multiply, some financially capable and prestigious sub-branches build branch ancestral halls; over time, ancestral halls become more numerous. E.g.

Langxia village Zhu clan has 18 ancestral halls, 10 existing; Furong village Chen clan has 18, 12 existing; Pengxi village Xie clan has 12. According to "Yantou Jin clan Genealogy," the origins and history of Yantou village Jin clan's 10 ancestral halls are:

Main ancestral hall built for founding ancestor Bo-er Fujun. Sir established base at Yantou, accumulated merit and benevolence, descendants prosperous — is never-removed founding ancestor. Second, third, fourth, fifth generations all worshipped in main ancestral hall.

Later only those who traveled as officials or had great merits to clan such as Duan-19, Duan-26, Sheng-44, An-24, An-50, Qian-35 etc. share worship inside; annual spring and autumn sacrifices, all clan descendants participate. East clan built for Ying-liu Fujun.

Sir not far from ancient times, scale rudimentary; Sir assessed land suitability, sought benefits, opened double canals for drought/flood, created genealogy to clarify main branch — special hall for worship, isn't it appropriate? Fifth-branch ancestral hall built for Rui-26 Fujun.

Sir is grandson of Ying-liu Fujun; whatever Ying-liu Fujun's plans insufficient, Sir could continue will and accomplish affairs, completing perfection — truly meritorious to ancestors, therefore specially built hall to worship. Old-third-branch ancestral hall built for Sheng-15 Fujun.

Sir embraced benevolence and righteousness, relieved distress and helped danger, has foundation of long-lasting auspiciousness — appropriate to build hall for worship. Two-residence second-branch ancestral hall built for Sheng-28 Fujun. Sir broad scale, deep气量, has century-long thinking — appropriate to build hall for worship.

Water Pavilion Hall personally built by Sheng-44 Fujun, now is Sir's special hall. Sir had great ambition, talent to glorify clan; originally created Water Pavilion for descendants' study, from then literature revived, received examination degrees, to this day academic succession continuous.

Especially able to cultivate fengshui, develop land benefits, establish sacrificial fields, build public enterprises. Whatever human planning cannot reach, large or small, all managed one by one, perfect. Since founding move to Yantou, ancestors not without construction, but in number of benefits and abundance of merit, should rank Sir first.

After Sir died, enshrined his tablet in Water Pavilion, turning study into hall, to extend his reward. Sixth-branch hall built for An-24 Fujun.

Sir's filial conduct especially noted, admired far and near; moreover had great virtue and merit, descendants all receive his blessings; created hall right of main ancestral hall for worship, named "Shunying" (Obedient-Response) — truly not false. Old-two-residence fourth-branch ancestral hall built for Gong-12 Fujun.

Sir inherited weak position, after two generations descendants multiplied, vine continuous — if not his accumulated virtue and benevolence how could reach this, his reward固 appropriate. Water Pavilion fifth-branch third-sub-branch ancestral hall built for Kuan-282 Fujun.

Sir open-minded, outstanding and uncommon, broadly施 grace to relieve poverty, much property to leave descendants; to this day sacrificial fields fill fields, sacrificial rites abundant, temple majestic — descendants enjoy their joy and benefit. Water Pavilion fourth-branch ancestral hall built for Min-242 Fujun.

Sir practiced filial piety and brotherhood, esteemed rites and righteousness, inside-outside no discord, poetry-books succession, school reputation, temple towering — also meaning of praising virtue and repaying merit.

Generally descendants who can make name known in world, glorify generations, teach children successfully, receive rank and honor, share worship in main ancestral hall. Others who can self-elevate, not be mediocre, and have merits to clan, all may share worship in branch hall, making sacrifices immortal.

As for those whose lineage ultimately ends, no seniority succession, but can donate property to hall for sacrifices, also may be attached to own branch's hall. This compassion for lonely, upholding unity of one root — all unchanging public principles.

From this record visible: branch halls' establishment mostly arises from outstanding individuals in a branch. E.g. Pengxi Xie clan, the second-branch hall enshrining Daxi's spirit tablet, built before third-branch hall enshrining his father Lixun's spirit tablet — because Daxi contributed to village winning Yushan, made merit.

Those with no descendants and no property to donate, spirit tablets竟 cannot enter ancestral hall. Visible within clan, opportunities not equal, determined by financial power and reputation etc.

ancestral hall construction costs, either from income of sacrificial fields under hall (note: sacrificial fields also called Taigong fields), or initiated by gentry donations, also apportioned by population; genealogy mostly records. Because construction cost large, ancestral halls often expanded through generations to final scale. E.g.

"Quchuan Ye clan Genealogy · Reconstruction of Ye clan Main ancestral hall Stele Record":

Ming Hongzhi Jiazi (1504), ... founded ancestral hall, revering clan and gathering kin. ... Hall only one layer, rudimentary. This dynasty Kangxi Guihai (1683) ... rebuilt, expanded land over twenty bow, wings with two corridors, having two layers, scale roughly complete. Qianlong Renchen (1772), ... rebuilt gate, front-back through three layers, spacious and deep, truly grand structure!

Due to repeated reconstruction, expansion, even relocation rebuilding, records brief, so很难 determine a hall's construction age. Generally, among existing ancestral halls in Middle Nanxi River villages, main halls mostly founded in Ming.

Huatan village Zhu clan main hall, according to "Zhenchuan Zhu clan Genealogy · Cassia-Fragrance Hall Record," initially built in Southern Song Chunyou Jiyou (1249), "late Yuan destroyed by war, only front hall several bays remained, generations repaired, not reaching former grandeur. To our Ming Jingtai era ...

rebuilt on original site." Chenghua era expanded again; early Qing destroyed by bandits, Kangxi era rebuilt. From such records, cannot determine whether existing main hall still luckily preserves部分 Song or Ming structure.

Main hall columns, to this day all retain wooden bases, but their appearance relatively new; and until early 20th century Nanxi River architecture still used wooden bases, so cannot rely on them to judge Zhu clan main hall main hall construction age.

宗祠的选址
中文

3. 宗祠的选址

大宗祠的位置，就是村落的礼制中心，非常重要。

在芙蓉村、苍坡村、岩头村和花坛村，大宗祠在村子的主要入口处。在塘湾、珠岸、溪口、豫章等村，大宗祠在村子的中央。坦下村的例子独一无二，它的大宗祠在寨墙之外的一块台地上。

大宗祠在村口，好处是交通便捷，有大型聚会，尤其是演出戏剧、外村观众蜂拥而至的时候，不致过于影响村子内的安宁。

芙蓉村陈氏大宗祠初创于明代，在村子东门内如意街的北侧。街的南侧是一座高约50厘米，长宽各6米的平台，叫做"演乐台"或者"迎宾台"，是举行仪典的时候供乐队用的。笔直的如意街到了迎宾台北拓宽成一个不大的长方形村口广场，东门也因此略向北偏，没有正对如意街。陈氏大宗祠的形制虽然是典型的，但是它的主轴线走东西向，和如意街平行，因此要从大宗祠前院的南门、光宗门，进入前院，然后左转才面向大宗祠正门。

岩头村的金氏大宗祠是明嘉靖年间由乙丑（1565年）科进士霞峰公主持建造的，在名为"仁道门"的北门内，进士街的西侧。进士街走南北向，大宗祠的主轴线和它平行，大门朝南。这种关系与芙蓉村陈氏大宗祠和如意街的关系相同。祠前有一座为霞峰公造的"进士"牌楼，朝向街道而与大宗祠成直角。与大宗祠夹路相对的是清嘉庆戊辰（1808年）为世辉公夫人谢氏立的贞节牌坊。仁道门、大宗祠、贞节坊和进士牌楼组成了岩头村的礼制中心，在村口炫耀宗族的成就和荣誉。

花坛村的朱氏大宗祠在村子西端"溪山第一"门内，街的北侧，大门朝东，主轴线也和大街平行。街的南侧是一座小小的三官庙。大宗祠之东不远，一座很大的牌楼横街而立，可惜已经坍塌，只剩下石柱座了。和芙蓉、岩头一样，这个礼制中心是主街的起点。

苍坡的李氏大宗祠在村子的正门、南门之内的水池的北岸。它的主轴与南门进村的街相垂直，大门临街。这和岩头、芙蓉、花坛三村的大宗祠不同，但是，李氏大宗祠的主轴仍与苍坡村的主要街道笔街平行，并且因为与笔街一起朝向西面的笔架山，所以成了全村唯一的一所朝西的房子。这个情况提示，上述三村的大宗祠与主街平行，很可能也是因为风水的关系：主街与大宗祠，一起朝向某个地标或者某个方位，因而大宗祠以侧面临街。

大小宗祠的选址和朝向，确实要有堪舆风水的根据。《珍川朱氏合族副谱》里列举了花坛、廊下等几个村落里朱氏各房派的宗祠的建造经过，每个宗祠都有上好的风水。例如"明德堂"："相阴阳而度隰原，……枕积谷而面玉屏，把双峰而扼参水，飞凤翔其南，天马踞其北。奥图巩固，星峦环拱，诚斯土之壮观也。此真可以妥先灵乎？"又如"桂馥堂"："基地宏开，规模大起。度原则地跨双龙，陟巘而峰临五岳。屏山掩映于其前，浃水萦纡于其右，诚雄图也。斯诚可以妥先灵而足见仁人孝子之用心矣！

"再如"追远堂"："其地前则面谷之山，右则环珍带之水，相其阴阳，卜云其吉。盖种德者厚，故流泽孔长。"所以，《乐安朱氏合谱·凡例》中说："古迹备书，欲子孙爱惜，仿祖也，亦一族风水所系。"

年月久远，又经历剧烈的社会变动，现在村子里，故老耆旧们已经不能一一指陈大小宗祠的风水了。不过，照它们的分布看，似乎小宗祠也趋向于靠拢大宗祠。这大约是因为大宗祠的风水好，或者是因为有攀附心理。靠近大宗祠能沾一点光彩。在芙蓉村，现存的14座大小宗祠，一律朝东，这大约是与"前横腰带水，后枕纱帽岩"这个全村上上大吉的风水格局有关。蓬溪村的12座宗祠，则一律朝向东南，即巽方，那里有一座笔峰和一池墨沼，关系到科甲功名。

坦下村的陈氏大宗祠造在寨墙之外，偏于一侧。它所在的那块台地被村民们叫做"风水坍"。坦下村是宋末元初蒙古兵烧尽芙蓉村后，由村人进士陈虞之一起响应文天祥号召，并肩抗元的白泉村陈氏子侄们建立的，很可能，由于兵祸连连，寨墙造得早而大宗祠造得较迟，所以才有了风水坍在寨外的情况。

English

3. Site Selection of ancestral halls

Main ancestral hall's location is village's ritual center, very important.

In Furong, Cangpo, Yantou and Huatan villages, main ancestral hall at village's main entrance. In Tangwan, Zhuan, Xikou, Yuzhang etc. villages, main ancestral hall at village center. tanxia village's example unique: its main ancestral hall on a terrace outside village wall.

Main ancestral hall at village entrance, advantage is convenient transportation; when large gatherings, especially opera performances with outside village audiences flocking in, not overly affect village interior peace.

Furong village Chen clan main ancestral hall initially built in Ming, north of ruyi street inside village east gate. South side of street is a platform about 50cm high, 6m long and wide, called "Music Performance platform" or "Welcome-Guest platform," for band during ceremonies.

Straight ruyi street at north of Welcome-Guest platform widens into a small rectangular village entrance plaza; east gate therefore slightly north-offset, not directly facing ruyi street.

Chen clan main ancestral hall's form although typical, but its main axis runs east-west, parallel to ruyi street; therefore must enter front courtyard from south gate of front courtyard (Guangzong Gate), then turn left to face main ancestral hall main gate.

Yantou village Jin clan main ancestral hall built in Ming Jiajing era supervised by jinshi Xiafeng Gong (1565), inside north gate called "Rende Gate," west of jinshi Street. jinshi Street runs north-south; main ancestral hall main axis parallel to it, main gate faces south. This relationship same as Furong Chen clan main hall and ruyi street.

Before hall a "jinshi" paifang built for Xiafeng Gong, facing street and at right angle to main hall. Opposite main hall across street is chastity paifang erected in Qing Jiaqing Wuchen (1808) for Shihui Gong's wife Lady Xie.

Rende Gate, main hall, chastity paifang and jinshi paifang form Yantou village's ritual center, at village entrance boasting clan's achievements and honor.

Huatan village Zhu clan main ancestral hall at village west end inside "Xishan No.1" gate, north side of street, main gate faces east, main axis also parallel to main street. South side of street is a small Three-Officials temple.

Not far east of main hall, a large paifang stands across street, unfortunately collapsed, only stone column bases remain. Like Furong and Yantou, this ritual center is starting point of main street.

Cangpo Li clan main ancestral hall at north bank of pool inside village main gate (south gate). Its main axis perpendicular to street entering from south gate, main gate faces street.

This differs from Yantou, Furong, Huatan three villages' main halls; however, Li clan main hall's main axis still parallel to Cangpo village's main street Bijie (Brush Street), and because together with Brush Street faces west's Brush-Rack Mountain, becomes village's only west-facing house.

This situation suggests: above three villages' main halls parallel to main street, likely also due to fengshui: main street and main hall together face a landmark or direction, thus main hall side faces street.

Large and small ancestral halls' site selection and orientation, indeed require fengshui basis. "Zhenchuan Zhu clan Combined Genealogy" lists construction history of Zhu clan sub-branch ancestral halls in Huatan, Langxia etc. villages; each ancestral hall has excellent fengshui. E.g. "Mingde Hall": "Assess yin-yang and measure lowlands...

Pillow Jigu and face Yuping, hold Shuangfeng and control Can River, flying phoenix soars south, heavenly horse crouches north. Map solid, stars and peaks surround — truly this land's grand sight. This truly can comfort ancestral spirits!" Another "Guifu Hall": "Site grandly opened, scale greatly risen.

Measuring plain land spans double dragons; ascending peak faces five mountains. Screen mountain sets off before, Jia River winds right — truly grand plan.

This truly can comfort ancestral spirits and show benevolent people and filial sons' dedication!" Another "Zhuiyuan Hall": "Its land front faces valley mountain, right encircles precious-belt water; assess yin-yang, divination says auspicious.

Because virtue planted thick, thus grace flows long." Therefore, "Le'an Zhu clan Combined Genealogy · General Rules" says: "Ancient sites all recorded, wanting descendants cherish, imitate ancestors, also related to clan fengshui."

Years long past, and经历剧烈 social change; now in villages, elders can no longer point out large/small halls' fengshui one by one. However, from their distribution, seems small halls also tend to gather near main hall. This probably because main hall's fengshui good, or because of attachment psychology — near main hall can share some glory.

In Furong village, existing 14 large/small halls all face east; this probably related to village's top auspicious fengshui pattern "front crossed by belt water, back pillow official-hat rock." Pengxi village's 12 halls, all face southeast, i.e. xun position; there stands a brush peak and a pool of ink, related to examination success.

tanxia village Chen clan main ancestral hall built outside village wall, off to one side.

Its terrace called by villagers "fengshui Collapse." tanxia village established after late Song-early Yuan Mongol soldiers burned Furong village, by village jinshi Chen Yuzhi together responding to Wen Tianxiang's call, with anti-Yuan Baiquan village Chen clan nephews; likely due to continuous war disasters, village wall built early and main hall built later, thus才有 fengshui collapse outside wall situation.

宗祠的形制和用途
中文

4. 宗祠的形制和用途

作为礼制建筑，宗祠的形制和外形比较保守，比较定型，也比较封闭，外面用高高的砖墙包围起来。在楠溪江村落里，居住建筑大多外向开敞，而封闭的宗祠在村落里显得很特殊。宗祠和住宅在建筑风格上的对立，是雅言文化和民俗文化对立的表现之一。

楠溪江大多数的大宗祠和一部分小宗祠的形制是一个正厅、左右两庑和门屋。在门的内侧，面对正厅，常有一座戏台。戏台大多数是向前凸出于院落中的，少数就在门厅里，门屋的高度和进深因而加大。正厅与门屋一般是七开间，悬山式屋顶，两庑是三开间。采取这种形制的，至少有芙蓉陈氏大宗祠、苍坡李氏大宗祠、溪口叶氏大宗祠、塘湾郑氏大宗祠、豫章胡氏大宗祠、桐州蒋氏大宗祠、珠岸陈氏大宗祠和坦下陈氏大宗祠。廊下朱氏小宗祠也是这一类。芙蓉村陈氏大宗祠在这类宗祠中形制比较完备。

它朝东，正门前有院子，院中央开一方水池，叫相承池，左右各辟一院门，朝南的叫光宗门，朝北的叫耀祖门。相承池之东，对着宗祠大门，有一堵照壁。岩头的金氏大宗祠，形制有点逾越，在正厅前造了一对钟鼓楼。这种做法在全国都少见。钟鼓楼之间是一方水池，充满整个院子。

有些小宗祠只有正厅一座，没有两庑，正门只是随墙木门或砖门一道而已，更没有戏台了。这种形制的祠堂里最重要当推鹤垟村的谢氏宗祠叙伦堂，这祠堂里供奉着第一位山水诗人谢灵运的神位。在埭头村和塘湾村，都有这类祠堂而前面设半月形水池的。也有一些小宗祠，如蓬溪村的东宗祠和西宗祠，芙蓉村的二房祠和三星祠等，有正厅、两庑和门屋，没有戏台，因而形成一个方正的四合院。这是《鲁班经》上的宗祠形制。

正厅和两庑，前檐全部开敞，空间与院落融成一体。正厅的后檐墙前设神橱，神橱里供奉历代祖先神主牌位。神橱前沿有细木镂花的罩，精巧玲珑，涂朱描金，是宗祠里最华丽的部分。神橱前置长条的香案，雕镂精致，造形与神橱统一，成为艺术的整体。香案上陈设烛台和香炉，每当朔望和春秋祭日，烛光摇曳，炉烟缭绕，造成一种庄严肃穆、促人追思缅怀的气氛。

在正厅的次间，檐廊之内，有木构架架设钟、鼓。钟在左，鼓在右。有些宗祠，以板状的铜器代钟，外观像磬。

神橱的前上方，以明间后坡的檩子为依托，悬挂各种足以荣耀宗族的匾额，如"圣旨"、"进士"之类。芙蓉村的陈氏大宗祠里，挂着的一方"圣旨"匾，是元顺帝为抗元牺牲的芙蓉村进士陈虞之平反昭雪，并且褒扬旌封的。

正厅的所有柱子都挂着楹联，内容不外乎颂扬祖先的功业德行，夸耀宗族的历史光荣，用来激励子孙。例如鹤垟村谢氏宗祠叙伦堂有一副楹联是：

江左溯家声，溉水捷书，勋绩于今照史册；

瓯东绵世泽，池塘春草，诗才亘古重儒林。

上联说的是谢安、谢石、谢玄，下联说的是谢灵运。

溪口戴氏大宗祠的楹联之一是：

理学朱程昔承道统；溪山邹鲁今逞文明。

宗祠里常年有祭祀活动，这些祭祀的仪式各略有不同，都写入宗谱，子孙不可怠慢。《岩头金氏宗谱·家规》里规定："出入必告，朔望必举，时物必荐。四时祭祀各用仲月。祭物须洁，不在繁多。"《珍溪朱氏合族副谱》在《族范》里详细规定了宗祠祭祀的方式和规格：

元旦：本日五鼓，齐谒大宗祠，少长咸集。该值年子孙设香烛酒果于神位之前。行四拜礼，毕，族长、次长分尊卑以次坐定，令少者自下而上拜贺履端之庆。毕，则各诣小宗祠参拜。

春分：该值年先期洒扫祠宇，宰牲具馔，请族长诣祠、省牲，随佥执事率办厥明，咸以冠巾为率，行三献礼，毕则会馂。

清明：用小牢祭始祖之庙，行三献礼，随以次祭于墓所，毕则会馂于祠。其馀小宗祠效此例。

端午：用牲、醴、角黍、乌饭等祭，行三献礼，祭毕则会馂。

重阳：用小牢祭始祖庙，其馀小宗祠惟备香烛酒果拜祭而已。至人家祖先，祭用三牲，行三献礼。

秋分：与春分同一例。

冬至：与秋分同例。外用糯米圆，谓之献圆。

除夜：该值年用果酒香烛交下年值祭者。

可见，在农耕文明时代，宗法制的血缘村落里，宗祠里的祭祀活动是既频繁又隆重的。而且每次参加的人很多。

English

4. form and Use of ancestral halls

As ritual architecture, ancestral halls' form and appearance relatively conservative, standardized, and enclosed, surrounded by high brick walls. In Nanxi River villages, residential architecture mostly outward and open; enclosed ancestral halls appear very special in villages.

The opposition in architectural style between ancestral halls and residences is one manifestation of opposition between elite culture and folk culture.

Most main ancestral halls and some branch halls in Nanxi River have form of one main hall, left/right side wings (liangwu), and gate house. Inside gate, facing main hall, often a stage. Most stages protrude forward into courtyard;少数 in gate hall, gate house height and depth therefore increased.

Main hall and gate house generally seven bays, overhanging gable (xuanshan) roof; side wings three bays. At least Furong Chen main hall, Cangpo Li main hall, Xikou Ye main hall, Tangwan Zheng main hall, Yuzhang Hu main hall, Tongzhou Jiang main hall, Zhuan Chen main hall, and Tanxia Chen main hall adopt this form.

Langxia Zhu branch hall also this type. Furong village Chen main hall relatively complete among this type. It faces east; before main gate a courtyard, center opens a square pool called Xiangcheng Pool (Inherited-Pool); left/right each a courtyard gate — south-facing called Guangzong Gate, north-facing called Yaozu Gate.

East of Xiangcheng Pool, facing ancestral hall gate, a screen wall. Yantou Jin main hall, form somewhat exceeding norms, built a pair of bell-drum towers before main hall. This practice rare nationwide. Between bell-drum towers a square pool, filling entire courtyard.

Some branch halls only one main hall, no side wings, main gate just a wooden or brick door along wall, even no stage. Most important among this type is Heyang village Xie clan hall Xulun Hall (Ethics-Order Hall), which enshrines first landscape poet Xie Lingyun's spirit tablet.

In Daitou and Tangwan villages, such halls with semicircular pool in front exist. Also some branch halls, e.g. Pengxi village east and west ancestral halls, Furong village second-branch hall and Sanxing Hall etc., have main hall, side wings and gate house, no stage, thus forming a square four-courtyard. This is ancestral hall form in "Lu Ban Jing."

Main hall and side wings, front eaves all open, space merges with courtyard. Before main hall rear eave wall set a shrine cabinet (shenchu), inside enshrine successive ancestors' spirit tablets. shrine cabinet front has fine wood carved canopy, exquisite and delicate, painted red and gold — most ornate part of ancestral hall.

Before shrine cabinet set long altar table, exquisitely carved, form unified with shrine cabinet, becoming artistic whole. On altar table display candlesticks and incense burners; every new/full moon and spring/autumn sacrifice days, candlelight flickers, incense smoke curls, creating solemn and respectful atmosphere prompting remembrance.

In main hall secondary bays, within eave porch, timber frames support bell and drum. Bell left, drum right. Some ancestral halls use plate-shaped bronze object instead of bell, appearance like chime.

Above front of shrine cabinet, relying on central bay rear slope purlin, hang various plaques glorifying clan, such as "Imperial Edict," "jinshi" etc. In Furong village Chen main hall, a hanging "Imperial Edict" plaque — Yuan Shun Emperor rehabilitated Furong village jinshi Chen Yuzhi who died resisting Yuan, and praised and enfeoffed him.

All main hall columns hang couplets, content nothing but praising ancestors' merits and virtue, boasting clan's historical glory, to激励 descendants. E.g. Heyang village Xie clan Xulun Hall has a couplet:

Jiangzuo trace family fame, Gaishui victory dispatch, merits to this day illuminate history;

Oudong continue generation grace, pond spring grass, poetic talent through ages valued in scholarly world.

First line refers to Xie An, Xie Shi, Xie Xuan; second line refers to Xie Lingyun.

Xikou Dai main hall one couplet:

Neo-Confucianism Zhu-Cheng formerly inherited moral orthodoxy; Stream mountains Zou-Lu now display civilization.

ancestral halls have year-round sacrificial activities; these rituals slightly differ, all written into genealogy, descendants cannot neglect. "Yantou Jin clan Genealogy · Family Rules" stipulates: "Going out/entering must report, new/full moon must hold, seasonal foods must offer. Four-season sacrifices each use mid-month.

Sacrificial items must be clean, not about quantity." "Zhenxi Zhu clan Combined Genealogy · clan Norms" details ancestral hall sacrifice methods and standards:

New Year's Day: this day fifth watch, all visit main ancestral hall, young and old gather. That year's responsible descendants set incense candles wine fruit before spirit tablets. Perform four-bow rite; after, clan leader, deputy leader sit by seniority order, let juniors from below upward bow congratulations for New Year. After, each go to branch hall to pay respects.

Spring Equinox: responsible person beforehand clean hall, slaughter animal prepare food, invite clan leader to hall, inspect animal, then assign deacons; at dawn all in formal attire, perform three-offering rite, after share sacrificial food.

Pure Brightness: use small animal sacrifice at founding ancestor's temple, perform three-offering rite, then in order sacrifice at graves, after share sacrificial food at hall. Other branch halls follow this example.

Dragon Boat festival: use animal, wine, zongzi, black rice etc. sacrifice, perform three-offering rite, after sacrifice share food.

Double Ninth: use small animal sacrifice at founding ancestor temple; other branch halls only prepare incense candles wine fruit for worship. As for family ancestors, sacrifice use three animals, perform three-offering rite.

Autumn Equinox: same as Spring Equinox.

Winter Solstice: same as Autumn Equinox. Additionally use glutinous rice balls, called "offering round."

New Year's Eve: responsible person use fruit wine incense candles hand over to next year's responsible sacrificer.

Visible, in agricultural civilization era, patriarchal kinship villages, ancestral hall sacrificial activities both frequent and grand. And each time many people participate.

戏台和演戏
中文

5. 戏台和演戏

永嘉自元明以来，盛行戏剧。楠溪江中游，村村都有戏台，附设于宗祠和庙宇里。小小一个廊下村，至少有两座宗祠、两座庙宇及戏台。各村大宗祠几乎都有戏台。宗祠一般比庙宇重要，宗祠的戏台也比庙宇的戏台重要。《蓬溪谢氏宗谱》记载，大宗祠约建于明代中叶，到清代乾隆四十年（1775年）左右，已经破败，"初大厅圮而基存，故演戏与元宵花灯必于此处。惟五月十三在关帝庙演戏。道光初年，众修大宗祠，建前进，并建戏台，始在大宗祠演戏。春迎龙船山与关帝二庙神主至大宗祠，冬迎仙客殿与陈十四圣母至大宗祠庆祀，而五月十三之戏遂废"。关帝庙的戏，连一天都不演了。

在宗祠里演戏，是上层的雅言文化与下层的民俗文化的特殊结合。《鹤垟谢氏宗谱》里曾经规定，在书院里"小曲、谣辞、艳史等类片纸不得留"。作为礼制建筑的宗祠，本来应该比书院更加严肃，却容纳了戏剧，而戏剧，即使温州人写的《琵琶记》，也免不了"小曲、谣辞、艳史"。以戏剧敬祖酬神，说明封建的正统文化不得不对乡俚的娱乐要求让步。

清代初年的学者刘献廷（1648-1695年）在他著作的《广阳杂记》卷二里说：

余观世之小人，未有不好唱歌看戏者，此性天中之《诗》与《乐》也；未有不看小说、听说书者，此性天中之《书》与《春秋》也；未有不信占卜、祀鬼神者，此性天中之《易》与《礼》也。圣人六经之教，原本人情，而后之儒者乃不能因其势而利导之，百计禁止遏抑，务以成周之刍狗，茅塞人心，是何异壅川使之不流，无怪其决裂溃败也。

这段话里指出两点：一点是，正统的儒者要"百计禁止遏抑"唱歌、看戏、读小说；另一点却是，这种"遏抑"已经"决裂溃败"，也就是说，民俗文化终于冲破了雅言文化的藩篱，迫使正统道学家们接受了"小人"们的"人情"。戏台之成为宗祠的重要部分，正是这种社会历史现象的见证。

当然，雅言文化对民俗文化是既利用也限制的，它会在一定程度上使戏曲成为宣扬道德的工具。《渠川叶氏宗谱·重修渠川叶氏大宗祠碑记》里说：原来造于康熙熙辛（按：原文有误）年间的大宗祠，到光绪甲辰（1904年），"旧建舞台倾圮，乃舍旧维新。越明年，乙巳，谋于众曰：台之改作，勿以戏观。族人致祭，岁时伏腊，团结一堂，演剧开场，以古为鉴，伸忠孝节义之心，枨触而油然以生"。于是就重建了舞台。这说明，重建舞台的最有力的论据，是"伸忠孝节义之心"。祠堂与戏台就这样在雅言文化与俗文化既互相矛盾，又互相利用的情况下结合在一起了。

戏台一般紧接宗祠的门厅，隔院子面对正厅。台面通常是正方形，面阔和进深都是一间，间宽4.5米左右。渠口叶氏大宗祠和塘湾郑氏大宗祠，戏台台面在柱子外再拓展0.5米，比较宽畅。绝大多数宗祠里，戏台向院子内凸出，结构与门屋脱离而空间则是连续的。豫章胡氏大宗祠和桐州蒋氏大宗祠，戏台就在门厅里，没有凸出，门屋因而必须高大一些。这种做法一般用在廊庑为两层的时候，这种情况下，门屋必定比较高。这两种做法，都以门厅和两侧次间为后台，次间和梢间上部常做夹层，扩大后台。梢间下层是宗祠出入口。

戏台的三面完全敞开，观众围着它看戏。台面高约1.5米，沿台面边缘有大约30厘米高的小栏杆，保护演员的安全。背靠门厅的一侧是太师壁，右边是名为"出将"的上场门，左边是名为"入相"的下场门。太师壁后有一米多宽的候场台，通门厅和夹层的小楼梯在这里上下。台面左侧有一个不大的副台，是伴奏乐队用的"文场"，有独立的扶梯。

戏台位于中轴线上，紧挨门厅，正对大厅，为的是演戏敬祖敬神，让祖宗和神灵能欣赏演出。但戏台因此堵塞了中轴线上的交通。而每逢举行重要的仪式时，有些舆轿和圣物必须从正门沿中轴线进入正厅。所以，戏台面的台板通常不是整块的，沿中轴线有一排活动台板，可以抽掉，给舆轿让路。因此，宗祠正面必有三个门，正中门一般不开而从两个梢间的门出入。

凸出于院中的戏台上面覆歇山屋顶，檐口较高。它形体完整，翼角飞扬，在正厅和廊庑的檐口水平线衬托下，非常抢眼，成为院落的艺术中心，强过于正厅。院落、两廊和正厅前部在戏剧演出的时候是观众席。有些大宗祠，如豫章胡氏的，两廊有楼，楼上是女眷们看戏的地方。因为所演的都是地方戏曲，载歌载舞，所以观众也不妨从侧面看戏。除了渠口叶氏大宗祠院落地面分三段，逐段升高十馀厘米外，一般地面都是水平的。因为最大视距不过10米，台面高1.5米，所以视线遮挡问题不大。院子里看戏的观众一律站着。

凡有戏台的宗祠，无论在建筑艺上还是在功能形制上，都发生了演出建筑与崇祀敬神建筑之间的矛盾，双重性格，互争轻重。这矛盾反映的是娱乐和敬祖的矛盾，乡民的现实生活与封建的伦理之间的矛盾。

戏台上方的藻井十分华丽，是楠溪江最精致的木作之一。塘湾村郑氏大宗祠和渠口村叶氏大宗祠的戏台上，都是斗八藻井，由四面枋子上平身科斗拱后尾出两翘后抹角成八角，再斜上出三翘，近似镏金斗拱。这些斗拱都作装饰化变形，精雕细刻。流云卷草，宛转曲折，而且满绘颜色鲜艳的彩画。连檐椽和飞檐椽都绘满了彩画。芙蓉陈氏大宗祠的戏台上的藻井是正方形的，角科和平身科斗拱后尾各出两翘，托住一方平顶天花，镜面上用细木条组成几何格子图案。这藻井并不很华丽，但十分精致大方，艺术水平很高。

English

5. Stage and Theatrical Performance

Yongjia since Yuan-Ming dynasties, drama prevalent. Middle Nanxi River, every village has stages, attached to ancestral halls and temples. Tiny Langxia village has at least two ancestral halls, two temples and stages. Each village's main ancestral hall almost has a stage.

ancestral halls generally more important than temples; ancestral hall stages also more important than temple stages. "Pengxi Xie clan Genealogy" records: main hall roughly built mid-Ming, by Qing Qianlong forty year (1775) already dilapidated, "initially main hall collapsed but foundation remained, so drama and Lantern festival lanterns必定 here.

Only May 13 drama at Guandi temple. Daoguang early years, all repaired main hall, built front section, and built stage, then began drama at main hall.

Spring welcome Longchuan Mountain and Guandi two temple spirit tablets to main hall; winter welcome Xianke Hall and Chen Shisi Holy Mother to main hall celebration; May 13 drama thus abolished." Guandi temple drama, not even one day performed.

Performing drama in ancestral hall is special combination of upper elite culture and lower folk culture. "Heyang Xie clan Genealogy" once stipulated, in academy "little tunes, folk lyrics, romantic histories etc.

not a single sheet may remain." As ritual building ancestral hall, originally should be more solemn than academy, yet accommodated drama; and drama, even Wenzhou people's "Pipa Story," inevitably has "little tunes, folk lyrics, romantic histories." Using drama to honor ancestors and repay gods, shows feudal orthodox culture had to让步 to village entertainment demands.

Early Qing scholar Liu Xianting (1648-1695) in his "Guangyang Miscellaneous Records" volume 2 says:

I observe common people, none who don't like singing and watching drama — this is innate "Book of Songs" and "Music"; none who don't read novels, listen to storytelling — this is innate "Book of Documents" and "Spring and Autumn Annals"; none who don't believe divination, worship gods — this is innate "Book of Changes" and "Book of Rites." Sages' Six Classics teachings, originally based on human feelings; but later Confucians cannot guide according to tendency, instead千方百计 prohibit and suppress, insisting on Zhou dynasty's straw dogs, blocking human hearts — no different from damming river preventing flow, no wonder it breaks and collapses.

This passage points out two points: one, orthodox Confucians want to "千方百计 prohibit and suppress" singing, drama, novel reading; other, this "suppression" already "broke and collapsed" — i.e.

folk culture finally broke through elite culture's barriers, forcing orthodox moralists to accept common people's "human feelings." Stage becoming important part of ancestral hall is precisely witness of this social-historical phenomenon.

Of course, elite culture both uses and limits folk culture; it will to some extent make drama a tool for promoting morality.

"Quchuan Ye clan Genealogy · Reconstruction of Quchuan Ye Main Hall Stele Record" says: originally main hall built in Kangxi Xinsi (note: original text error) era, by Guangxu Jiachen (1904), "old stage collapsed, then abandoned old for new. Next year, Yisi, discussed with all: stage reconstruction, not for drama viewing.

clan members sacrifice, seasonal festivals, unite in one hall, drama opening, using ancient as mirror, extending loyalty-filial-chastity-righteousness hearts, touched and naturally arising." Thus rebuilt stage.

This shows strongest argument for rebuilding stage was "extending loyalty-filial-chastity-righteousness hearts." ancestral hall and stage thus combined under elite culture and folk culture both contradictory and mutually utilizing.

Stage generally directly adjacent to ancestral hall gate house, across courtyard facing main hall. Stage surface usually square, facade and depth both one bay, bay width about 4.5m. Qukou Ye main hall and Tangwan Zheng main hall, stage surface extends 0.5m beyond columns, more spacious.

In vast majority of ancestral halls, stage protrudes into courtyard, structure separate from gate house but space continuous. Yuzhang Hu main hall and Tongzhou Jiang main hall, stage inside gate house, no protrusion; gate house therefore must be taller. This practice generally used when side corridors two-story; in this case gate house必定 taller.

Both practices use gate house and both side secondary bays as backstage; secondary and end bays upper often make mezzanine, expanding backstage. End bay lower is ancestral hall entrance/exit.

Stage's three sides completely open, audience surrounds it watching. Stage surface about 1.5m high; along stage edge about 30cm high small railing, protecting actors' safety. Side against gate house is taishi wall; right is "Chujiang" (Exit-General) entrance door, left is "Ruxiang" (Enter-Minister) exit door.

Behind taishi wall over 1m wide waiting platform, small staircase to gate house and mezzanine goes up/down here. Stage left side has a small auxiliary stage, for accompanying band "wenchang" (civil section), with independent ladder.

Stage located on central axis, close to gate house, directly facing main hall — for drama honoring ancestors and gods, letting ancestors and deities enjoy performance. But stage thus blocks central axis traffic. And when important ceremonies held, some sedan chairs and sacred objects must enter main hall from main gate along central axis.

Therefore stage surface boards usually not one piece; along central axis a row of movable stage boards, can be removed to let sedan chairs pass. Therefore ancestral hall front必定 has three doors; central door generally not opened, enter/exit from two end bay doors.

Protruding stage above covered by hip-and-gable (xieshan) roof, eave relatively high. Its form complete, wing corners flying; under main hall and corridor eave horizontal line衬托, very eye-catching, becoming courtyard's artistic center, stronger than main hall. courtyard, two corridors and main hall front during drama are audience seats.

Some main halls, e.g. Yuzhang Hu, two corridors have upper floors, upstairs where female relatives watch drama. Because performed are local operas, singing and dancing, audience can also watch from side. Except Qukou Ye main hall courtyard ground divided into three sections, each rising over 10cm, generally ground level.

Because maximum viewing distance no more than 10m, stage 1.5m high, sightline obstruction not big issue. Audience in courtyard all standing. Any ancestral hall with stage, whether in architectural art or functional form, has contradiction between performance building and worship-god building — dual character, competing importance.

This contradiction reflects contradiction between entertainment and ancestor worship, between villagers' real life and feudal ethics.

caisson (zaojing) above stage very ornate, one of Nanxi River's most refined woodwork. Tangwan village Zheng main hall and Qukou village Ye main hall stages, both dou-eight caisson, from four-side lintels' pingjian-ke dougong rear tails two upturns then mitered into octagon, then diagonally upward three upturns, similar to gilded dougong.

These dougong all decoratively transformed, exquisitely carved. Flowing clouds and curling grass, winding and twisting, and fully painted bright colored paintings. Even eave rafters and flying eave rafters fully painted.

Furong Chen main hall stage caisson is square; jiao-ke and pingjian-ke dougong rear tails each two upturns, supporting a flat ceiling; mirror surface uses thin wood strips forming geometric grid pattern. This caisson not very ornate, but very refined and generous, artistic level high.

异型祠堂举例
中文

6. 异型祠堂举例

作为礼制建筑的宗祠，建筑形制比较保守，变化比较少，但在楠溪江仍然有些宗祠的形制与众不同。例如花坛村的敦睦祠（"乌府"）和西岸村的"大石祠堂"。

（1）敦睦祠是为明代正统年间（1436-1448年）山东道监察御史、江西按察司佥事、兵科给事中朱良暹造的。据《明实录》卷120载，英宗正统九年（1444年）八月，"赐江西按察司佥事朱良暹敕命，并封赠其父母与妻子"。敦睦祠为纪念这件特殊的荣誉而造，因此规格形式壮丽逾常。

它正厅面阔七开间，通长大约22.5米，明间面阔达5.3米左右。进深约11.0米。中央三间设神橱，后檐墙略向后凸出约1.2米。左右两个尽间有夹层，屋面因此局部升高。10米进深的悬山式顶，山墙面本来显得笨拙，屋面有了错落就好多了。两廊各三间，进深约5.3米。它们的空间与正厅的挂角相连，很宽畅。敦睦祠最大的特点在它的门屋：中央五开间的门，就是院落的宽度，将近14米，两翼还各有5.3米，从内部空间看，其实是两廊的梢间。门屋进深不过2米，有40厘米高的台基。

台基在明间缺一个3米宽的口子，让石板路直达正厅前阶。五开间的门，中央三间采用牌楼式，明间高，次间略低。这楼有很复杂、很华丽的斗拱。除明间两攒平身科外，其余都是柱头科。中栋柱上的斗拱，先出四跳丁头拱，然后再叠三层下昂，还有不少装饰化的处理。柱子既有侧脚也有生起。明间里外的四个抱鼓都是木质的，门枕也是木质的。

9.8米深的院子里曾经有戏台，现在还有柱顶石可以辨识。因为门屋其实就是一座牌楼门，进深不过两米，所以戏台脱离门屋而独立，没有后台。戏台台面可拆卸，于重大仪典时让出中轴线，供舆轿出入。

敦睦祠正厅结构用鸳鸯厅式的前后两榀屋架相接，当地称"二分房"。这两榀屋架的前后最高两步钉密接的方椽子，不留椽档。在它们之间的谷地，用草架支承起脊檩来，再与两榀屋架的前后坡形成统一的两坡顶。这种做法，首先是为了避免大厅内部空间过于高旷，同时也可以少用几棵高大的柱子，木材易于得到。

敦睦祠坐北向南，大门临横过花坛村前沿的大路。门前原来有品字形三座牌坊，左右横跨大路的是"乌府"和"黄门"，隔路面对大门的是"奕世簪缨"。前两座与祠堂同时建造，后一座稍晚，但也在明朝。这三座牌坊和祠堂大门一起构成大街上很壮观的街景之一，它们有重要的规划性意义。

（2）西岸村的大石祠堂造于清初，是金氏三十五世祖金大绅夫妇的家祠，规模不大，形制和风格都没有礼制建筑的特点，倒很像一所园林建筑。它位于街道的西侧，周围一圈围墙，东向临街一座木门楼。进门楼是一片6.0米公尺宽的水池，水池在左右两端向后延伸，从三面包围了一幢五开间四合院。四合院也以水池代替中央的院子，它宽3.7米，长7.0米。从木门楼经石板桥到前进过厅。前进敞开前后檐廊，以座凳栏杆临水池。后进正厅在山坡上，比前进高1.4米，前檐完全敞开，栏以美人靠。美人靠下是蛮石挡土墙，直插入水池中。

两厢各三间，厢内有楼梯登上正厅。厅里设神橱安牌位，小木作很精致。这正厅的一个很有趣的特点是，它地面上保留三块山坡上的原岩。最大的大约2.8米长，1.5米宽，高出地面大约0.9米。因为金大绅的房派名为"大岩房"，这座祠堂原名为"大岩房祠堂"，所以，有意在正厅留下三块岩石，以应堂名。这做法很有点幽默感。后人漫称它为大石祠堂。村中金姓后裔流行另一种说法：建造宗祠，金大绅亲自参加劳作，在负责凿去这三块岩石时，不慎伤了手，流了很多血。大绅受到启发，特意留下它们，以教诫后人，凡要成事，必要辛苦，甚至流血。

从此房派为"大岩房"，祠堂得名为"大岩房祠堂"。

祠堂不大，在不大的祠堂里，空间和景观因素变化很大，而且层次多，呈现的画面丰富，各向流动，没有礼制建筑的封闭和肃穆之感。

这座祠堂的木墙门的构架很精巧，采用楠溪江常见的多层丁头拱。里外四个抱鼓都是木质的。作为家祠，它的前后进房子的构件尺寸和空间尺寸都小于一般的宗祠，而与住宅的相同，加以风格也是开朗亲切的，所以与花坛村的敦睦祠大异其趣。礼制建筑竟生活化到了，人文气息很浓。

西岸村的大石祠堂，从它的整体构思到正厅里的三块原岩，从小木装修到水池的运用，都显示出它的兴建者的心里充满了破格创新的强烈愿望，对陈规旧习满不在乎的超脱。兴会所至，通变自如。

English

6. Examples of Atypical ancestral halls

As ritual architecture, ancestral halls' form relatively conservative, changes few; but in Nanxi River still some ancestral halls' form与众不同. E.g. Huatan village Dunmu Hall ("Wu Fu") and Xi'an village's "Big Stone ancestral hall."

(1) Dunmu Hall built for Zhu Liangxian — Ming Zhengtong era (1436-1448) Shandong Circuit Investigating Censor, Jiangxi Provincial Surveillance Commissioner, and Military Section Supervising Secretary.

According to "Ming Shilu" volume 120, Yingzong Zhengtong nine year (1444) August, "bestow Jiangxi Provincial Surveillance Commissioner Zhu Liangxian imperial edict, and enfeoff his parents and wife." Dunmu Hall built to commemorate this special honor, therefore specifications and form magnificent beyond ordinary.

Its main hall facade seven bays, total length about 22.5m, central bay facade reaches about 5.3m. Depth about 11.0m. Central three bays set shrine cabinet; rear eave wall slightly protrudes backward about 1.2m. Left/right two end bays have mezzanine, roof therefore locally raised.

10m depth overhanging gable roof, gable wall originally appears clumsy; roof with staggered levels much better. Two corridors each three bays, depth about 5.3m. Their space connects with main hall's guajiao (hanging corner), very spacious. Dunmu Hall's biggest feature is its gate house: central five-bay gate, i.e.

courtyard width, nearly 14m; two wings each 5.3m; from interior space, actually are two corridors' end bays. Gate house depth only 2m, has 40cm high platform. platform at central bay lacks a 3m wide opening, letting stone slab road reach main hall front steps directly.

Five-bay gate, central three bays adopt paifang style, central bay high, secondary bays slightly lower. This building has very complex, ornate dougong. Except central bay two sets pingjian-ke, rest are zhutou-ke (column-top dougong).

On central ridge column dougong, first four jumps dingtou-gong (corbel bracket), then stack three layers xia'ang (lower ang), plus不少 decorative treatment. Columns both cejiao (inward slant) and shengqi (height rise). Central bay inside/outside four baogu (drum stones) all wooden; door枕 also wooden.

9.8m deep courtyard once had stage; now column-top stones still identifiable. Because gate house actually is a paifang gate, depth only 2m, so stage detached from gate house and independent, no backstage. Stage surface可拆卸, at major ceremonies yields central axis for sedan chairs in/out.

Dunmu Hall main hall structure uses yuanyang-hall (mandarin duck hall) style front/rear two roof frames joined, locally called "erfen fang" (two-part house). These two roof frames' front/rear highest two steps nail closely connected square rafters, no rafter gap.

Between them in valley, use caojia (hidden frame) to support ridge purlin, then with two roof frames' front/rear slopes form unified two-slope roof. This practice, first to avoid main hall interior space too high and vast,同时 can use fewer tall columns, timber easier to obtain.

Dunmu Hall sits north facing south, main gate faces road crossing Huatan village front. Before gate originally had pin-shaped three paifangs; left/right across road are "Wu Fu" and "Huang Men"; across road facing gate is "Yishi Zanying" (Generations of Officials). First two built same time as hall, third slightly later but also Ming.

These three paifangs and hall gate together form one of main street's very spectacular streetscapes; they have important planning significance.

(2) Xi'an village Big Stone ancestral hall built early Qing, is Jin clan 35th generation ancestor Jin Dashen and wife's family hall; scale not large, form and style lack ritual architecture characteristics, rather like a garden building. It located west side of street, surrounded by a wall, east-facing street a wooden gate tower.

Entering gate tower is a 6.0m wide pool; pool extends backward at left/right ends, from three sides surrounding a five-bay four-courtyard. Four-courtyard also uses pool instead of central courtyard; it 3.7m wide, 7.0m long. From wooden gate tower via stone slab bridge to front passage hall.

Front hall opens front/rear eave porches, with bench railing facing pool. Rear main hall on hillside, 1.4m higher than front; front eave completely open, railing with meiren-kao (beauty's rest). Below beauty's rest is rough stone retaining wall, directly inserted into pool. Two wings each three bays; inside wings have staircase to main hall.

In hall set shrine cabinet with spirit tablets; small woodwork very refined. This main hall's very interesting feature: its ground retains three original rocks from hillside. Largest about 2.8m long, 1.5m wide, about 0.9m above ground.

Because Jin Dashen's sub-branch named "Big Rock Branch," this hall originally named "Big Rock Branch Hall," therefore intentionally left three rocks in main hall to match hall name. This practice has a bit of humor. Later generations casually call it Big Stone ancestral hall.

village Jin clan descendants流行 another saying: building ancestral hall, Jin Dashen personally participated in labor; responsible for chiseling away these three rocks, accidentally injured hand, bled much. Dashen inspired, specially left them to teach descendants: to accomplish anything, must work hard, even bleed.

From then sub-branch "Big Rock Branch," hall named "Big Rock Branch Hall."

Hall not large; in not-large hall, space and landscape factors vary greatly, and many layers, presented画面 rich, flowing in all directions, no ritual architecture's封闭 and solemn feeling.

This hall's wooden wall gate frame very refined, adopts Nanxi River common multi-layer dingtou-gong. Inside/outside four baogu all wooden.

As family hall, its front/rear buildings' component dimensions and space dimensions smaller than ordinary ancestral halls, same as residences; plus style also open and friendly, therefore very different from Huatan village Dunmu Hall. Ritual architecture竟生活化 to this extent, humanistic atmosphere very strong.

Xi'an village Big Stone ancestral hall, from its overall conception to three original rocks in main hall, from small wood decoration to pool use, all show its builder's heart full of strong desire to break rules and innovate, detachment caring nothing about old conventions. Where inspiration leads, adapt freely.

祠堂的保护
中文

7. 祠堂的保护

祠堂是宗族凝聚力的标志，意义重大，各村都有严格的管理、维修制度，并且将这些制度载入宗谱，全族必须恪守。例如《岩头金氏宗谱·家规》里说：

祠堂周缭以墙，中设大门，严以锁钥，非祭之日，守祠者不得擅开，纵放闲人出入。族人出入，子孙亦毋得邀友聚饮，演武喧哗。……祠堂坟屋，稍遇倾圮，亟当议修，量以坟租、祠租内暂行抽贮，以给支费。……（按：不得假此变卖祭田）。如有贤子子孙捐资乐助者，即当登记于谱。

虽然各村都有保护规定和措施，宗祠的木结构仍然很容易糟朽。在宗族财力不济的时候，或者经历时时变，宗祠可能倾圮。所以，遗留至今的宗祠，大多经过多次修葺或者重建。只有花坛村的朱氏大宗祠，正厅可能有宋代遗构。可以断定大部分还是明代原构的，大约有廊下村的朱氏小宗，花坛村的敦睦祠、芙蓉村的陈氏大宗祠，岩头村的金氏大宗祠正厅和钟鼓楼等等。

English

7. protection of ancestral halls

ancestral halls are symbol of clan cohesion, significance great; each village has strict management and maintenance systems, and these systems written into genealogy, entire clan must abide. E.g. "Yantou Jin clan Genealogy · Family Rules" says:

ancestral hall surrounded by wall, center set main gate, strictly locked; on non-sacrifice days, hall keeper may not擅自 open, letting idle people in/out. clan members in/out, descendants also may not invite friends gather drink, practice martial arts noisy. ...

ancestral hall and grave houses, slightly encounter collapse, immediately discuss repair; measure from grave rent and hall rent temporarily set aside, to cover expenses. ... (note: may not use this pretext sell sacrificial fields). If worthy descendants donate willingly, immediately register in genealogy.

Although each village has protection regulations and measures, ancestral halls' timber structure still easily rots. When clan financial resources insufficient, or经历 social changes, ancestral hall may collapse. Therefore, ancestral halls surviving to today, mostly经过 multiple repairs or reconstructions.

Only Huatan village Zhu clan main hall, main hall may have Song dynasty surviving structure. Can断定 most still Ming dynasty original structure, approximately Langxia village Zhu branch hall, Huatan village Dunmu Hall, Furong village Chen clan main hall, Yantou village Jin clan main hall and bell-drum towers etc.

本篇术语 Key Terms

祠堂的保护 / Protection of ancestral halls 宗族凝聚力 / Clan cohesion 守祠者 / Hall keeper 坟租 / Grave rent (Fenzu) 祠租 / Hall rent (Cizu) 祭田 / Sacrificial field (Jitian) 糟朽 / Timber rot 倾圮 / Collapse (Qingpi) 修葺 / Repair (Xiuqi) 明代原构 / Ming dynasty original structure 宋代遗构 / Song dynasty surviving structure
10
巫风与泛灵崇拜
中文

九、崇祀建筑

楠溪江村落里，极少真正的佛寺和道观，大量的庙宇，都是"淫祠"。虽然《白虎通·五祀篇》说，"非所当祭而祭之，名曰淫祀。淫祀无福"，但乡民们仍然不理会佛家、儒家正统的教条，向非所当祭的祈福。

1. 巫风与泛灵崇拜

在自然经济的农业社会里，一切都物质化和生活化，村民们既不理解、也不需要精神性的、哲理性的宗教，他们的心态是实用主义的，当现实生活发生困难，有了欠缺的时候，他们祈求强有力的救助。于是，他们崇祀着一些"有求必应"的，掌管着现实生活的各个方面的杂神和半神。村民们按实际需要向这位或那位神灵叩头烧香。关于这些神，有许多灵异传说，却丝毫不讲伦理和哲理。迷信是有的，宗教却没有。杂神大多是地方性的，由一些有特殊经历的村夫村妇们变成。只有少数，在较大范围里受到崇拜，如沿海各地普遍建"天后宫"，祈求航海平安。

这种情况与南方盛行巫风有很深的关系。嘉靖《浙江通志》说："始东瓯王信鬼，故瓯俗多敬鬼乐祠。"嘉靖《温州府志》也同样说，"汉东瓯王信鬼，俗化焉，尚巫渎祀。"南宋大诗人陆游在《野庙记》里把瓯越间的巫风淫祀写得非常生动：

瓯越间好事鬼，山椒水滨多淫祀。其庙貌有雄而毅、黝而硕者，则曰将军。有温而厚、晰而少者则曰某郎。有媪而尊严者则曰姥，有容而艳者则曰姑。其居处则敞之以庭堂，峻之以陛级。上有老木，攒植森拱，萝茑翳其上，鸱鸮室其间。车马徒隶，丛杂怪状，农作之氓怖之，大者椎牛，次者击豕，小不下犬。虽鱼菽之荐，牲酒之奠，缺于家可也，缺于神不可也。一朝懈怠，祸亦随之。盖孺畜牧栗栗然，疾死丧，氓不曰适丁其时而自感其生，悉归之于神。

负有推行儒家教化之责的地方长官一贯反对这种泛灵论的杂神崇拜，明代以后，多次禁止，有几位县令，如文林，曾经"毁淫祠"，但是没有什么效果。

杂祀并非宗教，没有专门的仪典、经籍和神职人员，更不要求专门的建筑形制。一座庙并不限于祭祀一位神灵，除了主祀之外，常有几位陪祀。主祀和陪祀不一定有什么关系。所以一座大殿里可以杂陈许多神灵。《蓬溪谢氏宗谱·蓬溪四殿记》里记载着：

惟闻仙岩殿杂塑娘娘与杨府圣王、伏魔大帝、陈十四圣母娘娘诸神像。今仙岩殿三进，前进中置炉案而无神像，堂中两旁塑立功曹四，左边塑土地神，右边塑柴氏龙王像。中进中左塑孝祐夫人，足下踏虎。右塑石压娘子、毛氏夫人。后宫石室无梁柱。中进娘娘五，曰刘一、刘二、衰三、衰四、衰五尊神。相传神为斯溪刘进士之妹，衰氏是其嫂也，二月踏青，至大碏山上，因而出圣。……其庙止斯溪与蓬溪，别处未有闻者。其神极灵，有求必应。

各位杂神群聚一堂，在楠溪江是极普遍的现象，就是这个蓬溪村，关帝庙里竟有济公和孙悟空也一同在享受人间烟火。可以群处，当然就可以替换，例如西岸村西北角的关帝庙，本来为镇镶"反弓水"而造，后来改为供奉主管生育的娘娘，连庙里的楹联都没有换。关平和周仓成了娘娘的功曹。

"有求必应"是实用主义崇祀活动的前提，"神"的来历都可以不予深究。例如芙蓉村以南五里的下坞村有一座"陈五侯王庙"，或称"陈五官庙"，据明代洪武二十二年（1389年）李贞撰的《宋陈五侯王庙碑记》说："陈五官庙坐镇一乡，民居数千馀口，咸依密佑，多历所年，祈祷随感而应，灵显不可殚述。"但是，"神之所自，及侯爵锡何时，庙额之所以为'灵应'，均已不可考。"人们满怀虔诚之心，以香火供奉了一千年之久的"神"，竟来历不明。

那些所谓"可考"的，也不过根据些神异传说。例如温州府属各县普遍供奉的杨府爷或杨府圣王，据乾隆《永嘉县志·建置·坛庙》引万历《温州府志》说："临海神杨氏，失其名，相传兄弟七人入山修炼，后每若灵异。"又引《旧志》说："按神姓杨，名精义，唐太宗时人，生十子，俱入山修道，一夕拔宅飞升，同登仙籍，由此著灵。"廊下村有一座太尉庙，全称"敕封元帅太尉镇公庙"。这位太尉的身份历史已经杳不可考，但从庙里的楹联和匾额看，曾经非常神奇灵异。例如，有一方匾额是"返风灭火"，另一方是"拯溺援危"。楹联之一是：

采猎入林，数十神灵孤掌斗；

逆流至括，半千里路一棺浮。

如此这般的灵异是成神的必要条件。塘湾村、苍坡村也各有一座太尉庙，大概也祭祀这位神灵。

除了发财、送子、疗痘、治病以及"无求勿应"什么都管的杂神如杨府爷、胡公等等之外，还有"三官大帝"，就是天官赐福紫微大帝、地官赦罪清虚大帝和水官解厄洞阴大帝。他们就是尧、舜、禹三位远古的帝王。三官庙在楠溪江是数量最多的庙，几乎所有的凉亭都供奉他们，处处可见，照例早已不专管生产，而兼管人事生活一切事项了。

香火最盛的是小楠溪的胡公庙。庙在传说中陶弘景（456-536年）修炼的陶公洞里。胡公是北宋的胡则，浙江省永康县方岩村人，曾守温州。《宋史》说他"果敢有才气，以进士起家"。宋仁宗明道元年（1032年），在他任户部侍郎的时候，上疏奏免衢婺两州身丁钱，因而受到人们感恩崇祀，浙东各地都建胡公庙。楠溪江的庙宇大多为一村所有，陶公洞里的胡公庙则有大量外地香客，甚至有远从温州市区来的。

English

IX. Sacrificial Architecture

In Nanxi River villages, very few genuine Buddhist temples and Daoist temples; large number of temples are "licentious shrines" (yin ci). Although "Baihu Tong · Five sacrifices Chapter" says, "Sacrificing to what should not be sacrificed is called licentious sacrifice.

Licentious sacrifice brings no blessing," but villagers still ignore Buddhist and Confucian orthodox doctrines, praying to what should not be sacrificed.

1. Shamanism and Animism

In natural economy agricultural society, everything materialized and生活化; villagers neither understand nor need spiritual, philosophical religion; their mentality is pragmatist. When real life encounters difficulties, has deficiencies, they pray for powerful rescue.

Thus, they worship some "every prayer answered" miscellaneous gods and demigods governing various aspects of real life. villagers kowtow and burn incense to this or that deity according to actual need. About these gods, many supernatural legends, but no ethics or philosophy at all. Superstition exists, religion does not.

Miscellaneous gods mostly local, transformed from some villagers with special experiences. Only少数, worshipped in larger scope, e.g. coastal areas普遍 build "Tianhou Palace" (Mazu temple), praying for navigation safety.

This situation deeply related to prevalence of shamanism in south.

Jiajing "Zhejiang General Annals" says: "Originally Dong'ou King believed in ghosts, therefore Ou customs多 respect ghosts and enjoy shrines." Jiajing "Wenzhou Prefecture Annals" similarly says, "Han Dong'ou King believed in ghosts, customs transformed,崇尚 shamanic licentious sacrifice." Southern Song great poet Lu You in "Wild temple Record" vividly described shamanic licentious sacrifice among Ou-Yue:

Ou-Yue area likes serving ghosts; mountain tops and river banks many licentious shrines. Their temple images: heroic and resolute, dark and sturdy — called General. Warm and generous, fair and young — called某郎. Old and dignified — called Old Woman; beautiful and charming — called Maiden. Their dwellings: spacious courtyards and halls, high steps.

Above old trees, densely planted, vines cover, owls nest. Carriages and attendants, clustered strange forms; farmers make them, common people fear them; large slaughter oxen, medium slaughter pigs, small no less than dogs. Even fish and bean offerings, animal and wine libations — lacking at home acceptable, lacking for god unacceptable.

Once slack, disaster follows. Children and livestock tremble; illness and death, people don't say just met their time and naturally felt life, all attribute to god.

Local officials responsible for promoting Confucian enlightenment consistently opposed this animist miscellaneous god worship; after Ming, repeatedly prohibited; several county magistrates, e.g. Wen Lin, once "destroyed licentious shrines," but little effect.

Miscellaneous sacrifice not religion; no specialized rituals, scriptures, clergy, even less require specialized architectural form. A temple not limited to sacrificing one deity; besides main worship, often several accompanying worship. Main and accompanying not necessarily related. Therefore one main hall can display many deities. "Pengxi Xie clan Genealogy · Pengxi Four Halls Record" records:

Only heard Xianyan Hall mixed sculpt maidens and Yangfu Holy King, Demon-Subduing Great Emperor, Chen Shisi Holy Mother and other deity images. Now Xianyan Hall three sections; front section center sets incense table no deity image; hall both sides sculpt four merit officers; left sculpt Earth God; right sculpt Chai clan Dragon King image.

Middle section center-left sculpt Xiaoyou Lady, foot treading tiger. Right sculpt Stone-Pressing Maiden, Mao clan Lady. Rear hall stone room no beams-columns. Middle section five maidens, called Liu Yi, Liu Er, Shuai San, Shuai Si, Shuai Wu honored deities.

Legend deities are this stream Liu jinshi's younger sisters; Shuai clan is her sister-in-law; second month spring outing, to Daque mountain, thus became holy. ... Its temple only this stream and Pengxi, nowhere else heard. Its deity extremely efficacious, every prayer answered.

Various miscellaneous gods gather in one hall, extremely common phenomenon in Nanxi River. Even this Pengxi village, Guandi temple has Jigong and Sun Wukong together enjoying human incense. Can coexist, of course can replace; e.g.

Xi'an village northwest corner Guandi temple, originally built to suppress "reverse-bow water," later changed to enshrine maiden governing childbirth; even temple couplets not changed. Guan Ping and Zhou Cang became maiden's merit officers.

"Every prayer answered" is premise of pragmatist worship; "god's" origin can be not investigated. E.g.

Xiawu village five li south of Furong village has a "Chen Wuhou King temple," or "Chen Wuguan temple"; according to Ming Hongwu twenty-two year (1389) Li Zhen's "Song Chen Wuhou King temple Stele Record": "Chen Wuguan temple guards one township, residents several thousand mouths, all rely on secret protection, many years passed, prayers随感而应, efficaciousness cannot fully describe." However, "god's origin, and when marquis title bestowed, why temple name 'Lingying' (Efficacious Response), all already untestable." People with full pious hearts, incense worshipping for a thousand years "god,"竟 origin unknown.

Those so-called "testable," also only based on supernatural legends. E.g.

Wenzhou prefecture counties普遍 worship Yangfu Ye or Yangfu Holy King; according to Qianlong "Yongjia County Annals · Establishment · Altars-temples" quoting Wanli "Wenzhou Prefecture Annals": "Linhai deity Yang clan, name lost; legend seven brothers entered mountain cultivation, later each seemed supernatural." Also quoting "Old Annals": "According to deity surname Yang, name Jingyi, Tang Taizong era person, had ten sons, all entered mountain cultivation; one evening whole house ascended to immortality, together registered in immortal books, thus became efficacious." Langxia village has a Taiwei (Grand Commandant) temple, full name "Imperially Enfeoffed Marshal Grand Commandant Zhen Gong temple." This Grand Commandant's historical identity already untraceable; but from temple couplets and plaques, once very miraculously efficacious.

E.g. one plaque "Reverse Wind Extinguish Fire," another "rescue Drowning Aid Danger." One couplet:

Hunting into forest, dozens of spirits single palm fight;

Against current to Kuo, half thousand li road one coffin floats.

Such supernaturalness is necessary condition for becoming god. Tangwan village, Cangpo village each has a Taiwei temple, probably also worship this deity.

Besides miscellaneous gods governing wealth, child-giving, smallpox treatment, illness cure, and "no request unanswered" everything like Yangfu Ye, Hu Gong etc., also "Three Officials Great Emperor" — Heavenly Official Bestowing Blessing Ziwei Great Emperor, Earth Official Remitting Sins Qingxu Great Emperor, Water Official Resolving Misfortune Dongyin Great Emperor.

They are Yao, Shun, Yu three ancient emperors. Three-Officials temple in Nanxi River is most numerous temple; almost all pavilions worship them, everywhere visible; as usual早已 not exclusively govern production, but concurrently govern all human life matters.

Most incense is Xiaonanxi's Hu Gong temple. temple in legendary Tao Hongjing (456-536) cultivated Tao Gong Cave. Hu Gong is Northern Song Hu Ze, from Fangyan village, Yongkang County, Zhejiang Province; once governed Wenzhou.

"Song History" says he "resolute and talented, started as jinshi." Song Renzong Mingdao first year (1032), when he served as Vice Minister of Revenue, memorialized to exempt Qu-Wu two prefectures' personal head tax, thus received people's gratitude worship; eastern Zhejiang everywhere built Hu Gong temples.

Nanxi River temples mostly owned by one village; Hu Gong temple in Tao Gong Cave has large number of outside pilgrims, even from far Wenzhou city.

庙宇选址
中文

2. 庙宇选址

庙宇的选址变化很大，据堪舆风水之说，一般不宜造在村子里，所以多造在村边不远处，如水云村的平水圣王庙、娘娘宫，塘湾村、廊下村的太尉庙、凤南宫和芙蓉峰下的广福寺，等等。岩头村的几座庙则集中在村外大约1公里的双浚头。

伪托朱熹著的《雪心赋》说"坛庙必居水口"，就是村落的自然环境（常为小流域）里诸水汇总流出这个环境单元的地方。水口忌直露无碍、流水无情而去。最好是有山，左右掩映，不教人见到去水。如果"仍见去水，则主易成而易也败，发福不能常远也"。这就要用桥梁、庙宇之类来增加层次，遮拦水口，从而"关锁内气"。塘湾村的山隍庙、蓬溪村的关帝庙和岩头村的塔湖庙就在村子的水口。

有些庙被造来克服风水的"凶"相，如关帝庙常造在面对"反弓水"的地方。

蓬溪村在一个四面环山的盆地里，这盆地只北端有一个缺口，楠溪江中游的支流鹤盛溪由东北而来，到这缺口就转向西北而去。村子的东侧，也就是盆地中央，有一条小溪由南向北流，到缺口汇入鹤盛溪。所以北端缺口就是蓬溪村的水口。这里有一个小小的凸出高地霞港头略略遮挡水口，但层次不够，因而需要有一座庙宇增加水口的闭锁性。同时，对蓬溪村的位置来说，东北来西北去的鹤盛溪在这里形成了一个反弯，按堪舆家说法，这种"反弓水"是大凶的形势，因而在小高地上造了一座关帝庙，借"伏魔大帝"的神威克服"反弓水"的凶相。它既掩映水口又镇胁"反弓水"。

同样位置在"反弓水"前，用来镇胁风水凶相的是西岸村西北角上的小小的关帝庙。

堪舆家把村子的来水口叫"天门"。天门要坦直，不像水口那样要关闭，但却要有堂皇的庙宇楼阁做装饰。楠溪江村落里装饰天门最盛的是岩头村，它在村北大约1公里的水门，双浚头，造了三座庙，一座是三圣庙，一座是太阴庙，一座是卢氏娘娘庙。

乡村的庙宇，人文性很强，连同天门、水口，都贴近乡人的日常生活。岩头村双浚头娘娘庙前数百年的老树浓荫蔽天，树下有茶亭，亭内设灶，每年从端午到重阳，免费供应茶水和柴禾。亭傍大路，晨夕过往行人不断，茶亭中常有荷锄负薪的人歇脚，成为一个郊外的交谊场所。

经常成为休息交谊场所的是三官庙。三官大帝虽然掌管农业丰歉，关系到人们存亡的命脉，却没有像样的庙宇，一般都栖身在路亭、凉亭和谯亭里。把亭子的后檐用砖墙封住，墙上设神龛。这样的三官庙遍布各处。如芙蓉、花坛、廊下、岭下、下烘头、下园、溪南等等村落里外都有。同时，重要的水源旁，往往有路亭式的三官庙。如渡头村东门大口井、西岸村东北角瓠瓜井，溪南村南边的泉源井，芙蓉村西门外1里左右的水渠头上等等。三官中除尧、舜之外，还有专管水的禹，而且三官总管农业，与水源的关系当然最大。

三官庙和土地庙常有极小的，高不及数尺，模型而已，置于树下石上，依然香烟袅袅。埭头村卧龙冈下有一口井，井旁造了一个小小的神龛式的三官庙。西岸村有一个三官庙，就在村中心大街的丁字路口，面对一条直街，背靠在墙上，整个像一座壁龛。亭子里的三官庙也有很精致华丽的，如花坛东门的谯亭，神龛和藻井都很饱满紧凑、比例协调、色彩和谐。

有一些庙造在村子里，与身旁的开放空间结合，形成公共中心。如周宅村的土地庙和它前面的小广场，西岸村的杨府爷庙和瓠瓜井等等。岩头村的塔湖庙，苍坡村的仁济庙和太阴庙，都在村子的大型公共园林里，而且是园林的重要组成部分。它们其实就在村子的水口，位于巽方。

早年的佛寺道观，大多在离村落的浅山区，如芙蓉峰上下的崇果寺和广福寺，岩头村北五鯈溪旁长蛇坑十八垅的普安寺，等等。佛教徒讲究静修，弃绝尘寰。不过，近年来真正的宗教更形衰落，崇果寺和普安寺已经荒败，广福寺因为改成供奉卢氏娘娘、陈十四娘娘等七位娘娘才得以略有香火，维持下来。

English

2. temple Site Selection

temple site selection varies greatly; according to fengshui geomancy, generally不宜 built inside village, so mostly built near village edge, e.g. Shuiyun village Pingshui Holy King temple, Niangniang Palace; Tangwan village, Langxia village Taiwei temple, Fengnan Palace, and Guangfu temple below Furong Peak, etc. Yantou village's several temples集中 at Shuangjuntou about 1km outside village.

Falsely attributed to Zhu Xi's "Xuexin Fu" says "altars-temples must reside at water mouth" — i.e. in village's natural environment (often small watershed) where all waters汇总 flow out of this environmental unit. water mouth忌 straight exposed unobstructed, flowing water mercilessly leaving.

Best to have mountains, left/right screening, not letting people see outgoing water. If "still see outgoing water, then主 easy success but easy failure, fortune cannot last far." This requires bridges, temples etc.

to increase layers, block water mouth, thereby "lock internal qi." Tangwan village Shanhuang temple, Pengxi village Guandi temple, and Yantou village Tahu temple are at village's water mouth.

Some temples built to overcome fengshui "inauspicious" appearance; e.g. Guandi temple often built facing "reverse-bow water."

Pengxi village in a basin surrounded by mountains; this basin only has a gap at north end; Nanxi River middle tributary Hesheng Stream comes from northeast, at this gap turns northwest. village's east side, i.e. basin center, has a small stream flowing south to north, at gap joins Hesheng Stream.

Therefore north end gap is Pengxi village's water mouth. Here a small protruding highland Xiagangtou slightly screens water mouth, but layers insufficient, thus need a temple to increase water mouth's closure.

Meanwhile, for Pengxi village's position, Hesheng Stream coming northeast going northwest forms a reverse bend here; according to geomancers, this "reverse-bow water" is greatly inauspicious situation, thus built a Guandi temple on small highland, borrowing "Demon-Subduing Great Emperor's" divine power to overcome "reverse-bow water" inauspicious appearance.

It both screens water mouth and suppresses "reverse-bow water."

Similarly positioned before "reverse-bow water," used to suppress fengshui inauspicious appearance, is Xi'an village northwest corner small Guandi temple.

Geomancers call village's incoming water mouth "Heavenly Gate" (Tianmen). Heavenly Gate should be open and straight, unlike water mouth which should be closed; but needs grand temple-pavilions as decoration.

Nanxi River villages most elaborately decorate Heavenly Gate is Yantou village; at water gate about 1km north of village, Shuangjuntou, built three temples: one Sansheng temple, one Taiyin temple, one Lu clan Niangniang temple.

village temples, strong humanistic character, together with Heavenly Gate and water mouth, all close to villagers' daily life.

Yantou village Shuangjuntou Niangniang temple before it centuries-old old tree dense shade covers sky; under tree tea pavilion, pavilion内 stove; every year from Dragon Boat festival to Double Ninth, free supply tea water and firewood.

Pavilion beside main road, morning-evening passing pedestrians continuous; tea pavilion often has people carrying hoes or firewood resting, becoming a suburban socializing place.

Often becoming rest-socializing place is Three-Officials temple. Three Officials Great Emperor although governs agricultural abundance/shortage, related to people's survival lifeline, yet no decent temple; generally dwell in road pavilions, cool pavilions, and watch pavilions. Seal pavilion's rear eave with brick wall, wall set shrine niche.

Such Three-Officials temples everywhere. E.g. Furong, Huatan, Langxia, Lingxia, Xiahongtou, Xiayuan, Xinan etc. villages inside/outside all have. Meanwhile, important water sources旁 often have road-pavilion-style Three-Officials temple. E.g.

Dutou village east gate large-mouth well, Xi'an village northeast corner Hugua well, Xinan village south spring-source well, Furong village west gate outside about 1li water channel head etc.

Among Three Officials besides Yao, Shun, also Yu specifically governing water; and Three Officials overall govern agriculture, relationship with water source of course greatest. Three-Officials temple and Earth God temple often extremely small, height less than several chi, just models, placed under tree on stone, still incense curls.

Daitou village Wolong Hill below has a well, beside well built a small shrine-niche-style Three-Officials temple. Xi'an village has a Three-Officials temple, right at village center main street T-junction, facing a straight street, back against wall, whole like a wall niche. Three-Officials temples in pavilions also very exquisite and ornate; e.g.

Huatan east gate watch pavilion, shrine niche and caisson both full and compact, proportion coordinated, color harmonious.

Some temples built inside village, combined with adjacent open space, forming public center. E.g. Zhouzhai village Earth God temple and its front small plaza, Xi'an village Yangfu Ye temple and Hugua well etc.

Yantou village Tahu temple, Cangpo village renji temple and Taiyin temple, all in village's large public garden, and important component of garden. They actually at village's water mouth, located xun position (southeast).

Early Buddhist temples and Daoist temples, mostly in shallow mountain areas away from villages; e.g. above/below Furong Peak Chongguo temple and Guangfu temple, Yantou village north Wutiao Stream beside Longshekeng Shibalong Pu'an temple, etc. Buddhists value quiet cultivation, renouncing worldly dust.

However, in recent years true religion further declined; Chongguo temple and Pu'an temple already ruined; Guangfu temple because changed to enshrine Lu clan Niangniang, Chen Shisi Niangniang etc. seven Niangniang才得以 slight incense, maintained.

庙宇的形制
中文

3. 庙宇的形制

庙宇并没有发育出自己特有的形制来，大多沿袭宗祠，而且大多不如宗祠那样高大宽阔，也没有那样庄严华丽。这种情况，鲜明地反映出中国传统文化的一个基本特点，这是一种伦理性很强的文化，儒家思想在其中占着主导地位，不迷信"怪、力、乱、神"。民俗文化中的泛灵论杂神崇拜，作为不圆满的现实生活的补救，虽然屡禁不止，也只能居于次要地位。

廊下村的太尉庙和凤南宫，形制和规模却与一般大宗祠完全相同，有正殿、两庑、门厅以及门厅之内面对正殿的戏台。正殿也是七开间，两庑都是三开间。正殿和两庑都敞开，不加外檐装修。不过，太尉庙正殿前中央凸出三开间装饰华丽的拜亭，这在楠溪江还是独一无二的。

岩头村公园中琴屿上的塔湖庙，原名孝祐庙，造于明代嘉靖年间。朝北。三进两院，三开间。大殿后的小院落是个水池，满植莲花。后进有楼，楼上奉祀孝祐卢氏尊神，右侧陪祀袁氏夫人。《岩头金氏宗谱》说袁氏"御灾捍患，报名如响，为一方之保障焉"。楼上为敞厅，南侧全部敞开，设一排栏杆椅，下临右军池，远眺芙蓉岩下芙蓉村，田畴如画。尽处淡烟轻霭，缥缈中微露白墙青瓦，小小一簇村舍。这庙宇多少有园林建筑的特色，登高望远，是园林"借景"的手法之一。

塔湖庙也有戏台，但造在大门外，与门相对。戏台屋顶取歇山式，翼角高挑，有后台，用悬山顶。因为是一座独立的建筑，所以轮廓特别活泼。戏台与大门之间8.5米的空地和庙的门屋里是男人们看戏的地方，妇女们则只能在两侧池水对岸远远地看戏，池水是防止男女混杂的鸿沟。庙门三间全部用可拆卸的板门扇隔离内外，演戏时取下，门屋就是极好的观众厅。

起源于永嘉的南戏，本来是为娱神的。唐代顾况诗"何处乐神声，夷歌出烟岛"，说的就是永嘉的风俗。明代劳宜斋著《瓯江逸志》说：

温郡之俗，好巫而近鬼，大率佛事道场，靡不尽心竭力以为之，不惜重费。乃若正月初旬，以至灯市，十馀日昼夜游戏，男女杂沓。竞制龙灯，极其精工，大龙灯一条，所费不下数十金。锣鼓喧阗，举国若狂。

清中叶道光、咸丰间永嘉县令汤成烈纂县志稿，也说："报赛侈鬼神之会，……士女游观，靓妆华服，阗城溢郭，有司莫之能禁。"楠溪江各村，从秋收到来年春种，几个月农闲时节，地方戏常有演出，宗祠和庙宇就是演戏的场所。而看戏的时候男女杂沓，一向是"有司"们认为应该禁止的败俗。

双浚头的三圣庙、太阴庙和娘娘庙，水云村的平水圣王庙和娘娘宫，塘湾村的山隍庙，霞美村的娘娘庙，形制和规模都与一般的小宗祠相同。只有一座五开间的正殿，前面是围墙院落，一座随墙砖门楼。平水圣王庙形式比较特别，它的五开间正殿两端向前出轩一间，这一间上采用歇山顶，而正殿却是普通的悬山顶。构造很复杂，但构件搭接合理，全部露明，充分显示了结构的美。立面也很丰富生动。它的院墙正门与正殿不同轴，不平行，显然是为了风水的缘故。这墙门正对着溪对岸的一座轮廓整齐的山峰。《鲁班经》规定，大门的朝向就是房屋的朝向。这座庙孤处于旷野之中，不知为什么正殿没有稍稍转一个角度以便与墙门一致。

楠溪江中游比较大的庙宇是岩头村以北五鯈溪旁长蛇坑的普安寺和旧卢岙村的圣湖庙。它们都是三进四合院。

普安寺在一个山坳里，四周林木茂盛，苍茫一片。近寺万竿翠竹，竹丛里一座12.6米长的三跨石板桥架在深涧上。桥造于宋宁宗庆元三年（1197年），是全永嘉最古老的。过桥折由东南角到达庙门前。庙的总进深51.10米。第一进是门屋，很简单。第二进是大殿，九开间，进深14.48米，奉如来像一尊。这座大殿是楠溪江中游现存最宏大的建筑物之一。第三进正房依山坡而成两层，上层升高2.40米。西厢已经倒坍。大殿西侧有跨院，院中有水池一口。普安寺的一个特点是廊庑发展为厢房，前后院都有上下两层，总面积相当大。一部分是禅房，一部分做客房，供香客住宿，因为它的位置很荒僻。

全部建筑的风格都是民居式的，朴素而平和。

圣湖庙亦称圣母宫，祀舍身饲虎救母的卢氏，在塘湾村东南、邻近泰石村的一个滨江山湾湾北缘的山坡上。山湾湾地势很低，原来有座卢岙村，就是卢氏孝祐娘娘的故里。宋徽宗乾道二年（1166年），一场洪水毁灭了卢岙村，以后没有恢复，只剩圣湖庙孤孤单单地面对着沧桑变幻。每年雨季水涨，这一片山湾被江湾吞没，汪洋千顷，一起叫做圣母潭。圣湖庙背靠圣母山（原名田螺山），俯视山湾和江湾，景色辽阔，山水重重叠叠，曲曲折折，远处峰峦如屏，点缀些散落的农舍。江上风帆，缓缓来去。

庙的总面阔19.10米，总进深38.80米。第一进是门屋和戏台。第二进是大殿，七开间，两个梢间外侧都有一道屏障，全用花格组成，很别致。神龛里卢氏娘娘足踏一头猛虎。大殿前的院落很宽敞，两侧是双层的廊庑。由于地势关系，大殿高于院落1.30米，很利于看戏，可以看到舞台台面。大殿后的院落十分局促，不过是个天井。天井之后的第三进，顺势上了山坡，高于第二进4.90米。殿里供的是卢氏的母亲，专管子息。特殊的是它的左右两轩，正在天井侧上方，做成了方形的阁子，用歇山顶。

它们不但使小小天井里突现了参差玲珑的画境，也使庙宇的侧面起伏有致。这一部分的做法，很像水云村的平水圣王庙，不拘一格，随兴发挥，显出乡村工匠的智慧。

圣湖庙的建筑风格比较堂皇，近于宗祠，不同于民居。

English

3. form of temples

temples did not develop their own unique form; mostly follow ancestral halls, and mostly not as tall and wide as ancestral halls, nor as solemn and magnificent.

This situation clearly reflects a basic feature of Chinese traditional culture: it is a strongly ethical culture, Confucian thought occupies dominant position, not superstitious about "strange, force, disorder, gods." Folk culture's animist miscellaneous god worship, as remedy for imperfect real life, although repeatedly prohibited but not stopped, can only occupy secondary position.

Langxia village Taiwei temple and Fengnan Palace, form and scale however completely same as ordinary main ancestral halls: have main hall, two side wings, gate hall, and stage inside gate hall facing main hall. Main hall also seven bays, side wings three bays. Main hall and side wings both open, no exterior eave decoration.

However, Taiwei temple main hall front center protrudes three-bay ornately decorated worship pavilion, unique in Nanxi River.

Tahu temple on Qinyu in Yantou village park, originally named Xiaoyou temple, built in Ming Jiajing era. Faces north. Three sections two courtyards, three bays. Small courtyard behind main hall is a pool, full of lotus. Rear section has building; upstairs enshrine Xiaoyou Lu clan honored deity; right side accompanying worship Yuan clan Lady.

"Yantou Jin clan Genealogy" says Yuan clan "resists disasters and wards off misfortunes, responds like echo, is protection of this area." Upstairs is open hall; south side completely open; set a row of railing chairs; below faces Youjun Pool; distant view Furong village below Furong Rock, fields like painting.

Far end light mist and haze, ethereal中 faintly reveal white walls blue tiles, small cluster of village houses. This temple somewhat has garden architecture features; ascending high to view distant, is one of garden "borrowed scenery" techniques.

Tahu temple also has stage, but built outside main gate, opposite gate. Stage roof adopts hip-and-gable style, wing corners high; has backstage, uses overhanging gable roof. Because it is an independent building, outline particularly lively.

8.5m open space between stage and gate, and temple's gate house, are where men watch drama; women can only watch from far across pool on both sides; pool is gulf preventing men-women mixing. temple gate three bays all use removable board doors to separate inside/outside; during performance removed, gate house becomes excellent audience hall.

Nanxi (Southern Opera) originated in Yongjia, originally for entertaining gods. Tang dynasty Gu Kuang poem "Where is music-god sound, barbarian songs emerge from misty island" — describes Yongjia custom. Ming dynasty Lao Yizhai's "Ou Jiang Yi Zhi" says:

Wenzhou prefecture customs, like shaman and near ghosts, mostly Buddhist affairs and Daoist rites, all do with utmost effort, not sparing heavy expense. As for early first month, to lantern market, over ten days day-night games, men-women mixed.

Compete making dragon lanterns, extremely exquisite; one large dragon lantern, cost no less than tens of gold. Drums and gongs noisy, whole country as if mad.

Mid-Qing Daoguang-Xianfeng era Yongjia county magistrate Tang Chenglie compiled county annals draft, also said: "Thanksgiving sacrifices extravagantly hold god-spirit gatherings, ...

men-women sightseeing, dressed up, filling city and suburbs, officials cannot prohibit." Nanxi River villages, from autumn harvest to next spring planting, several months slack season, local operas often performed; ancestral halls and temples are performance venues.

And watching drama men-women mixed, always is "officials" consider should prohibit decadent custom.

Shuangjuntou Sansheng temple, Taiyin temple and Niangniang temple; Shuiyun village Pingshui Holy King temple and Niangniang Palace; Tangwan village Shanhuang temple; Xiamei village Niangniang temple — form and scale all same as ordinary branch ancestral halls. Only one five-bay main hall; front is walled courtyard, a brick gate tower along wall.

Pingshui Holy King temple form比较 special: its five-bay main hall both ends forward protrude one bay xuan (porch); this one bay uses hip-and-gable roof, while main hall is ordinary overhanging gable roof. construction very complex, but component joints reasonable, all exposed, fully displays structural beauty. Facade also rich and lively.

Its courtyard wall main gate not coaxial nor parallel with main hall, obviously for fengshui reason. This wall gate directly faces a neatly outlined mountain peak across stream. "Lu Ban Jing" stipulates, main gate orientation is building orientation.

This temple isolated in wilderness,不知 why main hall did not slightly turn angle to align with wall gate.

Nanxi River middle relatively large temples are Pu'an temple at Longshekeng beside Wutiao Stream north of Yantou village, and Shenghu temple at former Lu'ao village. They are both three-section four-courtyards.

Pu'an temple in a mountain col, surrounding forest lush, vast一片. Near temple ten thousand bamboo; in bamboo grove a 12.6m long three-span stone slab bridge spans deep ravine. bridge built in Song Ningzong Qingyuan three year (1197), oldest in all Yongjia. Cross bridge turn from southeast corner to temple gate. temple total depth 51.10m.

First section gate house, very simple. Second section main hall, nine bays, depth 14.48m, enshrine one Tathagata statue. This main hall is one of Nanxi River middle existing grandest buildings. Third section main house依 hillside into two stories, upper raised 2.40m. West wing already collapsed.

Main hall west side has attached courtyard, courtyard has a pool. Pu'an temple one feature: corridors develop into wing rooms; front/rear courtyards both have upper/lower two stories, total area相当 large. Part is meditation rooms, part guest rooms for pilgrims lodging, because its location very remote.

All building style is residential-style, simple and peaceful.

Shenghu temple also called Holy Mother Palace, worships Lu clan who sacrificed self to tiger to save mother; on hillside at north edge of a riverside mountain bay southeast of Tangwan village, near Taishi village. Mountain bay terrain very low; originally had Lu'ao village, was Lu clan Xiaoyou Niangniang's hometown.

Song Huizong Qiandao two year (1166), a flood destroyed Lu'ao village, later not recovered; only Shenghu temple alone facing vicissitudes. Every rainy season water rises, this mountain bay swallowed by river bend, vast expanse, together called Holy Mother Pool.

Shenghu temple backs Holy Mother Mountain (originally Tianluo Mountain), overlooks mountain bay and river bend, scenery vast, mountains and waters layered, winding; distant peaks like screen, dotted with scattered farmhouses. River sails slowly come and go.

temple total facade 19.10m, total depth 38.80m. First section gate house and stage. Second section main hall, seven bays; both end bay outer sides have a screen, all made of lattice, very unique. In shrine niche Lu clan Niangniang foot treads a fierce tiger. courtyard before main hall very spacious; both sides double-story corridors.

Due to terrain, main hall 1.30m higher than courtyard, very favorable for watching drama, can see stage surface. courtyard behind main hall very cramped, just a skylight courtyard. Third section after skylight,顺势 up hillside, 4.90m higher than second section. Hall enshrines Lu clan's mother, specifically governs childbirth.

Special is its left/right two xuan, right above skylight side, made into square pavilions, use hip-and-gable roof. They not only make small skylight courtyard suddenly present irregular exquisite painting scene, also make temple side undulating.

This part's practice, much like Shuiyun village Pingshui Holy King temple, not constrained by one pattern, improvise, showing village craftsmen's wisdom.

Shenghu temple's architectural style比较 grand, close to ancestral hall, different from residence.
